# Agent notes (nw-tracker)

> Global, project-agnostic rules (fail-fast, provenance, testing, i18n principles,
> commit etiquette, …) live in **GUIDELINES.md**. This file carries the app-specific
> conventions and mechanics.

## Language and i18n

- **UI copy via i18n (es + en)**: the UI is bilingual. All user-facing strings live in **`client/src/i18n/master.json`** — the single source of truth, same nesting as the locale files but every leaf holds both languages side by side: `{ "en": "Save", "es": "Guardar" }`. `locales/es.json` / `locales/en.json` are **generated** from it by `npm run i18n:generate -w nw-tracker-client` (`client/scripts/i18n-generate.mjs`); never hand-edit the locale files — `client/src/i18n/localeSync.test.ts` fails on any drift. The master stays nested (not flat dotted keys) because some leaf keys contain literal dots (e.g. `panelAccounts.addAccount."accountType.equity"`). Do not hardcode Spanish or English labels in components except via `t('…')` / `i18n.t('…')`.
- **Translation keys in English**: use dotted paths such as `dashboard.cards.netWorth`, `brokerage.groups.mutual_funds`. Code identifiers use English slugs (`mutual_funds`, `real_estate`); display copy lives in `master.json`.
- **Shared labels**: use helpers in `client/src/i18n/index.ts` (e.g. `ccExpenseCategoryLabel`, `flowsPlBucketLabel`; a nav node's label is the server's, resolved with `resolveNavTreeLabel`) so the sidebar, dashboard cards, and charts read the same keys.
- When adding UI text, add the key (both languages) to `master.json` first, run `npm run i18n:generate -w nw-tracker-client`, then reference it from code. Migrate existing hardcoded strings when touching an area.
- **UI language preference**: `client/src/languagePreference.ts` (localStorage `nw-tracker.language`), seeded once from `navigator.language` (Spanish → `es`, anything else → `en`; non-browser/vitest contexts stay `es` so label assertions are deterministic), then the stored value is authoritative. Changed from `/panel/settings` via `DisplayPreferencesContext.setLanguage` (calls `i18n.changeLanguage`, persists, and keeps `<html lang>` in sync; the context change re-renders `AppTree` top-down). The decimal separator remains an independent setting. **Do not cache `t()` results** in `useMemo`/state without the language in the deps — translate at render time.
- **Dates**: numeric dates render as ISO `YYYY-MM-DD` (timestamps `YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM`) in every language — never `dd-mm-yy` / `toLocale*` short styles, which are ambiguous across locales. Month-name labels (`dic 2026` ↔ `Dec 2026`, chart ticks `dic 16` ↔ `Dec 16`) go through `client/src/formatDateLabel.ts`, which reads `i18n.language` at call time — same no-caching rule as `t()`.
- **Enforced by `npm run check:conventions`** (root; also runs at the end of root `npm run typecheck`): flags accented Spanish in client string literals/JSX (comments exempt), `Intl.NumberFormat` outside `format.ts`, hardcoded-locale `toLocaleString` without date options, and month-name tables outside `formatDateLabel.ts`, and `isFetching` / `isRefetching` in `client/src/components` / `pages` (see «Loading states»), and ticker-suffix parsing (`.endsWith(".SN")`, `lastIndexOf(".")` on a ticker, `\.SN$` regexes, `LIKE '%.SN'`) in `server/src` / `client/src` / `ingest/src` outside `server/src/priceProviders/` (see «Instrument registry and price providers») (2026-09-26: `formatYmEs` and two private copies had kept month labels Spanish in the English UI). It also flags currency hardcoding (2026-11-12, docs/any-currency-plan.md): `formatClp` / `formatUsd` (and `formatClpUfDay`, `formatUsdFine`) in `client/src/components` / `pages`, a unit or currency compared with a literal code, fixed currency lists and `"clp" | "usd"` unions outside `rates.ts` / `valueConvert.ts` / `displayUnits.ts`, and `_clp` / `_usd` / `_eur` keys in `server/contracts/src/dto`; these ratchet against `scripts/check-currency.baseline.json` (a file may not gain one; `node scripts/check-conventions.mjs --write-baseline` locks in a shrink, `--force` accepts growth), and a migration above 312 with `CHECK (… IN ('clp' …))` fails outright (reference `currencies(code)`). Escape hatch: `// convention-ok: <reason>` on the line.
- **Dependency guard, also in `npm run check:conventions` (2026-11-07; `scripts/check-deps.mjs`, baseline `scripts/check-deps.baseline.json`).** It builds the TypeScript import graph of `server/src`, `client/src`, `ingest/src` and `server/contracts/src` (tests excluded, type-only imports counted, `.js` resolved to `.ts`/`.tsx`, `nw-tracker-contracts` to its `index.ts`) and fails when a file joins an import cycle the baseline does not list, when a new edge crosses a forbidden boundary (contracts → server/client/ingest, client ↔ server, ingest ↔ server: `server/contracts` is the only shared code), or when `cacheRegistry.ts` imports a module it did not (the guard named the deleted `aggregationCache.ts` until 2026-11-09 and never fired). It prints the largest cycle, the edge count against the baseline's and the top fan-in, fan-out and transitive-reach files. Cycles may only shrink: after a change that shrinks them, `node scripts/check-deps.mjs --write-baseline` locks the smaller graph in (docs/untangle-plan.md F1). Shared formats (numbers, dd/mm and ISO days, day arithmetic, year-months and Spanish month names, accent folding, RUT and account numbers, merchant text, the zod building blocks) live in `server/contracts/src/formats/`, each rule pinned by a case file in `server/contracts/data/` (`npm run test -w nw-tracker-contracts`); the Python parsers assert the same files.
- **Number formatting**: all numbers (CLP, USD, UF, units, percents) share one decimal-separator convention from `client/src/numberFormatPreference.ts` (localStorage `nw-tracker.decimalSeparator`, seeded from the IANA timezone; toolbar toggle overrides). Always format via `client/src/format.ts` helpers — never `Intl.NumberFormat` / `toLocaleString` with a hardcoded locale for numbers (dates: see the Dates bullet above — ISO numerics + `formatDateLabel.ts` month names). A separator change re-renders (not remounts) the tree via `AppTree`; **do not cache formatted strings** in `useMemo`/state unless `decimalSeparator` is in the deps — memoize raw numbers and format at render time. **Typed numbers (2026-09-27):** every number field reads through `parseNumberInput` / `parseNumberInputOrThrow` (format.ts). A number that fits one convention is read that way («1500,5», «1.500.000», «1,234.56»); only a single mark followed by exactly three digits («1.500») is decided by the setting (1500 with comma decimals, 1.5 with period decimals); anything else is rejected with `common.numberInvalid`, and forms keep their own sign/range/integer rules. Prefills and placeholders use `formatNumberInput`. Never `Number()` / `parseFloat` / `.replace(/\./g, "")` on typed text, and no `type="number"` inputs (the browser parses those by its own locale). The flows amount filter (`flowsAmountFilter.ts`) also takes an amount copied from the table: a leading `$` / `US$` and the negative «($500)».

## Fail fast + provenance — app specifics

The fail-fast/no-fallbacks and free-text-is-provenance rules live in **GUIDELINES.md**. App-specific instantiations:

- Existing docs follow the no-fallback rule (e.g. CC matrix month = `period_to` only, no filename/statement-date guesses) — extend it to new code.
- **Notes are provenance (2026-07):** movement/account `note` text is for humans; runtime never branches on note substrings and never reads `cfraser/` files on request paths. Structured homes for machine state: `movements.flow_kind` (deposit/mortgage/brokerage kinds), `loan_payments` + the property's UF `valuations` (were `depto_payments` until migration 258), `movement_mirror_merges`, `accounts.import_key`, `credit_card_account_config.card_last4` / `.nav_retired` / `.superseded_target_last4` (the config `notes` column was dropped in migration 160).

## Personal data pipeline (cfraser) — see PARSERS.md

Docs for the personal import pipeline — credit-card PDF parsing quirks, Fintual
certificado, AFP UNO certificates — live in **untracked `PARSERS.md`**
(gitignored, imported by CLAUDE.md; main checkout only, like the `cfraser/` data it
documents). If missing, restore from `~/Documents/backups/nw-tracker/`. The excel/CSV
reconstruction pipeline (import:excel, DAP/cuenta-ahorro/depto-expense CSV importers)
was retired 2026-07-10 — the DB plus snapshots are the single source of truth; new
movements are entered manually or via the document importers below.

## CC chart / Pasivos convention

**"owed on that date", not billing-month totals.** Per-card chart lines, the Pasivos group Total, `accountMarkClpAtYmd`, and the net-worth dashboard Pasivos line all read stored `valuations` via `latestCreditCardValuationRowAsOf`. **Today is walked, not live-formula'd (2026-07-23):** every date — today included — is `last anchor strictly before it + evidence dated after it` (`ccOwedWalkClpAtYmd` in `ccOwedWalk.ts`, the single choke point `latestCreditCardDisplayedBalance` now uses; `as_of_date` comes back as the walked-through date so callers don't re-apply the window). The live billing formula (facturado + cupo en cuotas − cuota a pagar next mes) is a DIFFERENT framing of the same debt, so valuing today with it while history was walked dumped the whole frame gap into today's delta — ·0101 read +1x.xxx (exactly one TGR cuota, 9x.xxx ÷ 6) on a day with no purchase. Today's stamp is written from the walk too, so tomorrow's anchor is in the same frame. Billing surfaces (Balance total, detalle por mes, cupo) keep the live formula — that frame is their point. Consequence: a card's displayed balance is the evidence walk (·0781 7.xxx.xxx vs live 7.xxx.xxx), and it re-syncs to statement truth at each month-end anchor; there is no separate "Saldo pasivos" ref line (removed — Total carries the same series). The month-end points `upsertCreditCardValuationsFromLedger` writes are billing-detail `balance_total` = owed-at-cierre = **facturado + post-close plan remainder** (the plan remainder `installmentRemainingClpByCalendarMonth`, cuotas due strictly after the month — the just-billed cuota rides inside facturado only) + post-cierre window activity (charges +, PAGOs −) — evidence-based, re-synced after web-paste repairs in `mergeCcAccountFromParsedRows`. **The old `− cuota a pagar next mes` term was removed 2026-08-12:** it netted a double count that only exists when the cupo figure still CONTAINS the billed cuota (the bank's live cupo frame); against the post-close remainder actually used it sank every closed month-end anchor one billed cuota below true owed — provable at the floor (·0781 2025-01-31 anchor $2.xxx.xxx < $3.xxx.xxx of unbilled cuotas after the facturado had been paid in full), and visible as «deuda en cuotas» crossing above «saldo total» on every month whose payment landed on/before month-end (monthly view) plus the matching daily residuals. Anchors re-stamped the same day (see `server/scripts/restamp-cc-anchor-frame.ts`). **Today-stamps self-purge on contradicted evidence (2026-07-23):** `upsertCreditCardValuationsFromLedger` also stamps a `today` valuation on every CC write, freezing what the live formula said that day. Those stamps are only valid until dated evidence arrives for an earlier day — a paste of last week's purchases would otherwise leave the walk climbing through them and then snapping back, dumping the whole import into *today's* delta (·0781+·0101 read +3xx.xxx just past midnight on 2026-07-23). So the writer takes **`affectedEvidenceFromYmd`** and deletes every non-month-end stamp from that date through yesterday before rewriting (month-end rows are statement-derived and the same run re-syncs them; the purge runs before the has-points early return — removing wrong data must not depend on having new data). **Every caller that adds or removes dated evidence passes it**: the merge import (`earliestInsertedTxDate` over genuinely INSERTED lines — dedupe-skips contribute nothing, so re-pasting is idempotent — plus new `purchase_date`s and the header pagado date), line deletions (`earliestTransactionDateForLineIds`, read BEFORE deleting), and manual installment create/edit/delete (edits use min(old, new) date). Pure recomputes omit it. Trade-off: a purged stamp loses that day's live-frame correction; the walk carries from the previous anchor. Older manual audits stay relevant only for balance-convention changes (six stale ·0781 rows from the missing-PAGO era were purged 2026-07-22; four contradicted 2026-07 stamps purged 2026-07-23).

- **A card's P/L is its cost of financing (2026-07-22), loss-negative like the mortgage.** Liability accounts mark as positive debt, so every P/L surface uses `pl = prior − owed − flow` — daily series, monthly `nominal_pl`, dashboard `delta_day`, group P/L bars. For a card, P/L = **−(section-3 charges: intereses, comisiones, impuestos)** dated on the day each was charged (`ccFinancingCostDaily.ts`; the classifier is the same `isClp/UsdSection3FinancingChargeMerchant` predicate the financing chart sums, so the two agree — held by `ccFinancingIdentity.test.ts` against the financing chart's section-3 sum). Everything else that moves owed is **flow**, and it is **derived** (`flow = −Δowed + financing`), never summed from events: the stored anchors are live-formula stamps, so at a cierre the billing frame re-splits the same debt and the balance steps ~1M against a walk that saw no transaction (·0781 2026-06-19: +1.xxx.xxx) — measurement noise in the buy/pay legs, not cost. Cards therefore read `pl = 0` on ordinary days and only the bank's charges show up. **Interest inside installment cuotas is financing cost too (2026-10-05; `ccInstallmentInterest.ts`):** for a plan its statements print with a rate above 0, interest = the plan total (Σ printed cuotas) − the printed principal (every cuota line's `amount_clp`), dated on the purchase date — the owed walk adds the whole total that day, so the derived flow stays at the principal. A 0% plan that rounds a few pesos above its principal (BCI) has none, and a plan no statement has printed yet (hand-entered, from the feed) has none until one does. The dashboard's financing summary reads it in the purchase's billing month; the old annual-rate model (`annual_interest_pct`, always 0) was removed. **In «deuda en cuotas» the interest bills cuota by cuota (2026-10-05; `ccInstallmentInterestBillingEvents`):** the plan-debt walk adds such a plan's principal on the purchase date and each cuota's share of the interest at the close of the statement that bills it (projected close for one not billed yet), as the bank's owed counts it; the retirement still removes whole cuotas. Adding the total at purchase had put the unbilled interest above the owed line (·0161 PARIS 2023-02/03). The owed walk and the P/L keep the whole total on the purchase date. On the real DB: two ·0161 plans of 2023, 2x.xxx + 2x.xxx. **The fx move on unpaid dollars is P/L too (2026-11-02):** dollars owed at the end of the previous day × the change in the debt-side rate (`ccRevaluationCostByDate`, see «Card dollars in pesos»). Card flows are NOT personal capital: they stay out of `/flows/deposits`, the deposits reconciliation, and the "aportes acum." companions, which is also why the `pago_tarjeta` mirror transfer's card leg emits nothing (`accountDeposits.ts` — the cash leg does emit its withdrawal). The mortgage's daily flow leg is `mortgageSheetPaymentEventsThroughDate` (depto ledger, same source as its dashboard cards).
- **One schedule framing (2026-07-13): the installment plan schedule is facturación/statement months everywhere.** `purchaseFirstDueYm` + index gives the month each cuota **bills**; per-month plan sums equal the statements' billed-cuota sums exactly, and `cuotaAPagarNextMesClp` / facturaciones read the billing month itself (never +1). Anchor precedence: cuota-01 statement month → cuota-00 preamble's statement month + 1 → stored `first_due_month` (web-paste nudge, write-once evidence) → manual guess = the purchase's **own** cycle as the bank closes it (`billingMonthContainingPurchase`; date-based so it can't drift; the next statement's evidence relocates it either way — cuota-01 pins the month, a cuota-00 preamble moves it to statement + 1). **Manual-source anchors clamp to the first pending facturación (2026-07-27):** the stored/guessed month for a plan with no statement cuota evidence advances to `last fully-closed month + 1` — an imported statement that didn't bill the plan is negative evidence, so an evidence-less manual plan can't inflate a closed month's cuota sums; statement-driven (never the calendar), so a lagging import queue keeps the date-based guess. **A facturación only closes when every statement currency the card's history carries is imported** (`lastPdfBillingMonthForAccount` / `accountRequiresUsdStatementClose` — the July-2026 USD twin arriving alone must not advance `open_billing_month` or retarget web pastes, and it settles only its OWN currency's lines in the open web-paste bucket — since 2026-09-26 `reconcileOpenWebPasteAfterPdfClose` settles per currency before the full close, because keeping the whole bucket counted the first twin's lines twice until the second arrived (the September 2026 ·0781 USD statement, imported hours before its CLP twin, billed US$x.xxx,xx for US$x.xxx,xx); a dormant never-billed USD side keeps closing on CLP alone; and a USD twin still missing once the bank has closed a LATER facturación — a later statement in either currency, a feed-observed close, or an announced close that has passed — is not coming, so the month closes on CLP alone: `pdfClosedBillingMonthsForAccount`, 2026-09-26, after every USD-quiet cycle had stayed «open» for good, the retired ·0161's CLP-only 2025 months among them, which the merged group ledger then flagged open as well. The bucket reconcile keeps the stricter per-twin check, `hasEveryStatementTwinForBillingMonth`: a CLP-only month's dollar lines still wait for a USD statement. A card that stopped billing, `cardStoppedBilling` (`cards/lifecycle.ts`), gets no provisional facturación rows, mirroring its detalle — ·0161's November statement announced a December close that no statement will ever follow). Web-paste-converted plans carry the issuer card_group (`'santander'`/`'BCI'`), PDF statements the parser groups (`'A'`/`'B'`/`'INTL'`) — the manual↔statement twin reconcile treats the web-paste group as compatible with any PDF group and tolerates ±2 days of purchase-date skew on indexed cuota lines (exact merchant/amount/cuotas still required). Nota-cancelled plans (full-principal NOTA DE CREDITO matched by posting **statement month** — banks backdate the transaction date, never the statement — or by a strictly-later transaction date) are excluded from every schedule aggregate via the loader's `schedulePurchases`; live cupo therefore matches the printed «cupo utilizado en cuotas». A fully **settled** ledger no longer force-actives a card (`cardStoppedBilling` (`cards/lifecycle.ts`) requires outstanding principal, else the tail heuristic decides).
- **Cuotas por facturación (`payload.months`)**: one row per facturación event — `month` (facturación) + `pay_by_date` (statement PAGAR HASTA when closed; derived ~10th of the next month for open/projected) + `total_clp` + `debt_after_clp` (suffix sum; last row 0). Rows roll off once their pay-by passes (Chile day — the same clock that flips a fully-paid purchase from active to completed); `totals.next_calendar_month` reports the **pay** month of the next payment event. Purchase tables show `purchase_date` + `purchase_billing_month` (the bank's cycle containing the purchase date, `billingMonthContainingPurchase`), not the calendar purchase month.
- **Facturación proxy earnings are keyed by the facturación that billed the money (2026-09-26; `ccInvestmentProxy.ts`).** `facturacion_proxy` (the facturaciones table's proxy column, looked up by `billing_month`) invests each purchase from its purchase date until the pay-by of the facturación that billed it. A printed cuota is keyed by the plan schedule's month (`first_due_month` + N − 1) and withdrawn on its payment row's `pay_by_date`; a one-shot line is keyed by `facturacionMonthByStatementDate` (a stale bucket's lines → the open month) and withdrawn on that facturación's pay-by as the table shows it (`facturacionPayByIsoResolver`), so open and provisional web-paste lines take part. Superseded lines and count-less cuota purchases are left out, as the facturado leaves them out; an unparseable line date throws. Before: cuota gains sat one row late (pay-by month), bucket lines dropped out, and dd/mm/yy PDF rows were skipped.
- **Months after the open facturación are plan-only projections** (`projected: true` rows from `appendProjectedBillingDetailRows`), **pay-frame**: facturado is null (nothing billed yet) and saldo/cupo at month-end M = plan remainder after (M−1)'s close — a cycle's cuotas leave the debt on their **pay-by** (~10th of M+1), not at the close that bills them. One facturación step per month, flat from today through the open month-end, plus a trailing zero month after the final pay-by. Identity: chart month-end M = calendar-table row M's cuota + `debt_after_clp`. Closed months keep the statement frame (printed cupo; billed-unpaid cuotas ride inside facturado) and today keeps the live value — the seam is smooth because bank-frame(M) ≡ pay-frame(M+1). `latestBillingDetailRow` skips projected rows so the live «Balance total» / today's Pasivos point stays the open-month rolled balance. **The historial CHART's two lines are the daily data sampled (2026-10-05; `ccHistorialLinesAtMonthEndsClp`, passed as `linesForMonths` to `buildCcHistorialChartSeries` by the card payload and by `creditCardGroupLedgerResponse` summed over the same masters as the daily block):** «saldo total» (the owed walk) and «deuda en cuotas» (the plan-debt walk) on each month's last day, TODAY for the current calendar month, the plan tail after today (the plan debt once its last pay-by has passed); bars stay per facturación. Payment frame at every month, so the monthly and daily charts show the same numbers on the same day. Until 2026-10-05 only the cuota line was sampled (since 2026-09-26) and «saldo total» read the detalle row, whose open month was a roll-forward from the last statement that left out cuota plans bought in the cycle: converting the ·0101 payment line (1.xxx.xxx) to a plan dropped October's saldo below its cuotas. That roll-forward now adds them too (`installmentPurchasesClpAfter`, plans bought after the closed month's CALENDAR month-end — its cupo already carries the ones bought between its close and month-end). The detalle table's «cupo en cuotas» keeps the statement frame above (facturado + cupo = balance). A closed month used to plot its post-close remainder, one billed cycle below the daily line whenever the payment landed after month-end (·0901 31/08: 7,xx M monthly vs 8,xx M daily).
- **Historial bars (2026-09-26): one stacked bar per facturación**, bottom to top «Facturado CLP» (CLP facturado − the cuotas: únicos and the bank's charges, net of notas de crédito — negative when credits outweigh them, drawn below zero with `stackOffset="sign"`), «Facturado CLP (cuotas)» (the facturación's `cuota_a_pagar_clp`: the plan schedule, which equals the statement's billed cuotas) and «Facturado US$» (pesos at the rate the facturación was actually paid, see «Card dollars in pesos»; the tooltip shows «US$ X ($Y)» like the facturaciones table), one amber hue with the cuotas darkest; the tooltip reads top to bottom like the chart (saldo total, deuda en cuotas, total facturado over its segments). Built server-side in `buildCcHistorialChartSeries`; a month the plan only projects carries just its cuotas, and an open month no line has landed in yet (billing detail has it, facturaciones do not — no statement, no bucket) splits its detail facturado as CLP: with no lines there is nothing in dollars. **A group's bar is the Σ of its cards' own bars** (`memberSeries`), and the merged billing-detail row adds a projected member's cuota to a month another member has billed (`mergedTotalFacturadoClp` — the table's «≈ cuota» for that card alone), so the group table and the bars agree; neither is split again from merged facturaciones: a month one card has billed or opened while another only projects has no merged facturación carrying the second card's cuotas (the old grey «Pagos de cuotas» bar dropped ·0101's 3x.xxx from October 2026 on the Pasivos pages that way). **Day mode draws one stack per billing month, like the monthly chart (2026-10-11)** (`facturacionBarsOnMonthCloseDates`, from the same cached bundle as the monthly bars): the Σ of the members' bars for the month, dated on the latest close among the members that billed it (the facturaciones table's close, a projected month's `closeEvidenceForBillingMonth`); a member with no bar in the month lends neither pesos nor its close, and a card alone keeps its own closes. Until then each card's bar sat on its own close, so ·0901 (~24th) and ·0101 (~26th) read as two facturaciones two days apart on the group pages; the client runs the grid on past the plan tail to a later close, draws fixed-width 6 px segments (Recharts shrinks `barSize` to a sub-pixel day band) and lists the bars within `athTooltipIndexTolerance` rows in the tooltip. The «Coste financiero vs facturado» chart went with the old grey bar: its per-month financing cost is no longer on the card pages (the billing-month builder behind it, `buildCreditCardFinancingPlByBillingMonth`, went on 2026-10-28 with the dashboard card's summary — see «One month series per account»), and so did the per-purchase `extraOffsets` schedule shift it alone consumed — request param, client state and the legacy offset inputs removed end to end; stale `nw-credit-card-extra-offsets:<id>` localStorage keys are swept at boot (`DisplayPreferencesContext`).

- **Card dollars in pesos: values follow the date's rate, costs keep the paid rate (2026-11-02; `ccOwedWalk.ts`, migration 259, `docs/holdings-cards-plan.md`).** A card has no currency: it owes a sub-balance per currency it bills in (`CcOwedNative`, a map by currency, pesos always — 2026-11-12, any-currency C3; the fictional euro issuer `euronova` is the test case, `ccEuroCardOwed.test.ts`), and its VALUE on a date (the owed walk, the month-end anchors, the Pasivos lines, the cash netting, the detalle's month frame, the daily CC block) is each one converted at that date's rate with `convertCurrency` (debt side: dollars owed in pesos at the buy rate, a currency in credit at the asset side; live today). Evidence stays in the currency the bank states it (`normalizedPostCloseLines` entries carry `currency` + `amount`: a dollar line, payment, header leg or traspaso leg in dollars; a line in a third currency in its own, its pesos at the debt side of its facturación's debt-frame date). **A statement line stores `amount` + `currency` + `cuota_amount` (migration 319):** a line's currency is its own, not always its statement's (an open bucket is one peso statement row holding dollar lines); `amount_clp` / `amount_usd` / `valor_cuota_mensual_clp` / `_usd` are read-only generated columns with the values they always had (a peso line: pesos, dollars NULL; a dollar line: 0 pesos, its dollars; any other currency: NULL in all four), so writers write the stored columns and readers not yet moved keep reading the legacy names; the kind is `cc_line_kind_native` (`cardLineKind276.ts`), the import fingerprint hashes the legacy shape (unchanged hashes), and `legacyAmountColumn` (`cards/statementStorage.ts`) is the one mapping, the walk's anchors are per currency in `cc_owed_anchors` (the month-end ones are the statement frame split by currency: `ccStatementOwedByCurrencyByMonth` builds it at three uniform dollar rates and throws unless the balance is affine in the rate), and `valuations` keeps one CLP row per anchor date = its value at that date's rate (the group charts and the liabilities overview read it). A traspaso de deuda moves dollars out of one sub-balance and pesos into the other on the same day. The fx move on unpaid dollars is P/L (user decision 2026-11-02): `ccFinancingCostByDateInUnit(card, unit)` (`ccFinancingCostDaily.ts`) = section-3 charges + plan interest (in pesos `ccSection3FinancingClpByDate`, what `ccFinancingIdentity.test.ts` checks; in another unit each charge in its billed currency at its own day's debt-side rate) + `ccRevaluationCostByDate(card, unit)` (`ccOwedWalk.ts`: each sub-balance owed in another currency than the unit (every currency the card owes in) × the day's change of its rate into the unit — in pesos the dollars (and euros) owed at the end of the previous day × the change in the buy rate, in dollars the pesos owed × the change in the sell rate; the dollars are dollars). **A dollar payment counts at the pesos actually paid (user decision 2026-11-02):** where it has its real rate on file (`usdDebtPaymentsForAccount`: the divisas purchase paired with it, a traspaso de deuda's peso leg, the traced cost of dollars paid from a USD cash account, `usdCashCostLots.ts`), the payment day's peso term adds `pesos paid − dollars paid × the day's rate`, so the paid dollars' fx P/L that day is `dollars × (real rate − the previous day's debt rate)` and the payment's flow is the pesos paid; a facturación's whole currency P/L is the pesos paid less its charges' pesos when made, and a traspaso's step against the buy rate (the bank's conversion) is fx P/L, its two legs netting to zero flow. A payment with no real rate on file keeps the day's rate. The derived flow `−Δowed + cost` in the unit (daily series: the card's own dollar values in USD, `ccDerivedFlows`; monthly rows: `ccFinancingCostBetweenInUnit`; the dashboard day cell) is the evidence at each event's day rate, P/L = −cost = Δvalue − flow in that unit, and days sum to months (one map). For a card shown in USD, phase 2 reads `ccOwedByCurrencyAtYmd` (the CLP entry points cannot express it). The statement frame itself (`balance_statement_clp`: the Cupo card's used figure, the stored snapshot) still converts at the facturación's pay-by − 1 (`balanceUsdFxDateIso`). Re-stamp: `server/scripts/restamp-cc-owed-anchors.ts [--apply]`. What a facturación SHOWS it cost — its row's «Facturado US$» pesos, the historial bars and its expense lines (Expenses page, facturación modal) — is the rate actually paid: the dollar-debt payments after its close and up to the next close, weighted — divisas purchases (`pago_tarjeta` transfers with a USD counter leg: the pesos that left checking, the dollars the card was credited) and traspasos de deuda (`cc_traspaso_deuda_links`); today's rate (`fxForLiveMtm`) while it is unpaid and its pay-by is still ahead (the pay-by day included); the pay-by − 1 debt rate when the pay-by passed with no payment on file (older ·0161 facturaciones paid by means the data does not record). Rows carry `usd_rate_clp` / `usd_rate_source` (`paid` | `live` | `pay_by`; a merged group row names a rate only when its members agree), and expense lines read their facturación's rate from the same cached rows (`facturacionUsdRateResolver`), so the modal adds up to the row. A dollar payment line (`isCcUsdPaymentLine`: ABONO DE DIVISAS, a PAGO / MONTO CANCELADO in dollars, a traspaso's USD leg) is printed on the statement after the facturación it pays, so it shows the pesos actually paid for it (`usdPaymentLineClpResolver`, 2026-09-27): the divisas purchase paired with it (found through the payment's evidence key, see «Checking↔CC payment mirrors»), a traspaso's CLP leg (as the owed walk values it), else the rate of the facturación it paid — the one whose (close, next close] window holds its date (`facturacionPaidOn`, the window its paid rate sums over). What adds up to the row is the modal's Gastos + Coste financiero; the Abonos pay the previous facturación and never enter it. **Which lines are Coste financiero is the server's call (2026-10-28; `markFinancingCostLines` in `flowsExpenses.ts`):** every expense line carries `is_financing_cost` when it is a positive one-shot card line whose merchant the card's P/L counts as a section-3 charge (`isClp/UsdSection3FinancingChargeMerchant`, `ccStatementLineRules.json`), and the modal only picks it; the client's own copy of the rules (`ccStatementSection3.ts`, deleted) had drifted and filed the CMR card's «Servicio Administracion» / «Impuesto ITE» (2020) under Gastos. Import dedupe keeps the statement-date fx (it only compares amounts). On the real DB: ·0901 22 facturaciones paid + September live; ·0161 54 paid + 23 pay_by.
- **A card payment made in dollars counts at the pesos those dollars cost (2026-10-09; `foreignCashCostLots.ts`, migration 231; any foreign currency since 2026-11-12).** A `pago_tarjeta` transfer from a USD cash account (`currency = 'usd'`, no counter leg — Santander USD paying the card from a Fintual wire) enters the facturación's paid rate (`usdDebtPaymentsForAccount`, still dollar-only) at a traced cost (`foreignOutflowPesoCostByMovement`: any foreign cash account paying a card in its own currency, priced in that currency; a conversion from another foreign currency carries the pesos the converted slices cost; a `market` lot at the currency's stored mid through `rates.ts`, the dollar's the `fx_daily` close): dollar lots walked FIFO through the USD cash accounts in scope (the payment's source plus, recursively, every USD cash account that sent dollars into one in scope), each lot a `purchase` (a CLP→USD transfer's pesos), an `own_transfer` (the pesos the source's outflow consumed) or `market` (any other dollar inflow at `fxRowOnOrBefore` of its date; no rate throws); a shortfall above a cent throws, as does a dollar payment from a non-USD-cash account or one the tracer did not price. **A broker withdrawal reserves at request time:** each `broker_withdrawal_requests` row takes its gross FIFO off the source queue at `requested_at`, later buys cannot spend it, its booked transfer (`incoming_wire_bookings`) carries ALL the reserved pesos for the net (the delivered dollars bear the fee) and the fee draws the rest at 0; a booked reservation must end empty and a booking whose transfer was deleted throws. Events run by Chile date, within a day by time (all timed) else arrivals first and then by id (the ledger writes a buy and the wire that funded it on one day in either order: Fintual USD 2024-12-10), with requests placed among their account's events — and a request's day must have a time on every other event of its account, else it throws. Times live in **`movement_event_times`** (migration 231: movement → ISO UTC `occurred_at`, `source = 'broker_mail'`, message id), stamped by the Fintual branch of `broker.notifications` with the mail's send time for every row it writes, promotes or matches as a duplicate — so re-sending the staged mails stamps movements already in the ledger; a stamp is never overwritten (`event_times_stamped` in the details). The first case: a 2026-10-05 request reserved only that morning's dollar purchase, so the 10-09 card payment counts at that purchase's rate, not the day's; the 10-07 SOXQ buy spends what the request left of 10-05 plus 10-07's dollars.

- **Facturado financing links (`cc_facturado_financing_links`, «Financiamiento de facturado en cuotas»; two fixes 2026-09-21).** A facturado of one card (·0101) paid through installment purchases on another (·0901) is declared as a link; `applyCcFacturadoFinancingProjection` (`ccFacturadoFinancingProjectionLines.ts`, the last step of the expenses payload) then hides the financing cuotas everywhere (`excluded`), keeps the financed purchases in their month in «Total» mode and, in «Por cuota» mode, splits each financed purchase equally over the payment's distinct cuota BILLING months as `split_only` lines dated the 28th, carrying the source amount as `installment_total_clp` so the «/$total» superscript shows. **Financing cuotas are matched by exact `purchase_key`, so a statement cuota line must carry the same with-total `installment-h:` key its plan-derived siblings do:** `installmentLedgerTotalForStatementLine` (`ccExpenseCategories.ts`, the one resolver behind both the flows builder's `installment_total_clp` stamp and `resolveCcExpensePurchaseKey`) reads the plan through the line's own `cc_installment_payments.parser_row_id` first, then identity when the matching plans carry one total (twin plans of identical purchases share theirs), then the printed `valor_cuota_mensual_clp` among same-identity plans with different totals, else the legacy no-total key (which the category/notes readers still resolve). The identity-only lookup refused the June-2026 twins (two RECAUDACION EX PLAZA LYON plans dated 2026-06-30, 1.xxx.xxx + 1.xxx.xxx = the ·0101 June facturado), so the August-billed cuota-01 lines carried the legacy key, the link saw only the Sep/Oct plan-derived cuotas and split June's Lider spend into HALVES labelled 1/2 over Sep–Oct instead of thirds over Aug–Oct. **The interest gap is Σ cuotas − the closed statement's `monto_facturado`** (`statementFacturadoClpForBillingMonth`: CLP, non-web-paste, keyed by statement month like the lines; duplicate copies collapse on their identical total, disagreeing totals throw; no closed statement → no gap line; up to one peso per cuota is rounding) — never Σ cuotas − Σ financed lines, which read the financed card's own cuotas billed that month (the ·0101 August link printed 3x.xxx of phantom «bills» over Sep–Nov) and notas de crédito as ± interest. Those cuotas stay in the financed month on their card, so the all-months total is still Σ cuotas. A facturado paid as a one-shot purchase (the RECAUDACION EX PLAZA LYON one-shots) needs no link: its `no_cuenta` category keeps it out of gastos.

- **Financing links declare themselves (2026-10-05; `ccFacturadoFinancingAuto.ts`).** After every card write (`mergeCcAccountFromParsedRows`, the feed import's end, a plan created by hand or from a tagged line) the matcher looks for a closed CLP facturado (any card, month not yet linked) that cuota purchases on ANOTHER card paid: on one day, purchases whose principals (the printed one for an interest plan, else the total) add up to it to the peso, dated after its close and up to 10 days past its pay-by — the merchant is not used. Plans get the link (`upsertCcFacturadoFinancingLink`); a feed line tagged as a cuota purchase with no count yet counts as a payment too but waits for its plan to be linked. Two fitting combinations, or a purchase fitting two facturados, are reported and left alone; an existing link is never touched. When the paid card shows no payment of that amount around the date (payment lines −3…+5 days, or a statement header naming it) a PAGO is planted in its open bucket (batch kind `cc_financing_payment`) — BCI has no daily feed, so its payment otherwise waited for the next statement while both cards counted the debt; the statement that prints the real PAGO settles the bucket line. Replayed on the real DB with the two hand-made links removed, it rebuilt both exactly (June: plans 160 + 161; August: 170) and flagged nothing else in the history. **One matcher for every way of paying (2026-11-09; migration 297 `cc_facturado_payment_charges`).** The pool is every peso purchase on another card master — a cuota plan (at its principal), a feed line tagged as a cuota purchase whose plan does not exist yet, and a one-shot purchase (`card_purchase`, no installment flag) — each counted once by flow (copies of one purchase share it), dated after the facturado's close and up to 10 days past its pay-by, and only where the merchant opens like a place the paid card's issuer is paid at (`facturado_paid_at_merchant_prefixes` on `CC_ISSUERS`: for BCI Lider «RECAUDACION», «EXPRESS», «EXP LIDER», «HIP LIDER», «HIPER LIDER», «LIDER» — a Lider store's counter, «EXPRESS PLAZA L…» in the feed and «RECAUDACION …» on the statement; none for Santander and CMR, so their facturados are no longer matched, which never happened in the data). On one paying card and one day, the combinations adding up to the facturado to the peso are found; **the fewest payments win** (one charge beats any pair, a pair beats any five); one combination of that size is the payment, several interchangeable ones (same card, day, kinds, merchants and amounts — identical twins) take the lowest ids, any other tie is ambiguous and nothing is written; a purchase fitting two facturados, or a day with more than 10 candidates, too. **A match is recorded once** — its `cc_facturado_financing_links` row, the plans as `finances_facturado` flow links, the one-shot charges as `cc_facturado_payment_charges` rows by flow (flow merges move them) — and the facturado is never matched again; a record with no plans whose charges no longer hold a one-shot line (a pending listing that settled under another flow, a charge that settled as a cuota purchase) is dropped and the month matched afresh. A match with a tagged line waits for its plan before it is recorded; its PAGO does not wait. Each one-shot charge is filed as an internal transfer (an «Único» row) unless the line has an «Único» row, a per-line override, a declined automatic category or a general rule giving it «no cuenta» or internal transfer (the Lider store's rule is groceries; «RECAUDACION» is «no cuenta»). The PAGO is planted as above. **A partly financed facturado** (charges plus plans) spreads only the financed share, (facturado − Σ charges) ÷ facturado, of each financed purchase over the cuota months, keeps the rest in its own month (`financing-proj-rest:` split_only lines; a mortgage line's carrying and amortization split the same way), and its interest is Σ cuotas − the financed principal. A link made by hand replaces the record's charges, and the manager lists only records with plans. Near misses (a recent facturado with no payment on file and no match, while one day's charges at the issuer's places add up to half of it or more) are reported in the plan, never written. Replayed on a copy of the real DB: the three hand-era links untouched, the ten one-charge Lider payments 2025-09 → 2026-07 recorded with nothing else written, and October 2026's 1.xxx.xxx charge of 2026-11-09 recorded, filed as a transfer and its PAGO planted.
- **Facturación closes come from the bank's own evidence, not the config cycle (2026-09-26; migration 185, `ccBillingCloses.ts`).** The bank publishes a close well before its statement, twice. **Announced:** current statements print the following cycle — Santander «PRÓXIMO PERÍODO DE FACTURACIÓN 25/08/2026 24/09/2026» (June 2026 on), BCI Lider «Próximo Período de Facturación 27/08/2026 26/09/2026» — parsed into `cc_statements.next_period_from` / `next_period_to` (`pdf_next_period_*` in `cc_statement_reconcile.py`), and the Santander statement JSON's FechaProxFact fills `next_period_to` (JSON-written statements carry it; for a PDF-owned close the JSON importer fills it when missing and reports a disagreement). The gap between a statement's period end and its printed next start is the issuer's **close-day rule** (`closeDayOffsetDays`): Santander starts the next cycle ON the close day — a purchase dated the close day bills next month (the 24/09/2026 rows sat in «por facturar» the day after the September close) — BCI the day after. **Observed:** the Santander unbilled feed always opens with a **SALDO INICIAL** row (per currency) dated at the latest close and valued at that facturación's «Monto total facturado» (verified in both currencies on the July 2026 close); the scraper keeps it apart (`saldoInicial` on each slide — it was discarded until 2026-09-26) and `import:santander-movements` records it in `cc_feed_billing_closes` (a repeat that changes the date or the amount throws). Precedence per billing month: statement → feed → announced → estimate (`closeEvidenceForBillingMonth`). **The estimate is the day of the account's latest known close carried into the month** (clamped to its length: ·0901 the 24th, ·0101 the 26th); the config cycle only estimates for a card with no close on record (2026-09-26 — the default 20 had put every projected close and daily bar four to six days early). Never «fix» the config cycle to the real close day instead: the config close is the open buckets' `statement_date` identity (`statementCloseDdMmYyyyForBillingMonth`), which must stay off the real close days or a bucket shares its date with the incoming statement. **The open month advances on the bank's close** (`lastClosedBillingMonthForAccount` = latest statement close, a later feed-observed close, or a later announced close whose next cycle has started); the months between the last statement and that close are **provisionally closed** (`provisionallyClosedBillingMonthsForAccount`): facturado = the feed's SALDO INICIAL (USD at the pay-by) when observed, else the app's estimate; the billing-detail row is statement-framed (`as_of_kind: statement`, as_of = the real close, `provisional: true`) so the next month's balance roll starts from it; facturaciones carry `is_provisional_close`, `close_date_source` and, with the bank's figure, `provisional_estimate_total_clp` (the client marks the month with a bare asterisk — the same mark a projected row carries, the hover hint tells them apart; since 2026-10-26, it read «provisoria» before — an estimated close «≈», and a pay-by no statement prints yet «≈» too: `pay_by_source` = `statement` for a printed PAGAR HASTA, `derived` for the issuer rule on open and provisional months, see «A derived pay-by follows the issuer», null with no pay-by). **The open month's cupo subtracts what a provisional close billed** (`provisionalBilledCuotasClp` in `cupoEnCuotasForBillingMonth`): the live cupo marks a cuota billed only once a statement prints it, so until the statement arrives it still carries the provisional month's cuotas, which that month's row already bills (2026-10 ·0901 read 7,xx M against September's 5,xx M). **Not negative schedule evidence:** the manual-plan clamp keeps reading `lastPdfBillingMonthForAccount` — a close without its statement does not say which cuotas it billed. **Open buckets keep their config `statement_date`** — an identity (`cc_statements` is unique on source + date and several sums group lines by statement date, so a bucket must never share the incoming statement's date); displayed closes come from `closeDateForBillingMonth`. **Feed routing:** everything a post-close feed lists is unbilled by definition, so a row goes to the month after the observed close (`rows_billing_month`) at the earliest — and, since 2026-10-26, to its own cycle when that is later (`feedLineBillingMonths` in `cardUnbilledMovementsApply.ts`: the later of the feed's month and `billingMonthContainingPurchase` of the row's date; one import carries lines for several months, one bucket each through `lineBillingMonths` on `ccWebPasteToCsvRecords`, the hand paste unchanged). The bank rolls its unbilled list the night AFTER the close day, so the close-day evening listing still opens with the previous SALDO INICIAL: a purchase dated the close day (November's first day on Santander) used to sit in the closing month's bucket, inflating its provisional estimate, until the next listing showed the new close. Bucket lines under earlier months whose one-shot key the feed still lists move forward (`moveOpenBucketLinesByDedupeKey`, once per target month — close-day purchases, pending authorizations that settled after the close). The repair pass moves bucket lines from the next cycle's first day (`nextPeriodStartIsoForBillingMonth`), not from the day after the close, and creates the open bucket only when a line moves into it — it runs on every card merge, and the eager create left the retired ·0161 an empty «open» facturación each month (2026-05 → 09, which the merged group ledger then flagged open as well; the five were deleted 2026-09-26, snapshot `20260926-151347-before-retired-cc-empty-bucket-cleanup`). **Statement reconcile matches instead of deleting a date window** (`reconcileOpenWebPasteAfterPdfClose`): a bucket line on the statement (same merchant, amount, date within a day — `webPasteLineMatchesStatementLine`) is deleted; an unmatched line inside the billed cycle (up to, not including, the next cycle's first day) is deleted as a pre-auth the statement settled differently; an unmatched line outside it moves to the open facturación; a later bucket's line is deleted only when the statement bills it. The old inclusive window deleted Santander close-day purchases until the next feed re-listed them. **Stale buckets** (carried into the open month by the read paths) are only those of statement-closed months — a provisional month's bucket is its own evidence. **One rule decides which facturación a statement's lines belong to (2026-09-26):** `facturacionMonthByStatementDate` (the statement's own month; a stale bucket's, the open month). The open-month facturado and payment sums read it through `statementDatesForFacturacion`, and the expense lines' `billing_month` is stamped from it, with a `web_paste` flag for the lines the modal may delete. The facturación modal is a plain filter on that month (`flowLinesForFacturacionMonth`), so it lists exactly what the facturado counts. The client's own copy of the rule, `statementsForFacturacionMonth`, was removed: it put every earlier bucket in every later month and treated a month as closed on any one statement, which listed September 2026's CLP bucket under October while only September's USD statement had arrived. **Cross-check:** a statement for a feed-observed close must print the same date and bill exactly the SALDO INICIAL (CLP to the peso, USD to the cent) — `assertFeedClosesMatchStatements` throws inside every card merge and on every feed import. A pasted «SALDO INICIAL» row is skipped (`skipped_saldo_inicial`), never imported as a 1,xx M charge.

- **A derived pay-by follows the issuer (2026-10-26; `CcDerivedPayByRule` on `CC_ISSUERS`, `derivedPayByIso` in `ccInstallmentPayBy.ts`, `ccDerivedPayByRuleForAccount` in `ccIssuerForAccount.ts`).** No bank document announces the next facturación's PAGAR HASTA — the statement prints only the next cycle's DESDE / HASTA, the Santander JSON's `FechaVenc` is the current statement's own pay-by — so an open, provisional or projected month's pay-by is derived from its close by the issuer's rule, backtracked over every statement on file: **BCI** the 10th of the month after the close as it falls, weekends included (14 of 14, three on a Saturday or Sunday); **Santander** the 10th moved back to the previous Chile business day (exact on 16 of the current card's 23, the other seven one day earlier than predicted, never later — the printed date lands on the 8th–10th with no exact rule from the close, and «close + 11 business days» fits the 2017–2024 history better but not the current card); **CMR** close + 16 days (the 30th). The issuer is the master's config column, `credit_card_account_config.issuer` (`ccIssuerSlugForMasterAccount`; an account without a config row, or with an issuer `CC_ISSUERS` does not list, throws). Every reader goes through it: the facturaciones rows and the resolvers the expense lines and the facturación proxy read, the debt fx date (`balanceUsdFxDateIso`), the plan ledger's open and projected months (`closeDateForBillingMonth` of the month, no longer a literal 10th) and the statement import's fallback for a statement that printed no pay-by. The Chile holiday calendar (`marketHolidays.ts`) covers the years a forward estimate needs; a historical statement carries its printed date. The row still reads «≈» (`pay_by_source` = `derived`): BCI's is exact in practice, but it is not the bank's print. Until then every issuer took a bare 10th — right for BCI, one or two days late on Santander in half the months, which overstated the facturación proxy's floating window by a day.
- **A dollar statement with no printed total bills its own lines (2026-10-27; `ccUsdStatementBilled.ts`).** The Santander international statement JSON has an all-null header, and `facturadoFromStatement` read a dollar total only from the header, so while October 2026's dollar PDF had not arrived its facturación row (and the «próximos pagos» panel) showed the pesos alone. Now such a statement bills `usdBilledFromStatementLines`: its charges and credits, net, with the previous facturación's payments (`isCcUsdPaymentLine`) left out; `feedCloseStatementMismatches` holds that figure to the feed's dollar SALDO INICIAL, which would have caught the sign bug above (lines 80 vs feed 30 in the test). Only one such statement existed when this landed (·0781 2026-10-26), so no history moved.
- **«Tarjetas: próximos pagos» on the home dashboard (2026-10-27; what is still owed since 2026-10-29; `dashboardCcUpcomingPayments.ts`, `UpcomingPaymentsPanel.tsx`).** The page bundle's `cc_upcoming_payments` lists, per card master that is not inactive (`cardStoppedBilling`, `cards/lifecycle.ts`; superseded masters already out of `listCreditCardMasterAccountIds`), two of the facturaciones rows the card page's table shows (`billingDetailCacheForAccount(...).facturaciones`, never rebuilt): the open one (the latest `is_open_month` row — a card with no close on record reads every month open) and the latest one before it, each with what it still OWES. Per currency, the closed one owes its amount less the payments made after its close (pesos: the card's payment lines and statement headers, `listCcPaymentEvidenceRows`, one per key — a receipt's planted PAGO counts the day it is paid; dollars: `usdDebtPaymentsForAccount`) and less the open cycle's credits (`openFacturacionCredits`: notas and refunds, which the bank applies to the balance first — two dollar facturaciones, 2025-11 and 2026-07, were paid short by exactly their notas); the open one gets back the credits the closed one used (its facturado nets them) and loses what payments and credits put above the closed one's amount (an overpayment carries). Only the latest closed one can owe: the next statement bills whatever an earlier one left. Dollars still owed are at today's rate (`fxForLiveMtm`). A row owing nothing is left out — when paid, not at its pay-by; a closed one still owing after its pay-by stays with `overdue` (counted in the current month of `totals_by_month`) until the next close. Rows carry `amount_clp` (owed), `billed_clp` (the row's `facturado_total_clp`, shown «de $X» once part is paid), the ISO close with `close_observed` (statement or feed SALDO INICIAL = ✓; announced or estimated = ≈), the pay-by with `pay_by_source` (derived = ≈) and the open/provisional flags. Drawn as «Próximos pagos», grouped by the month the payments are due (2026-11-02): the block's `months` (pay month YYYY-MM, its total, its payments in card order; an overdue row falls in the current month) replaced `cards` + `totals_by_month`, one card per month with its total on the heading line, in a `.cards` block styled like the «Flujos» period cards. The panel is always shown: an empty list reads «Nothing due in the coming months», and a placeholder bundle (null: unknown, not absent) with no stored copy shows it dimmed and empty. Pesos in both display units. Checked on the real DB: every closed facturación since the start settles to zero (·0781 24, ·0101 15) but the unpaid current ones. A BCI payment counts once a card line shows it (its statement, or `cc:manual-payment`): BCI has no feed or receipt mail.
- **«Próximos pagos» covers every payment due, not only the cards (2026-11-02; `upcomingPayments.ts`, `UpcomingPaymentsPanel`).** The page bundle's `upcoming_payments` (it replaced `cc_upcoming_payments`) lists items grouped by the month they are due, `UPCOMING_PAYMENTS_HORIZON_MONTHS` (6) from the current one; the panel shows two and «Show more» the rest. Sources: the cards (the facturaciones above, unchanged); the **mortgage**, every cuota from the next unpaid one, «on» its best day for the UF (`buildMortgageUfReminder`, else the 11th), its minimum (the amortization projection, `buildDeptoPaymentScenarioRows`) to maximum (the bank's posted máxima) in pesos at that day's UF — never read from the sheet; **gastos comunes** per place with `hoa` statements, every month without waiting for the mail (the bill of month B paid in B + 1 by the day the latest statement named, at its amount until that month's statement arrives), paid when a gastos comunes bill row covers the month or the statement is settled; **contribuciones** per owned property, four cuotas due on the last day of April, June, September and November, as many paid as the year has bill rows, the first paid one's amount holding for the year, else last year's same cuota readjusted by the UF; and **declared payments** (`recurring_payments`, migrations 254, 264 and 267, `GET|POST /api/upcoming-payments/recurring`, `DELETE …/:id`; reworked 2026-11-05 in `recurringPaymentMatch.ts`, pure, with `upcomingPayments.ts` loading the entries, the expense lines and the UF). An entry is **manual** (`manual = 1`: paid by hand, listed in «Próximos pagos» until a line pays it) or **automatic** (charged to the card: never listed; its occurrences come back in the block's `automatic_recurring` with status paid / overdue / upcoming and the line that paid it). It recurs **yearly** (its month and day each year) or **monthly** (its day each month; `due_month` stays null). Its **amount** is pesos or UF (`currency`); a UF amount is the UF of the due day, projected past the last published one and estimated there. Its lines are matched by a set of merchant **patterns** (`recurring_payment_patterns`, case- and accent-insensitive, merchant or shown name) bought within `match_days_before` (14) before the due date to `match_days_after` (7) after it and, when `amount_tolerance_pct` is set, within that % of the expected pesos: a UF entry's amount × the UF of the line's day or of any of the five days after it (a pending listing is priced at its posting day); a peso entry's placeholder. The placeholder of a peso occurrence is the most recent matched payment of the same entry, or of a sibling (same name and frequency) paid earlier in the same calendar year, so the first of two cuotas sets the second's amount; else the stated amount. It is estimated («≈») until paid. **A payment whose document says what it covers** (a vehicle permit's expiry, `RecurringLine.covers_through` from the line's `payment_receipt.vehicle_permit`) is that payment whatever its amount, and a later occurrence of the same payment due before its expiry is `covered` (not listed): a whole-year permit paid in March covers the August cuota; a first cuota expires on the second's due day, so it never does. Occurrences are settled in due-date order from 400 days before their entry was declared (history feeds the placeholders) and a line pays at most one, the nearest in days; an occurrence before its declaration is never asked for. **Pending listings under another name (same date):** the bank lists some automatic charges first under another name (the car insurance, an amount in UF, as «CONVENIO P.A.T.» on the 3rd/4th, posted as «SEG AUTO SANTANDER» on the 6th/7th at the same pesos — the UF of the posting day); both names are patterns of one automatic entry. `withRecurringPaymentNames` (`recurringLineNames.ts`, in the expense-line pipeline after the receipts) gives every card line that fits an automatic entry (`automaticRecurringMatchForLine`: pattern, window, amount) `recurring_payment` (its name and every pattern); `expenseLineDisplayNames` shows such a line under the vendor catalog's name for the entry's first other pattern its own merchant does not carry (the posted name: «CONVENIO P.A.T.» → «Seguro Auto Santander»), the entry's own name when the catalog knows none, with no link label; a line whose own merchant the catalog knows keeps a «≈» link with the entry's name, and — when only the bank's name decided its category and it found none — the category of the entry's most recent charge that has one; another «CONVENIO P.A.T.» of other pesos stays unclassified. `replaceRecurringPendingLines` (`ccRecurringPendingLines.ts`, after every web-paste / feed import) deletes a bucket line under one of an automatic UF entry's patterns once a line on the same card under another of its patterns lists the same pesos 0–7 days later, those pesos fitting the entry at the later day's UF (assignments carried, `recurring_pending` in the import result); only one-to-one pairs. Future UF everywhere here comes from `ufProjection.ts` (`projectedUfClp`, `ufProjector`), one rule for «Próximos pagos» and the expense lines. Future UF is the last published one grown at the last twelve months' rate (`projectedUfClp`). Unpaid past its date = overdue, counted in the current month. **An item whose last day is today or tomorrow (2026-11-11)** carries `due_soon` (`today` | `tomorrow`, Chile day, never on an overdue one; set by `groupUpcomingByMonth`), and the panel draws a breathing blue dot between its date and its amount — full on the day, darker and fainter the day before; its period is 7,5 s ± 0,2 s per mount, held still under reduced motion. Every API write drops the cached home bundle (not its account rows), since bill rows and declared payments sit outside the aggregation funnels.
- **One cupo en cuotas rule, one purchase-cycle rule (2026-09-27; `ccCupoFrame` in `ccBillingViews.ts`, `billingMonthContainingPurchase`).** A month's cupo is keyed on the open facturación (bank close evidence), never the calendar month: the open month carries the live figure net of what a provisional close billed, plus pending cuota purchases; every other month the plan remainder after it. The detalle, the stored `cc_billing_month_balances` snapshot and the month-end valuation fallback all read it: `recomputeCcBillingMonthBalances` writes the rows, builds the detalle from them and copies each month's cupo and balance back, so `cupo_utilizado_clp` / `saldo_total_clp` equal the card page's «cupo en cuotas» / «balance total» by construction (the stored cupo used to follow the calendar month, and the stored saldo matched no detalle row). Facturado stays per currency; `saldo_total_usd` is the USD statement's printed deuda. The installment ledger's current month reads the Chile clock (`currentCalendarYm` was UTC, a month ahead from 21:00 on a month's last day). Which facturación a purchase belongs to is `billingMonthContainingPurchase` everywhere — the manual first-cuota guess, feed-typed plans, the web-paste nudge, `purchase_billing_month`, the ledger facturado projection and the mortgage UF reminder (cierre / first post-cierre day = `closeDateForBillingMonth` / `nextPeriodStartIsoForBillingMonth`; it used to put a payment made between the config 20th and the real close in the next facturación). `billingMonthForPurchaseDate` is gone; the config 21→20 cycle only estimates a close for a card with none on record and keys the open buckets.
- **The detalle table shows the chart's month frame; the statement frame stays beside it and the facturaciones table shows it (2026-10-26; `overlayBillingDetailMonthLines` in `creditCardChartSeries.ts`).** Two framings of a card's cuota debt: the STATEMENT frame — a closed month's cupo is the plan remainder after its close (billed cuotas ride inside facturado, facturado + cupo = balance, the identity the statement prints) — and the MONTH frame the historial chart draws (`ccHistorialLinesAtMonthEndsClp`: the owed walk and the plan-debt walk on the month's last day, today for the current month; a billed cuota leaves the plan debt only on its pay-by, ~10th of the next month). Every closed month therefore reads one billed cycle apart between the two, and the «Detalle por mes» table, labelled «Deuda en cuotas» like the chart line, showed the statement frame (·0901 October 2026: 5.647.829 / 8.414.502 against the chart's 7.517.789 / 8.412.553, the gap being October's 1.870.008 of billed cuotas). Now the bundle's rows (`CcBillingDetailStatementRow`: `cupo_statement_clp`, `balance_statement_clp`) keep the statement frame, which every internal reader needs — the month-end anchors (`ccLedgerStatementClosingPointsClp`, the `latestBillingDetailRow` readers), the stored `cc_billing_month_balances` snapshot, the «Cupo» card's used figure — and the response assembly (`creditCardInstallmentsResponse`, `creditCardGroupLedgerResponse`) samples the lines ONCE for the chart's months and overlays the rows' `cupo_en_cuotas_clp` / `balance_total_clp` (now `number | null`) from that same map, so a table row equals the chart point of its month by construction; a group's merged rows are overlaid from the group's own sample, never Σ of the members' month frames. The lines must not be sampled inside the cached bundle (`billingDetailCacheForAccount`): the daily walk reads that bundle and the anchors are written from it. The facturaciones table gains «Deuda en cuotas» (`deuda_en_cuotas_clp` = `ccCupoFrame(...).cupoAfterCloseClp(month)`, the cuotas left unbilled after that close — the statement's «saldo capital cuotas»; the open month by the same rule, not the live figure; null without a plan ledger; summed in the group merge). A statements-only card's chart now draws «saldo total» from the owed walk too (it drew facturado + 0 before). **The open facturación has its rows from the close day on (same date):** `buildFacturacionesInner` emits the open month's row (`openFacturacionRow`: facturado = únicos + cuota a pagar through the detalle's own open-month helper (`ccBillingViews.ts`), the close from `closeDateForBillingMonth` with its source, the «no row yet» pay-by rule, `deuda_en_cuotas_clp`) even with no bucket line yet, and `buildBillingDetailByMonth` adds `frame.openBillingMonth` to its months (as-of today, `manual`) without waiting for the snapshot row `recomputeCcBillingMonthBalances` stamps on the next card write — on 2026-10-26, the announced October close of ·0901, the card read «Facturación abierta —» and the tables had November only as a projection until the night's feed. An inactive card gets neither. **The open row reads zeros, not «—» (same date):** a cycle that has billed nothing in dollars yet carries `facturado_usd` / `facturado_usd_clp` = 0 (`openFacturacionRow`; a closed month keeps null = no dollar statement), and the facturación proxy emits an aggregate for every facturación the table shows (`aggregateProxyByFacturacion(..., facturacionMonths)`: gain 0, `blended_return_pct` null when nothing floated — the cell reads a bare «$0»; «—» only on a card with no proxy at all). Verified on a copy of the real DB: every detalle row equals its chart point on every card and Pasivos page, and the anchors and snapshot of ·0901 recompute identically under the committed and the new code. **«Compras activas» order is the server's** (`ccActivePurchaseOrder.ts`, applied in `ccInstallmentsDbApiPayload`; the client only renders): `remaining_installments` ascending, then `first_due_month` (a plan with none last), then label — the client's own sort broke a tie alphabetically, so three plans with 3 cuotas left read EXPRESS PLAZA L (first cuota 2026-11) above two whose first cuota billed 2026-10.

- **Cuota purchases from the card feed, the type-aware first-due nudge and the feed mirror (2026-09-26; migration 186, `ccCuotaPurchaseKinds.ts`, `ccFeedCuotaPurchases.ts`, `ccFeedMirror.ts`).** The Santander feed lists a purchase in cuotas at its full principal and types it: «CUOTA COMERCIO» bills cuota 1 at the close AFTER the purchase cycle (the purchase cycle's statement prints 00/N — 10 of 10 on 2026 statements), «N/CUOTAS PRECIO CONTADO» / «TRES CUOTAS … CONTADO» at the purchase cycle's own close (9 of 9); BCI bills «cuota comercio» in its own cycle (4 of 4) but has no feed. The feed carries no cuota count; a «cuota comercio» purchase comes with a same-day stamp-tax row («IMPTO. DECRETO LEY 3475», same merchant) = principal × 0,066% × (cuotas + 1) months, capped at 0,8% — it matched every plan in the statement history (27 exact, 5 capped), so `cuotaCountFromStampTax` reads the count up to 11 cuotas. ingest's `santanderCardFeedPayload` (`ingest/src/santander/cardFeed.ts`) annotates each typed row (`cuota_purchase`: kind, count, source, tax; an unknown cuota-ish description throws; the «CUOT: … OPER: …» billing references are not purchases). The feed import then (1) **creates the plan** when the count is known and no plan covers the purchase — an ordinary `source = 'manual'` plan (so the statement's twin reconcile replaces it) with provenance in `cc_feed_installment_plans` and `first_due_month` from the type and the purchase's real cycle (`billingMonthContainingPurchase`); creating it removes the one-shot line already on file, and the feed row imports as an installment overlap; (2) **tags** the bucket line of a cuota purchase whose count is unknown (`cc_statement_lines.cuota_purchase_kind`) — every «precio contado» and a capped «cuota comercio»: the line stays (owed = full principal from the purchase date either way) but `facturadoClpFromOpenMonthStatementLines` leaves it out of the billed estimate and `cupoEnCuotasForBillingMonth` counts it as installment debt (all tagged lines «now» for the open month, the month's own for a closed one — for a precio contado with the bank's SALDO INICIAL total that double-counts its first cuota until the statement's plan supersedes the line). **The walk and the charts carry a tagged purchase too, and the card page asks for its count (2026-09-26):** `pendingCuotaPurchaseLines` (`ccBillingViews.ts`) is the one list every reader uses — the daily «deuda en cuotas» walk takes the tagged principal on its purchase date and holds it flat (no count, no schedule, nothing to retire), so the monthly historial, which samples that walk, carries it too; the projected billing-detail rows add it to every month so the balance line never sits under the cuota line; when its plan arrives the line goes and the plan's purchase event takes over on the same day. Expense line rows carry `cuota_purchase_kind` (badge «En cuotas · ¿cuántas?» and the existing make-installment dialog relabelled «¿Cuántas cuotas?»), the card payload lists `pending_cuota_purchases` for a notice above the facturaciones table that opens the line's month, and `convertStatementLineToInstallmentPurchase` pins a tagged line's first cuota by its type — a cuota comercio a cycle later than the manual own-cycle guess. Before this, both kinds billed their whole principal in their cycle: 2xx.xxx of the September 2026 gap. **Type-aware nudge:** `applyWebPasteInstallmentFirstDueNudges` pins a hand-entered plan's first cuota from the feed row's type when the row is its purchase row (principal-sized), even over an earlier pin (the old open-month rule had put the 28/08 Plaza Lyon plan in September); untyped rows keep the open-month rule, and a plan with statement payments is never touched. The default guess (`purchaseFirstDueYm`, own cycle) stays — right for BCI and «precio contado». **Feed mirror:** every fetch returns the whole unbilled list, so after the import a current-cycle bucket line (dated on/after the feed's SALDO INICIAL close) that no feed row accounts for — same amount, matching merchant incl. the paste's 15-character cut, dates ≤ 4 days apart — is something the bank dropped (a voided pre-authorization, or a pending row restated under another name/date: «CONVENIO P.A.T.» 03/09 vs «SEG AUTO SANTANDER» 07/09) and is deleted with the usual read-before-delete valuation re-sync. Only a currency the listing proves complete (`feedMirrorCurrencies`, `mirror.proof` on the result): its slide carried the SALDO INICIAL row, or — since 2026-10-19 — the bank's own cupo utilizado for it, from the same session's product summary, equals the close on record (the listing's, else the latest `cc_feed_billing_closes` row) plus every listed row to the cent, the identity the cupo check judges by; a failed Dólares tab passes neither, and the peso identity only holds without cuota plans outstanding. On 2026-10-19 the dollar slide listed all 20 rows but no SALDO INICIAL, so the row-only rule left a pending APPLE.COM/BILL US$13,22 of 16/10 beside its 17/10 restatement and the cupo check failed the run by exactly that line (removed by replaying the file once the rule landed). Never payments/divisas abonos (a receipt-planted PAGO precedes the feed), never the closed cycle's lines, and more than 12 deletions on one card throws with the list instead.

- **Same-statement twin installment purchases (2026-09-26; migration 187).** Identical purchases made the same day, each in cuotas, print one identical cuota line each on every statement (same merchant, date, amount, N/T), told apart only by the parser's `#dupN` dedupe keys. A plan is (loan identity, `cc_installment_purchases.twin_index`): `groupInstallmentLoanChains` (`ccInstallmentLedgerMerge.ts`) gives each installment row its occurrence among the same loan's same-cuota rows on its statement (in `#dupN` order), builds one chain per twin and links every payment to its own twin's line — the twins are indistinguishable, so pairing the Nth line of one statement with the Nth of the next is exact. The twin index is part of `installmentPurchaseLedgerDedupeKey`, so the loader's duplicate-fingerprint guard, the «Total»-mode loader and the one-shot overlap matcher keep twins apart (a hand-entered plan is always twin 0). The set shares one `installment-h:` key (category, «Único», notes and financing links apply to all of it), «Total» mode keeps one total per twin (`installmentTotalSlotsByKey`), and the expenses display dedupe collapses the Nth identical cuota line of a statement only with the Nth rendering of that slot elsewhere (`sameStatementOccurrences` — web paste, ledger payment line, a re-imported copy). `assertTwinPlansStartTogether` fails the merge when a twin's first statement payment differs from its twin 0's: a misread duplicate line, or a twin set from before twin plans. Before this the builder keyed plans on the loan alone, and three identical 12-cuota purchases from 2024-12 held ONE plan — cuota a pagar 1x.xxx short for twelve facturaciones, month-end anchors 2xx.xxx → 1x.xxx low, «Por cuota» and «Total» each showing a third of the spend; backfilled with `server/scripts/repair-cc-installment-twin-plans.ts` (twin loans only, report-first, `--apply`). Acceptance check for every card: `npx tsx scripts/verify-cc-billed-cuotas.ts` (from `server/`) — each closed facturación's printed cuotas equal its `cuota_a_pagar_clp`, nota-cancelled plans excepted.

- **A cuota purchase's 00/N row stores its principal, and a hand-entered plan the statement cannot match fails the import (2026-09-27).** The e-mailed Santander statement lists the cycle's cuota purchases in section «4. INFORMACIÓN COMPRAS EN CUOTAS EN EL PERÍODO» (parser layout `wide_master_periodic_summary`), and the parser stored their monthly cuota as `amount_clp` — unlike every other installment layout, and invisible until a manual plan met such a row: `reconcileManualInstallmentPurchasesForStatements` compares a stored line's amount (max of amount and cuota) with the plan's total, so the September 2026 import left a hand-entered plan beside the statement's own plan for the same purchase (the bank printed it under another merchant name) — the purchase counted twice in «deuda en cuotas», the card's cupo, Pasivos and net worth, which the printed «SALDO CAPITAL CUOTAS» showed at once. The row now stores the principal (the cuota stays in `valor_cuota_mensual_clp`; the plan builder already took the largest of the three amounts, payment rows read the cuota field and both operaciones reconciles skip the layout by name, so plans, payments and statement totals are unchanged). The reconcile also throws — inside the merge transaction, so the import writes nothing — when a manual plan a statement of the batch covers stays unmatched while a statement plan carries its identity (same total and count, date ±2 days). Repaired by the ordinary re-parse + incremental re-import: the reconcile deleted the manual twin, converted a manual plan that was its contract's only row to `pdf`, and moved a facturado-financing link onto the statement plan; five older statements with such rows re-imported with the principal, no plan or anchor changed. Details in PARSERS.md «Credit-card PDFs».

- **A re-imported line keeps its flow (2026-09-28; since F9 `flows/cardLineAdoption.ts`, `docs/untangle-flow-identity.md` «Step 5 as built»).** What a user put on a card line — its category («Único» row), big group, note, pieces, bill link — hangs off the line's flow (`flows`, one per purchase), not off the line's id, so a re-import that gives every line a new id (and, after a parser change, a new parser row id) no longer orphans it (686 keys and every card split once did). `mergeCardStatements` records the account's lines before writing (`adoptReimportedCardLines`) and folds each vanished line's flow into its new line's: same parser row id first, then identity (account, currency, date, amounts, cuota numbers, merchant letters and digits; twins in id order), then the one surviving copy of that identity (a duplicate statement copy removed). A target that already holds a different value keeps it; unpaired lines are reported on the merge result. A re-parse that splits one printed line into several (or merges several) hands identical assignments to every new line; a new identical line beside a kept one copies only its category and big group (it may be its own purchase). Where the replacing line is known but its merchant differs, the plan carries it (`CardLinePlan.carry` → `adoptReplacingCardLines` in the funnel's delete step): a bucket line the statement settles (`reconcileOpenWebPasteAfterPdfClose`), a cut/pending re-listing dropped for its fuller twin (`removeTruncatedMerchantDuplicateLines`), a planted payment replaced by the bank's line. Two things are not flow state and stay carry rules: a per-line category override (keyed by line id, it would cascade away; `snapshotLineCategories` / `restoreLineCategories`) and a merchant rule — when no rule reaches the replacing line's name, the rule is added under it, since the statement prints the merchant differently from the paste (BCI appends « (T)»: a rule on `FARMACIA CENTRAL` left `FARMACIA CENTRAL (T)` «sin categoría» after the 2026-09 ·0101 import). **Merchant rules match the statement's name too** (`merchantRuleCandidateKeys`, exact first): the name without the PDF's glued « COMPRAS P.A.T.» column, then its cut at the web table's 15 characters. The one-off history repair, `scripts/archive/repair-cc-expense-orphaned-line-keys.ts`, is archived (it imports the deleted `ccExpenseLineRekey`).

## Ingest API (2026-09-30; plan in `docs/ingest-split-plan.md`)

- **Two packages meet here.** `ingest/` (workspace `nw-tracker-ingest`, formerly `scraper/`) reads the outside world; the server applies what it sends and becomes the only DB writer as sources move over. They share only **`server/contracts`** (workspace `nw-tracker-contracts`): the envelope (`schema_version`, `feeder_id`, `source {channel, ref, label?, fetched_at?}`, `payload`), the result / error bodies, the kind registry `INGEST_KINDS`, a typed client (`createIngestClient`, validates before sending) and a JSON Schema export (`npm run schema -w nw-tracker-contracts`). `ingest` never imports the rest of the server; the server never imports `ingest`.
- **Kinds are canonical, never bank-shaped:** a kind says what the data means (a card's open-cycle listing, an observed close, a cuota-purchase hint); bank decoding lives in the feeder, so any feeder can produce it.
