One `schema_version` per kind; a change old payloads would not satisfy bumps it. **A currency is any lowercase ISO 4217 code (2026-11-12; `currencyCodeSchema` in `server/contracts/src/formats/schemas.ts`)** — the contracts cannot read the database, so the server holds each code to its `currencies` table on receipt (`server/src/ingestCurrencies.ts`: `requireLoadedCurrency`, and `pesosOrDollars` / `dollarsOnly` where a handler still takes only those — card sub-balances, bank and broker cash, mirrors, wires); an unknown code is a 422 `unknown_currency`, a known one the handler cannot take a 422 `unsupported_currency` naming the currency and the kind (`IngestRefusal`, rolled back, never a 500). Widening was not a bump (every old payload still passes). `payment.processor_receipts` keeps any code as provenance, unchecked.
- **`POST /api/ingest/<kind>`** (`server/src/routes/ingest.ts`) checks envelope → version → payload before a handler runs (4xx `unknown_ingest_kind` / `unsupported_schema_version` / `invalid_envelope` / `invalid_payload`), then hands it to the **receiver** (`server/src/ingestReceiver.ts`, 2026-11-07), which runs the kind's handler from `INGEST_HANDLERS` (`server/src/ingestHandlers.ts`; its type makes a kind without a handler a compile error, and registration throws on any mismatch) inside one IMMEDIATE transaction and answers `{status: applied | duplicate | conflict, kind, schema_version, retry, dry_run, message?, conflicts?, details?}`. Handlers return `{details, retry?}` and never pick a status; the receiver owns three rules. **Store once:** a kind declares what its `source.ref` names (`ref: document | read` on `defineIngestKind`); a `document` kind's ref + payload sha256 is kept in `ingest_documents` (migration 269) once applied with a final outcome, so the same payload again answers `duplicate` with the first answer's details (nothing runs) and another payload under the ref `conflict`; a `read` kind (a fetch window, a file replaced in place, a staged set sent every run) runs on every send. Rows a resend must state the same (a mail's notice, a bill, a receipt, a merchant document, a balance observation) go through `storeOnce`, which compares field by field and collects every disagreement. **Conflict:** a handler throws `IngestConflict` (pointers `{at: <table>:<key>, fields, message}` plus optional details, e.g. the mortgage lender reading's field differences); the whole document rolls back and the feeder gets `conflict`, never a 500. **Dry run:** the envelope's `dry_run` (the only way to ask; `client.send(kind, payload, source, {dryRun})`) runs the handler in the same transaction and rolls it back, so the report is what a real send writes — payloads carry no `apply` any more (13 kinds bumped their `schema_version`), and process caches are bypassed inside a transaction (`dbInTransaction`), so nothing a dry run read outlives it. `retry: true` (a receipt waiting for its card line, an ambiguous payment receipt) tells the feeder to keep the document staged; `rolledBack(fn)` is the same rollback for tests and report-only scripts. `GET /api/ingest/kinds` lists what is accepted.
- **Access (`server/src/ingestAuth.ts`; the rule itself is `ingestAccessRefusal` in `server/contracts/src/access.ts`, shared with the ingest service's `/runs` and `/parse`, as are the default ports `DEFAULT_SERVER_PORT` / `DEFAULT_INGEST_SERVICE_PORT`):** local connections only (the socket address, never `req.ip`); `INGEST_TOKEN` adds a required `Authorization: Bearer`, and `INGEST_ALLOW_REMOTE=1` — refused at boot without a token — lets a remote feeder in. `DEMO_MODE` refuses everything (`ingest_disabled`). Ingest bodies may be up to 32 MB (the rest of the API keeps 2 MB): a whole document set travels in one request.
- **A request the server never answered is sent again (2026-10-09; `createIngestClient`, `server/contracts/src/client.ts`).** A connection-level failure — refused, reset, cut mid-request — is retried at 2 s, 8 s and 20 s (`DEFAULT_CONNECTION_RETRY_DELAYS_MS`; each retry logged by `ingestClient()` in `ingest/src/serverApi.ts`), then thrown as `IngestConnectionError` (base URL, code, attempts, elapsed), which `describeIngestFailure` names — «did not answer (ECONNRESET; 4 attempt(s) over 30 s)», and the old «not reachable» wording for ECONNREFUSED. An HTTP answer, whatever its status, never retries. Repeating a request whose answer was lost is safe by contract: a kind answers `duplicate` to a payload it already applied, a task run twice finds its work done, a report sent twice is refused as `run_not_waiting`. Why: on 2026-10-09 00:32 a `launchctl kickstart -k` of the server (a session deploying a code change) cut hourly run 246's Transfer-mails send mid-flight; the step failed as a bare «fetch failed» with nothing lost (the 2-day window re-sends). The service's `reportWithRetry` keeps its own 15-minute loop on top, for a server that stays down.
- **A Gmail session that loses its connection is run again (2026-10-30; `withImapSession` in `ingest/src/email/imapSession.ts`).** Every IMAP fetcher (the document fetchers, `archiveMails` behind the transfer / payment-receipt / utility mails, the broker poll, the ClaveÚnica code reader) opens Gmail through it: a failure of the connection itself (`ECONNRESET`, imapflow's `ETIMEOUT` «Socket timeout», `NoConnection`, …, never an answer carrying a server code such as `AUTHENTICATIONFAILED`) reruns the whole session after 15 s and then 60 s, and imapflow's `error` event has a listener, so it no longer crashes the process. A rerun is safe: each document is written and recorded in the document ledger before the next is read, and the read-only fetchers clear their list at the start of the session. Why: on 2026-10-29 the Mac slept on battery all evening, waking ~45 s every five minutes, and every run from 18:34 to 22:40 lost one mail step to a reset connection (the 22:00 nightly: Transfer mails and Payment receipt mails).
- **Run protocol (2026-09-30, Phases 2a + 2b; built, switched on by `ingest/switch-schedule.sh to-server`).** The server decides WHEN and WHAT: `ingestRunScheduler.ts` ticks every 30 s on the wall clock (timers stall in sleep, the clock does not, so the first tick after a wake sees a missed slot) and `decideIngestRun` (`ingestRunPolicy.ts`, pure) picks one action: the nightly for the latest 22:00 Chile slot (answered by an `ingest_runs` row, a queue job enqueued for it, or any daily run recorded after it; skipped when a daily run finished < 60 min ago), else the hourly poll for the latest :30 slot — and queues it (see «The job queue» below: a run in flight makes it wait, never skip). An hourly request may carry the Santander **balance poll** (`decideSantanderBalancePoll`; see «The hourly Santander balance poll» below): every hour unless the login is latched, the bank was tried in the last 35 min, or the 22:00 slot is less than 45 min away. Its input is the feeder's bank facts from the latest run report (`santander_state`: last attempt, last successful card-feed fetch, login latch). Requests go to the ingest service (`INGEST_URL`, default `http://127.0.0.1:3002`); a run the service did not take is `not_started` and its job goes back to the queue, offered again 30 s later (both kinds; until 2026-10-30 the hourly was given up) — a nightly badges «Ingest service unreachable» once per slot. **A request is idempotent per run id (2026-10-03):** the retry carries the same id, the service answers 202 again for the run it is running and `already_ran` (409) for one it finished, the server reads both — and a «busy» naming its own run — as started, and a report for a run marked `not_started` is accepted; the service answers before the run starts, and only a slot's second unreachable answer badges. On 2026-10-02 a request sent as the Mac woke timed out at 10 s while the service took it (11 s), the server read the service's «busy: run 50» as someone else's run for 20 minutes, refused the report, and asked again — run 50 ran twice, the second time into the bank's 30-minute guard. **The server records each run** when its report arrives (`completeIngestRun` → `recordDailyRun` / `recordHourlyEmailRun`, the same titles and badge rules as the shell runners' `record:*`; a dry run records nothing, a quiet hourly nothing), and a run whose job stops heartbeating for its 5-minute lease is `lost` and recorded as a failed run (the old 3 h / 90 min timeouts now only cover rows from before the queue, `job_id` null). `ingest_runs` (migrations 200–201) is the scheduler's memory, one row per (kind, slot); `GET /api/ingest/runs` lists it. **The ingest service** (`ingest/src/service/`, LaunchAgent `com.user.nw-tracker-ingest` in the GUI session) takes one run at a time (409 busy) and runs it with the TypeScript runners (`ingest/src/runner/`: `runNightly` / `runHourly`; each step runs one ingest command in `ingest/` — `npm run <script>` there, server tasks through `server-task`, never the root pass-through scripts — and returns `{ok, result}`, the `StepResult` the command reported (`ingest/src/stepResult.ts`: the command writes `{staged, inserted, fetch, differs, read_brokers}` to the file the runner names in `NW_INGEST_STEP_RESULT`), so the gates read structured results, never stdout; output appended to `cfraser/daily-run.log` / `email-run.log`, the hourly log rotating at 5 MB). It may decline a requested bank fetch (latched login, bank tried < 35 min ago — reported `vetoed`, not an attempt), raises the desktop alert on a failed nightly, and reports steps, activity, the fetch outcome and the bank facts to `POST /api/ingest/runs/<id>/complete`, retrying ~15 min while the server is down. `INGEST_RUN_DRY=1` makes every run a dry run. **The shell runners are retired (2026-11-07, F13):** `daily-run.sh`, `email-run.sh`, `run-lib.sh`, the two timed LaunchAgents, `switch-schedule.sh to-launchd` and the server's `record:daily-run` / `record:email-run` / `check:daily-run-recent` are gone; the service's runners are the only runners. **A run started by hand (2026-10-28; `startManualIngestRun`, `POST /api/import-sync/ingest-runs`, «Corridas de banco y correo» on the import sync page):** «Run now» queues the hourly poll or the nightly outside its slot — a mail that just landed — as a job like any other (slot = the request instant, `not_before` now, reason `manual: …`; a run of that kind already queued is moved up instead), started as soon as the ingest service is free and carrying what a run of that kind started then would carry (`runRequestExtras`, the one rule for slot and manual runs, decided at START, not at enqueue: the Santander balance poll, AFP UNO, AFC, payslips, the MetLife read). It answers the slot it overlaps (an hourly at 14:40 is the 14:30 poll; a nightly that just finished makes 22:00 skip). Refused only on an instance whose scheduler is off — the feeder reports to the primary server, which must know the run id. `GET` on the same path lists the run in flight, the latest of each kind and the next slots; the page polls every 5 s while a run is going. **Enabling:** `INGEST_SCHEDULER_ENABLED=1` only in the INSTALLED server plist (set by the switch script, kept by `install-server-agent.sh`), never the root `.env`; the scheduler is off on any instance with `BACKGROUND_JOBS_ENABLED=0` whatever the flag says. `switch-schedule.sh to-server` removes the retired timed agents if a machine still has them; `status` shows what is loaded. Rehearsed on a copy of the real DB with the service in dry-run mode: an on-time hourly, a nightly 1186 min late, and an hourly whose payday fetch the shell's marker correctly withheld.
- **The job queue (2026-10-30; `docs/job-queue-plan.md`, migration 246, `jobQueue.ts`, `jobQueuePolicy.ts`, `jobQueueExecutor.ts`, `jobClaims.ts`).** Every long-running job of the primary — the nightly and hourly ingest runs, «Run now», a deploy — is a row of `jobs`; exclusion is declared as the resources a job holds while running (`ingest-service`; `server-restart`, exclusive), never as clock windows; each kind's priority, resources, lease, dispatch and ingest run kind are one row of `JOB_KINDS` (`jobQueue.ts`), and the executor dispatches through a table typed on its `executor` kinds, and a job that cannot start waits and runs late, never dropped. **One decision, `pickNextJobs`** (pure, unit-tested): a running job silent for its lease (`heartbeat_at` or `claimed_at` + `lease_ms`) is `lost` and holds nothing; a queued job past `stale_after` is `superseded`; the rest by priority (deploy 100 > nightly 50 > hourly 40), then `not_before`, then age; a job starts when none of its resources is held; `server-restart` is granted only when nothing runs, nothing starts while it is held, and once a job needing it is due nothing of lower priority starts (the queue drains, so an hourly every hour cannot starve a deploy). **Coalescing:** at most one QUEUED job per `dedupe_key` (partial unique index) — `ingest.nightly`, `ingest.hourly`, `deploy`; enqueuing it again updates that row (newest payload and reason, earliest `not_before`, newest `stale_after`): an hourly that waits past the next slot is absorbed by it (the slot it runs is the newest), a second push moves a waiting deploy's sha. A running job is never modified. **Producers:** `ingestSchedulerTick` (enqueue the due slot), `startManualIngestRun`, the deploy agent (`POST /api/jobs`). **Executor** (`startJobQueueExecutor`, on with `JOB_QUEUE_EXECUTOR_ENABLED`, which defaults to the ingest scheduler's flag — the primary): a 30 s tick plus one at once on every enqueue / completion (`notifyJobQueue`); it writes off lost jobs (an ingest job's run → `lost`, recorded as a failed run; any other kind → «Job lost: <kind>» notification), supersedes stale ones, and dispatches ingest jobs: claim (worker `ingest-service`), write the `ingest_runs` row (`job_id`; the Santander and other request extras decided now), `POST /runs` with `job_id`; busy / silent → `releaseJob` back to the queue (not an attempt). At start a SCHEDULED job a daily run already covered is superseded (a nightly with a daily run recorded after its slot or < 60 min ago; an hourly whose slot is before the last daily run) — the rule `decideIngestRun` applied while the nightly used to «wait» and the hourly to «skip». **Workers:** the ingest service heartbeats `POST /api/jobs/<id>/heartbeat` every minute while a run lasts (ingest lease 5 min) and its run report completes the job (`completeIngestRun`; a report for a `not_started` run, whose job was released after a lost answer, completes it too); the deploy agent claims (`POST /api/jobs/claim`), heartbeats between steps and completes (`POST /api/jobs/<id>/complete`; deploy lease 20 min). Worker endpoints use the ingest access rules (local, optional `INGEST_TOKEN`, never the demo). **Page:** «Cola» on the import sync page (`GET /api/import-sync/jobs`, `JobQueuePanel`): running (worker, last heartbeat, lease end), queued with WHY it waits — structured (`resource` + holder, `exclusive`, `draining`, `not_before`), worded by the client — and the latest finished; Cancel (queued only; a cancelled slot is recorded `skipped` and not queued again) and Retry (a failed or lost ingest run, queued as a manual run). `scripts/replay-job-queue.ts` replays recorded slots (plus `--deploy` instants) through the policy in memory. `ingest_runs` stays the detail table (Santander request / outcome / state, steps), so the catch-up and payday rules, `lastDailyRunAt` and «Corridas de banco y correo» read it as before.
- **`bank_account.movements` v2 (2026-09-30, Phase 3 source 1; v2 2026-10-10): the checking «últimos movimientos» xlsx, and the dollar account's feed.** A bank account's recent movements (a partial listing, not a statement): `{ account: {issuer, product: "checking", currency: clp | usd, number?}, movements: [{date, description, currency, amount (signed; whole pesos, or dollars to the cent), document_no, wire_reference}], rejected_rows }` — every row in the account's currency, `wire_reference` the bank's reference of a credit it files as an incoming wire (null elsewhere). `ingest/src/santander/checkingMovements.ts` decodes the workbook (Fecha dd-mm-yyyy · Detalle · Cargo · Abono; a date after today is the bank's posting day, kept); `npm run import:checking-movements -w nw-tracker-ingest` (spawned by `import:cfraser-inbox`, so both schedulers keep it) sends each staged file, archives it date-stamped under `cfraser/checking-ultimos-movimientos/imported/` and, as before, fails the step after archiving when a row could not be read. Server: `applyBankAccountMovements` → `importCheckingPartialMovements` (row type `PartialBankMovement`, shared with the cuenta-vista paste) + a `checking_recent_xlsx` batch, identical to the old import. Verified: the 48 archived files decode to the same 1151 rows the server parser read, and on a DB copy both the upload and the inbox command re-applying last night's file gave the old batch's counts. **Uploads go through the service:** the card page's «recent movements» box posts to the server, which hands the file to the ingest service's `POST /parse/santander.checking_xlsx` (`requestFeederParse`, `ingestFeeder.ts`; contract `parse.ts`: 200 `{kind, schema_version, payload}` or 422 `not_this_format` / `unreadable`) and applies the payload; `not_this_format` (a monthly cartola uploaded there) keeps the server's own cartola-xlsx path until the cartolas move. The upload needs the ingest service running (503 otherwise). `ingest/src/formats/chileanNumber.ts` is a copy of the server's, pinned by the same case table, until the server's last parser goes.
- **`card.payment_receipt` v1 (2026-09-30, Phase 3 source 2): Santander's card-payment receipt mails.** `{ issuer, paid_on, debt_currency: clp | usd, amount_clp (pesos out of checking), amount_usd, card_last4 }`, source ref = the mail's message id (it keys the synthesized payment, so a resend never writes a second one). `ingest/src/santander/paymentReceipts.ts` decodes the staged mail text; `npm run import:santander-receipts` (root → ingest; spawned by `import:cfraser-inbox`) sends each staged receipt and archives it to `processed/` unless the server answers `ambiguous` (kept, retried). Server: `applyCardPaymentReceipt` → the unchanged `applyPaymentReceipt` (re-date the next-workday debit, or synthesize the transfer + plant the card line). The overdue alarm for synthesized payments no feed ever listed is its own server command, `check:synthetic-cc-payments`, which the inbox pipeline runs after the receipts on every run. Verified: the 7 receipts on disk decode identically, and re-applying them through old and new code to two copies of the real DB gave the same outcome per receipt and identical tables.
- **`broker.movements` v1 (2026-09-30, Phase 3 source 4): the Racional crawl.** One read = one crawl stamp in `cfraser/racional-movements/`: the list rows (`movements-<stamp>.json`) decoded by `ingest/src/racional/movements.ts` (formerly `server/src/racionalMovements.ts`) into canonical movements, plus Racional's dividends API record (`dividends-<stamp>.json`); either may be missing. `import:racional-movements` (an ingest command now, `--apply` to write) sends each staged read oldest first; the server (`applyRacionalRead` in `racionalMovementsImport.ts`) plans them with the same rules (day-sum dedupe, cash legs left for mirror-pairs, a blocked row holds back the whole list, conflicts and dividends with no ledger row reported as `problems`), writes, stores the dividend breakdowns and moves its coverage. Ingest then moves its crawl cursor when the list applied and archives the crawl's files to `imported/` when nothing is left to fix; anything else stays staged and is sent again next run. A row it cannot decode (an unmapped kind, a changed API shape) is never sent and fails the step. Verified on a copy of the real DB over the 7 staged crawls: with a comisión, a dividend and two breakdowns deleted, the old script and the new path re-wrote the same rows and breakdowns, and ended on the same cursor and coverage. **A portfolio liquidation is booked from the row's detail view (2026-10-21).** Racional lists the liquidation of a managed portfolio as one «Retiro» row (class `pending-withdrawal` until the money reaches the wallet two days later, `withdrawal` after), and the list cannot tell it from cash sent to a bank — on 2026-10-20 the «Portafolio IPSA» liquidation was set aside as a bank withdrawal and the cursor moved past it. The crawl now opens every withdrawal row's detail (staging the row's `status`, pending / complete), `parseRacionalLiquidationDetail` reads the «Montos vendidos» table (ticker, price, pesos, share count per position), the «Además, se retiraron $X de lo que tenías de caja» line and the sale day of the timeline's «Venta» step, and `racionalRowToMovements` decodes the row to one `sell` per position plus a `portfolio_cash_withdrawal` (the enum widened in place, every v1 payload still valid); a detail without sold amounts stays the cash `withdrawal` (manual), and a Retiro the crawl could not open is `incomplete` and blocks its read even though it is a manual cash leg. The server plans the sell as `stock_sell` ETF → Racional CLP with its units (`racionalEquityAccountId`: Racional prints the bare Bolsa symbol, the account carries `.SN`) and the caja pesos as a plain transfer caja → Racional CLP — the mirror of the buy (movements 11376 / 11377) — deduped by the day-sum rule, so the re-read when the money lands, or a liquidation booked by hand, is a duplicate; the emptied caja ends the comisión nudge. The list key strips the `pending-` state (`racionalRowKindClass`; stored cursors are compared through `normalizeRacionalListRowKey`), so the cursor survives the class change; the list amount is the requested figure and may differ from the printed sale, and a key the crawl no longer finds re-stages the window, which the dedupe absorbs. A `stock_sell` into CLP cash also made the wallet read as an equity account (`accountUsesEquityMtm` exempted USD cash only); it exempts `isClpCashAccount` too, and `stockUnitsInflowByMonthEnd` is gated on it.
- **`broker.movements` for Fintual (2026-10-30): the dollar wallet's cash interest, read live.** Alpaca's sweep profit sharing («Ganancia dólares» in the app, posted around month end, one credit a month) has no mail; the Alpaca cartola prints it ~10 days later and `brokerDividendStatementApply` only reports it. `import:fintual-wallet` (ingest, `ingest/src/fintual/walletInterest.ts`; `--apply` to write) asks Fintual's `/gql/` for `stocksWalletCashInterests` (the field the wallet's movements page reads, operation `WalletMovements`) with the evening poll's `FINTUAL_COOKIE`, and sends every credit as a `broker.movements` with broker `fintual` (the contract refuses anything but `interest` movements and a dividends record from Fintual). The server (`fintualWalletInterestApply.ts`) books each credit the ledger lacks as a single-leg `savings_earnings` on Fintual USD on Fintual's day; a `savings_earnings` row on that account and day for the same cents is the credit (duplicate), a different amount that day is a problem and writes nothing. Runs every hourly poll (a booked credit counts as activity) and in the nightly beside the Fintual mail imports, under `NW_TRACKER_FINTUAL_APPLY`. An expired cookie fails the step.
- **`broker.notifications` v1 (2026-09-30, Phase 3 source 3): Fintual and Racional money mails.** `ingest/src/brokers/brokerEmail.ts` (formerly `server/src/brokerEmailParse.ts`) classifies the staged `cfraser/broker-emails/scan-*.json`, collapses copies by Message-ID and turns each money mail into a canonical notification: what it STATES (`kind`, `ticker`, `fund_name` of a buy, `goal_name` of a retiro, `amount` / `gross_amount`, `units`, `price`, `clp_amount`, `paid_to` bank | broker_balance, the subject as provenance). `import:fintual-emails` / `import:racional-emails` (now ingest commands, `--apply` to write) send one broker's WHOLE staged set each run — re-sending is idempotent, and a notification that could not be booked yet retries by being sent again. The server (`brokerNotificationsApply.ts`) runs the same planners on it (`planFintualEmailBatch` / `planRacionalEmailMovements`, now fed notifications, the fund-name → ticker map and goal-account lookup unchanged) and decides what the mail does not say: whether a notification is bookable (`brokerNotificationIsBookable` — a gross-only dividend never is), and whether the Racional crawl must open (`racionalFetchDecision`: nudges after the newest clean read in `broker_read_coverage`, plus the monthly comisión). `details` carry the plan, the Fintual retiros synthesized from a mail whose bank credit never appeared (the command exits 1, as before) and the Racional crawl decision, which the command writes to `cfraser/.broker-email-decision.json` (removed first, so a failed send never opens the browser). `check:broker-emails` is a local report now (classification and unrecognised templates). Verified on a copy of the real DB: the 14 Fintual and 9 Racional mails plan identically to the old scripts, and six deleted movements were re-written row-for-row the same by both paths. **Fintual deposits and dollar purchases (2026-10-07):** «Recibimos tu depósito» (`deposit`, amount from the body) books checking → «Fintual CLP» from the mail, as Racional's deposit does (a bank debit already on file is left for a human to convert); «Compraste dólares» (`wallet_funded`: dollars, pesos, rate) books Fintual CLP → Fintual USD as a `fx_exchange` with the dollars as counter leg; a buy of a fund no account holds creates the stock account in the bucket the other Fintual stocks sit in (Invesco PHLX Semiconductor ETF → SOXQ). Wires to Fintual go through Fintual CLP from now on; the ~85 historical deposits stay booked checking → goal (79 bought cuotas the same day).
- **A broker adapter (2026-11-07; `brokerAdapter.ts`).** Per broker: its cash account by currency, the account holding a ticker as it prints it (Racional's bare Bolsa symbol → `.SN`), how far a printed dividend may sit from its `dividend_payout` row (Fintual ±5 days, `BROKER_SETTLEMENT_WINDOW_DAYS` — the one settlement window every Fintual match uses; Racional ±2), and where a deposit mail may already be in the ledger. The two rules written once against it: `planDepositFromMail` (checking → broker pesos; a same-pesos transfer inside the lead days is a duplicate, a plain checking debit inside the debit window is for a human to convert) and `pairDividendPayout`. Checking is found as the checking importers find it (`checkingAccountId`), never by a legacy import key. `brokerPlanRow` is the one report row; the split handler takes the adapter. **Two accounts may hold one ticker (2026-11-10; `accountIdForEquityTicker(ticker, { brokerCashAccountId })`, `accountsWithEquityTickerAtBroker` in `accountEquityTicker.ts`):** the demo's Roth IRA holds SPY beside the brokerage SPY account, so among several holders the lookup keeps the one whose shares were bought or sold against the broker's cash account (the evidence `equityBrokerHoldings.ts` groups a position by); one holder anywhere resolves without it, none or several at that broker throws. The Fintual import passes Fintual USD for a dividend and for a buy with several holders; the Fintual adapter's `equityAccountId` does the same.
- **`bank_account.statements` v1 (2026-10-02, Phase 3 source 7): Santander's monthly cartolas.** `{ account: {issuer, product: checking | demand_deposit}, apply, force_reimport, statements: [{document, period_month, period_from, period_to, opening_balance, closing_balance, month_closing_balances, movements: [{date, branch, description, document_no, amount (signed)}], skipped_rows, notes}], unreadable: [{document, error}] }`. Ingest reads the cuenta corriente's xlsx (`ingest/src/santander/checkingCartolaXlsx.ts`, formerly `checkingCartolaParse.ts`'s reader; saldo identity checked) and both accounts' PDFs through the moved Python parsers (`ingest/python/parse-checking-cartola-pdfs.py`, `parse-cuenta-vista-cartola-pdfs.py`, `cartola_layout.py`; their JSON in `cfraser/` as before); `import:checking-cartolas` / `import:cuenta-vista-cartolas` (ingest; `--dry-run`, `--force-reimport`, `--skip-pdf-parse`, `--only-pdf=`, and for the corriente `--xlsx-only`, `--only-xlsx=`, `--dir=`) send one payload per account, and `import:cfraser-inbox` calls them, after `organize:checking-cartola-xlsx` files the inbox xlsx. The server (`applyBankAccountStatements`, `bankAccountStatementsApply.ts`) runs the unchanged `importCartolaList` + `finishCartolaImportRun` (period dedupe, phantom months, the cuenta vista's month split, anchor re-derivation); the old `--wipe` is `npm run wipe:cartola-ledger -w nw-tracker-server` (report-first; on the real DB it fails on FK children, as the old one did). The monthly xlsx upload parses through the service (`/parse/santander.checking_cartola_xlsx`). **A stored month is replaced only by count** (`cartolaImportShouldReplaceExisting`: more movements, or any where the stored one had none): it used to read the stored PDF's text for «sin movimientos», and the annual 2017-10-31 cuenta vista cartola, which prints it for its empty months, re-imported its 11 months (104 movements, new ids) on every `--cuenta-vista` run. Verified on copies of the real DB with four corriente August movements and both accounts' August import records deleted: old and new paths left the same movements (by content) and the same cartola records; the old one also renumbered those 104 vista rows.
- **`bank_account.transfer_notices` v1 (2026-10-06): who each checking transfer was with.** A cartola names a transfer at most by a truncated payee («Transf a INMOBILIARIA Y CONSTR», «Transf. Internet a otro Bancos»); Santander's mails state the counterparty in full — name, RUT, bank, account, e-mail, comment. `fetch:santander-mail-archive --from --to` stages the bank's transactional mails (envelopes first, only transfer / payment / facturado subjects downloaded) under `cfraser/santander-mail-archive/`; `ingest/src/santander/transferMails.ts` decodes every layout since 2015 (2016–18 «Label : value», 2019–23 «Comprobante … realizada con fecha», including the variant that prints two labels then both values, 2024+ «Datos de origen / destino», incoming «ha instruido / realizó una transferencia», an employer's payroll wire, transfers between own products, scheduled ones) into `{kind: outgoing | incoming | between_own_products | schedule_created, date, amount, from, to, comment}`; `import:transfer-mails` (nightly after the card feed, last 21 days; `--archive=`, `--days=`, `--dry-run`) sends them. The server (`bankTransferNotices.ts`, migration 214) stores each notice once (`bank_transfer_notices`; a resend must state the same) and rebuilds every pairing on each apply (`movement_transfer_notices`, one notice per movement side): same amount on the account the mail names (`bank_account_numbers` — the cuenta vista is declared too), posted from the mail's day up to 6 days later, mails in sending order, each bank row once, so the recipient's echo of an own transfer and a schedule notice pair with nothing. Among same-day candidates it prefers a transfer to the account the counterparty is (`transfer_counterparty_accounts`, by RUT or account number — the fund manager, the BancoEstado ahorro, the card accounts; personal data, live DB only), then a single-leg row, then a transfer elsewhere; rows still tied that differ only by id are twins and pair in order, any other tie is reported and left unpaired. On the real DB: 1.016 notices since 2015, 942 paired (every one from the cartola years), 0 ambiguous; the rest are before the bank history, echoes, or schedule notices. **Banco de Chile's mails, and movements written from a mail (2026-10-07; `ingest/src/bancochile/transferMails.ts`, `transferNoticeMovements.ts`, migrations 219–220):** `import:transfer-mails` reads two mailers — Santander and `serviciodetransferencias@bancochile.cl`, which mails the recipient of its clients' transfers (the user's father's wires; «Transferencias de Fondos de <sender>» and the 2024 «Aviso de transferencia de fondos»; the Rut and account printed are the recipient's) — and pairs every mailer's notices together against every numbered account, whichever bank holds it. The hourly poll runs it with `--days=2`. A notice mailed in the last 3 days whose bank row has not arrived gets its movement written from the mail (`mailMovementForNotice`): an `incoming` transfer's credit; a transfer between two numbered accounts («Transferencia entre productos», one mail per transfer — the second mail of a transfer to an own account at another bank is an incoming notice to an unnumbered account, which writes nothing) as one transfer; a payment to a known counterparty (`transfer_counterparty_accounts`) as a transfer into its landing account (`landingAccountFor`: its only account, or its one `import:panel|kind=clp|` balance — Fintual CLP, Racional CLP; card numbers are left to the payment receipts); anything else as a debit. The broker's own mail («Recibimos tu depósito», Racional's deposit mail, whose duplicate check now reaches 5 days back) writes the same transfer when it lands first and recognizes this one when it lands second. A written transfer is confirmed through the importers' transfer-leg skip (`confirmTransferNoticeMovementForTransferLeg`). The row is dated the mail's day, noted `import:<issuer>-mail|<sent at>|Transf a <payee> (<bank>)` / `Transf. de <sender>` (+ ` — <message>`), recorded with its signed pesos in `transfer_notice_movements`, and never written twice for one mail, even after its movement is deleted. Both checking importers keep that row and skip the bank's listing as `superseded_by_mail` (same signed pesos, the mail's day from the business day before the bank day through it, or a movement already confirmed on that exact bank day), stamping the bank's day as its posting day and the bank's description; a movement no feed lists within 2 business days fails the step (`overdue`). Its note carries the sender's name, not the bank's RUT digits, so `propose-card-reimbursements.ts --payer-note-token` must name the sender for such rows. On the real DB: 121 Banco de Chile mails since 2024, 120 paired with their bank rows, the 2026-10-07 wire written as movement 12997; that day's Fintual wire receipt paired with the deposit transfer the Fintual mail had written (12994).
- **`bank_account.incoming_wires` v1 (2026-10-08; `incomingWires.ts`, migration 228): dollar withdrawals booked from mail.** Santander USD (#108) has no movement feed, so a Fintual dollar withdrawal is booked from three mails. Fintual's «Retiro en dólares confirmado» (`broker.notifications` kind `withdrawal_requested`: net, gross, destination account, pay day — `due_on` is the next date with the printed day of the month) can still be cancelled, so it is only stored (`broker_withdrawal_requests`). Banco Security's «Envío de Transferencia» (a plain-text copy of the MT103 it sent: 32A value date and amount, 50K ordering customer, 59F beneficiary account) and Santander's «AVISO DE LIQUIDACION DE ORDEN DE PAGO RECIBIDA» (order number and amount; no account, so the bank's one declared dollar account) are `bank_account.incoming_wires` notices (`incoming_wire_notices`; a resend must state the same), decoded in `ingest/src/wires/` and sent by `import:transfer-mails` (hourly `--days=2`, nightly 21 days; `--issuer=wires` runs only these). A wire is the notices of one transfer (same account via `bank_account_numbers`, leading zeros ignored; same cents; value dates within 2 days; one per side). A request pairs with the one wire into its destination account for exactly its net, from its request day to 7 days past its pay day, with no notice naming another ordering customer; the pair writes a transfer broker cash → that account for the net on the wire's value date, plus a `cash_fee` of gross − net on the broker cash account (a hand-entered transfer and fee in the window count as the booked ones). Every apply of either kind re-pairs everything stored; `incoming_wire_bookings` records each request once and survives its movements' deletion. A request 3 days past its pay day with no wire fails the step; a wire no request claims is reported. First case: the 2026-10-08 withdrawal (US$1.xxx,xx − 10 fee), movements 13017 + 13018.
- **`payment.processor_receipts` v3 (2026-10-06; v2 and v3 2026-10-07): who a processor charge actually paid.** A charge that names only the processor («PAGOS.FLOW.CL (WEB)», «FLOW *PINARES.CL», «PAGO FACIL») says nothing about the shop; the processor's receipt mail does: payee (name, RUT, e-mail when printed), amount, time, order, concept, the statement descriptor. `archiveMails` (`ingest/src/email/santanderMailArchive.ts`, which Santander's archive now calls too) stages one sender's mails; `ingest/src/payments/paymentReceiptMails.ts` holds one decoder per processor (Flow, both its layouts; Pago Fácil, dated by the mail since it prints no time), a non-receipt from the same sender (an invoice still to pay) decodes to null and an unreadable receipt throws; `import:payment-receipt-mails` (nightly after the transfer mails, last 45 days; `--days=`, `--from= --to=`, `--archive=`, `--dry-run`) sends them. The server stores each receipt once (migration 215, `payment_processor_receipts`; a resend must state the same) and pairs receipts with expense lines **when the lines are built** (`withPaymentReceipts` in `paymentProcessorReceipts.ts`, last step of the expenses payload), never stored, since a card line's id changes on every re-import: same pesos (an interest plan's total line by the principal its statements print, `ccInstallmentInterestForAccount`), the line's purchase day from the day before the payment to five days after, receipts in payment order and each line once, nearest day first, then a line whose merchant names the processor; identical lines are twins and pair in order, any other tie stays unpaired. Every line of a paired purchase (its cuotas too) carries `payment_receipt`, shown under the merchant like a transfer's counterparty. On the real DB: 51 receipts since 2019, 39 paired; unpaired the 11 Klare test payments (no charge) and a 2019-12 one inside the mail-rebuilt months. **Shop order confirmations ride the same kind (2026-10-07):** a Shopify store's «Confirmación de pedido» (from the shop's domain or `t.shopifyemail.com`, so it is fetched by a Gmail query — `archiveMails` takes `gmraw` instead of a sender) decodes to platform `shopify`: the shop (the subject's bracket, else the text before «Pedido #…», else the sender's display name), the items and «Total $X CLP». Orders and processor receipts are paired separately (`matchPaymentReceiptsToExpenseLines`), so one charge can have both — a Konko order paid through Pago Fácil — and the line shows the order. On the real DB all 12 Shopify orders since 2021 paired, most of them behind MERPAGO* / MERCADO PAGO charges. **Shops' own order confirmations (2026-10-07)** ride the same kind and pair beside the processor receipts (`ORDER_CONFIRMATION_SOURCES`): Calvin Klein's «Tu pedido ha sido realizado exitosamente» (VTEX; Total must equal the payment block's Valor), adidas's «hemos recibido tu pedido» / «Gracias por tu pedido» (the preparing, shipping and pick-up mails decode to null), Club Dominó's «¡Pedido Confirmado!» (the branch in the payee, the order's printed time, the total with the tip), Eventbrite's order mail (only a total marked CLP is read, free orders are null; no paid order exists yet, so that path is untested on a real mail) and miCoca-Cola's «Hemos recibido tu pedido … con éxito» / «Pago Aprobado» (from contacto@micoca-cola.cl — the DispatchTrack mails carry no amounts; the total must equal subtotal + descuentos + despacho). **v2 adds dollars** (`currency: usd`, to the cent; migration 216) for DynaVap's order mails, which pair only with a line carrying the same dollars to the cent — never with a peso line at the day's rate, which any purchase of the week could fit. On the real DB 111 of the 112 shop orders paired (miCoca-Cola 97, Calvin Klein 3, adidas 5, Club Dominó 1, DynaVap 5); the unpaired one is a miCoca-Cola order cancelled and refunded the same day. Another processor or shop platform is one decoder (plus a merchant hint for a processor). **v3 (migration 221): split charges and receipts without an amount, for MercadoLibre** («Compraste …» mails, `decodeMercadoLibreOrder`). An order from several sellers is charged once per seller: «Pagaste $ 15.725 1x $ 8.865 y 1x $ 6.860» → `charges` (stored in `payment_receipt_charges`, must add up to the amount), and its lines pair all or none, each showing «n/N» beside the payee; «6x $ 9.245» alone is one charge in cuotas. Since 2026 the mail prints no amount at all (`amount: null`): the product link is what matters, so such a receipt pairs after every amount-bearing one, with the unclaimed line on the nearest day (−1…+5) whose merchant is the store's own statement name (`STATEMENT_NAME_FOR_AMOUNTLESS`: «MERCADO LIBRE», «*N PRODUCTOS») or one of its sellers (`SELLER_NAMED_CHARGE`: «MERCADOPAGO *TIENDAUNO») — never a bare «MERPAGO*», which is also MercadoPago's in-person payments; a printed amount stays a hard check. Sellers are printed from mid-2024 («Información del vendedor … RUT»), earlier receipts name «Mercado Libre». **Amazon (2026-10-07): a receipt per shipment.** Amazon charges the card's dollar side once per shipment, the day it ships, for the shipment's total — not the order total, which counts an import-fee deposit and items charged with other shipments. So `amazon` receipts are the shipping mails (`decodeAmazonShipment`: «Shipment Total» 2013, «SHIPMENT TOTAL» 2017–2021, «Shipment total» 2024, «Total» in the «Shipped: "…"» layout; a $0 shipment charged nothing and decodes to null). Shipments of one order that no line carries one by one pair with one line of their sum, from the latest (two shipments of 2025-09-16/17 were charged as one 54,61). The order confirmations are `amazon_order` (`ORDER_SUMMARY_OF`): read only for an order none of whose shipments paired, up to 30 days after it was placed — a gift-card balance paid part of a shipment and the card was charged the order total, or a shipment had no mail; a 2013 confirmation priced by Amazon's currency converter decodes to null. On the real DB 44 of the 49 Amazon lines paired; the other five have no mail stating their dollars. **LATAM, Uber, rides and subscriptions (2026-10-09; migration 233).** LATAM's purchase mails (`ingest/src/payments/latamMails.ts`: «Confirmacion de compra» 2018–21, the English «E-Ticket Confirmation» in dollars, «Confirmación compra» 2021–23, «Ya compraste tu viaje» 2025 on) state the itinerary and booking (`latam`); a ticket change that charged a fare difference is `latam_change`. Uber's (`uberMails.ts`): `uber` trip receipts carry the ride (`trip`: from, to, start and end on the Chile clock, km; the line shows each address up to its first comma, `trip_label`, the tooltip the full ones), `uber_eats` the restaurant as payee, `uber_one` the monthly membership with `subscription: true`; the card amount is what the card was charged (Uber Cash legs dropped), and a ride or order in another currency decodes to null. Uber first mails a trip or order summary («not a payment receipt»): `uber_trip_summary` / `uber_eats_summary` are read only for a trip or order no receipt paired (`ORDER_SUMMARY_OF`, keyed by the trip's printed start or the order's xid). From 2017 to 2021 Uber BV charged peso rides and orders in dollars on the card: such a peso receipt also pairs with a dollar line that names Uber, at the day's rate within 4 % (`PESO_RECEIPT_CHARGED_IN_DOLLARS`), an exact amount ranking first on a day. **A subscription's charge reads «Suscripciones»** (`withSubscriptionCategory`, the last step of the expense lines): when only the bank's name for the charge decided its category — a merchant rule or none (`resolveCcExpenseCategory`'s `fromBankName`; the mail names the real vendor, the bank only the processor: «UBER» covers rides and Uber One alike) — and its link says the charge is a subscription's; an «Único» row or a category set on the line itself stays: an App Store receipt whose items all renew, a charge inferred from a subscription's cycle or a monthly run, an Uber One receipt. On the real DB: 9 of 13 LATAM charges paired (the rest predate the mails or are one card charge for two mails, 2025-06-03), 478 of 499 Uber lines, the App Store's renewing charges (Apple One, iCloud, Apple TV+ …) moved from «sin categoría» to Suscripciones, and 23 Uber One charges from the «UBER» rule's Transporte. **Receipts abroad, Despegar and stays (2026-10-10; migration 234).** A receipt may be in any currency (`currency`: lowercase ISO 4217; table rebuilt with foreign keys off). The card's dollar statement prints each charge's original amount (`amount_orig`, carried on dollar lines with `amount_orig_currency` = the import's clp / usd label or null for any other): a receipt in another currency pairs with the dollar line whose original amount is the same to the cent (never by conversion), and a peso receipt also pairs with a dollar line whose peso origin is its amount (Airbnb in Chile billed on the dollar statement). Uber's receipts abroad decode in their own currency, dated by the mail (the printed local times name no zone). Despegar (`despegarMails.ts`) states the airline's and its own charge separately («desglosado en un cargo correspondiente a LATAM … y otros correspondientes a Despegar») → `charges`. Lodging (`stayMails.ts`): `booking` (confirmations and «Your updated booking»), `airbnb` (the reservation confirmation; card rows only, so a stay paid with credit is null), `stripe` (a property's own receipt through Stripe), `accor`; each carries `stay` {check_in, check_out, city}. A Booking stay the property charges has no `statement_descriptor` and pairs from the mail's day to five days past check-out, nearest to the mail's day or to check-in; only a reservation's latest Booking mail is paired (`ONE_MAIL_PER_ORDER`); a line printing the receipt's own reference («AIRBNB * HM…») wins a tie. A stay no line carries at its stated amount (a card surcharge, city tax on top, quoted in dollars and charged in pesos, or the property charging only its own fees) links as a guess («≈», `guess_basis`) to every untaken line from the day before check-in to the day after check-out whose merchant has the property name's first distinctive word (four or more letters, accents folded, lodging words and the stay's city left out: «Hotel Ejemplo Lisboa» → EJEMPLO): on the real DB four stays (a card surcharge, a city tax charged apart, two peso charges, a tourism fee). On the real DB: Booking 10 lines, Airbnb 8, Stripe 2, Accor 1, Despegar's two charges, and Uber lines without a receipt 21 → 3 (16 Brazil rides of 2021 were paid with a card the app has no statements for). **Vehicle permits (2026-11-05; migration 268, `ingest/src/payments/vehiclePermitMails.ts`).** Municipalidad de Maipú mails the permiso de circulación as a PDF (`permisocirculacion@maipu.cl`, «Permiso de Circulación»; `PaymentProcessor.pdfAttachment` stages it with its `pdftotext -layout` text). It has no printed labels, so fields are read by position and shape, and every one must agree with its copies: folio, permit year, payment day, plate (old `AB1234-5` or new `ABCD12-K` shape), the two accounting parts that add up to the amount, the amount printed three times, the tasación, the expiry, the car. Expiry 31/08 of the year = cuota 1 of 2; 31/03 of the next year = cuota 2 of 2, or the whole year when paid before August (the second cuota is only payable from August). Sent as processor `municipalidad_maipu` with `vehicle_permit` {permit_year, plate, cuota, cuotas, expires_on} (optional on the contract; `permit_*` columns), payee «Municipalidad de Maipú», concept «Permiso de circulación 2026 · 1ª cuota», which is also the line's link label (no category rule per permit). The four permits since 2025 decode; Peñalolén's mails (2016–2024) only linked to PDFs its server no longer serves. **Ticket vendors and the event (2026-11-02; migrations 255–256, `ingest/src/payments/ticketMails.ts`).** A ticket receipt carries `event` {name, starts_on, starts_at, ends_on, venue} (optional on the contract, like `trip` and `stay`; `event_*` columns): `puntoticket` («Comprobante de Compra - Orden: N»; an event's layout, or an add-on such as parking bought apart with no event; items + despacho = total), `toliv` (tolivmarket.com, toliv.com since 2026-10; tickets + comisión = total; a guest-list ticket with no payment block is null), `ticketmaster_cl` («Tu compra para X se ha realizado con éxito»; a date printed without its year is the first one on or after the mail, its weekday checked), `ticketmaster_nz` (NZ$), `paylogic` (euros; it mails the same order again whenever the tickets change state, so only an order's FIRST mail is paired — `ONE_MAIL_PER_ORDER`, where Booking keeps the latest), `aitickets` and `ticketplus` (no amount printed: pairs by the charge's name, «TICKETPLUS», on its day; courtesy tickets and invitations are null). Free orders are null everywhere. The merchant stays the vendor (vendor catalog: PuntoTicket, Toliv, Ticketmaster, AI Tickets, Ticketplus, Paylogic) and the row's link label is the event with its day (`link_label.event_on`, ISO), or what was bought when a vendor's receipt names no event; no category rule per event. Eventbrite's decoder does not fill `event` (its mails print the date in four languages, no real sample to check). On a copy of the real DB every ticket receipt since 2017 paired (15 PuntoTicket, 5 Toliv, 2 Ticketmaster, 2 Paylogic orders, AI Tickets, Ticketplus); unpaired by design: Toliv's in-event bar charges («TUU*toliv»), purchases at the festival itself, and a «WPAY PLUS CRED TICKETP» with no mail.
- **`store.receipt` v1 (2026-10-03, Phase 3 source 8): a grocery receipt.** `{ document: {kind: email | pdf | photo, key, photo_taken_on}, receipt: {chain, number | null, branch, city, purchased_at | null, purchase_date_source, items: [{position, barcode, description, qty, qty_unit, unit_price, total, discount, discount_labels}], receipt_discounts, payments: [{method, amount}], loyalty_points} }`, one receipt per request. Ingest owns the documents: the photo inbox (`ingest/src/grocery/inbox.ts`), OCR and the chain parsers (`ingest/python/parse-grocery-receipts.py`, `grocery_receipt_*.py`, the Swift OCR helper), the staged corpus and its import stamps (`stagedReceipts.ts`); `npm run import:grocery-receipts` (ingest; `--dry-run`, `--full`, `--reparse`, `--skip-parse`; stops at once when nothing is pending, so `import:cfraser-inbox` runs it on every pass) sends each receipt whose stamp is not current and stamps the outcomes the server marks `final` (a receipt waiting for its card line is sent again every run). When a document takes a receipt over (`replaced`), ingest clears the displaced document's stamp and sends it again in the same run, so it is stamped `skipped_duplicate` — the old importer found that case by reading the stamp's row in the DB; a wiped DB now needs `--full`. The server (`applyStoreReceipt`, `storeReceiptApply.ts`, formerly `groceryReceiptsImport.ts`) keeps identity and ownership, the receipt + item writes, alias classification, the card-line rules and branch learning, and drops the card's caches when it writes a line. Migration 205 named `grocery_receipts.source` after the document kind (`lider_email` → `email`, `manual_pdf` → `pdf`). Verified on copies of the real DB with the five newest receipts deleted: a `--full` re-import through the old importer and through the new path gave the same outcome for all 94 staged receipts and identical receipts and items (creation timestamps aside).
- **`employment.payslips` v2 (2026-10-03, Phase 3 source 9; v2 2026-10-07): payslips (liquidaciones de sueldo).** `{ parser_version, payslips: [{document, period_month, employer: {name, rut}, pay_period_label, kind: salary | severance, earnings: {base_salary, meal_allowance, transport_allowance, bonus, taxable_total, non_taxable_total, total}, deductions: {pension, health, income_tax, unemployment_insurance, voluntary_pension, other, total}, net_pay, indices: {uf, utm, pension_cap_uf, unemployment_cap_uf}, lines: [{position, side: haber | descuento, section: imponible | no_imponible | legal | other | null, label, amount}]}] }` — every payslip in one request, because pairing each with the deposit that paid it is decided over the whole set. **v2 carries every printed line** (`payslip_lines`, migration 222, replaced as a whole on re-import): the parser reads each layout's HABERES | DESCUENTOS block (one column-aware reader for the text layouts — the right column starts after the first row's left amount; Talana's non-taxable lines are the trailing haberes adding up to its TOTAL NO IMPONIBLE header cell — and one for the OCR'd scans, where «(46,639)» is a negative haber) and fails unless the lines add up to every total the payslip prints and haberes − descuentos = líquido; the contract re-checks the same sums. Each line gets its kind at import (`payslipLineKinds.ts`: rules over the label without accents and Talana's « (LQ)», first match wins; an unknown label fails the import before anything is written; migration 223). The printed AFP line is split when read: mandatory = 10 % of the payslip's (already capped) taxable base, commission = the rest — it lands on each AFP's published commission (PlanVital 0,41 %, Modelo 0,77/0,58 %, UNO 0,69/0,49/0,46 %). **A contribution's payroll month lives in `pension_contribution_periods`** (migration 223; one movement can pay several months), written by the AFP certificate read, the AFC certificate import and the AFP ledger rebuild, history read once from the notes (`server/scripts/backfill-pension-contribution-periods.ts`); a payslip's contributions are the movements whose period is its month. **Finiquitos are payslips** (`earning_type = 'severance'`, file `<YYYY-MM>-finiquito.pdf` beside the month's payslip): the Dirección del Trabajo's electronic form (`dt_finiquito`: every non-zero «- Concepto $ amount» of PAGOS and DESCUENTOS; TOTAL PAGOS BRUTOS, TOTAL DESCUENTOS, SUBTOTAL and SUMA LÍQUIDA must hold) and a notarial scan read by OCR (`finiquito_scan`). **Rebuilt payslips** (`payroll_work_earnings.origin = 'rebuilt'` + `rebuilt_basis`, migration 225; the older `synthetic:` rows were marked so) are written by `server/scripts/rebuild-payslips.ts --plan=<json> [--apply]` (report-first; plan in untracked `cfraser/payroll-rebuilt.json`): lines derived from a stored row's breakdown, the USD contract as its fee less the transfer fee in dollars (`contractor_fee` / `transfer_fee`; a USD payslip's lines carry cents), new payslips with their deposit, and `payslip_advances` (the deposit that paid an advance a payslip nets). Every payslip's lines must add up to its totals and net pay. **Gross vs net (`payrollBreakdown.ts`, `payroll_breakdown` on `/api/income`):** per payslip, payroll month and year, gross split by line kind (taxable, non-taxable, indemnities, contractor fees), each deduction group (the AFP line split into the 10 % and the commission; `other_deductions` = insurance + fees + a finiquito's combined contributions + advances) and net — in pesos and dollars (a peso payslip at the buy rate on its pay day, a dollar one at its stored rate); a payslip whose deductions are not gross − net throws. The income page's «Bruto vs líquido» chart and table only pick. **Payroll deductions are gastos** (`payslipExpenseLines.ts`, source `payslip`, merged into the expenses payload): health, the Isapre top-up and life insurance → «Salud», the AFP commission (AFP line − 10 % of the taxable base) → «Comisiones AFP», the tax withheld → «Impuestos», the USD contract's transfer fee → «Comisiones»; savings (AFP 10 %, AFC, APV), advances and a finiquito's combined contributions never. Dated to the payslip's month (`occurred_on` = the day the pay arrived); category fixed (`purchase_key` `payslip:<payslip id × 1000 + position>`, the assign route refuses the source, the lines table shows the category without controls, no big group); kept out of real-estate bill matching. **The Expenses page tracks spending from net pay (2026-11-03):** these lines carry `pre_tax` (`isPreTaxExpenseLine`) and its gastos views and line sections leave them out unless its «Include pre-tax expenses» box is on (`include_pre_tax=1` on the gastos endpoint, `useExpensesIncludePreTax` per browser); home «Últimos movimientos» never lists them; the flows overview always counts them. **The flows overview adds them back to income** (`payslipGastosLines`, monthly and daily), so «net» = income − expenses − deposits does not count money that never reached the account twice. **Legal parameters and checks (2026-10-08):** `payroll.parameters` v1 (migration 226: `payroll_parameters`, `payroll_afp_rates`) carries Previred's monthly «Indicadores Previsionales» — UF, UTM, the pension/health and unemployment caps in UF, each AFP's worker rate (10 % + commission), the employer's share into the account (pension reform: 0,1 % from 2025-08; read from the document each month, never hardcoded), the indefinite contract's AFC rates. `npm run import:previred-indicators` (ingest; `--from=`, `--no-fetch`, `--dry-run`) downloads the archive's missing months into `cfraser/previred-indicadores/<YYYY-MM>.pdf`, reads them (`previredIndicators.ts`; a field it cannot read fails) and sends them; the nightly runs it with the payslips. All 113 months 2017-05 → 2026-09 matched `uf_daily` (month-end) and `utm_daily` exactly. Payslips carry the AFP they name (`pension_fund`, optional on the contract; rebuilt ones from the plan). `payslipChecks.ts` recomputes each peso salary payslip when read — capped base, AFP at its published rate, health ≥ 7 %, AFC 0,6 % (when charged), income tax by the monthly brackets (the annual table's factors in UTM, health deductible up to the legal 7 %), AFP credited vs 10 % + employer share — and records differences, never refusing; taxable pay follows the line's printed section (Talana's teletrabajo allowance is taxable), the kind only when none is printed. A payslip newer than the newest published month is `pending`; an older month without parameters throws. `/api/income` carries `payslip_checks` (payslip table «Revisión» column), `/api/tax-return` the year's `payroll_withholding` (withheld vs table, per month). On the real DB every document payslip's tax matches the table for 2017 and 2022–2025; the differences left are employers' (Dealsyte 2018-10→12 uncapped base, Webdox 2026-09 deducting the Isapre top-up) and the rebuilt months. **F22 settlement (2026-10-08; migration 227, `f22Settlements.ts`):** how each filed form was settled — the refund it asked for (code 87) or the payment (code 91) — against linked bank evidence in `f22_settlements` (account, day, pesos; the movement for a checking row; a card payment is found again by account, day and pesos, and evidence that is gone throws). Links are proposed by `server/scripts/link-f22-settlements.ts [--extra=…] [--apply]` (report-first: a «DEV IMPUESTO» checking credit up to 6 % above the refund — the Tesorería pays it reajustado — or a «SII» / «F22» / «TGR» card line of exactly the payment), never matched at runtime; the tax-return page's «Liquidación del F22» shows asked vs received/paid and the adjustment. **Offsets (migration 229):** a refund the Tesorería kept to pay another year's debt («compensación», no account) is one `offset` row under the refund's year naming `offset_tax_year`, read by both years (`offset_kept` / `offset_paid`; `--offset=<refund year>:<debt year>:<date>:<pesos>`). A refund linked to a movement outside checking (AT2023, paid in cash at BancoEstado and deposited into the savings account) is an income line (`F22 AT<year>`), its deposit reconciles as `resolved_tax_refund` and gets no ahorro-split checking mirror. On the real DB: AT2018–2021 and AT2023 refunds received, AT2022's corrected return (01/07/2024, observation V58 on the AFP withdrawal) paid 4xx.xxx by card in 2022 plus 4x.xxx by card and the AT2025 refund (1xx.xxx) on 2025-05-14, AT2026 paid by card. Plan: `docs/payroll-plan.md`. The parser moved to `ingest/python/parse-payroll-liquidaciones.py` (`npm run parse:payroll-liquidaciones`; it had been importing the OCR helper that left `server/scripts/` with item 6, so it could not run from there), and `npm run import:payroll-liquidaciones` (ingest; `--dry-run` lists every field a re-import would change, `--no-strict`) sends `cfraser/payroll-parsing-output/all.json` and exits 1 on a payslip no deposit pays, or several could. The server (`applyEmploymentPayslips`, `payslipsApply.ts`, formerly the server script) keeps the upsert by document, the pairing (`findPayrollAutoLinkMovement`) and the hand-made links it never overwrites. With it the last Python left `server/scripts/` (its `statement_values.py` copy and `.pdf_deps` went too). **Fetched from the employer's Buk portal (2026-10-06; `ingest/src/buk/fetchMain.ts`, `npm run fetch:buk-payslips`):** two-step sign-in (the WORK e-mail in `cfraser/buk-fetch.json`, password in Keychain `nw-tracker-buk`), the full liquidaciones table at `/profile/employees/<id>/liquidaciones` (the portal shortcut's `?source_request=Shortcuts` variant lists only three months, labelled «Septiembre 2026»), each missing month saved as `cfraser/liquidaciones/<YYYY>/<YYYY-MM>.pdf` plus Buk's web view as `.html` beside it. The parser's `buk` layout («$ amount» cells) fails unless every section total equals its lines and haberes − descuentos the líquido; health = the 7% plus «Adicional Salud». **Schedule:** the server's nightly request carries `payslips` (`decidePayslipsRun`): `fetch: true` from the 1st through the 15th while the previous month has no payslip, else `fetch: false` (parse + import only) while the newest payslip has no deposit paired; the runner imports `--no-strict`. **Pairing reads the income lines**, which include the daily feed's credits (see «One source for a checking account's movements»), so a payslip pairs with the salary the night it lands instead of a week later. When the cartola supersedes that daily row, `prunePartialMovementsSupersededByCartola` moves the payslip link and the row's `checking_income_movement_overrides` entry onto the official row (`carryMovementKeyedIncomeState`) — both reference movements without a cascade, so a link left behind would fail the cartola import on the foreign key. Verified: the moved parser read all 73 liquidaciones to the stored parse field for field, and on copies of the real DB with five payslips deleted and every automatic link cleared, the old script and the new path wrote identical rows (70 linked, the three 2017 ones unpaired; `imported_at` aside).
- **`broker.dividend_statement` v1 (2026-10-03, Phase 3 source 10a): Fintual's «Acciones» documents.** `{ broker: "fintual", document: {kind: monthly_statement | certificate, name, label}, dividends: [{date, symbol, gross, withholding, net, per_share, position_qty, record_date, withholding_rate_pct, withholding_jurisdiction, tax_country}], interest: [{date, amount, description}] }`, one per document. Ingest (`fintual/acciones.ts`, `accionesDocs.ts`) lists the PDFs `fetch:fintual-docs` stages under `cfraser/fintual-acciones/`, reads each with `pdftotext -layout` and states what it prints — the withholding jurisdiction too (`US` where the Alpaca cartola prints an NRA line; the certificado names none); `npm run import:fintual-acciones` (ingest, `--apply` to write; the root script delegates, so both runners keep calling it) prints the old report and exits 1 on a conflict. The server (`applyBrokerDividendStatement`, `brokerDividendStatementApply.ts`, formerly `fintualAccionesImport.ts`) keeps the pairing with the ledger's `dividend_payout` rows and the `movement_dividend_details` writes. Verified on copies of the real DB with the eight Fintual breakdowns deleted: the old script and the new path re-wrote them identically.
- **`fund_account.transactions` v1 (2026-10-03, Phase 3 source 10b): the Fintual certificado de transacciones.** `{ provider: "fintual", document, max_month, transactions: [{date, investment: {id, name}, medio, clp_in, clp_out, units_in, units_out, unit_value}] }` — the CSV's rows that move pesos or cuotas, as printed (its daily balance rows, ~8.700 of them, were read for a Reserva balance and APV-A fields nothing used, and are not sent). Ingest (`fintual/certificadoInbox.ts`, `certificado.ts`) installs a certificado dropped in the inbox as `cfraser/fintual-certificado-de-transacciones.csv` and reads it; `npm run import:fintual-cert` (ingest; `--apply`, `--from-inbox` installs first, `IMPORT_MAX_MONTH`) prints the reconcile, and `import:cfraser-inbox` calls it with `--from-inbox` (report only) on every run — nothing to do without a new certificado. The server (`applyFundAccountTransactions` in `fintualCertImport.ts`, the aggregation in `fintualCertificadoTransacciones.ts`) keeps the goal → account mapping, the reconcile against curated flows and the valor-cuota backfill. Verified on copies of the real DB with six cert rows deleted: the report was byte-identical and `--apply` re-added the six identically through both paths.
- **`unemployment_fund.documents` v1 (2026-10-03, Phase 3 source 10c): the AFC certificate and cartolas.** `{ provider: "afc", certificate: {legs: [{period_month, employer_rut, employer, taxable_income, amount, paid_on}], total}, statements: [{period_from, period_to, opening: {date, balance}, closing: {date, balance}, contributions, other_income, gain, total_income, commissions, other_outflows, account_use, total_outflows detail: [{employer, pay_month, amount}]}] }` (v2 2026-11-07: the account is the one on the fund's series, and the one-off rebuild options are gone) — the contract re-checks the printed identities (legs = TOTAL, closing = opening + income − outflows). Ingest (`afc/documents.ts`, the parsers moved from the server) reads the PDFs; `npm run import:afc-cert` (ingest; same flags as before) prints the server's report. The server (`applyUnemploymentFundDocuments` in `afcCertImport.ts`) runs the rebuild exactly as the script did — contributions, withdrawal units, true-ups, re-measured closing withdrawals, ledger checks — in one transaction it rolls back without `apply`, and returns the report text. The two rebuild options name ledger rows, not document content; they ride in the payload because the run is manual. Verified on a real-DB copy with the real 2026-09-22 certificate and the Jan–Apr 2026 cartola: the old script and the new path printed the same 116-line report.
- **`unemployment_fund.balance` v1 and the nightly AFC read (2026-10-17; `ingest/src/afc/fetch.ts`, `ingest/src/claveUnica/`, `server/src/unemploymentFundBalanceApply.ts`, migration 240).** `npm run fetch:afc [-- --apply --background]` signs in to the AFC's sucursal virtual (personas.afc.cl) **with the AFC's own clave** (`ingest/src/afc/login.ts`: the portal's `input#rut` — the Angular wrapper carries the same id — the password field and the type=button «Ingresar con contraseña AFC», clicked; the page's invisible reCAPTCHA makes its own token; a refused attempt is reloaded once; Keychain `nw-tracker-afc`, account = the RUT in `cfraser/afc-fetch.json`; one second, no second factor — 2026-10-19). `--clave-unica` takes the **ClaveÚnica** path instead, kept for any ClaveÚnica service — `ingest/src/claveUnica/login.ts`: the service's «ClaveÚnica» button, the RUN (typed compact, the form formats it) and clave from Keychain `nw-tracker-clave-unica` (`CLAVE_UNICA_KEYCHAIN_SERVICE`, account = the RUT), Enter then the «INGRESA» control when the form is still up, «Continuar» on the «te enviaremos un código» page, and the 6-character code read from the mailbox by `codeMail.ts` (sender `no-reply@digital.gob.cl`, subject «ClaveÚnica - Código de validación de autenticación», one base64 HTML part inside a multipart container, found through the MIME structure; only a mail newer than this run's «Continuar» click counts, and after 30 s without one the newest inside the code's 5-minute validity, since ClaveÚnica re-uses a still-valid code instead of mailing again; hundreds of such mails sit in the mailbox, every ClaveÚnica sign-in sends one) typed into the six boxes and confirmed. Reusable for any ClaveÚnica service. Then the read: (1) the home page's own summary call (`apis.afc.cl/sva-portada/v1`: `saldo`, `fechaUltimaCotizacion`; it answers 500 «Error al obtener datos de la portada» some loads, shown as «Saldo no disponible», which the read reports as an unavailable balance, never 0) goes to the server as `unemployment_fund.balance` — `applyUnemploymentFundBalance` records it in `unemployment_fund_reads` beside the app's value of the account that day (`accountMarkClpAtYmd`); **report only**, the step prints site / app / difference and the last credited month against the ledger's; (2) the certificate of paid contributions over the whole history, produced **by the page** (`/documentos/certificados` → the «Cotizaciones pagadas» card → «Histórico» in its chooser → «Descargar»; the API behind it, `sva-certificados/v1/certificado-cotizaciones-pagadas`, refuses a direct call with «Error sin captcha» — the page attaches its invisible reCAPTCHA token, never worked around) arrives as a page-built blob kept by browser.ts's download stash (emptied in place: the init script holds its own reference), is saved as `cfraser/afc-certs/cotizaciones-latest.pdf` and sent with **every cartola staged under `cfraser/afc-certs/cartolas/`** as one `unemployment_fund.documents` — idempotent, so a contribution the fund credits (paid through Previred by the ~13th) is written at its pay date the night it shows, and the parser is the one `import:afc-cert` uses. **The cartolas come by mail:** `npm run fetch:afc-cartolas` (`ingest/src/email/afcCartolas.ts`; nightly with the other mail fetchers) stages the PDF attached to `cartolasafc@afc.cl`'s «Cartola cuatrimestral / Anual Seguro de Cesantía» (June, October, February), keyed by mail date + message id, decrypted in place with qpdf (password = the RUT's digits without the verifier, derived from the config). The mailed rendering prints `Al 31/12/2025 … $ 3.652.167` and a footnote on the detalle's Total line where the sucursal virtual's download prints `Al 31-12-2025 … $3.652.167`; `parseAfcCartola` reads both. **A cartola true-up is keyed on its saldo DAY** (`AFC ajuste cartola — saldo YYYY-MM-DD`, `afcCartolaTrueUpNoteKey`): a period's saldo final is the next period's saldo inicial, the same figure, so both cartolas describe one row; rows under the legacy `saldo inicial|final <day>` key are matched and rekeyed on their next update. **Schedule (2026-10-21):** a nightly request carries `afc_fetch` (`decideAfcFetch`: `reason`, `expected_period`, `overdue`) from the **14th of the month after the newest salary payslip's month** until that month's contribution is in `pension_contribution_periods` on the AFC account — Previred's electronic deadline is the 13th and the fund credits a few days later (September 2026: paid on the 9th, credited the 21st); a month without a payslip costs no read, and once the following month has ended without the credit the request is `overdue`, so the fetcher (`--expect=YYYY-MM --overdue`) fails its step when the certificate still lacks it. The runner also reads the AFC, whatever the server said, on a night `fetch:afc-cartolas` staged a mail (the cartola's true-up must not wait for the next payslip month). The runner step is «AFC (apply)» after AFP UNO (`NW_TRACKER_AFC_APPLY=1` in the ingest plist, report only without it). Until 2026-10-21 every nightly read it. The supervised capture (`-- --capture [--minutes=N] [--code-from-stdin]`) tours the portal's pages by their hrefs (the sidebar is collapsed, its entries unclickable) and keeps every API call under `cfraser/afc-captures/`. The first apply (2026-10-17): 86 contributions unchanged, the three mailed cartolas (May–Aug 2025, annual 2025, Jan–Apr 2026) added the commission true-ups at 2024-12-31, 2025-04-30 and 2025-08-31 and re-measured 2025-12-31's; balance 0 on both sides, last credited month 2026-04 on both.
- **`mortgage.sheet_payments` (2026-10-11 → retired 2026-11-11).** The user's Numbers sheet of cuotas, read in place, cross-checked against `loan_payments` and writing a cuota the ledger lacked; no run read it after the 00:30 poll of 2026-11-11 hung 37 minutes on an iCloud placeholder, and the kind, its apply, `mortgage_sheet_imports` (dropped by migration 308) and the readers went the same day. Cuotas come from MetLife's portal (`mortgage.lender_statement`) or the app's form; the one-off repair script is `server/scripts/archive/repair-mortgage-ledger-from-sheet.ts`. **The card line that paid the cuota is paired when read, never stored (`mortgagePaymentCardMatch.ts`):** each regular cuota against the mortgage-merchant card lines (`isMortgageCcExpenseMerchant`) for the same pesos dated the payment day through 3 days after, each side once; the ledger payload carries `card_matches` / `card_lines_without_payment` (the mortgage table's «Tarjeta» column and a notice above it), and the nightly task `mortgage_card_match_check` (`check:mortgage-card-match`, after the card feed) fails when either side has waited 5 Chile business days inside the last 45 (a cuota the card never charged; a charge with no cuota in the ledger).
- **`utility.bill` v1 (2026-10-28): utility bills and gastos comunes statements, with their consumption, become the real-estate page's bill rows.** `{ bills: [{provider, service: electricity | gas | water | internet | hoa, service_account (N° cliente, as printed), bill_number, issued_on, amount (pesos), due_on, period_from, period_to, kwh, m3, estimated_reading, message_id}] }` — canonical: the provider is a slug, kWh only on electricity, m³ only on gas or water, one bill per (provider, bill number). `import:utility-bills` (ingest, `ingest/src/utilities/`; nightly after the payment receipt mails, and since 2026-11-11 every hourly poll with `--only-if-new`, which sends only when the default window holds a message id the previous `<provider>-recent.json` did not — so a statement is stored the hour it is mailed; a send that fails is retried by the nightly; `--days=` default 70, `--from= --to=`, `--archive=`, `--dry-run`) stages each provider's bill mails under `cfraser/utility-bill-mails/` and decodes them: **ENEL** («Cuenta Enel de este mes <N° cliente>», `no-responder@chile.enel.com`: N° boleta and its date, the amount, the due date, the kWh with «Consumo real» / «Consumo estimado» — some bills print none — and the NEXT period; a bill's own period is the one its predecessor announced, read when that bill is in the same window, a next period that does not follow it throws) and **Metrogas** («Boleta Metrogas Nro. …», `pagoenlinea@emailmetrogas.cl`: N° boleta, N° cliente, the two-month period, amount, due date; no issue date, the mail's day stands for it; mailed twice to two addresses, copies collapsed; its m³ is only in the PDF behind the mail's link, not fetched, so `m3` stays null). Other mails from those senders decode to null, a bill mail a decoder cannot read throws. The HTML text of ENEL's mails drops accented letters, so labels are matched without them. **Gastos comunes** (`hoaStatementMails.ts`): each building administration that mails its monthly statement is configured in untracked `cfraser/hoa-mail.json` (`{senders: [{from, provider}]}`; personal data, no file = none). A mail whose subject speaks of gastos carries one PDF per unit named after the unit («1203.pdf»), staged with its `pdftotext -layout` text by `archiveMails`' `pdfAttachment` option (PDFs under `cfraser/utility-bill-mails/attachments/`); the unit's «Total a pagar» is the amount, «Depto» the `service_account` (must equal the file name), the statement date («Santiago 08 de octubre de …») the issue day, its «dentro de los N días» term the due day, and the bill is the month before the statement date, numbered `<unit>-<YYYY-MM>`: the header's month name and a month the subject names must agree (the header's year is not read, one statement printed the next year; subjects name the month in full, abbreviated with a dot, or not at all). Other subjects (an assembly notice, a gas inspection report that also comes as «<unit>.pdf») decode to null; a gastos mail without one unit PDF, or one that does not read as a statement, throws. The server (`utilityBillsApply.ts`, migration 244) stores each bill once in `utility_bills` (a resend stating anything else is a conflict and stores nothing; a period one send lacks is filled from one that has it) and follows every bill not settled: the place comes from **`utility_service_accounts`** (provider + account number as printed, compared without punctuation or leading zeros → `expense_accounts` id; personal data, live DB only) — unmapped writes nothing; the charge is the one whole purchase among `loadGastosLinesForRealEstateMatching()` of exactly the bill's pesos, merchant of the kind (`merchantMatchesExpectation`: ENEL → electricidad, METROGAS → gas), dated from the issue day through due + 20 days (an automatic gas charge landed 13 days after its due date; a gastos comunes statement, paid by transfer later in the month: its issue day + 35) — a transfer whose mail names its counterparty must be paying someone else (`transfer_counterparty.direction` out), and among several the ones whose counterparty name matches the place win; several are `ambiguous` unless exactly one is already filed under a bill (a bill paid twice, the second refunded), none `waiting`, `overdue` 5 days past the window. A charge no bill row holds → the row is created (`assignPurchaseToRealEstateExpense`) with the consumption, in the bill's month (`utilityBillMonth`: the month of the period's last day when it falls on the 15th or later, else the month before — the period's last day, or the issue day: ENEL's monthly period closes on the 1st–5th, Metrogas's on the 23rd–25th, so the charge lands in the month after); a charge already filed under a bill by hand → its kWh / m³ filled when empty. A created row covers the months its printed period spans (`utilityBillMonthsCovered`: a two-month Metrogas period 2, a monthly bill 1). A row of another place, kind or amount, or a different consumption stored, is a `conflict`, never overwritten. A bill that created or filled its row is settled (`expense_entry_id`, `settled_at`) and never followed again, so a row deleted by hand is not written back; nothing is written for a bill whose charge is not in. **The other way round, for a place that receives gastos comunes statements:** a gastos comunes payment no bill row holds and no waiting statement could take, made since the place's first statement and over 10 days ago, is listed in `unbilled_hoa_payments` (the statement for its month never arrived) and printed by the step, never filed. The step exits 1 on a conflict, or on an overdue bill issued in the last 75 days.
- **`mortgage.payment_receipt` v1 (2026-11-11; migration 307, `mortgagePaymentReceiptsApply.ts`): a lender's receipt for a cuota paid online.** `{ lender, transaction_id, paid_on, amount_clp, credit_number, dividend_number, due_on, card_last4 }`, source ref = the mail's message id; feeder `import:metlife-receipts` (ingest, MetLife Hipotecario's «Pago de dividendo realizado»; `card_last4` is the feeder's declaration, the mail names no card). The cuota is charged to the BCI Lider card, which has no daily feed, so the charge only showed with the statement ~3 weeks later.
