The server stores the receipt once in `mortgage_payment_receipts` (by lender + transaction id; a resend stating other fields is a conflict, the message id is provenance) and plants the charge in the card's open web-paste bucket the same day (batch kind `cc_lender_receipt`, merchant per lender in `LENDER_CARD_MERCHANT`: «TOKU *METLIFE HIPOTECAR»; an unknown lender or card throws), so the card balance and the mortgage card-match check read it at once; `card_line` is `planted`, or `on_file` when the card already lists it. A charge, not a payment: it is not in `cc_planted_payment_lines`; the statement's «TOKU *METLIFE HIPOTECAR (T)» of the same pesos and day settles it (`reconcileOpenWebPasteAfterPdfClose`). **Feeder (`ingest/src/metlife/`):** `import:metlife-receipts` (`--days=` default 45, `--dry-run`) reads the mails with `archiveMails` and sends one request per mail; the hourly poll runs it with `--days=3`, the nightly with the default window. The card is `paid_with_card_last4` in untracked `cfraser/metlife-fetch.json`, which also holds the portal login (`rut`, Keychain `nw-tracker-metlife`). **The portal (https://w3.metlife.cl) is not read yet:** `fetch:metlife -- --capture [--code-from-stdin] [--minutes=N]` signs in (RUT + clave, then the «Clave Dinámica» mailed by `accessmanagement@metlife.com`, valid 15 minutes, read by the generic mailed-code reader `ingest/src/email/codeMail.ts` that ClaveÚnica's now wraps) with guessed selectors, and records the session under `cfraser/metlife-captures/` while the user opens the payment and balance pages; the cuota quote (min/max UF, interés, seguros, amortización) and the balance after paying are to be built from that capture, replacing the retired mortgage-sheet read.
- **`mortgage.lender_statement` v1 (2026-11-11; migration 308, `mortgageLenderStatementApply.ts`, `mortgageLenderQuotes.ts`): MetLife's portal, read by the feeder.** One reading (`read` kind): the credit (balance UF, last paid cuota), `pending` (the cuota quoted now: min / max in UF and pesos, fee, amortization, interest, charges) and `history` (recent cuotas; a paid one's parts and insurance in pesos, the pending one's insurance in UF). The loan is the one whose `lender_pattern` words name the lender (`METLIFE`); none or several throw. **Quotes:** each pending cuota goes to `mortgage_lender_quotes` (keyed lender + credit + cuota, with the loan; insurance UF from its history row) only when its figures differ from the latest stored row (`stored: false` otherwise; `first_seen_at` = the first reading's). **Paid cuotas:** one the ledger has (by cuota number) is cross-checked — total, principal, extra principal, interest, the two insurances to the peso, the payment day, and for the credit's last paid cuota the balance after it to 0,0002 UF — and any difference is an `IngestConflict` naming every field, nothing written (the quote rolls back too). One the ledger lacks is written through `commitMortgagePayment` (origin `import`: the CHECK allows import / manual, and a portal is an import) only when it is the credit's last paid cuota, else `waiting` (no balance after it is stated): the lender's pesos, day and parts (extra principal given, so no split from the minimum), balance = the credit's, min / max = the latest stored quote of that cuota or null when it was paid before any reading (both columns nullable); refused as a conflict when the app's principal differs from the lender's by more than a peso, the balance chain (before − principal − extra at the day's UF) misses the stated balance by more than 0,0005 UF, the cuota carries a charge the ledger has no column for (default interest, collection fee, other insurance), or a stored `mortgage_payment_receipts` row of the cuota's due month states another total. **Readers:** the scenario table's next row (`buildDeptoPaymentScenarioRows(ledger, nextCuotaQuoteForLoan(...))`: min and max from the quote, `quoted: true`, «bank quote» beside «next» in both renderings) and «Próximos pagos» (the next cuota at the quote's UF figures, `estimated: false`; later cuotas stay projections). **Schedule (`ingestRunPolicy.ts`):** `decideMetlifeRead` → the request's `metlife_read`, either run kind: `posted` while a payment receipt of the last 10 days names a cuota due after the ledger's last one; else `quote` from 07:00 Chile on the 11th through the month's end while no stored quote is due next month or later; never within 60 min of the last run that asked (`ingest_runs.metlife_request`, migration 308), never without a MetLife loan. The 11th gets an extra hourly slot at 07:00 (`metlifeQuoteSlotAtOrBefore`, `extraHourlySlot` of `decideIngestRun`) while the quote is missing: the bank posts the cuota overnight, and the :30 grid takes over from 07:30.
- **The card page's paste box goes through the ingest service (2026-10-03, Phase 3 source 11).** `POST /api/accounts/:id/imports/cc-web-paste` checks the account is a card master, sends the text to the service's `POST /parse/card.web_paste` (`ingest/src/cards/webPaste.ts`; the server's `ccWebPasteParse.ts` keeps the issuer's sign rule) and imports what it answers: a `card.pasted_listing` (`server/contracts/src/pastedListing.ts`) — a parse-only result, not an ingest kind, because a paste names no card; its lines keep the merchant and the signed amount as the issuer's table prints them, and the server normalizes the merchant (`webPasteLinesFromPastedListing`) and applies the issuer's sign rule, then builds the canonical card line (`cardUnbilledMovementsApply` / `cards/lineWrites.ts`), as every listing source does. The paste needs the service running (the route answers 400 with the reason otherwise). The Lider BCI «últimos movimientos» CSV importer was retired the same day (no file since its scheduled export stopped on 2026-08-07); `liderCardLines.ts` keeps the Lider master lookup and the same-day-same-amount check the grocery receipts use.
- **Server tasks and the inbox pipeline in ingest (2026-10-03, Phase 4).** The run steps that work on the server's own data are server tasks, `POST /api/ingest/tasks/<task>` (`contracts/tasks.ts`, `server/src/ingestTasks.ts`, same access rules as the kinds): `cc_payment_mirrors` (`dry_run`), `synthetic_cc_payments_check`, `cc_bank_cupo_check` (`recheck`), each answering `{task, ok, report}`. `npm run server-task -w nw-tracker-ingest -- <task>` calls one, and the root scripts `convert:cc-payment-mirrors`, `check:synthetic-cc-payments`, `check:cc-bank-cupo` point there, so the runners and the shell fallback kept their step commands; the server scripts that ran them as second database writers were deleted. `import:cfraser-inbox` is an ingest command (`ingest/src/inbox/pipelineMain.ts`, the organize manifest in `inbox/organizeManifest.ts`): it loads the root `.env` (the qpdf step reads the PDF passwords from it), runs the ingest commands in the old order and asks for the two tasks — it never opens the database. Its `--sync` flag is gone (the server's scheduler syncs market data; `npm run sync:all -w nw-tracker-server` stays a manual tool).
- **`card.statement` (v1 2026-10-01; v3 since the canonical card line, F13): one facturación of a card account.** `{ account: {issuer, number}, statement_number, close, pay_by, next_close, titular_last4, apply, statements: [{currency, document, billed_total, payments_total, lines}] }` — the statement and its lines are the canonical card statement (`server/contracts/src/cards/canonicalLine.ts`, which every card document source converts into once, at its boundary; v3 changed the line shape, so the ingest service and the server deploy together); a line is `{kind: purchase | installment | charge | payment | credit_note | credit, transaction_date, posting_date, merchant, amount (debt-positive; an installment line bills its cuota), origin_amount, country, place, card_last4, authorization_code, installment: {number, count, cuota_amount, total_amount} | null, raw_text}`. Ingest (`ingest/src/santander/statementJson.ts`) decodes the staged Santander JSON — fixed-point amounts, the CodTxs sign map (an unknown code is refused at decode, for every close: 071 SERVICIO USO INTERNACIONAL and 701 its IVA were mapped on the way in, positive, proven by the 24/09/2026 header identity); **international rows take their direction from their code too (2026-10-27; `INTERNATIONAL_COD_TXS`)**: 2000 / 2003 purchases, 2020 ABONO DE DIVISAS (credit), 2510 NOTA DE CREDITO, 0067 MONTO CANCELADO (payment) — the trailing sign of `MontoTransaccion` is not trusted on its own, since the bank printed the 09/10/2026 dollar payment (paid from the dollar account) as `000001xxxxx+`, which had stored a US$1.xxx,xx payment as a purchase and left ·0781 owing ~2,xx M too much from that day; an unknown code is refused. The `card.statement` contract rejects a payment / credit / nota line that is positive and a purchase / installment / charge line that is negative, pairing by extracto, the archive selection and moves — and `import:santander-statements` (an ingest command now) sends one facturación at a time, oldest first per account, printing the server's per-statement report. The server (`cardStatementApply.ts`, `cardStatementCrossCheck.ts`) keeps ownership, the PDF cross-check, the stale-echo and routing guards, the write and its verification, and answers each side's outcome (`clean | written | pending | empty | skipped | dirty`), which is what ingest archives on. **A JSON-owned close is rewritten only when it changed (2026-10-30):** the feeder sends the open facturación every night until its PDF arrives, and both currencies' records are compared with the stored `import_fingerprint` (`filterUnchangedStatementRecords`); unchanged reads `clean` and writes nothing, any change rewrites both currencies together. Until then every night rewrote the same 75 lines of the 26/10/2026 ·0781 close.
- **`merchant.purchase_document` v1 (2026-10-02): App Store receipts and subscription notices → which app each charge paid for.** A card statement names every App Store charge «APPLE.COM/BILL»; Apple's mails say what each bought. `fetch:apple-mail` (`ingest/src/email/appleMail.ts`, All Mail, message-id ledger, 45-day window, `--days=` for history) stages each receipt («Your receipt/invoice from Apple.») and subscription mail (confirmation, renewal, expiring, price increase) as its HTML under `cfraser/apple-mail/`; `import:apple-mail` decodes them (`ingest/src/apple/appStoreMail.ts`: items with app / product / price / renews / period / artwork link, the total and the card; a notice's app, plan, price, period and purchase / next-charge / expiry dates; a digest of several renewals → several notices; anything unrecognised throws and stays staged) and sends one source per mail. The server stores them (migration 204: `merchant_document_sources` — a resend with the same payload is a duplicate, a different one a conflict — `merchant_receipts` + items, `merchant_subscription_notices`) and the matcher (`deriveMerchantChargeLinks`, `merchantExpenseNotes.ts`) names every App Store charge **when the expense lines are built**, as a link shown under the charge like a processor receipt (`payment_receipt`, `withPaymentReceipts` in `paymentProcessorReceipts.ts`; «→ app» from a receipt, «≈ app» inferred), never as a note (2026-10-06): (1) a receipt pairs 1:1 with the charge on its card for the same amount (the peso origin to the peso, or dollars at the day's rate within 4% for dollar-only feed rows), −1…+5 days, exact amounts first, then the nearest date; an item that prints only its product («1 Boost») takes its app from another receipt with the same artwork or a notice whose plan it is, else it is reported and its charge left alone; (2) a subscription's price on its billing cycle (anchor ± whole periods, ±3 days) within 500 days of a subscription receipt's charge or a notice date; (3) a charge a month after a named charge of the same amount — its link, else a note the user wrote (never seeded by a one-off purchase). Kinds 2–3 are guesses (`guess: true`) and wait out the receipt window (charge older than 5 days) so a late receipt still claims its charge; several candidate apps name the nearest. The page shows the app's printed name; the short label (reusing the notes the app's earlier charges carry, else its name before the tagline) only decides which notes the link makes redundant: `npm run match:app-store-notes -- --prune-notes [--apply]` deletes a note equal to its link's label and appends « · label» to a kept note on a receipt-named charge that does not mention it. Nightly: fetch with the other mail, import after the card feed; hourly: import when a fetch staged mail. History (2015 → 2026-10-01, 381 notes) had been written once by hand from the same mails; on 2026-10-06 all 381 equalled their link's label and were deleted, the 381 links (133 from a receipt, 248 inferred) unchanged before and after.
- **`card.parsed_statements` (v1 2026-10-02; v2 with the canonical card line): every card statement the PDF parser read.** Columnar — `{full, columns, rows}`, the 50 columns of `cc-statements-parsed-all.csv` (`CARD_PARSED_STATEMENT_COLUMNS`) once, then each line's values as the parser printed them; the whole corpus (~6.800 lines) is ~4 MB. `parsedStatementRowsToCanonical` (`server/contracts/src/cards/parsedStatementRows.ts`) turns the rows into canonical statements (Chilean numbers, ISO days, the line kind), so the CSV's cells are read in one place; ingest converts before sending, and the server converts what it still receives with the same function. The line identities the ledger keys on (`dedupe_key`, `row_id`) ride in the canonical line's `document` provenance. `npm run import:cc-statements` (ingest, `ingest/src/cards/`; `--dry-run`, `--full`, `--csv=`) reads the CSV exactly as the server's reader did and sends it; `import:cfraser-inbox` calls it after `parse:cc-pdfs`. The server (`applyParsedCcStatements`, `ccParsedStatementsApply.ts`) routes each line to its card account by `card_last4` through the registry's redirects, filters unchanged statements and merges each account's changed ones in one `mergeCcAccountFromParsedRows` call, as `import:cc-parsed` did — which stays for manual runs (`--account-id`, `--santander`) and its two reload modes (`--wipe`, `--replace-ledger`), its merge path calling the same function. A line no account takes is a problem and nothing is written. **Step 2 (2026-10-02):** the parser, the inbox organizer, qpdf repair / OCR and their tests live in `ingest/python/` (ingest npm scripts `parse:cc-pdfs`, `organize:inbox`, `repair:cc-pdfs-qpdf`, `restore:cc-corrupt-pdfs`, `check:cc-parse`, `test:python`; the root scripts delegate, and `import:cfraser-inbox` calls them), with their own `python/.pdf_deps/`; `statement_values.py` / `cartola_pdf_kind.py` have one copy each, and the line rules moved to `server/contracts/data/ccStatementLineRules.json` (`CC_STATEMENT_LINE_RULES_PATH`). The account page's statement upload asks the ingest service to parse each PDF (`POST /parse/card_statement.pdf` → `card.parsed_statements`; the service must be running) and imports the lines as before. The parser no longer falls back to `~/Downloads` when its source folder holds no PDF (it renames what it reads; a checkout without `cfraser/` once renamed 29 downloads) — it fails.
- **`pension_account.certificates` v1 (2026-10-01): AFP UNO, read from its private site.** `npm run fetch:afp-uno [-- --apply --background]` (`ingest/src/afpUno/fetchMain.ts`) signs in at www.uno.cl (RUT + clave from `cfraser/afp-uno-fetch.json` / Keychain `nw-tracker-afp-uno`; the page's own invisible reCAPTCHA makes its token, and a refused attempt is reloaded once, as a person would; «UNOnline» opens a sign-in drawer whose fields are readonly until clicked, so each is clicked and typed), reads what the home page asks the portal API for — the stated balance (`productos-voluntarios/consulta`) and the recent movements (`afiliado-movimientos/movimientos-portal`, CCO) — and asks the same API, with the headers the page sent, for the two certificates as base64 PDFs: contributions (`afiliado-certificado/cotizaciones`, 12 períodos, each with its fecha caja) and movements (`afiliado-certificado/movimientos-pdf`, the last 12 months, everything in cuotas by período). `certificates.ts` reads both from `pdftotext -layout` (wrapped rows, glued columns; any row it cannot account for throws — the full-history movements certificate nets to the stated balance exactly). The server (`pensionAccountCertificatesApply.ts`) builds the rows the ledger should hold in the shape `pensionLedgerShape.ts` gives it (see «The AFP account's ledger and price» under Global sync): one row per day a printed valor cuota first shows in `afp_uno_cuota_a`, every certificate row bought or sold at that price netted into it (a contribution, one the unemployment insurance paid — 111138, which the home list never shows, so the certificate is always read — commissions, reliquidaciones), pending while the price is not in the series; each contribution must also be on the contributions certificate; a withdrawal is its own row and is never written here (matched when present). Missing rows are written only when nothing disagrees — a ledger row inside the window the certificate does not list, a present row with other pesos, or a ledger total that would differ from the stated balance (to 4 decimals: the ledger keeps 4, UNO states 2, and 0,0002 cuotas is ~20 pesos) writes nothing and fails the step. **The website's pesos are checked against the app's own value (2026-10-07; `checkPensionStatedValue`):** UNO states cuotas × valor cuota = pesos; the app's value (`accountMarkClpAtYmd`, plus the rows the read would write) on the day that valor cuota shows in `afp_uno_cuota_a` must equal the stated pesos to 1 peso, else the read fails (`value_check` on the result: match / mismatch / waiting — the last while the app's series lacks that price yet). It caught nothing on its first run, because the ledger had just been corrected: the rebuild had left the pre-UNO rows at 111,3702 cuotas against the 111,37 UNO credited, 20 pesos above the website; the rebuild script now puts that rounding residue on the last row before the transfer-in. Every read is recorded in `pension_account_reads` (migration 203). **Schedule (2026-10-07):** every nightly request carries `afp_uno_fetch` (`decideAfpUnoFetch`). The read sends the home page's balance first as **`pension_account.balance`** (`applyPensionAccountBalance`): stated cuotas equal to the ledger's (to 4 decimals) → the value check alone, and that is the night's read; different → anything that moves cuotas changed them, so the certificates are read and sent as above (`--certificates` reads them regardless). The old calendar rule (from the 10th until a clean import) is gone. Writes need `NW_TRACKER_AFP_UNO_APPLY=1` in the ingest service's plist (report only without it). PDFs are kept (`cfraser/afp-uno-certs/<issued>-cotizaciones.pdf` / `-movimientos.pdf`) only when rows were written from them; otherwise deleted. Verified on a real-DB copy: the 2026-09-30 certificates match ledger rows 11833–11842 exactly, and with 11840 / 11842 deleted the apply re-wrote them identically.
- **`card.unbilled_movements` v1 (2026-09-30), the first kind: the Santander card feed.** `ingest/src/santander/cardFeed.ts` decodes a staged `card-movements-*.json` (dates, the two `Importe` conventions, D/H → debt-positive amounts, SALDO INICIAL → `close {date, billed {clp, usd}}`, the feed's cuota types → `cuota_purchase.first_cuota_bills` `next_cycle` (cuota comercio) / `purchase_cycle` (precio contado), the stamp-tax count, the 18-digit cupo rows → `issuer_balances`); `npm run import:santander-movements` (root → `nw-tracker-ingest`) sends each file (source ref = the file name, which keys the feed close and the cupo capture exactly as before), prints the server's `details` and archives the file to `imported/` only once applied — the first failure stops the run, a server that is down included (`SERVER_URL`, default `http://127.0.0.1:3001`; `INGEST_TOKEN` from the env or the root `.env`). Server side: `applyCardUnbilledMovements` (`cardUnbilledMovementsApply.ts`, formerly the file importer) maps `{issuer, number}` through `masterAccountIdForIssuerCardAccount` and each line through `webPasteLineFromCardListingLine` (`cardListingLines.ts`: debt-positive → the issuer's web-UI sign via `webPasteAmountFromDebtPositive`, so the web-paste import stores it exactly as before) and invalidates each card's caches (in-process writes do not bump `data_version`). Verified before the old code was deleted: the 55 archived feed files decode to identical import lines, closes and cupo rows (2148 lines, zero differences), and re-sending the latest file to a copy of the real DB changed exactly what the old importer changed (batch id and seen-at timestamps aside). The payment-receipt path builds its planted card line canonically (`santanderReceiptCardLine`), pinned to ingest's decoding of the same feed row by one literal in both test suites. **Server tests never import `ingest/`** (outside the server's TypeScript root): they build canonical payloads with `server/src/test/cardListingPayloads.ts`; bank decoding is tested in `ingest/` (`npm run test -w nw-tracker-ingest`, no DB).

## Unattended daily bank run

- **The nightly run** (the ingest service's `runNightly`, asked for by the server's scheduler at 22:00 Chile, see «Run protocol»): fetch Santander → the mail fetches → `import:cfraser-inbox` → the card feed, payment mirrors and bank checks → the mail imports → broker mail → Racional, AFP UNO, AFC, payslips when asked → `import:santander-statements`. The service must be a LaunchAgent in the GUI session (`com.user.nw-tracker-ingest`; headless Chrome is blocked upstream, so the fetcher needs a window server). Personal specifics live in PARSERS.md. **Mail is read by one fetcher (2026-11-07; `ingest/src/email/mailFetcher.ts`):** every search on All Mail with a DST-safe `SINCE` (`searchMailSince`), the six document sources (`SANTANDER_DOCS_SOURCE`, `LIDER_BOLETAS_SOURCE`, `LIDER_STATEMENT_SOURCE`, `FINTUAL_ACCIONES_SOURCE`, `APPLE_MAIL_SOURCE`, `AFC_CARTOLAS_SOURCE`) only DECLARE the sender, the subject → document kind and each mail's documents, and `fetchMailDocuments` walks the window (`DEFAULT_MAIL_WINDOW_DAYS`), skips what the document ledger holds, writes and records; `fetch:<source>` is `email/fetchMailMain.ts <source>`. `archiveMails` (the text archives the transfer, receipt, wire and utility decoders read) lives beside it; the MIME helpers are `email/mime.ts`. Staged documents go to the server through one loop (`ingest/src/stagedFiles.ts` `sendStaged`): a document the server is done with moves to `imported/` beside it, one answered `retry` or `conflict` stays.
- **The BCI Lider facturación arrives by mail too (2026-08-28): `fetch:lider-statements`** (`ingest/src/email/liderStatements.ts`) pulls the monthly «Estado de Cuenta Tarjeta Lider Bci Visa» PDF from `estadodecuenta@correo.tarjetaliderbci.cl` into `cfraser/inbox/` — the only automated entry path the ·0101 statement has since the 08:00 CSV scrape retired. **The ledger key is the mail date + message id, NOT the attachment filename** (the santanderDocs rule inverted): BCI reuses the same attachment name every month (a client number), so a filename key would silently skip every month after the first; the saved inbox name is date-prefixed for the same reason. The statement is the bare-number PDF attachment — the «Inserto Seguros.pdf» insert some mails carry must never reach the inbox (a non-statement PDF there aborts the organizer), and any other attachment shape is an ERROR (non-zero exit), never a guess. The PDF is encrypted (`LIDER_CC_STATEMENT_PDF_PASSWORD`); the pipeline decrypts, and the organizer names it canonically from the peeked close (`peek_bci_lider_meta` — whose bare `PAGAR HASTA` fallback was removed 2026-08-28: it matched the anterior section's «a Pagar … Hasta» and named the August statement after JULY's close, quarantining it as a duplicate of the real July file).
- **PDF/JSON statement imports never dedupe against web-paste lines (2026-08-28).** `importCcStatementsMerge` skips a row whose key/fuzzy twin already exists on the account — but an open web-paste bucket line is NOT durable evidence for a PDF import: the same merge's `reconcileOpenWebPasteAfterPdfImports` deletes the bucket's in-period lines right after the line loop, so a skip against one deleted the purchase from BOTH places (2026-08 ·0101: 9 lines / 1.xxx.xxx CLP vanished; the owed walk read ~226k against a 1,xx M facturado while the header-driven detalle stayed correct). The dedupe/fuzzy scans take `excludeWebPasteSources`, set per statement from its own source (`ccExpenseLineDedupe.ts` / `ccCrossImportDedupe.ts`) — web-paste imports keep full scans, since deduping a re-paste against its own bucket and against PDF lines is their point. Paired hardening: the BCI `monto_facturado` import check is a HARD gate (the `bci_incomplete_parse` hatch was removed — its >8% arm was permanently tripped by `pdf_total_operaciones` collected 2× from the concatenated dual-rendering text; the collector now reads a single rendering, `cc_statement_reconcile.py`), so a repeat of this class fails the import loudly instead of skipping. The gate's identity: `parsed_operaciones + parsed_cargos_abonos = Monto Total Facturado` — BCI section-3 rows (parser layout `bci_lider_cargos`) net into cargos_abonos by LAYOUT, not the Santander merchant regex (which misses «IMPUESTO DL» and merchant-named abonos like a Lider.cl return nota); PAGOs count as mid-period payments, never cargos.
- **Santander is split by document rhythm (2026-08-06): the web session is DAILY MOVEMENTS ONLY, the monthly PDFs come from Gmail.** `fetch:santander` now runs three steps — `card-movements`, `checking-movements`, `card-statements` (the facturación **JSON**; the PDF endpoint has never once succeeded, and since 2026-09-26 the step no longer clicks «Ver estado de cuenta» at all — the page requests the PDF by itself on each view and the step only logs that answer; the click-and-wait had cost eight of the step's ten minutes every night); since 2026-10-05 the step runs only when a statement is due (`statementSchedule.ts`): from the close the account's latest staged national JSON announces (`FechaProxFact`) until a newer extracto is staged, giving up 10 days past it — a card that stopped billing (·0161, last announced 2025-12-22) stops counting — and always when nothing is staged; `--force`, `--capture` and `--only=card-statements` fetch anyway. The summary lists the skip as an ok step with its reason — and the `cartola` step was **removed**. Both monthly documents arrive as mail attachments from `mensajeria@santander.cl` and are fetched by **`npm run fetch:santander-docs`** (`ingest/src/email/santanderDocs.ts`, same IMAP/Keychain app-password path as the broker-email detector): «Estado de Cuenta Tarjeta de Crédito» (the credit-card facturación) and «Cartola Mensual de Cuentas.» (cuenta corriente + the cuenta vista accounts). Attachments are written to `cfraser/inbox/` under the **bank's own filenames**, which is the entire contract — the organizer already recognises Santander's attachment naming (`1_<seq>_<account>_<date>_CC.pdf` cuenta corriente, `_CM` cuenta vista, `_LC` línea de crédito) and classifies `80_*` credit-card statements by peeking the PDF text, so every downstream stage is unchanged. Taking them from mail costs no bank session, no 2FA and no window server.
  - **CC payment receipts register the payment the day it happens (2026-09-08; re-dating since 2026-08-07).** Santander mails a receipt for every card payment — «Pago Deuda Nacional TCR» (CLP debt, `Monto del pago`) and «Comprobante Pago (abono) de la deuda facturada en dolares» (USD debt — carries `Equivalente en pesos`, the exact checking leg, plus USD amount and fx) — and `fetch:santander-docs` stages the body text as JSON under `cfraser/santander-payment-receipts/`. `import:santander-receipts` (ingest decodes the receipt, `ingest/src/santander/paymentReceipts.ts`, and sends a `card.payment_receipt`; the server applies it in `santanderCcPaymentReceipts.ts`, and `check:synthetic-cc-payments` raises the overdue alarm; the inbox pipeline always runs both) then does one of two things. **No bank row yet** — the daily xlsx only arrives with the nightly session, so a morning payment has none for hours: the checking → card `pago_tarjeta` transfer is **synthesized from the receipt**, dated the payment day (cross-currency shape for the dollar abono: CLP from-leg = the receipt's pesos, USD counter leg = the abono), recorded in **`santander_synthetic_cc_payment_transfers`** (migration 181, twin of the Fintual retiro table), and the card's own credit line («PAGO» / «ABONO DE DIVISAS», built through `santanderMovementRowToWebPasteLine` so it is byte-identical to the feed's row) is planted in the open web-paste bucket (batch kind `cc_santander_receipt`) — checking balance, deposits line and the owed walk all read the payment during the day. The nightly run then confirms instead of duplicating: the xlsx/cartola debit dedupes as `superseded_by_transfer` and stamps `confirmed_on` (both checking importers call `confirmSyntheticCcPaymentForTransferLeg`), the feed's credit line lands on the one-shot dedupe key, and the mirror converter finds no single-leg debit to pair. A synthesis still unconfirmed past two business days fails the receipts step loudly (the debit never appeared in any feed). A payment on the month's last business day is synthesized like any other (2026-09-30; it used to wait for the bank row): the bank row that lands next month brings its posting day, see «Bank posting day». **Bank row already imported** (a payment after the 14:00 cutoff is dated by every bank feed at the next workday, but the receipt states the REAL payment date): «Pago Deuda Nacional TCR» (CLP debt, `Monto del pago`) and «Comprobante Pago (abono) de la deuda facturada en dolares» (USD debt — carries `Equivalente en pesos`, the exact checking leg, plus USD amount and fx). `fetch:santander-docs` stages the receipt body text as JSON under `cfraser/santander-payment-receipts/` (message-id-keyed in the document ledger) and `import:santander-receipts` (run by `import:cfraser-inbox` after the xlsx import) re-dates the matching checking debit — single-leg, exact pesos, bank date inside `bankDateMatchesTransferDate`'s posting window, across a month boundary too (the bank date is kept as the posting day, see «Bank posting day»). **`occurred_on` moves; the bank date stays twice** — as the row's `movement_bank_postings` posting day, which the cartola checks read, and in the note, which is the xlsx note-key dedupe identity; `prunePartialMovementsSupersededByCartola` carries the payment day and the posting onto the official cartola row so the correction survives the monthly import. Unmatched receipts stay staged and retry; ambiguity (two same-amount debits in window) refuses; an unparsable receipt throws (template change must surface). Module: `santanderCcPaymentReceipts.ts`.
  - **Planted payment lines are replaced by amount, not wording; card payments entered by hand (2026-10-09; migration 232, `ccPlantedPayments.ts`, `ccManualPayments.ts`).** Every card payment line the app plants before the bank lists the payment is registered in `cc_planted_payment_lines` (line → card, `source` = the planting batch kind `cc_santander_receipt` | `cc_manual_payment`, currency, amount, paid day; cascades with its line). When a card import that is not itself a planting (feed, paste) brings a credit line worded as a payment (the payment classifiers, or any merchant opening with PAGO / ABONO) in the same currency and amount (USD to the cent, CLP to the peso), dated from the planted day to 5 days later, and the pairing is one-to-one within the import, the bank's line replaces the planted one: assignments carried (`carryCcExpenseAssignments`), the planted line deleted, valuations re-synced from its date (`replacePlantedPaymentLinesFromImport`, inside `importCcWebPasteLines`). The bank's identical line (the receipt path's byte-identical row) still dedupes on the one-shot key and is confirmed in place; several candidates either way change nothing and are reported (`planted_payments` on the import result and in `import:santander-movements`' output). The statement reconcile also settles a planted payment the statement prints under another wording (`plantedPaymentMatchesStatementLine`). A payment made at a branch or from the dollar account, with no receipt mail, is recorded by hand: `npm run cc:manual-payment -w nw-tracker-ingest -- --from=<id> --card=<id> --amount=<n> --currency=clp|usd --date=YYYY-MM-DD [--transfer=<movement id>]` (`POST /api/ingest/tasks/cc_manual_payment`) writes the `pago_tarjeta` transfer (or adopts an existing one) in the source account's own currency (cross-currency refused), plants «PAGO» / «ABONO DE DIVISAS» (batch kind `cc_manual_payment`) and records it in `cc_manual_payments`; running it twice changes nothing. The bank's replacing or identical line stamps `confirmed_on`; one unconfirmed 5 Chile business days after its day fails `check:synthetic-cc-payments`.
  - **The daily checking «ultimos movimientos-Cuenta Corriente.xlsx» is imported by the inbox pipeline (2026-08-07).** `fetch:santander`'s `checking-movements` step drops it in `cfraser/inbox/`; `import:cfraser-inbox` hands it to ingest (`import:checking-movements`: decoded by `ingest/src/santander/checkingMovements.ts`, sent as `bank_account.movements`, applied by `applyBankAccountMovements` — the same apply the UI upload reaches through the ingest service's `/parse`) and ingest archives it date-stamped under `cfraser/checking-ultimos-movimientos/imported/`. **Rows dated after today are correct data**: Santander's bank day ends at **14:00**, so a wire received/sent after the cutoff posts on the next workday (Friday afternoon → Monday) — the xlsx shows that forward posting date, the monthly cartola repeats the same date, and the cartola import dedupes against the incremental row. Never "fix" the date to the calendar day of the fetch.
  - **The xlsx must name the peso account (2026-10-09; `checkingUsd.ts`).** The peso decoder refuses a workbook whose headers are not the «($)» ones (a dollar file would have landed in the peso account with its cents mangled) or one without the account line, and sends row 2's account number (`account.number`); the server checks it against `bank_account_numbers` (`assertListingNamesAccount`, by the listing's currency). The fetcher step `checking-usd-movements` (after the peso download; also in the hourly movements-only fetches) walks the movements page's product carousel, identifies the dollar account by the transactions request's body (`usd_checking_account_number` in `cfraser/santander-fetch.json`; Apigee `/current-accounts/transactions` on `openbanking.santander.cl`, which the recorder keeps, or the Tibco `mvtosYDeposiDocCtas` fallback — never by what is on screen) and saves request, raw response, screenshot and meta under `cfraser/santander-captures/<stamp>-usd/`. Capture only: no download, nothing to the inbox, never fails the run («USD account not reached» + diagnostics).
  - **The dollar account's feed confirms the ledger and never inserts (2026-10-10; `ingest/src/santander/checkingUsdFeed.ts`, `server/src/usdAccountMovementsConfirm.ts`).** `import:checking-usd-movements` (ingest; `--dry-run`; nightly and hourly right after `import:santander-movements`) decodes each staged capture — the Apigee answer lists `movements` newest first: ISO `accountingDate` (the posting day) / `transactionDate`, `operationTime` HHMM, `movementAmount` and `newBalance` as fixed-width digits with two implied decimals and a trailing «-» on a negative figure, `chargePaymentFlag` D/H (must agree with the sign), `expandedCode` (the wording) + `observation` (the reference) = the description, a running `movementNumber`; a «Rec Or de Pago» credit's observation «REC OR DE PAGO <order>» is its `wire_reference`; the bank's error code, a shape change, a zero amount or balances that do not chain from row to row (a row left out) throw — checks the request named the configured dollar account in USD and sends it as `bank_account.movements` in dollars, archiving the capture under `santander-captures/imported/`. The server maps a listing in any currency but pesos by its number (`bank_account_numbers` in the listing's currency; unnamed or undeclared is refused; any currency since 2026-11-12, `confirmForeignAccountMovements`) and reads it as evidence: a row a transfer leg on the account explains to the currency's coin inside the peso xlsx's posting window (`findMatchingForeignTransferLegId` over `signedNativeDeltaForAccountMovement` — the wire's booking, a `pago_tarjeta` from the dollar account, a conversion's counter leg) is `confirmed` and its bank day recorded as the leg's posting (`movement_bank_postings`); a wire credit whose booking has not landed is `awaiting_wire_booking` (sent again next run; the wire step raises the alarm); anything else is `unexplained` — never written, and the command exits 1 naming it, since every row this account can show is on file from its own document first (a card payment by hand: `cc:manual-payment`; a conversion: a transfer) and the next capture confirms it. Batch kind `usd_account_feed` (whatever the currency: a stored kind). First run on the 2026-10-10 captures: the 10-08 wire (movement 13017) and the 10-09 branch card payment (13019) confirmed, nothing else.
  - **The ledger key is the attachment FILENAME, not the month.** The facturación arrives as **two same-day mails** (the CLP statement and the USD one), so a month-keyed ledger would mark the month done on the first and never download its sibling — silent data loss. The bank's filename carries its sequence number and document date, so it is a stable per-document identity, and because each month brings fresh sequence numbers the once-a-month rhythm falls out for free: due when the mail lands, retried daily until it does, never fetched twice. `--force` ignores the ledger, `--dry-run` lists without writing, `--days=N` widens the search window (default 45).
- **Hourly e-mail poll (2026-08-10; imports whole pipeline since 2026-08-28):** the ingest service's `runHourly`, at :30 past every hour — the mail fetches (`fetch:santander-docs`, `fetch:lider-statements`, `fetch:lider-boletas`, `fetch:fintual-docs`, `fetch:apple-mail`, `fetch:emails`) and, **when a fetch staged something new this hour** (the fetches' `staged` step result), the full inbox pipeline (`import:cfraser-inbox` — so a monthly facturación imports the hour its mail lands, and the receipts import plus the payment-mirror task run with it) and the broker e-mail imports (`import:fintual-emails` / `import:racional-emails` under the plist's apply gates). The gate also counts a photo waiting in `cfraser/grocery-receipts/inbox/` (the one input no fetch stages), so a receipt AirDropped during the day imports within the hour; the grocery stage is incremental (per-receipt import stamps, 2026-09-06). Everything else stays nightly (Racional, statement JSONs), and the nightly inbox pipeline is the unconditional retry backstop for anything staged-but-unimported. The server records it (`recordHourlyEmailRun`, `dailyRunLog.ts`) under its **own titles** («Hourly e-mail poll») — never the daily ones, which `lastDailyRunAt` reads for the 22:00 repeat skip. **Bank session: the balance poll (2026-11-05).** A quiet hour records nothing; failures record every run but only the first of the Chile day badges (`notification`) — the nightly, which runs the same e-mail steps, remains the alerting authority.
- **The hourly Santander balance poll (2026-11-05; `bankProductSummaryApply.ts`, `ingest/src/santander/balancePoll.ts`, migration 266).** Since the app runs on the always-on server, every hourly poll logs in to Santander (`fetch:santander --balance-poll`, the Chrome window visible — the runners no longer pass `--background`), sends the login's product summary (card cupos + deposit-account balances, `cruceProductosOnline`) as **`bank.product_summary`** v1, and reads only the listings the server names: the server records the summary as a cupo capture and a balance snapshot (source ref `balance-poll-<stamp>`), judges each product at once and answers `fetch` — the card feed for a card that disagrees (or a summary without card rows), the peso checking xlsx for the cuenta corriente, the dollar account's capture for Santander USD; a cuenta vista has no listing and is only reported. The summary carries no feed close, so a card is compared without one; a close the app has not seen reads as a mismatch, costs one card-feed read and settles itself. The step prints `balance poll fetch: <list|none>` (and a `differs:` line per product), which the runner reads to import what was staged (card feed + mirror converter, the USD capture, the inbox pipeline for the xlsx) and then runs `check:cc-bank-cupo -- --recheck`. **A difference that survives its listing is a notification** («Bank balance differs from the app», `ingestTasks.ts`), once per difference: a fresh mismatch notifies when the latest EARLIER observation of the same product agreed or differed by another amount (`previousBankCupoVerdict`, the deposit verdict's `previous`, both by `observed_at` so the summary and the card feed of one session are one observation); the cupo and balance checks no longer fail their step on a mismatch (the nightly's included) — only a feed with no summary does. Nothing else in the hourly poll changed. The catch-up (`santanderCatchUpDecision`, `.santander-catchup.json`) and the payday fetch (`santanderPaydayFetch.ts`) are gone; old `ingest_runs` rows keep their `catch-up` / `payday` requests.
- **Dividends are booked NET; the gross and the withholding tax live in `movement_dividend_details` (2026-09-23; migration 183, writer `movementDividendDetails.ts`).** A `dividend_payout` transfer (equity → cash) carries what the broker CREDITED — the only figure the balance walk and every P/L reader need, and the only one every source agrees on. The tax year needs the gross and the tax withheld abroad on top (Chile credits it against the local tax on the gross), so each dividend movement may carry one satellite row: `gross_amount`, `withholding_amount`, `withholding_rate_pct`, `withholding_jurisdiction`, `tax_residency_country`, `per_share_amount`, `position_qty`, `record_date`, `pay_date`, `broker_event_id`, `source` (`racional_api` | `fintual_cartola` | `fintual_certificado` | `ibkr_statement` | `manual`), `source_ref` — every nullable field filled only when the document prints it, a rate never computed from gross and tax. The writer (`upsertMovementDividendDetail`) is the one choke point: it throws when gross − withholding is not the movement's amount (a movement booked GROSS is exactly the bug that prompted this — see below), when two documents disagree about the same dividend, and otherwise lets a richer document replace a poorer one (`SOURCE_RANK`: statement/API > certificado > manual) while a poorer one only fills nulls; a same-rank document from another file is provenance only (no rewrite, so the nightly re-read cannot churn). **Sources:** Racional's `/users/movements/dividends` API (`DIV` gross, `DIVTAX`, `amount` net, `executionDate`, `isInterest`) — the crawl saves the raw response as `cfraser/racional-movements/dividends-<stamp>.json` on every run and pairs each new list dividend row with its record by Chile day + net (no detail click any more; the record carries the route id and the instrument), and `import:racional-movements` writes the breakdown on insert, on duplicates, and from the dividends file for history; Fintual's **Alpaca monthly cartola** («Cartola mensual de Acciones», `fetch:fintual-docs` → `cfraser/fintual-acciones/cartolas/`, message-id ledger — the attachment is named after the month only) whose Income section prints `Cash DIV @ <per share>, Pos QTY: <qty>, Rec Date: <d> $<gross>` plus `Div. Adj(NRA Withheld) … at 15% for tax country CHL; w8w9: w8 -$<tax>` (a dividend with no NRA line was credited in full — LIN, an Irish plc), and the on-request **certificado de transacciones y eventos de capital** (`…/certificados/`, bruto / impuestos / neto) — `import:fintual-acciones` (ingest: `fintual/accionesDocs.ts` parsers; server: `brokerDividendStatementApply.ts`, through `broker.dividend_statement`) pairs each printed dividend with the ledger's `dividend_payout` at the printed NET within ±5 days (the DRIP-day skew every Fintual source shares) and fails the step on a dividend with no row or several; sweep interest is reported, not booked. A future IBKR statement (Dividends + Withholding Tax sections) writes the same rows. **Why (2026-09-22):** the «Recibiste USD $X en dividendos de T» mail states the GROSS dividend and Racional credits the net after the 15% US withholding, so booking the subject figure (SOXX 2,75 vs 2,34, VEA 11,24 vs 9,55) left Racional USD 2,10 above the wallet after the SOXX buy that spent it to the cent; the two rows were repaired, both Racional dividend templates are nudges again (`gross_amount` on the event, never `amount`), and the crawl importer's day-sum mismatch is now a **`conflict`** that fails the step (`requires_manual` stays for the by-design cash legs) — the mismatch had sat as an «ok» log line for four days. Nothing reads the table on a request path yet; a tax-year rollup is a later tranche.
- **The Chrome 152 crashes were the checking xlsx download, and it no longer goes through Chrome (2026-09-09).** Every crash report since 2026-08-31 (SIGSEGV/SIGTRAP on CrBrowserMain, one per attempt, the 2026-09-09 one with a URL fragment of the page route as the faulting address) landed one to two minutes into the session — the moment «Descargar últimos movimientos» fires — and on 2026-09-09 both the first attempt and the relaunch died there. A probe showed the download is not a request at all: the SPA builds the xlsx in the page and clicks an `<a download href="data:…;base64,…">`, and Chrome 152 crashes handling that data-URL download under CDP. `browser.ts` now installs an init script that catches `a[download]` clicks carrying a `data:`/`blob:` href (both `a.click()` and a dispatched MouseEvent, the FileSaver style), keeps the bytes on `window.__nwInlineDownloads`, and `downloadTo` (wait.ts) races that stash against Playwright's download event and writes the file itself — the bank's filename is preserved, and a real HTTP download still takes the native path. The relaunch-once logic stays for whatever else can kill the browser.
- **The Santander session survives a Chrome crash and a homepage modal (2026-09-06).** Chrome 152 (auto-installed 2026-08-31) crashed its browser process one to three minutes into the session on three of the first six nights — real SIGSEGV/SIGBUS reports under `~/Library/Logs/DiagnosticReports/`, parent = the scraper's node — and every later step then failed with «Target page, context or browser has been closed». `runSantander` now detects a gone browser (`page.isClosed()` or Playwright's closed/crashed wording), relaunches Chrome **once** per run and retries the same step before continuing (`MAX_BROWSER_RELAUNCHES`; the persistent profile keeps the bank session, so the relaunch normally skips the password login — kept at one so a deterministic crash can't become a burst of logins; a failed relaunch marks the remaining steps skipped). Separately, the public homepage can open a marketing modal (`div.modal-overlay[role=dialog]`, «Santander Arena» first seen 2026-09-05) whose overlay swallows the click on «Ingresar» for the whole timeout; `login.ts` sweeps the fraud banner and any dialog's own close button (`SELECTOR.closeModal` — never an arbitrary button) before the click and retries once after a blocked click. **The private site also logs an idle session out (2026-09-06):** some minutes in, the SPA overlays «vamos a cerrar tu sesión por inactividad — ¿Necesitas más tiempo?» with a 10-second countdown; the statement step's 60-second waits do not count as activity. `sessionKeepAlive.ts` polls every 2 s beside the running step and clicks «Mantener sesión» (never «Cerrar sesión»); the step runner additionally re-logs-in when `isLoggedIn` is false before a step, so a lost race costs one login instead of every remaining step. Chrome is launched with `--hide-crash-restore-bubble` so the post-crash «Restore pages?» bubble never invites a human to restore old tabs into a scraper session. **When the scraper reports a closed page/browser, check the crash reports before touching selectors.** **The card carousel is advanced by a swipe when its arrow does nothing (2026-10-09; `swipeToNextCard` in `cards.ts`):** the bank's arrow stopped working that night, for a person too, so after an arrow click that produces no `consultaUltimosMovimientos` call the step drags across the active tile and waits again; «neither the arrow nor a swipe produced a new movements call» is the stop. **The session waits for the call each view makes, never for networkidle (2026-10-10; `docs/santander-session-trim-plan.md`):** the SPA's analytics traffic never goes idle, so every `settle()` timed out (~60 s of a ~100 s session). The login waits for the login button and then for a logged-in URL plus the landing's `cruceProductosOnline`; the card step reads whichever currency a card opens on, clicks the other tab once and never restores it (the carousel keeps the tab; the order alternates and the per-card CLP re-fetch is gone), gives the arrow 5 s before swiping, and stops once every card of the product summary was visited (the swiper no longer disables its arrow) — **and fails when a card the summary says owes money was never listed (2026-10-23; `unlistedCardsWithDebt`)**: no file is written, so the hourly catch-up fetches again, instead of the cupo check failing on the bank's side (a run 4 s after a wake from sleep read only the dormant card's slides; the night's purchase surfaced as a $5x.xxx mismatch); the statements step follows the same pattern after «facturados» resets to Pesos, and reads the tab bar only once rendered; the checking step waits for the peso `transactions` call and the download button. Card-only session 106 s → 31 s, full walk about half. A gateway STATUS 101 «Error al validar token» dropped one session on 2026-10-10 02:47 (first ever; not reproduced in four sessions after). **A rejected login no longer hangs the run (2026-09-11):** the bank answered the 22:00 login with its generic «Ocurrió un error al ingresar a tu banco en línea» toast (the user's own login attempt showed the same, so bank-side, and nothing in the scraper had changed since the previous night's clean run on the same Chrome 153), the window never left the public site, and `openSession`'s login threw BEFORE the try/finally that closes the session — Playwright's live browser connection kept the event loop alive, `main.ts` only sets `exitCode`, so the process idled for hours at «FAILED: page.waitForURL», the 11 pipeline steps behind it never ran and every hourly poll skipped itself behind the running `daily-run.sh`. Now `openSession` closes the context it launched when the login fails (a failed relaunch leaked its new context the same way), `main.ts` force-exits ten seconds after the run is over if anything still holds the loop (logged, so a leak stays visible instead of becoming a hang), and `login` polls for the rejection toast (`TEXT.loginRejected` — it renders in the top-level page beside the `#login-frame` iframe, not inside it) while the redirect is pending, failing within a poll with the bank's own words instead of a 90-second navigation timeout. **A failed login leaves evidence and a wrong clave latches logins off (2026-09-14):** every login failure writes a screenshot plus the frame and page text under `cfraser/scraper-diagnostics/` and quotes the frame text in the error (the «login form never appeared after opening the panel» branch only joined 2026-09-21 — it had thrown with nothing saved, which is what that night's failure read as; it now also records whether the `#login-frame` iframe element existed and its src, since the frame loads from the private-app host and a stall there is indistinguishable from a panel that never opened; **since 2026-09-25 that stall has a face and a retry:** the panel renders the bank's «No fue posible ingresar a tu banco en línea — Comprueba tu conexión a internet» card in place of the iframe, seen 40 s after a wake from hibernation — launchd fires a slept-through 22:00 the moment the Mac wakes, and the frame's document failed to load while the network was still coming back — so `login` clicks the card's «Volver a intentar» once and restarts the 45-second form wait (a second card fails with the bank's words), and `openSession` probes both login hosts before launching Chrome (`waitForHosts` in `ingest/src/network.ts`: any HTTP answer counts, up to 90 s, a wake younger than five minutes named in the log — a normal night pays under a second)) (09-12 and 09-13 had failed silently as «no redirect after 90s»; the screenshot showed the bank's «Alguno de los datos ingresados es incorrecto» toast, whose wording the check did not know). That wording means the stored Clave Digital is wrong (the Keychain item was unchanged since 08-05 and had logged in on 09-11 22:38), and presenting it nightly is how a bank blocks a clave — so `loginLatch.ts` records the rejection in `cfraser/.santander-login-rejected.json` and `runSantander` refuses to open a browser while it stands, failing the step loudly with the fix in the message. The latch clears itself when the Keychain item's modification date is later than the rejection (`readKeychainItemModifiedAt` — attributes only, never `-w`), on a successful login, or for one run with `--force`.
- **The payment-mirror converter runs twice per nightly run**: inside the inbox pipeline (after the receipts step) and again after `import:santander-movements`, because the card's PAGO / ABONO DE DIVISAS evidence for a same-day payment only lands with the feed — a payment the receipt path did not synthesize (manual paste, receipt mail never arrived) used to pair a night late. A run with no step recorded is a failure (`runStepsSucceeded`).
- **The 22:00 run skips only right behind another run (2026-09-26).** The scheduler supersedes the nightly when a daily run finished less than 60 minutes ago (`DAILY_RUN_REPEAT_WINDOW_MINUTES`, `ingestRunPolicy.ts`) — a manual run just before 22:00. It used to skip whenever ANY run had happened that Chile day, so a 12:55 manual run, or a catch-up run on waking from a night asleep, cancelled that evening's run and left the afternoon's card movements for the next night (2026-09-25).
- **Racional import: one read never blocks another (2026-09-27; since 2026-09-30 through `broker.movements`).** Staged crawls used to be re-read together every night, so a single bad one failed every night: on 2026-09-26 an old buy staged without its share count (the list rendered before Racional's dividends response, and the row fell out of the 10 rendered rows) threw before dedupe, although it was already in the ledger. Rows missing units or instrument are `incomplete`, dedupe runs first, and only an incomplete row that would be written blocks its own read (nothing from its list written, the crawl cursor does not move past it, step exits 1; later reads and their dividends still apply). The crawl cursor is ingest's (`cfraser/.racional-crawl-cursor.json`, `crawlCursor.ts`): the list key (`racionalListRowKey`, the crawl's own `rowKey`) of the first row of the newest read whose list the server applied, forward-only. A Racional e-mail nudge retires once a read that started after the mail applied cleanly (`broker_read_coverage`, migration 202 — it replaced `clean_crawl_at` in the old `.racional-import-state.json`), so a booked dividend no longer keeps the crawl running every night. Details in PARSERS.md «Racional (brokerage)».
- **The bank's own cupo is checked every night (2026-09-27; migration 188, `santanderBankCupo.ts`, `ccOwedByCurrency.ts`, `ccBankCupoCheck.ts`).** Santander's landing page requests a product summary once per login (`cruceProductosOnline`); its `TCR` rows state each card's cupo / utilizado / disponible in CLP and in USD (18-digit strings with two implied decimals in both currencies). The fetcher keeps them in the card-movements file (`cupos`, or `cupos: null` + `cuposError` when the session got none — the movements step never fails over it). `import:santander-movements` validates them before writing anything (cupo = utilizado + disponible to the cent; bank account and plastic route to the same master; one row per master and currency) and records them after the lines (`cc_bank_cupo_captures` / `cc_bank_cupo_snapshots`, idempotent per file). The step «CC bank cupo check» (`check:cc-bank-cupo`, nightly after the payment-mirror pass and after the hourly catch-up import) judges the latest capture once against the app's owed per currency: the last closed facturación's billed total (statement header, or the feed's SALDO INICIAL on a provisional close) + plan cuotas not billed yet over every plan bought through that day (`installmentRemainderAfterFacturacionClp` — no calendar month-end cut, unlike `installmentRemainingClpByCalendarMonth`) + every line filed under a later facturación (charges +, payments and notas −, cuota lines left to the plans). That is the bank's own identity (utilizado = SALDO INICIAL + saldo capital cuotas + unbilled feed rows), so pesos agree within a peso per unbilled cuota (the app's split puts the remainder on the last cuota, the bank rounds each) and dollars to the cent; a card that stopped billing uses its last statement close; a snapshot whose feed states another close than the app's latest is `indeterminate`. The report with both sides and the app's terms is a **log** (import sync; verdicts in `cc_bank_cupo_checks`); a mismatch is a notification once per difference and no longer fails the step (2026-11-05, see «The hourly Santander balance poll»), and a feed without a summary fails once. **A row the listing shows twice is two charges (2026-10-11; `webPasteListingDedupeKeys`):** a pasted table or the nightly feed is the complete «movimientos no facturados» list, so identical rows in it are twins (two Grindr subscriptions billed the same day: three pending «APPLE.COM/BILL US$13,28» for the night the check failed), never one row listed twice — the Nth one gets the parser's `#dupN` one-shot key and imports (the merge already treats a fresh `#dup` key with a colliding computed key as an affirmed twin and skips the fuzzy match for it), re-pasting matches each twin on its own key, the feed close's move-forward and the mirror (one-to-one) see the twins too, and the statement's `#dup` lines settle them at the close. The old «repeated in the paste» skip, which collapsed them and left the app one charge short until the statement, is gone. The card page shows the bank's figures and the verdict under «Cupo» (`bank_cupo` on the card payload; the «Cupo» value itself still mixes CLP with the dollars in pesos). The 2026-09-27 duplicate plan would have failed it the same night.
- **The bank's own deposit-account balances are checked nightly too (2026-10-05; migration 212, `bankAccountBalances.ts`, kind `bank_account.balances`).** The same product summary lists the client's deposit accounts (`CCC` cuenta corriente in pesos and dollars, `CCM` cuenta vista; `MONTODISPONIBLE` is the balance — the peso cuenta corriente matched its ledger to the peso on 2026-08-05). The fetcher keeps those rows in the card-movements file (`accounts`), `import:santander-movements` sends them after the card listing (same source ref), and the server records every row; only numbers declared in `bank_account_numbers` (account ↔ issuer + number + currency, set on the live DB — personal data) are judged, inside the «CC bank cupo check» step: the ledger in the account's own currency at the observation's Chile day (display readers) must equal the bank to the cent, each snapshot judged once, the report a log, a mismatch a notification once per difference (as for the cupo check). Declared: Santander USD (#108) and the peso cuenta corriente (#22; first reading 2026-10-05: $8xx.xxx both sides). **Any currency (2026-11-12, any-currency C4):** a row in any code of `currencies` is recorded and judged in the account's own currency to its coin — pesos against the peso checking ledger, any other against the cash ledger held in it (`ledgerBalanceInOwnCurrency`; a declared account held in another currency throws); the product summary asks for a listing only in the currencies the session reads (`CHECKING_LISTING_BY_CURRENCY`: pesos, dollars), any other mismatch is reported with no listing. The summary balance is live (the user's account of the bank), so a post-cutoff wire is in it as soon as it happens — the same moment the ledger counts the forward-posted row.
- **Fail-soft, but never silent.** Steps do not abort the run as a group — one bank being down still lets the rest import — so the outcome is *recorded*, not just logged: `recordDailyRun` (`server/src/dailyRunLog.ts`, from the run report `completeIngestRun` receives) writes an `app_messages` row per run — a plain `log` when every step passed, a **`notification`** (which the app badges unread) plus a macOS alert from the service when any failed; a run whose job stops heartbeating is recorded as a failed run (`lost`). **The first clean run after a failure is a `notification` too (2026-09-12)** — under the clean title (the titles drive `lastDailyRunAt` / `staleDailyRunDays`; kind is read by none of them), with a «Recovered: the previous run (YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM) had failed.» line: the notifications tab lists notifications only, so a resolved outage otherwise sat there unresolved forever (the 22:37 «fetch Santander» failure of 2026-09-11 on top, the 22:46 retry that fixed it invisible as a log row). Same rule for the hourly poll, where the recovery records even when the poll was quiet (`lastRunFailureToRecoverFrom` in `dailyRunLog.ts`); the next clean run finds a success on top and is a plain log again. **A recovery is a notification only when its outage was badged (2026-10-26):** the poll badges one failure per Chile day, so a second outage of the day is logs only and its recovery is a log too (recorded, with the «Recovered» line, so import sync shows the outage end) — until then the tab paired one failure with two «all ok» rows (five such rows re-kinded to log, snapshot `20261026-124049-before-recovery-notification-rekind`).
- The message body names each step and its duration **and the days since the last successful run** — the failure a per-run status cannot express is the run that never happened (machine asleep at 22:00, agent unloaded). `staleDailyRunDays()` is the read side for any surface that wants to show it.

## Global sync scheduler

- In-process scheduler (`server/src/globalSyncScheduler.ts`): polls every 15 minutes **only while any source is stale**; when all are fresh, stops polling and wakes at the earliest source `next_sync` wall time.
- **One scheduler kernel, one source table, one config (2026-11-07; `schedulerKernel.ts`, `globalSyncStale.ts` `SYNC_SOURCES`, `publisherSchedule.ts`, `config.ts`, `outboundFetch.ts`; docs/untangle-plan.md F14).** Every background job — the global sync, the live-quote poll and its two companions (the watchlist history depth, the sync dimming check), the ingest scheduler's 30 s tick, the job queue's executor, the warmer's 00:05 rollover and data-version check, the hourly backup check, the usage flush — is a `ScheduledJob`: it says when it next wants to run (a wall-clock instant) and what a run does; the kernel owns one timer per job, suspend detection (how late the timer fired, or the wall clock running ahead of the monotonic one), the network grace after a wake, the bounded retry budget refilled by a success (`every failure` for the live poll, `only while recovering from a wake` for the global sync), the in-flight guard and the enabled / awake gates. A sync source is one row of `SYNC_SOURCES` (label, configured, stale rule, publisher lag and its dimming hour, wake time, day kind, whether a forced run survives a still-stale success); the status rows, the scheduler's stale list, the dimming list and the step labels are read from it, and the ids live in the leaf `syncSourceIds.ts`. A business-day publisher is a `PublisherSchedule` (`{publishHour, lagBusinessDays}`): the SP (AFC, and the AFP read from the SP) lag 1, UNO's own API lag 2; the evening publish hour of Fintual, the AFP and the AFC is one constant, `EVENING_PUBLISH_HOUR_CHILE`, and the publisher-lag verdict is one function (`publisherLagFromPoll`). Every sync fetch goes through `fetchOutboundText` (user agent, a 60 s timeout unless stated, Latin-1 or UTF-8, Banco Central's rate gate).
- **Only the user's primary app instance runs background jobs.** Any secondary dev server sharing the real DB — every `.claude/launch.json` server config (already set) or an ad-hoc `npm run dev -w nw-tracker-server` — must set **`BACKGROUND_JOBS_ENABLED=0`**: two in-process schedulers on one SQLite file double-run every due sync (duplicate `Sync` rows in `app_messages`, identical diffs) and race writes ("database is locked"). See the env bullet under Server runtime modes.
- **Crypto EOD** (`crypto_eod`): due from 23:55 Chile; `equity_daily` trade dates are **UTC** days. Source: CoinGecko daily USD (includes weekends; Yahoo skipped gaps). Due close = last **completed** UTC day at sync time (never the in-progress UTC calendar day). Stale carryover persists after midnight Chile until that day is in DB (see `cryptoEodDueUtcYmd` in `equityEodSync.ts`). Backfill: `npm run backfill:crypto-coingecko-eod -w nw-tracker-server`.
- Manual “Marcar desactualizado” calls `notifyGlobalSyncScheduler()` to start polling immediately.
- **IPC is the Banco Central's base-2023 spliced index, checked against its own monthly variation (2026-09-29; `ipcSeries.ts`, `fetchIpcMonthsVerified` in `bcentralApi.ts`).** `ipc_daily` stores the index only (`G073.IPC.IND.2023.M`, first-of-month dates, 1998 →); every fetch also reads the published variation (`G073.IPC.VAR.2023.M`) and throws unless each month's index ratio matches it within 1e-5 points (full history: 1,2e-7). Both print plain decimals and go through `parseUsNumber` — the generic `parseBcentralNumber` read «133.823492253» as 133.823.492.253, and the base-2018 series had stopped at 2023-12, so the table held 90 corrupt rows until it was reloaded. The sync re-fetches the last stored month and `writeVerifiedIpcRows` throws when a stored month differs (a revision or rebase is never spliced on); reload with `npm run backfill:sbif-utm-ipc -w nw-tracker-server -- --replace-ipc`. The splice is not the INE's official variation in every month: the UF, which moves with the official one-decimal variation, disagrees with the rounded spliced variation in 15 of 128 months since 2016 (base changes of 2019 and 2024, rounding edges), so a tax reajuste that must follow the SII's figures cannot assume them equal.
- **The official IPC variation lives apart, in `ipc_official_monthly` (2026-09-29; migration 196, `siiOfficialIpc.ts`).** The INE's monthly variation as the SII prints it (`sii.cl/valores_y_fechas/utm/utm<year>.htm`, one decimal) — what the UF follows and what the SII reajusta with — plus the index as printed in the base of its day (ratios across the 2019 and 2024 base changes are meaningless; reajustes chain the variations, `officialIpcVariationPctBetween`). Every month is checked against the UF (growth from the 9th of the next month to the 9th of the one after, rounded to one decimal — it matched 2016-01 → 2026-08, all 128); only the two latest months may wait for their UF, anything older throws, and a stored month whose figures change throws. Filled by `server/scripts/backfill-sii-official-ipc.ts` (from 2016: `uf_daily` starts mid-2015); the monthly `sbif_ipc` sync step fetches this year's page (and last year's until its December is stored).
- **The AFP account's ledger and price (2026-10-06; `afpUnoOfficialSync.ts`, `afpDisplayFrame.ts`, `pensionLedgerShape.ts`, `spAfpFundUnits.ts`, migration 206).** The price is the Superintendencia de Pensiones' official valor cuota (`vcfAFPxls.php`, one fund per file, every AFP, yearly blocks with their own header — UNO from 2019-10 — and a «Valores Provisorios» block that reprints the current month), stored with the SP's own date in `pension_fund_unit_official` (every AFP, fund A; a stored value the SP prints differently is replaced and noted). **`afp_uno_cuota_a` is the display frame:** the official value of Chile business day D shows from the next business day (`AFP_UNO_DISPLAY_LAG_BUSINESS_DAYS` = 1), the day it is published — measured 2026-10-03 → 06: the SP printed Friday's value on Monday ~18:48, uno.cl's homepage ~21:59, nothing at 00:00. The `afp_uno` sync is due from 18:00 Chile on each business day (`AFP_UNO_PUBLISH_HOUR_CHILE`, Fintual's hour since 2026-10-07, so the two funds go stale together; the SP prints ~18:30–18:50) until the previous business day's value is stored, re-reads the current year each run, re-derives the last 60 display days and writes rows only through the day the latest published value shows (a pending value is never pre-written as a carry); a late SP is publisher lag («Esperando publicación», dims from 18:00). The uno.cl homepage scrape and Quetalmi are gone (both wrote the old 00:00 frame). **The ledger** is one row per day a certificate price first shows in that series (`pensionLedgerShape.ts`: rows netted per day, withdrawals their own row, provisions cancel; a price is looked for from six months before its período to eighteen after — the 2017 honorarios cotizaciones were credited by the Tesorería on 2018-05-24), so cuotas enter on the day their price does and a contribution never reads as a gain or loss. **One account per fund manager and fund (2026-10-22; migration 242, `afpAccounts.ts`, `pensionCertificateChain.ts`, `server/scripts/split-afp-ledger-into-fund-accounts.ts`; plan `docs/afp-fund-accounts-plan.md`):** «PlanVital-A» (2017-06 → 2018-08), «Modelo-A» (2018-08 → 2020-01) and «UNO-A» (2020-01 →) each hold the cuotas their certificate rows print, priced by their own fund's official valor cuota in the display frame — series `afp_<afp>_cuota_<fund>` (`afpDisplaySeriesKey`), declared on `accounts.fund_series_key`, which `cuotaLedgerSeriesKeyForAccount` reads (null means the kind's live series, `afp_uno_cuota_a`); `import_key` is `import:afp|afp=<afp>|fund=<fund>`. **A fund switch is a transfer between two fund accounts carrying both cuota counts:** `units_delta` the cuotas sold (the from-leg), `counter_units_delta` the cuotas bought (the to-leg; migration 242, read through `transferToLegUnits` / `transferFromLegUnits` in `movementTransfer.ts`, accepted by the create validation only on a transfer between two units accounts), `flow_kind = 'afp_fund_switch'`, `amount` the pesos the sale produced, dated the day the receiving fund's credit price shows; the sending fund's series ends on the day its sale price showed, so while the money is in transit the account keeps exactly the pesos that left (an on-or-before read), no phantom P/L couplet. The receiving fund credited more than was sent (2018: 546 pesos; 2020: 120.501, 2 %), which shows as its P/L on its first day. **The live UNO account is the afp account whose series is `afp_uno_cuota_a`** (`liveAfpUnoAccountId`): the nightly reads, the sync, the stale rule and the `account_sync_sources` inference resolve it that way, never by import key. A cuota-ledger account with no stored `valuations` gets its chart dates from its units events (`augmentChartDatesForCuotaLedgerAccounts`); the position ticker is `<AFP>-<FUND>`. The 2027 generational fund is one more account, series and switch. Until 2026-10-22 one account held everything in UNO cuotas through the transfer ratios (40,80 → 38,41, 115,66 → 111,37) and a synthetic series `afp_prior_funds_a` frozen in transit. Before 2026-10-06 the ledger summed three AFPs' cuotas at UNO's price (2019-10 → 2020-02 read +4%), its 2017–2019 values were the Excel's (2017 −11,6% against +3,3% official) and the price was stored two business days late from 2026-05-28. The chain is built from the full-history certificate by `server/scripts/split-afp-ledger-into-fund-accounts.ts --cert=<rows.json> [--apply --live]` (report-first; input from `npm run parse:afp-uno-certificate -w nw-tracker-ingest -- --pdf= --out=`; the locating, pairing and per-fund net checks live in `pensionCertificateChain.ts`): each row's pesos must be its cuotas × its official price, each fund's rows plus the switch into it must net to the cuotas it sold, and Σ cuotas today must be unchanged.
- **AFC Fondo de Cesantía (CIC) is a cuota ledger priced by the Superintendencia de Pensiones' public daily valor cuota (2026-09-21; `afcCicSeries.ts`, `cuotaLedgerAccounts.ts`).** Source **`afc_cic`** reads the SP's yearly CSV (`vcfAFCxls.php?aaaa=YYYY` — semicolon, Latin-1, Chilean numbers, one row per CALENDAR day with weekends/holidays as flat carries of the prior close, history since 2002, ~15 KB a year, no auth; the parser fail-fasts on any header/row shape change) into `fund_unit_daily` series `afc_cic` (CIC column only — the FCS is the solidarity fund). Day D is printed on D+1 and the trailing month is «provisorio», so every run re-reads the whole current year (plus the previous one while its Dec 31 is missing) and **replaces** a differing stored value, reporting it as a step note («restated …») — the SP is the authority. **Due rule (2026-11-01):** the SP publishes on Chile business days only, like the AFP's official value: from **18:00 Chile** (`AFC_CIC_PUBLISH_HOUR_CHILE`, the same hour as Fintual's cuota and the AFP, so the three dim together) a business day's publication prints the previous business day's row, so the expected row is the business day before the latest publish day (`afcCicExpectedYmd`: Friday's row from Monday 18:00, Thursday's all weekend), and the schedule wake is the first business day whose publication expects a row beyond the latest in DB (`afcCicNextDueYmd`). Until 2026-11-01 it expected the business day before TODAY, so every weekend waited on Friday's row from Saturday and dimmed the AFC until Monday evening (on Monday 2026-10-05 the CSV still ended at Thursday). **Publisher lag (2026-10-05):** each fetch records its time and the CSV's last printed day (`afcCicLastCheckedAt` / `afcCicLastPublishedYmd` in the sync state); a stale row whose fetch is under 30 min old, whose DB holds that printed day and whose printed day is still before the expected one is the SP's lag (`afcCicPublisherLag`, the panel shows «Esperando publicación»), and it dims only from 18:00 (`AFC_CIC_PUBLISH_HOUR_CHILE`) — the SP's CSV still ended at Thursday 10-01 on Monday 10-05. Disabled when no account carries `fund_series_key = 'afc_cic'`; migration 182 rebuilt the CHECK-enumerated `account_sync_sources` with the value and declared the excel-era AFC account (`import:excel|key=afc`, found by import_key like the AFP one) on the series. **Valuation:** `isCuotaLedgerKindSlug` (afp | afc) generalizes every former `=== "afp"` branch — position meta (`getAccountPositionMeta`, ticker «AFC CIC»), historical marks (`accountMarkClpAtYmd`), today's live value (`accountTodayValue`: AFC reads its ledger BEFORE the stored rows — those are the pre-ledger benchmark, not a mark; AFP keeps stored-first because its sync stamps a today row from the same cuotas × px), chart live last points, perf cuota inflows, and the dashboard/detail overrides — with `cuotaLedgerSeriesKeyForAccount` resolving both from `accounts.fund_series_key` (an AFP account without one prices with `afp_uno_cuota_a`; see «The AFP account's ledger and price»); an `afc` account WITHOUT a series key is not modeled in cuotas and keeps the plain stored-mark path (demo/CI). Manual AFC creates require `units_delta` (`movementUnitsPolicy`, unit «cuotas», reconciled against the series like AFP/Fintual), so the book-ledger edit is off for it. Full-history backfill: `npm run backfill:afc-cic -w nw-tracker-server`. The rates page gets an «AFC CIC» chart (`market_display_series` builtin, rates only — the marquee renders a fixed set of fund chips).
- **The AFC ledger is rebuilt from the AFC documents, not derived (2026-09-22; `afcCertImport.ts` on the server, ingest's `afc/documents.ts` reading the PDFs through `unemployment_fund.documents`; `npm run import:afc-cert -- --cert=<pdf> [--cartola=<pdf> …] [--replace-excel-rows] [--drop-ids=…] [--apply]`).** The AFC prints no cuotas anywhere, and none are needed: the cuatrimestral cartola closes in cuota arithmetic against the SP series to 35 pesos, which proves the CIC is valued at the SP valor cuota, each cotización is credited at the valor cuota of its **fecha de pago**, and the AFC's commission is deducted in cuotas (the one thing the SP series does not carry). So (1) the **certificado de cotizaciones previsionales acreditadas** (every cotización with período, empleador, monto, fecha de pago; two legs per período — trabajador 0,6 % + empleador 1,6 % — closed by a `TOTAL` line the parser must reproduce) becomes ONE movement per (período, pay date) with `units_delta = monto ÷ valor cuota(pay date)`, identity = note key `AFC cotización — período YYYY-MM (pago YYYY-MM-DD)` (idempotent re-imports; a changed printed amount is a `mismatch`, never overwritten); (2) withdrawals are priced at the valor cuota of their date, except one after which the account's own next stored valuation reads 0 with nothing else moving the ledger before it — that one **closes** with exactly the cuotas held; (3) each **estado cuatrimestral** yields a true-up per printed saldo (`AFC ajuste cartola — saldo inicial|final YYYY-MM-DD`): `units = saldo ÷ valor cuota(day) − ledger through the day`, `flow_kind = cash_fee` when the ledger sits above the saldo (the commission) or `savings_earnings` below — both are P/L to every deposit reader, never capital — refused when the period's cotizaciones in the ledger differ from the cartola's «Total de cotizaciones» (the certificate import is incomplete; a true-up must not absorb missing cotizaciones as «commission»), and closing withdrawals are re-measured after the true-ups. Without `--apply` the whole transaction is rolled back after printing, so the report IS the post-import ledger. The pesos ÷ valor cuota derivation script (`afc:derive-cuotas`) remains only as a benchmark tool for a ledger with no certificate.
- **Stale vs the publisher's lag (2026-09-15).** `isFintualSyncStale` deliberately keeps the source stale while the cuota expected for a publish day has not appeared (the 08-24 morning-carry rule), but every consumer read that as «the app failed to sync»: on 09-15 Fintual simply had not published Monday's cuota by Tuesday noon, the poll was running every 15 minutes, and the dashboard dimmed every Fintual account at 38% while the APV cards showed the live proxy. `fintualPublisherLag` (`globalSyncStale.ts`) classifies a stale Fintual row as the PUBLISHER's lag when the last poll is recent (`FINTUAL_PUBLISHER_LAG_MAX_POLL_AGE_MS`, two scheduler intervals — read from the goals snapshot's `fetchedAt`), nobody forced a run, the API's latest published day is behind the first expected publish day, and every held fund's bar for that published day is in the DB; anything else (sig mismatch, unreconciled positions, a missed poll) stays plain stale.
