The status row carries `publisher_lag {expected_ymd, published_ymd, last_checked_at}`, the payload adds **`stale_behind`** (= `stale` minus publisher lag) and `dashboardAccounts` dims from THAT list; `stale` itself is unchanged, so the scheduler keeps polling. The sync panel shows «Esperando publicación» with the two dates and the last check instead of «Desactualizado». **Publisher lag dims once the cuota is OVERDUE (2026-09-21):** the payload's dimming list is **`stale_dimming`** (`staleDimmingSources` — it replaced `stale_behind`): our own staleness always dims, a publisher-lag source dims from **18:00 Chile** (`FINTUAL_PUBLISH_HOUR_CHILE` in `fintualPublishDate.ts`, the one constant the stale rule, the schedule wake and the dim rule share) until the cuota is applied; before 18:00 — pre-open, the live proxy, the post-close hold — every Fintual account stays normal because the display holds all the publisher has. Wall-clock window by design: a cuota still missing at midnight reads normal again from 00:00 and dims at 18:00 the next day. On 09-21 18:15 the panel said «Esperando publicación» while the APV cards sat at full opacity, which is what prompted it. **A cuota already in hand needs no same-day poll (2026-09-20):** Fintual forward-publishes a holiday block's flat carries (09-17 published through Sunday 09-20), so on a publish day whose cuota is already applied (`fintualLastAppliedPublishYmd >= today`, signature unchanged, positions reconciled) `isFintualSyncStale` is false without a poll that day — the old «poll ran today» clause dimmed every Fintual account from Sunday 18:00. Paired schedule fix in `syncSourceSchedule.ts` «fintual»: the next wake is the first publish day whose 18:00 is still ahead (today before 18:00, else from tomorrow — a block-closing Sunday counts) and skips past an applied day; the old branch jumped to the publish day AFTER the next one, so from Saturday it scheduled Monday and the scheduler — which sleeps until the earliest `next_sync` when nothing is stale — never woke at Sunday 18:00. The applied day is threaded in explicitly (`SyncSourceScheduleOptions.fintualAppliedPublishYmd` from `syncSourceRow`), never read from the state file inside the schedule, so the schedule stays a pure function of its inputs.
- **A skipped Fintual day is a stand-in at the next value, healed from the public serie on any day (2026-10-17; `fundUnitDaily.ts` `gapFill`, `fintual_official_serie`).** Fintual publishes day D's cuota the evening of D (the "D+1" rule is the public serie's), and over a weekend / holiday block a second value dated the block's LAST día inhábil (Sunday 10-18; the 10-12 holiday Monday), a few days of fee accrual below Friday's. When the evening poll misses Friday (2026-10-16: three hours of dead network, every Yahoo request failing too — not a sleep) and the next poll finds only the block value, the gap days used to be written as copies of the PREVIOUS cuota (`fintual:carry-forward`), so Friday's mark was Thursday's price while today's read the new value and Saturday's Día showed Friday's whole move (Retiro −1,19 M). `upsertFundUnitSpotPreservingHistory` now takes `gapFill: "stand_in_new"`: the Fintual path writes every skipped day at the NEW value as `fintual:stand-in|from=<observation day>` (a placeholder to `isFintualCarryForwardFundUnitNote`, never published, so the verifier replaces it unconditionally, the RN composition anchor skips it and the newest-bar check ignores it); other callers keep the carry. The verifier used to run only after a goals poll, which is not due on a weekend once the block value is applied, so `fintual_official_serie` is its own source: stale from 12:00 Chile, at most every 2 h (`fintualOfficialSerieLastCheckedAt`), while a mapped series holds a placeholder dated within the last 7 days and on or before yesterday; the goals poll's check stamps the same field so the two never run minutes apart. The six 10-16/10-17 rows were rewritten to the 10-18 value by hand (snapshot `20261017-121444-before-fintual-stand-in-repair`). **Global sync waits for the network after a wake and retries once (2026-10-17; `schedulerKernel.ts`):** a suspend is measured from a late timer or wall-vs-monotonic clock divergence (the old «idle gap» log was the poll interval minus the run time, never a wake), a tick that follows one waits 10 s, and a failed post-wake sync is retried after 60 s and 180 s, the budget refilled by a success — the live-quotes rule, for the September hibernation wakes; a failure with no wake keeps the 15-minute poll.
- **Fintual valor cuota = Fintual's own share count, verified against the official serie (2026-09-17).** The evening poll prices each goal's fund from the site's `/gql/` «latest accrued balance» queries (`clReserveLatestAccruedBalance` / `clApvAGoalLatestAccruedBalance` / `clApvBGoal…` / `clGoal…`, cookie-authed like `GET /api/goals`; builder + parser in `server/src/fintualRealAssetNav.ts`): `investmentClosureDate` is the publish day, `portfolioSharesBalance.sharesBreakdown[].sharesQuantity` is Fintual's share count (APV-A sums the user-owned and state-bonus portfolios), and valor cuota = ticker valuation ÷ those shares — **never ÷ the ledger's cuotas**. The ledger lags Fintual whenever cash is in transit: a 100.000 retiro requested 09-16 had sold 68,93 cuotas on 09-17 (paid 09-21, the «Pagamos» mail lands the transfer on payday), and the one-day graph ÷ ledger-cuotas derivation (b1d2f8b) wrote four Reserva bars 1,63% low, which failed the goals-NAV reconcile and kept the source stale for the long weekend (repaired: `server/scripts/archive/repair-fintual-reserva-share-count-rows.ts`, snapshot `20260917-…-before-fintual-reserva-share-count-repair`). The goals-API `nav` counts the payout (`reservePendingBalance.pendingPaymentWithdrawalsAmount`), so ledger cuotas × true price equals it by identity until payday — the app keeps showing the money, as intended, and `expectedGoalsApiNavClp` = shares + pending deposits + pending payouts is what a «mismatch» log line compares against. A goal with no Fintual price this poll (empty, or its closure day lags the publish day) writes **nothing** for its v2 account (`syncFintualFundUnitsFromResolutions` skips; the NAV ÷ cuotas inference is gone for v2) and the missing-bar carry re-polls; a `/gql/` fetch or shape failure throws (never a guessed date or price); a goal holding several funds throws (one series per account). **Next-day verifier (`server/src/fintualPublicSeriePrice.ts`):** after every goals poll, `runFintualOfficialSeriesCheck` re-reads the last 14 days of the OFFICIAL public serie prices — `GET https://inversiones.fintual.com/api/managed_funds/serie/share_price?fund_serie=<id>&start_date&end_date`, no auth, history since inception, weekends included, publishes day D on D+1 so it can never serve the evening write — for every mapped series (`FINTUAL_PUBLIC_SERIE_BY_SERIES_KEY`: reserva2 → serie 1 Very Conservative Streep A, apv_a/apv_b → 7 Risky Norris APV, risky_norris → 6 Risky Norris A; ids from the fund-page bundle's `FINTUAL_MANAGED_FUNDS`, verified value-for-value over 20 months, max noise 6,4e-5 on weekend restatements): fills days no poll wrote (an EMPTY goal never publishes, so this is what keeps the RN-proxy anchor series current), replaces carry-forward placeholders, and **corrects** a published bar beyond `FINTUAL_PUBLIC_SERIE_TOLERANCE_REL` = 1e-4 — as a change line plus a **step error** («Errors:» in the sync log), because a correction means the evening derivation was wrong. Manual: `npm run fintual:backfill-cert-fund-units -w nw-tracker-server -- [--from=YYYY-MM-DD] [--series=…] [--apply]` (report-first; the old real_assets backfill is gone with the endpoint). Provenance notes: `fintual:gql:shares-publish|<import notes>` (evening), `fintual:public-serie:publish|serie=<id>` (official), legacy `fintual:real_assets:publish`; `isFintualPublishedFundUnitNote` is the one predicate that protects them from carries and the unreconciled-inferred cleanup.

## Live market quotes (intraday)

- **Scheduler** (`server/src/liveMarketQuotesScheduler.ts`): polls Yahoo every **2 min** while a priced market is open and every **5 min** otherwise, on the wall clock (see «Polls follow the market clock»). HTTP handlers read **`live_market_quotes` only** — no on-demand Yahoo from client requests. The tick also runs `ensureWatchlistEquityHistoryDepth()` (watchlist.ts): `equity_daily` history to the watchlist YTD/YoY anchors (~400d) for watchlist + composite tickers, one Yahoo attempt per ticker per Chile day — `GET /api/watchlist` serves DB only and never fetches.
- **Prices come from Yahoo's price stream; the poll is the backstop (2026-11-04; `yahooPriceStream.ts`, `yahooPricingData.ts`, `liveQuotesFlushPolicy.ts`).** The poll wrote every ticker at once, so every moved balance flashed in the same render. The server (never the browser) keeps one socket to `wss://streamer.finance.yahoo.com/?version=2` (unofficial, no key) subscribed to the watchlist series keys plus every live exchange-rate pair's Yahoo symbol (`CLP=X`, `EURUSD=X`: `liveFxPairs`, see «Every core pair has a live rate»); frames are `{"type":"pricing","message":"<base64 protobuf PricingData>"}`, decoded by hand: field 1 id, 2 price (float32, rounded to 27 priceHint), 3 time (sint64 ms), 7 marketHours (plain enum — 6 and 7 are NOT zigzag: 0 pre, 1 regular, 2 post), 12 change (previous close = price − change; the stream sends no previous close for equities); every other field is skipped by wire type, and a frame without id / price / time throws and drops the socket. Only regular-session prints of today's session inside the readers' own gates are kept (`streamTickInSession`: NYSE / Santiago session, crypto always, an exchange rate while its fx day is open), so the post-close held print and the fx day end keep the poll's rules. **Write rule (user's choice): a flash is a real move** — each second, every unit whose price differs from what it last wrote and whose last write is ≥ 30 s old is written, several together when several moved, but never sooner than `LIVE_QUOTES_FLUSH_GAP_MS` (default 5 s; 15 s on the demo, `render.yaml`) after the previous write that moved something, since each one rebuilds the caches (the warm after a tick, ~3,5 s, fits in the gap). A unit is a symbol, except the Risky Norris basket's ETFs, written together so the APV marks move once per interval. Writes go through `writeStreamQuotes` (`liveMarketQuotesSync.ts`): the poll's rows, change test, stamp and scoped invalidation. A symbol with a kept tick in the last 5 min is **covered**: the poll skips it (`liveStreamCoverage` → `syncAllLiveMarketQuotes(now, {skipTickers, skipLiveFx})`) and the stream re-stamps its row every 3 min at the same price (no change, no invalidation) so the 10-min freshness window never lapses on a quiet `.SN` or the dollar; anything uncovered (a closed market, a quiet symbol, a dead socket) is the poll's. While connected the poll keeps the 5-min cadence in session too (the open tick stays). Reconnects back off 2 / 10 / 30 / 60 s; 3 min with no frame while a subscribed market trades closes the socket. The client polls the stamp every 5 s and refetches by `X-Live-Inputs` (see «Polls follow the market clock»). The hosted demo connects only while awake. `LIVE_QUOTES_STREAM_ENABLED=0` = polling only; `LIVE_QUOTES_STREAM_URL` overrides the endpoint. Probe 2026-11-04: SPY ~1 frame/s, CLP=X and CFIETFIPSA.SN a frame every minute or more.
- **Wake and wholesale-failure handling (2026-09-15; the scheduler kernel since 2026-11-07, `schedulerKernel.ts`).** The Mac hibernated 07:10→10:53 (battery at 1%); the catch-up tick fired one second after wake, before Wi-Fi was back, every Yahoo request failed, the fx fell back to yesterday's EOD mirror and nothing retried for a full interval — five minutes in which every Yahoo-priced account read yesterday's close (day P/L exactly 0) while AFP/APV moved. Now a poll that fires an interval or more late (or after the wall clock ran ahead of the monotonic one) waits 10 s before fetching, and a poll where NO equity request succeeded (`tickFailedWholesale` — partial failures are Yahoo throttling and never retry; the fx leg is not consulted because its EOD fallback reports ok) retries after 15 s then 45 s: the budget refills only when a poll succeeds, so a continuous outage costs two extra attempts in total, a success cancels a pending retry, and a thrown sync (a bug, not the network) never retries.
- **Post-close hold + the mark date's fx frame (2026-09-21; `equityHeldSessionQuote` in `equityQuote.ts`).** Between a session's close and the EOD sync landing its bar, the live gate (`shouldUseLiveEquityQuote`) is off and every reader fell to the PREVIOUS bar: IPSA read a 0 day P/L from the 17:05 Chile cutoff until the 17:10 `stocks_santiago` run landed the bar, and NYSE tickers had the same gap between 16:05 and the `stocks_nyse` run. Now a session that has closed for the day (`equitySessionClosedForDay` — NYSE after 16:05 New York, Santiago after 17:05 Chile, never crypto) and has no bar yet **holds the session's last stored print** in `live_market_quotes` (Yahoo's `regularMarketPrice` re-serves the official close after the bell, so the print IS the close; freshness is not required, the session match is). One choke point, consumed by the quote resolver (marquee/watchlist), `equityBrokeragePositionMeta` (dashboard rows) and `computeEquityMtmClpDisplaySync` (today's marks). **Paired fx rule:** a today mark's fx is `fxForLiveMtm(markDate)` in every branch — live CLP=X while the mark date's fx day is open (until 17:05 New York), the stored close after — and `computeEquityMtmClp` reads `fxForLiveMtm(asOfYmd)` whatever the price source (a historical date never matches the live row's session, so it still reads the stored row). Before, the non-live branches priced today's bar at `fxRowOnOrBefore(today)` = FRIDAY's close for the hour between the NYSE close and the fx day end, so every USD holding was re-framed by the day's fx move and snapped back at 18:05 Chile (VEA read +1,27% instead of +0,05% while the marquee showed the live rate). The dashboard row's USD frame (`current_value_usd`) now divides by the same `fxForLiveMtm` — it used to divide a live-fx CLP value by the stored on-or-before row, understating USD holdings by the day's fx move all session. Pre-open is unchanged: the prior bar at the prior day's stored fx (USD cash floats from Chile midnight, equities from the open — a known asymmetry, not touched).
- **Polls follow the market clock; the client refetches when prices move (2026-10-27; `nextLiveQuotesTickAtMs`, `liveQuotesStamp.ts`).** A fixed `setInterval` from boot left up to five minutes of each session's start on yesterday's close (that day's first live poll was 10:31:12, then the 30 s warm debounce and the client's 5-minute refetch: up to ~10 min from the bell to the screen). Ticks now sit on wall-clock multiples of the cadence — **`LIVE_QUOTES_SESSION_INTERVAL_MS` (default 2 min) while a market the watchlist prices is in its regular session** (NYSE, Santiago; crypto keeps the base), `LIVE_QUOTES_INTERVAL_MS` otherwise — and each open minute gets its own tick 20 s later (`OPEN_TICK_DELAY_MS`). **Since 2026-11-04 a moved price is rebuilt on demand, and the client refetches only what read it:** a write that moves a price (stream or poll) publishes the live-quotes stamp at once and logs what moved (`noteLiveQuotesChanged(at, {tickers, fx, dimming, all})`, `fx` the pair keys that moved, a 500-entry log); the warmer no longer reacts to `live_tail` (rebuilding both currencies' bundles, flows, visited pages and Rentabilidad series on every move built mostly what nobody looked at), so the first request that needs a dropped response rebuilds it on top of the cached history. `GET /api/live-quotes/stamp?since=<stamp>` answers `{stamp, moves}` — the union of what moved after `since`, `all` when the log cannot tell (a `since` from before this process or before the log's oldest entry). Every GET API response carries **`X-Live-Inputs`** (`liveInputsHeader.ts`): the request runs in its own dependency node (`cacheDependencies.ts`; cache and live-tier hits link their entry's node, so a cached response names the same inputs as its build) and the header lists the tickers whose live quote it read plus one token per live rate (`$fx` = USD/CLP, `$fx:EUR_USD` any other pair) and `$dimming`, or `none`. The client keeps the inputs on each payload under a non-enumerable symbol (`client/src/liveInputs.ts`; `j()` attaches them, a default `structuralSharing` keeps them) and `useLiveQuotesStampInvalidation` (polled every 5 s while the tab is visible) invalidates only the queries whose inputs meet the moves; a payload with no inputs (a scalar, no header) refetches on every move. A response reads live prices only where it shows them: group-page line and row order is by **yesterday's close**, not today's value (`sortNavNodesByMarkDesc`, `orderGroupTabRowsBucketMajor` — an order read live put every price into the sidebar and the flows lists, and holds for the day now), and the account page bundle builds only its own dashboard row (`buildDashboardAccountRow`), not the whole listing. So the sidebar, messages, reminders, benchmark list and flows lists answer `none` and never refetch on a price move, and a stock or coin page refetches only for its own ticker, the dollar and the sync dimming. To see where a response reads live prices, record the stack of each `noteLive*Read` call while building it.
- **A tick drops only what read a moved price (2026-10-31; `cacheDependencies.ts`; the store itself is `cacheRegistry.ts`, see `docs/cache-registry.md`).** Every aggregation-cache entry and live-tier memo builds inside a dependency node that records the live inputs it read — a ticker (the live-quote row readers and the gates that decide whether to read them: `shouldUseLiveEquityQuote`, `equityHeldSessionQuote`, `getLatestLiveEquityQuoteRow`) or a live exchange rate by its pair key (`noteLiveFxRead("USD_CLP")`: `shouldUseLiveFxQuote` / `shouldUseLivePairQuote`, the live fx row, the observado window average; `EUR_USD` the same way, so a EUR/USD tick drops only what read EUR/USD); a lookup links the entry's node into whatever is built around it, hit or miss. The poll passes what moved (`publishLiveInputChange` / `invalidateMarketDataAggregations("live_tail", moves)`): among the entries an unscoped tick drops, only those that read a moved price, or whose inputs are unknown, go — a coin moving at night leaves the card ledgers, the mortgage and the liabilities' consolidation cached. A poll that also wrote the day's `fx_daily` close drops as before. A new reader of `live_market_quotes` must report through those functions. **So must every switch decided by the clock or the sync state (2026-11-02)** — a session, a publish hour, a holiday hold, a settled cuota: it reports what it would read whatever it decides. The Risky Norris proxy gate (`shouldUseRiskyNorrisProxyMtm`) did not, so the APV marks cached before the open survived every tick of the session and read 0 day P/L; it now reports the basket and the dollar. Two inputs that are not prices: the dashboard's sync dimming (`stale_dimming`, turning at 18:00 Chile) is reported with `noteSyncDimmingRead` and dropped when the dimmed set changes (`syncDimmingWatch.ts`, checked on every live-quotes tick, which also publishes the refetch stamp); and a global sync run whose Fintual poll changes the sync-state fields valuation reads without writing a row (the bar landed earlier, the NAV matched the DB) clears the caches after saving (`fintualValuationStateFingerprint`). A process-lifetime memo of database state belongs in `dbStateMemo` (the first-buy date was a plain Map, never cleared). **Inside a transaction the memo is the transaction's own (2026-11-09):** an entry lives while the transaction does (`dbTransactionEpoch` in `db.ts` moves when an outermost transaction begins or ends, when any transaction function throws — its savepoint rolled back — and on every `exec`) and until the change log records a write of a logged table (`mark_input_changes`, which the triggers fill inside the transaction too; a write that changes no logged row keeps it). Until then nothing was memoized inside a transaction, and every ingest document — handlers run inside the receiver's transaction — that rebuilt the expense lines reloaded the nav-tree model on every call: ~2 minutes of CPU per request on the home server, an hourly bank run of 8–15 minutes instead of 1,5. Chart-inactive (`accountChartInactive`) reads no price at all: an account valued from units (shares, coins, cuotas), a dollar account or a peso ledger (checking, a broker's peso balance, a portfolio caja) is inactive when its ledger holds none today; only the other accounts (the property, the mortgage, stored marks) read their month closes. An empty position reads no quote either: today's mark of a stock with no shares, or a coin account with no coins, is its sold-out 0, and the stock's position (`equityBrokeragePositionMeta`) carries no mark, all from the quantity alone, so their pages do not drop on their ticker's tick. Parity: `liveTickIncrementalRebuild.test.ts`, `cacheDependencies.test.ts`.
- **Response caches beside the bundle (2026-10-31; families in `cacheFamilies.ts`).** The card ledgers (`cc.ledger|`, per card and per group), an account page's bundle, a group page's valuation / tables / monthly P/L / Rentabilidad (`dashboard.page_bundle|account_detail|`, `…|group|`), the dashboard account rows (`…|account_rows|`, shared by the home bundle, the nav snapshot and the account bundle) and a group's daily-series response (`daily.series|response|group|`) are cached; every API write drops them too (`index.ts`, request kinds `api-write` / `machine`), since labels, notes and card config sit beside the logged tables. The group endpoints share one consolidation per (group, unit, account set) (`groupConsolidatedMonthlyRaw`). The card page payload no longer carries the statements with their lines, the stored balance snapshot, the per-purchase proxy or each purchase's payment rows (the page drew none of them). The warmer then rebuilds the pages the usage log says are opened, most visited first (`visitedPagesWarm.ts`).
- **Manual backfill:** `npm run live-quotes:sync -w nw-tracker-server`
- **Env:** `LIVE_QUOTES_SYNC_ENABLED` (default on), `LIVE_QUOTES_STREAM_ENABLED` (default on), `LIVE_QUOTES_FLUSH_GAP_MS` (default `5000`), `LIVE_QUOTES_INTERVAL_MS` (default `300000`), `LIVE_QUOTES_SESSION_INTERVAL_MS` (default `120000`, poll-only), `LIVE_QUOTES_RETENTION_HOURS` (default `48`), `LIVE_QUOTES_MAX_AGE_MS` (default `2 × INTERVAL_MS`).
- **EOD** (`stocks_nyse` / `crypto_eod` in global sync) still writes `equity_daily` after the close; live quotes are for intraday MTM / marquee / current-month P/L.
- **Cache invalidation (F2, 2026-11-07; `docs/cache-registry.md` is the full account).** One store (`cacheRegistry.ts`), every key in a family declared once in `cacheFamilies.ts`, one write signal: triggers log every change of a logged table, from any connection, into `mark_input_changes` (`markInputTriggers.ts`: `mark` rows carry the account and raw date a historical mark reads, `write` rows any other change; a table in neither list fails `cacheRegistry.test.ts`). The registry applies the log before a cached read, after every API write and on the warmer's poll; mark rows trim the per-account mark series (`markSeriesStore.ts`) and each family drops what read the change. Market writers of this process add a notice (`invalidateMarketDataAggregations`), so same-connection sync writes, which do not bump `data_version`, still reach the caches; a value no table holds (the live dollar rate the expense lines priced unpaid dollars at) is a declared input (`dropFamiliesWhenInputChanged`). A new cache is a family plus a key builder built through `getAggregationCached` — no invalidation call anywhere; a new table is a migration adding its triggers.
- **Risky Norris proxy MTM is framed on the NYSE session day, never the Chile calendar day (2026-09-11; `riskyNorrisProxyMtm.ts`).** **The Chile-holiday hold keeps the held session's close until the catch-up cuota lands (2026-09-17):** `inChileHolidayProxyHold` asks whether the session the proxy is holding (today's once open, else the previous NYSE session — Friday's on a weekend) fell on a Chile non-business day and whether the NEXT Chile business day's evening sync has settled; it is consulted before the trading-day gate, so a Friday holiday keeps Friday's close on screen through Saturday, Sunday and Monday pre-open, then Monday's live session, then Monday's cuota settles it. Before that the hold ended at New York midnight and the weekend snapped back to Fintual's forward-published flat carries (09-18..20), which is what the old `stubSettled(true)` tests encoded. A normal weekend after a Chile business day never holds. **The hold is per DATE, not just today (2026-09-19):** `riskyNorrisProxyAppliesOnYmd(ymd)` applies the same test to a historical date (the NYSE session on/before it fell on a Chile non-business day and the next Chile business day's cuota has not settled), and `getAccountPositionMeta` then values that date through `riskyNorrisProxyCuotaForMtm(series, now, asOfYmd)`, which carries the value on the SESSION the date reflects (live session, else the held one — never the calendar day), so every day of a held block values identically and its «Día» P/L is 0 by construction; **The per-date predicate keys on the sync state's last APPLIED publish day reaching the catch-up day (2026-09-21), never on `fintualGlobalSyncSettledForChileDay`:** that check also asks whether TODAY's positions reconcile with the last poll's NAV (`fintualCertV2PollReconciled` multiplies CURRENT cuotas by the publish-day price), which is false for hours whenever cash is in transit — the Reserva retiro's «Pagamos» transfer at 12:30 dropped 68,93 cuotas against the 09-17 signature, the live check answered false for every day it was asked about, and the per-date hold then valued 2025-05-01 (Chile holiday, NYSE open) through today's basket, whose SPYM has no bar before 2025-07 — a 500 on every daily surface until the evening poll re-signed; silently, every historical Chile holiday was being re-valued through the proxy in that window. only today's value used to be proxied, and at 00:07 Saturday the APV accounts read Friday's whole move again (+278k/+125k) against the flat 09-18 bar, then would have again on Sunday and Monday pre-open, and once more inside Monday's official bar. Friday's history is Friday's close, Monday live shows Monday's move only, and when Monday's cuota settles the predicate turns off and the block reverts to the official bars (the same retroactive reattribution a live day gets at its evening settle) — the settle is a Fintual apply, whose full `invalidateMarketDataAggregations` drops the `daily.marks|` cache those historical days sit in. The RN / APV Fintual accounts value today through the live/EOD basket proxy (`shouldUseRiskyNorrisProxyMtm`) from the NYSE open until the Fintual evening sync settles the cuota that reflects that session, then read the official cuota until the next open. "Settled" is asked about `nyseWallClock(now).ymd` (`fintualGlobalSyncSettledForChileDay`), and the Chile-holiday hold (`inChileHolidayProxyHold`) classifies that same session day: Chile runs 0–2 h ahead of New York depending on the two DST calendars, so between Chile midnight and New York midnight the Chile day has rolled while the settled session has not — asking "settled for Chile today" there was false and re-armed the proxy for the gap (2026-09-11 00:00–01:00: APV-A/B read +286k/+129k against the 22:15 goals NAV, snapping back at 01:00; a Saturday 00:30 took the holiday-hold branch and showed the Friday EOD proxy). Unreachable while both zones sat at UTC−4, exposed by Chile's 2026-09-06 spring-forward; 2 h wide from November 1 until Chile falls back. **The proxy's fx leg is the dólar observado frame (2026-09-11; `fxObservadoFrame.ts`).** Fintual values day D with day D's US closes and day D's CHILEAN INTERBANK fx — the Banco Central observado published on D+1 (`fx_daily_bcentral` is keyed by publication date), an average of trades in a market that closes 14:00 Chile — while the proxy used Yahoo's 17:05-New-York CLP=X close, so every late-afternoon peso move entered the proxy today and left it tomorrow. Measured over 66 sessions (weekday Chile holidays excluded, where Fintual prints a flat carry): US closes × observado(D+1)/observado(D) reproduces the official return with 18 bp rmse (corr 0,990; last five sessions within ±3 bp) vs 31 bp for the Yahoo close (err↔fx corr −0,41, lag-1 autocorr −0,28); every lag-1 price variant was uncorrelated (no lag in the equity leg), and anchor age was not the driver (cumulative error 31→43 bp from 1 to 4 sessions). `proxyClpFromMeta` now resolves BOTH fx legs (composition_date anchor and valuation day) through `observadoFrameFxForDay`: the first observado row dated after the day (a gap wider than 7 days throws); while that publication is pending (today, or yesterday before the 18:00 `sbif_usd` sync) the running average of the live CLP=X prints inside the 09:00–14:00 Chile window (≥3 prints; 2026-09-09: 925,55 vs the 925,97 published next morning), else the frame's most recent observation on an earlier day — yesterday's window estimate while its publication is pending too, else the last published observado (carrying the last PUBLISHED row would pair today's basket with an interbank fx two sessions old: −119 bp at 2026-09-11 00:40); a head older than 7 days throws (stale sync). `anchor_fx_clp` on `watchlist_composite_meta` is informational (the frame value at sync time); `fxClpWeekdayEodOnOrBefore` and the Yahoo weekend-bar rule are gone. **The composition anchor is the cuota's own session, never Fintual's positions date (2026-09-22; `resolveCompositionAnchor` in `fintualRiskyNorrisComposition.ts`).** The sync used to store the positions endpoint's `date` as `composition_date` with the latest cuota on or before it, so the basket's price base (closes on or before that date) and the cuota could belong to different sessions: on 09-20 (a Sunday inside the Fiestas Patrias block) the anchor was the 09-17 valuation carried flat under FRIDAY 09-18's closes — a NYSE session Fintual never valued until Monday — so Friday's +0,41% basket move never entered the proxy level and the APV cards read −1,04% for a −0,49% day; on 09-21 it stored 09-17 with the 09-16 cuota (the RN A serie lags a day), −190 bp. Now the anchor day is the latest Chile business day on or before the positions date carrying a published (non-carry-forward) Risky Norris cuota, the APV cuota must exist on that exact day (an APV serie that exists but lacks it throws; only a serie absent altogether yields null), and `composition_date` IS that day, so `basketReturnForHoldings` / `observadoFrameFxForDay` resolve on the session the cuota embeds. **The sync refreshes serie 6 and serie 7 from the official public prices first** (`RN_COMPOSITION_REFRESH_SERIES_KEYS`, the same verifier the Fintual poll runs) — the empty RN goal never publishes in the evening poll, so without it the anchor lagged the publisher by however old the last goals poll was — and runs at **18:30 Chile on Chile business days and the day after one** (`isFintualRnCompositionDueDay`; a Friday's cuota publishes on Saturday, a Sunday brings nothing), not 10:00. **Self-check on every run** (`compositionSelfCheck`): the previous anchor's EOD prediction for the new anchor day vs the official cuota, as a step note, and a step error beyond `RN_COMPOSITION_SELF_CHECK_ERROR_BP` = 40 bp (per-session noise measured 24 bp rmse, p90 29 bp over 79 sessions; the defect read −50 / −190 bp). The proxy's DAY change was never affected — the anchor cancels in the ratio — only its level. `fxForLiveMtm` / `fx_daily` stay the frame for every USD cash/equity account — only the RN/APV proxy embeds the observado.
- **The proxy basket is kept per Fintual positions date (2026-10-11; migration 238, `watchlistCompositeHistory.ts`).** `watchlist_composite_meta` / `_holdings` carry only the latest basket (delete + insert on every sync), so until then nothing recorded which ETFs entered or left, or when. `watchlist_composite_positions` (+ `_position_holdings`) holds one basket per (bucket, positions date — Fintual's «as of», the holdings' `synced_at`) in **Fintual's own tickers** (`holdingsAsStated`; the SPXS → SPY price mapping is pricing only — Fintual's SPXS is the Invesco S&P 500 UCITS ETF and the fund has never held a real SPY, so the 38 recovered «SPY» rows were renamed SPXS and the backfill maps them back), write-once through `recordCompositePositions`: a repeat statement is `unchanged`, the same positions date with other weights a `conflict` — the sync keeps the first statement, still re-anchors on the new one, and logs a step error; the sync's note names what entered and left against the latest recorded basket («basket changed vs 2026-10-01: −ESGV»), and the change line compares holding counts. Nothing on a request path reads it. History recovered from the DB snapshots (`server/scripts/backfill-rn-composition-history.ts`, report-first, `--apply`; opens each snapshot through the `sqlite3` CLI with `immutable=1` — better-sqlite3 cannot open a WAL snapshot read-only): 39 positions dates 2026-06-24 → 2026-10-08 as `source = 'snapshot'` (their `etf_weight_sum` is the sync log's one-decimal figure), 18 dates the log names have no snapshot and are lost. **Before the sync existed the record is the regulator's (2026-10-11; migration 239, `cmfFundPortfolio.ts`, `server/scripts/import-cmf-rn-composition.ts`):** the CMF's monthly «Cartera de inversiones extranjeras» of every fondo mutuo (`ffm_cartera.php` → `ffm_download.php`, `cartera=EXTR`, file `FFM_INV_EXTR_YYYYMM.txt`, ~0,5 MB a month, columns per Circular 1333; Risky Norris is RUN 9570 «NORRIS») lists each holding's nemotécnico («SPXS LN», «FTEC US») and its share of fund assets (the last column). The importer (`--fetch` downloads the months missing under `cfraser/cmf-ffm-cartera/`, report-first, `--apply`) records one `source = 'cmf'` basket per month-end — weights renormalized to the foreign-ETF sleeve, `etf_weight_sum` = the sleeve's share of assets — and leaves a date Fintual already stated alone: the two agree to the decimal on 2026-09-30, which is the check that the normalization matches Fintual's. Loaded 2018-02 → 2026-08 (102 months; 2018-01 predates the fund). The Wayback Machine held only two baskets (the positions API once, 2025-11-19, and one server-rendered fund page, 2026-01-24), both consistent with the filings. **The filing is also a nightly cross-check (`cmf_rn_portfolio` global-sync source, `cmfRnPortfolioSync.ts`):** from 09:00 Chile, once a day while the previous calendar month's filing is not on record (`cmfRnPortfolioLastMonth` / `cmfRnPortfolioLastCheckYmd` in the sync state; the first run reads the latest `cmf` month from the history), the sync asks the CMF for it, records it as a `cmf` basket (kept out when Fintual stated that very day) and saves the raw file under `cfraser/cmf-ffm-cartera/`, then compares it with the latest Fintual-stated basket of the fortnight before the month-end: a ticker one side lacks, or a weight more than 3 points apart (`CMF_RN_CROSS_CHECK_WEIGHT_ALARM`), is a step error, the gap otherwise a note. A month the CMF has not published yet is a note («not published yet»), retried the next day. What it shows: +AVDV 08-04, +XLE 08-06, SPYM replaced VGK 08-17 (VGK back at 0,2 % on 08-20, 5 % from 09-29), −EPP 08-18, −KOMP 08-19, −ESGV 10-04 (its 10,6 % moved into SPYM).
- **USD/CLP floats on the forex calendar (2026-09-08; `forexDay.ts`).** Yahoo **`CLP=X`** is fetched by every live-quotes tick while the **fx day is open — a weekday before 17:05 New York**, US and Chilean holidays included — and every CLP↔USD reader for today goes through `fxForLiveMtm`, so USD holdings move with the rate from Chile midnight to the New York close. At the day end the first closed tick writes the day's close into **`fx_daily`** from the same quote the readers froze on (`catchUpFxDailyIfMissingDueSession` → `syncYahooFxUsdFromYahoo`; the `yahoo_fx_usd` source is the nightly backstop, due at the same instant with carry-over), and **rows are write-once** (`insertFxRowsIfMissing`): Yahoo's daily bars — whose close is the London-midnight open, not the day's last print, and which disagree with its intraday feed by up to ~0,3% — only fill days never observed and never revise a row, so yesterday's P/L does not move overnight. **Weekends read Friday's close**: no Saturday/Sunday rows (Yahoo labels its Monday week-open bar by the New York date of the bar start, i.e. Sunday — the ingest skips weekend dates and migration 180 deleted the 607 Sunday rows since 2015, which had put weekend news moves into Sunday-dated month-ends). The old frame — live only inside the NYSE regular session, EOD at 17:30 Chile on NYSE trade dates — pinned the peso for whole US holidays (2026-09-07: 933,68 all day against a 931–935 market). Banco Central dólar observado is stored separately in **`fx_daily_bcentral`** (reference only; synced by `sbif_usd` from 18:00 Chile). Backfill (gap-filler, existing rows kept): `npm run backfill:yahoo-fx-usd -w nw-tracker-server`; `npm run backfill:sbif-fx-eur -w nw-tracker-server` (BCentral reference + EUR).
- **Every core pair has a live rate (2026-11-12; migration 323, `fxLive.ts`, docs/any-currency-plan.md C6).** A live pair is USD/CLP plus every core currency `currencies` loads from a Yahoo pair (`yahoo_pair_symbol`: EUR/USD from `EURUSD=X`), listed by `liveFxPairs()` and named by its pair key `<BASE>_<QUOTE>` (`USD_CLP`, `EUR_USD`): its `live_market_quotes` symbol (kind `fx`, the old `fx_clp_per_usd` renamed by migration 323, value = quote units per base unit), its dependency token (`noteLiveFxRead(key)`) and its `X-Live-Inputs` token (`$fx` for USD/CLP, unchanged; `$fx:EUR_USD`). **One session for every pair: the forex day of `forexDay.ts`** (a weekday, Chile calendar, before 17:05 New York) — EUR/USD trades round the clock on weekdays, but the day the app values is the one the dollar's fx day frames, so a euro and a dollar figure built at the same instant read rates of the same day. The poll fetches each pair while it is open (`fetchYahooLivePairRate`; skipped when the stream covers it, `skipLiveFxPairs`), the stream subscribes to each pair's Yahoo symbol under the same gate and coverage / re-stamp rules (`liveStreamCoverage().fxPairs`), and the first closed tick writes a pair's day close into `fx_rates` from the last live rate of that session (`writeLivePairDayCloseIfMissing`, source `live:EURUSD=X`, write-once like `fx_daily`; dated the Chile day, which is the London day at 17:05 New York, so the London-midnight Yahoo bar of `fx_pairs` finds the day stored and keeps it; a session with no live row is left to that bar). A day close drops everything a tick can reach, as the `fx_daily` close does. `rates.ts` reads a pair's live rate for today exactly as USD/CLP's (`livePairRateForDay`, fresh row, session on or before the date, else the stored close); a FLOW of today converts at the stored rate (`convertFlow` passes `storedOnly`), as pesos → dollars at the stored buy rate always did, so the daily series' past part (a history-tier value, which must read no live input) stays live-free in EUR. **EUR/CLP today** keeps the direct-pair rule but not its staleness: Banco Central's `eur_daily` carries no quote of today until it is published, so while EUR/USD has a quote dated today (live, or the written close) today's EUR/CLP is EUR/USD × USD/CLP of today (a mid; reported as both pairs); without one it reads `eur_daily` as every past day does. With no EUR/USD live row and no EUR/USD close of today, every payload is the one before.

## Page grid and tiles (2026-11-01; `docs/layout-grid-plan.md`, `styles/tokens.css`, `shell.css`, `page-grid.css`, `cards.css`)

- **The shell is two tracks; only the content carries the column grid.** `.layout--with-sidebar` is `var(--sidebar-w) minmax(0, 1fr)` (the sidebar a fixed 11.625rem — 3/4 of the old 15.5rem, sized to the widest nav label — sticky, outside the columns, with its own `--sidebar-bg` background (a shade above `--bg`) and no border; the user's call, 2026-11-01, over putting it on whole columns of a page-wide grid. **Its rows (2026-11-04; `AppSidebar.tsx`, `AppSidebar.module.css`):** text sits on one column per depth (`--sidebar-text-x` + depth × `--sidebar-indent`, 8px), whatever precedes it; every net-worth group with rows under it (the rows that show a chevron; a sub-bucket shown as a leaf, SOXX beside Acciones' stocks, has none) carries a 6px square in its card pill's colour (`markerRgb`, `mutedPillBackgroundRgb`) hung in the margin left of its text, and the expenses row («Gastos») the expenses favicon's red down-pointing triangle (`EXPENSES_FAVICON_SPEC` in `favicon.ts`, the one definition both read), drawn in the selected chart style (the box legend's hand-drawn square, `RoughSwatchBox`, and the triangle in the same hand, `RoughSwatchTriangleDown`, through `SketchMarker`, in every style but plain; imported directly, as `Pill` imports its `PillScribble` — a lazy load painted flat marks and bare pills on every page load until its chunk arrived); the open page's row has an inset rounded highlight and the groups above it read bright; a group opens and closes from a chevron hung in the same margin, which takes its square's place on hover or keyboard focus (a group without a square shows it while closed, and on touch screens always; on touch the square stays and is itself the button). No carets, rails, hyphens or accent bar. An account that holds nothing today (`accountChartInactive`) is not in the nav tree — dollar cash too since 2026-11-04 (Racional USD, Santander USD at $0); its old exemption dated from when inactive meant a long zero tail), capped at `--page-max` (105rem from 1440, left-aligned beyond it). Tokens on `:root`: `--content-cols` (4 on a phone, **12 from 880** on both desktop tiers, so a tile's span never changes between them), `--gutter` (1rem, 1.5rem from **1440**), `--row-gap` (= gutter), `--row-unit` (1.5rem, one UI line), `--margin-inline` (both sides of the content: 0.75rem on a phone, 1rem on the small desktop, 1.5rem on the big one), and the derived `--content-w` / `--col-w`, plus `--hairline`. Media queries cannot read custom properties, so the two tier lines are literals wherever a stylesheet needs them; 880 is also the tables' narrow line (`useIsNarrowViewport.ts`), so the app has one small-desktop line. `.layout-main` (track 2) reserves the ticker's strip at the top and keeps `overflow: auto`, so anything wider than the content scrolls inside the main column rather than the page; a table never should, it shows its cards instead (see «UI tables — desktop/mobile sync»). `.layout-main>.content` is the column grid; every page's `<main>` is one child spanning it, so a page that does not opt in stays block flow. The ticker (2026-11-03): on a phone a full-viewport bar and on the small desktop a bar across the content's width, both with the content reserving its strip (`.layout-main` padding-top); on the big desktop it covers the content's last 8 columns (columns 5–12) and the content starts at the top. The prefs dock's max-width derives from the tokens; the 1200px content cap is gone.
- **Whatever a route renders is on the grid by itself (2026-11-02):** `page-grid.css` makes the content region's direct child — a page's `<main>`, or a layout's — a subgrid of the content columns (`row-gap: var(--row-gap)`), so a page root declares nothing. A container INSIDE a page that holds tiles of its own says so with `.page-grid` (the same subgrid): a Loadable shell (`GroupInfoBase`, `AccountDetailSharedLayout`, the tax return), a page rendered inside the flows or panel layout that has its own root (groceries), a tile grouping sub-tiles. Each section is a **`PageGridTile`** (`components/layout/PageGridTile.tsx`: `<section data-tile=… class="page-tile">`) with its heading INSIDE it, so the row gap is the only space between sections — no outside margins (`.page-tile>:is(h2,h3,h4,.chart-panel-title-row):first-child { margin-top: 0 }`, `.chart-panel-title-row>:is(h2,h3,h4) { margin: 0 }` replaced the inline `marginTop` / `margin: 0` / `fontSize` the sections carried). A `.chart-grid` is a subgrid too where it is a page-grid child (two panels on six columns each from 880; `--full-line` all of them) and a plain 12-column grid with the same gutter elsewhere. Chart titles and surface controls keep `.chart-panel-title-row`; the controls' inline styles moved to `styles/controls.css`. **The account pages are on the grid too (2026-11-01; `AccountDetailSharedLayout`):** the content shell is a page grid, the strip a `page-grid` tile, and every section a tile — position, charts (`chart-grid` tiles), returns, detalle, the cartola months, each credit-card table, the mortgage form / sheet / scenarios / prepayments, the capability forms and the flows; a section made of several tiles (the card's cuotas block, the Pasivos credit-card and mortgage sections, the depto ledger block) is a `page-grid` container holding them with one dim marker, so the same components sit on the Pasivos group pages inside `GroupInfoBase`. The section spacing classes of `AccountDetailPage.module.css` (`.sectionTitle*`, `.subsectionTitle*`, `.chartBlock*`, `.positionBlock`, `.marginTopBase/.marginBottomBase`, `.tableWrapSpaced`) are gone.
- **A card's title is a pill in its bucket's colour (2026-11-02; `DashboardCardTitleRow` `colorRgb`):** the nav node's own `color_rgb` (the colour its chart lines use, set from the panel), drawn with the shared `Pill` on a muted version of it (`mutedPillBackgroundRgb`: lightness ×0.9, chroma ×0.8 in OKLCH); small and not uppercase, the text the pill's own colour with only its lightness moved (OKLCH: dark on a light pill, light on a dark one, `tintedTextOnRgb`). A card with no colour keeps plain text.
- **Card numbers flash on a change (2026-11-02, trial; `numberChangeEffect.ts`, `AnimatedNumberFlow` `changeEffect`):** when a strip card's balance or breakdown line changes in the same display unit after a settled render, the digits switch at once and the cell flashes green or red by direction (`flashOnChange`). The four P/L figures do not flash (2026-11-03), nor do the summary cards' balances — the page's own total in row 1 (Net worth, Inversiones) and an account page's hero (`CompactEntityCard`, 2026-11-04): they keep the rolling digits. Instant on, a ~200 ms hold, a 2 s fade; a new change restarts it. A currency switch, or a dimmed placeholder giving way to live figures, keeps the rolling digits (the breakdown and deposits have none). `NUMBER_CHANGE_FLASH_ENABLED = false` brings the rolling digits back everywhere.
- **Cards have no chrome and touch** (decided 2026-11-01: no padding, background, border or radius, no inset yet — the left-edge alignment of card text with the container edge is the next step; `--surface-emphasis` is gone, `--card-radius` only skins popovers and the modal). A strip is `.cards-strip` (`PortfolioEntityCardsStrip`: one `.cards.cards--summary` block over one `.cards.cards--detail` block; the accounts table a sibling `.cards-strip__table`), a subgrid of the content columns; each `.cards` block is **four equal columns of its own** across the strip with no gap (2026-11-02: as a subgrid of the page columns the gutters were split across the cards, so the outer ones came out ~12 px narrower and the two rows' card edges disagreed); a row the cards do not fill closes with a line drawn just outside its last card (`box-shadow`), on the pixel the next row's card draws its left border on, and the dividers are per-card borders stripped by a **fixed 4-up count** (`nth-child(4n+1)` no left border, `nth-child(n+5)` a top border; never auto-fill), the one line between the blocks the detail block's border-top. The summary row is the same 4-up block with two cells empty (quarter-width Net worth / Inversiones). Below 880 cards are 1-up, 2-up between 600 and 879. A bare `.cards` outside a strip (the flows periods, the mortgage / credit-card summary rows) is a plain 4-column grid with the same dividers. `.chart-box`, `.chart-box--pending` and `.table-wrap` lost their chrome too (the pending chart frame is the title row plus the reserved height).
- **The dashboard's big-desktop rail (≥1440):** `GroupInfoBase` takes `rail` (and `tileOrder`); with it the strip and `charts` (the net-worth chart) go into `.page-main` (columns 1–8) and the rail tiles into `.page-rail` (9–12, pulled back one gutter so the two stacks touch), two independent column stacks side by side (never shared explicit rows: a tall rail table would open whitespace inside the main stack); below that band (2026-11-02) the rail does not continue: the tiles take their own order and span from the layout table (`PageGridTile` `wide` → `--tile-order-l` / `--tile-span-l`, `dashboardTileWide`): flows chart | Δ by class, returns across, allocation | the detalle table, then the wide tables, then the export row and the accounts tree last, full width on every tier (2026-11-02; the export row always sits right above the tree). Below 1440 the two containers are `display: contents` and the tiles are page-grid items ordered by `--tile-order` = `DASHBOARD_LAYOUT[id].s.y` (`client/src/dashboardLayout.ts`, the one table of tile rectangles per tier in react-grid-layout's own shape — `GRID` is its `Responsive` props; `dashboardLayout.test.ts` asserts the reading order, the spans and the big-desktop placement). In the rail a `.cards` block is 2-up (a `.cards--list` block — the «Próximos pagos» panel — stays one card per row there and at 600–879; a `.cards--pair` block — the «Flujos» periods — stays 2-up below 600 too, 2026-11-02) and a parallel table shows its mobile rendering by the rail's width (`@container rail (max-width: 40rem)`). The home «Flujos» section is two tiles: `DashboardFlowsPeriods` (the two period cards, rail) and `DashboardFlowsChart` (main), both on the same react-query entry. On the small desktop (880–1439) the periods and «Próximos pagos» share a row, half the columns each (`SMALL_PAIRS` in `dashboardLayout.ts`, `PageGridTile` `smallSpan`; the periods' cards 2-up), and below the big desktop the tiles take their reading-order index (`dashboardTileOrder`), not a `y`. Group pages have no rail (one stack, same tiles). **The detalle tables are row lists, one rendering at every width (2026-11-02; `PerfDetailRows`):** monthly / yearly (`MonthlyPerfDetailTable`) and daily (`DailyPerfDetailTable`) rows read by symbols, not column labels (each figure names itself on hover): flows by an icon each in the text colour — expenses a shopping bag, income two stacked banknotes, contributions a coin stack with a coin leaning on it (the piggy bank is savings, on the home «Flujos» panel) — with the signed amount (negatives in parentheses) giving the direction; the net-worth rows' income and expenses are `income` / `expenses` on `GET /api/groups/net_worth/consolidated-monthly?installment_mode=`, from the flows overview's months; an account's units keep a → / ← arrow, the result as ▲ / ▼ with its % in the sign's colour, YTD and Σ quieter. While every row fits on one line the rows are subgrids of the list, one column per figure; below the list's breakpoint (2026-11-04: a container query on the list's frame, `perf-rows-wide` 58rem for three flow slots, `perf-rows-narrow` 46rem otherwise — never a measurement: measuring the rendered rows after every render switched them to one line and back on each refetch, which made Firefox flash the page scrollbar) a row is three lines: the period; the closing with the result beside it, YTD on the right; the flows (expenses, income, contribution), Σ on the right — on a phone YTD and Σ share a fourth line. The result's % is in parentheses. No desktop table beside mobile cards.

## Per-surface display controls (2026-07-26)

- **Período/Rango are per-surface, not global.** Every chart owns a compact Período (D/M/Y) + Rango control and every perf/flows period table a Período-only control (tables always cover FULL history and paginate at every grain). State: `client/src/surfaceDisplayPrefs.ts` — `useSurfacePrefs(surfaceId, defaultPeriod, defaultRange)` persists partial `{period, range}` JSON under `nw-tracker.surface.<pageKey>.<surfaceKey>` (missing field = caller default; per page instance; deliberately no cross-tab sync). UI: `components/ui/SurfaceControls.tsx` in `ChartPanelTitleRow`/heading rows. The global toolbar (`AppDisplayPreferencesBar`) carries **CLP/USD only**; the old `nw-tracker.metricsPeriod`/`.timeRange` keys are cleared at boot. Older bullets below that say "toolbar Período/Rango" describe this per-surface pair now.
- **Defaults**: home value charts + group/account/Pasivos valuation = Diario+3y; every P/L combo, flows chart and the CC charts = Mensual+3y (**the CC ledger surfaces are independent controls since 2026-09-22** — `CreditCardLedgerSurfaces.tsx`, one implementation for the card page (`cc.<id>.*`) and the Pasivos / credit-card group section (`liab.cc.<slug>.*`): `.historial` is D/M/Y + Rango and the `.detalle` table M/Y period only (the `.financing` «Coste financiero vs facturado» chart was dropped 2026-09-26, see «Historial bars»); they replaced the shared `cc.<id>.charts` pair and the group section's single M/Y section-heading control, and the group pages now get Diario through the daily-series CC block summed over the merged ledger's masters, see «CC per-day owed + daily extras»); tables = Mensual (+ full history); proportional charts = Mensual+Todo (the Pasivos root's coverage chart, which takes that slot, Mensual+3y); rates = range-only 1y. `(Mensual, Todo)` on any surface reproduces the pre-refactor view. **A Rango of N days is N days ending today, today included, on both sides (2026-10-28):** `timeRangeCutoffYmd` = today − (N − 1), the server's day windows (the daily series' points, the flows overview's day rows); it used to be today − N, so a client-clipped Diario chart (Depósitos, Ingresos, Tasas) spanned one day more than a server-windowed one. Every client clip goes through `clipPointsToTimeRange` / `clipRowsToTimeRange` (no inline `>= cutoff` filters).
- **Axes (2026-09-16).** Every value chart's Y axis starts at 0 for non-negative data and spans 0 for mixed-sign data — one helper, `buildNiceYAxis` (`client/src/components/charts/chartLayout.ts`); the valuation line charts used to float on a padded band around the data (`LineChartPanel` read the Patrimonio chart from $50M). Two deliberate exceptions: the Rates page keeps `buildNiceYAxisPositiveBand` (a quote series is about the move — a 0-anchored UF axis is a flat line) and the wealth-percentile top-% axis is a reversed log scale that cannot contain 0. X ticks are **phased on January**: month strides are divisors of 12 (1/2/3/4/6 — 9 and 18 were dropped) walked from the first month index that is a multiple of the stride, so a 4-month stride reads ene/may/sep whatever month the series starts in (a walk from the first data point read abr/ago/dic on a series starting in April); the day-grid helper phases its month-first ticks the same way instead of thinning from the newest month, and multi-year strides land on round years (even years for 2, 2015/2020/2025 for 5). Only the first-data-point push (year branch) and the appended last point may sit off-phase. Known residue: on a daily grid whose first month is partial, a tick that phases onto that month sits on the first grid day, not the 1st (`computeRegularMonthXAxisTicks` cannot tell a daily grid from a month-end series with a live last point).
- **Cards show all three periods at once** (no card period anywhere): metrics grid = Mes and Año deposits + P/L rows, a divider, then the dimmed Total row (lifetime deposits + P/L); the Día P/L sits on the balance row beside the main value, with no day deposits figure (`DashboardCardGroupMetrics.tsx`); then breakdown; the title balance-Δ chip and the server `title_delta` block were removed (nav-snapshot cache v8). Chart-inactive visibility = any-period activity.
- **Pies were replaced by proportional composition charts** (100%-stacked areas at every grain, own controls): server-built share series in `server/src/proportionalSeries.ts` — per date base = Σ max(value, 0) over member lines (negatives floor to 0, base 0 → null; Σ shares == 1) — emitted pre-clip as `group_allocation_proportional` + `nav_grouped_proportional`/`liab_grouped_proportional` (group payloads), `allocation_proportional` (home bundle + overview-daily) and `proportional`/`grouped_proportional` (`/api/daily-series`). Client renders via `ProportionalAreaChart` (shares are unit-invariant — keep-prev passes them through unscaled). **One level only (2026-10-08):** the home chart stacks one level below the «big» buckets of the net_worth nav tree (`compositionBigBuckets`, `dashboardComposition.ts`: net_worth's children, where a `nav_bucket` without a `dashboard_bucket_slug` — Inversiones — is replaced by its own children; liability and reference groups skipped). Its lines are each big bucket's first-level children — a child group is one line (Σ of its `listAccountsForGroupTab` accounts), a direct account one line (name, account colour) — in nav order, so lines are grouped by big bucket; label, i18n key and `color_rgb` come from the nav node and the client colours by the payload colour. Values are the dashboard's marks: monthly `slugMarkTotalsAtDatesClp` at the chart dates only (reusing the primary-lines totals for the same group), daily `accountMarkClpSeriesOnGrid` — so they agree at month-ends — and shares are unit-invariant, so both are computed in CLP. The linked credit-card netting (`cashNetOfLinkedCreditCards`) is subtracted from the savings line (`isCashSavingsNavNode`, same as the value map); the builder throws unless exactly one line carries it, if an account sits in two lines, or if a bucket account is in none. a group page's chart is always its direct children (the grouped bucket block, `nav_grouped_proportional` is that single block now) whatever the Agrupado toggle says, a leaf page its accounts. There is no `ungrouped` proportional block any more. Pasivos is unchanged.
- **Value map («Mapa», 2026-10-08; `navValueMap.ts`).** A finviz-style treemap in the composition chart's slot on the dashboard and on every net_worth group page with something to tile — sub-groups or accounts of its own (2026-11-05: leaf pages too, a leaf page's tiles being its accounts, even a single one; Pasivos keeps its chart); a «Composición / Mapa» select in the panel title row (stored per surface as `view` beside `<pageKey>.proportional`; the map's own Día/Mes/Año return window is `<pageKey>.map`, default Día; Rango does not apply). The server emits ONE tree, `value_map` (+ `value_map_color_bounds`), beside `card_metrics_by_slug` in the page bundle, nav-context and nav-snapshot payloads; the client picks the page's subtree by slug (`valueMapRootForPage`) and the nav-snapshot cache is v10. Nesting is the net_worth nav tree: a group with GROUP children is a frame (header strip with name + %, when there is room), a group without is the smallest tile, which carries its accounts (`leaf_accounts`): a leaf group opens into them at any depth when it holds two or more (Crypto shows Bitcoin and Ether on every page), a single account stays the group's own tile (apv-a) except on the page's own first level, which always opens (the dashboard shows its property account inside Inmuebles) — the page root opens into its own accounts even with one, the client decides by depth from the page root, a mixed group (Acciones: stocks beside Portafolio IPSA / SOXX) shows its own accounts as tiles beside the sub-group tiles, and those sub-groups are leaves exactly as the sidebar shows them (`mapNode` / `asLeaf` in `sidebarNavFromApi.ts`): one block, no `leaf_accounts`. Liability and reference groups are not in the map. **Additivity:** tile values are Σ of account rows' current values, each account claimed by one tile (a duplicate throws), so frames add up; the one non-row figure is the linked credit-card total, which the cash card nets (`cashNetOfLinkedCreditCards`) and whose footer sits on the savings card, so the SAVINGS tile carries it (checking is a sibling leaf group and stays raw) and the cash frame equals the cash card value. Every node carries its TRUE value, negatives included (a frame = Σ of all its children); a node worth ≤ 0 is then hidden (left out of the payload) but still counts in every ancestor — so when the card is bigger than savings + checking the whole cash bucket is hidden, even though checking alone is positive, and the root stays net worth. The client sizes a frame's area by its visible children; tooltips show the true value. The map shows whenever it is the selected view: during a unit switch or a refetch it keeps drawing the last data it had (old unit; `useHeldValueMap`), never swapping back to the composition chart. Size = value in the display unit; colour = the node's flow-adjusted % for the period (`row_pct` rule over the node's rows, a single row for an account tile; an empty member with no delta no longer blanks a group's %) against FIXED per-period bounds the server emits (day ±3%, month ±8%, year ±25%; null % = neutral). The layout is our own squarified treemap (`treemapLayout.ts`) — Recharts' `Treemap` cannot reserve a frame header — drawn by `ValueTreemap.tsx` with CSS tokens `--treemap-*`. Every frame keeps its header: a frame squarified smaller than its minimum (header + a body, and for a frame holding frames, room for their minimum too) is grown at its siblings' expense, and each node's area is its value at one global px-per-value plus the area its headers and padding take (re-measured over a few passes), so a tile inside a frame has the same area per value as one outside. A tile's label is shown only when it fits whole (text measured on hidden spans): name over % from ~36 px tall, one row from ~22 px, else none; a header drops its % first, then shortens the name. The tooltip always shows value, % and P/L; a click goes to the node's route.
- **A group valued through one proxy basket maps as the basket's holdings (2026-10-21; `proxyBasketBreakdown.ts`, the `proxy` input of `navValueMap.ts`).** When every positive account a node claims sits on the same proxy bucket (`proxyBucketSlugForSeriesKey` in `riskyNorrisProxyMtm.ts` — today the two Fintual APV accounts → `fintual_risky_norris_proxy`), the node becomes a frame of `kind: "holding"` tiles, one per ETF the fund STATES in its latest `watchlist_composite_positions` row (Fintual's tickers: SPXS, not its SPY price proxy; `price_ticker` is the Yahoo one), sized by stated weight × the sleeve's share of assets, plus a neutral «Otros» tile (`valueMap.otherSleeve`) for the non-ETF sleeve (`1 − etf_weight_sum`); accounts on the same proxy merge into one breakdown, accounts on different proxies or without one keep their own tiles, and a click goes to the node's route. A holding's colour is its time-weighted return over the days the fund HELD it inside the card period (day / month / year, the same anchors as the cards): each positions date resets the weights, weights step between dates, disjoint holding windows chain and days out of the basket contribute nothing — an ETF held in January, dropped, and held again from September reads both windows (SPXS 119/294 days in 2026: the CMF-era baskets name no SPXS). The tooltip adds the holding's contribution to the fund (plain Σ weight × daily return, unlinked) and «held N/M days»; the CLP leg goes through the observado frame, like the proxy itself. Fail-fast: a positions row in force without `etf_weight_sum`, a missing close, a non-USD price ticker or a period starting before the first recorded basket throws (the 2026-09-21 snapshot-recovered row had none; set to 0,985 by hand from the adjacent syncs). Client: `holding` on the value-map DTO, tooltip rows, nav-snapshot cache v12.
- **Flows period tables no longer clip by range** (tables include all); the «en el rango» companion totals follow the CHART's Rango. Table Diario views fetch `days=0` (full-history daily; first build is the known one-time server cost).
- **Yearly charts start with a partial first year (2026-09-26).** Every M/Y chart applies Rango then Año through `clipMonthsThenRollup` (`client/src/timeRange.ts`): months are cut at the range start (month-end ≥ cutoff), then rolled up, so a mid-year Rango's first yearly point covers only that year's months inside the range — flows pages, proportional charts and every P/L combo alike — a yearly bar adds up to the monthly bars of the same window, and each «en el rango» total reads the same at M and Y. Running levels are sampled at year-end, never re-summed (`rollupChartPointsByYear` `levelKeys`; `rollupPerfPointsYearly` `accumKey`). Flows payloads (`/api/flows/deposits`, `/api/flows/pl`, `/api/flows/expenses/real-estate`, the dashboard's `inversiones_deposits_chart`) carry monthly blocks only; yearly rows roll up client-side (the P/L year table too). Tables stay full history.
- **The /flows P/L page is market P/L of the deposit accounts (2026-10-27; `FLOWS_PL_BUCKETS` in `flowsPl.ts`).** Four buckets: brokerage, retirement, cash (cash_eqs WITHOUT the accounts under Checking accounts — their P/L is the plan fee and the dollar account's fx, a gasto or cash) and real estate (the property's P/L with its mortgage interés + seguros added back — `flowsMortgageCarrying.ts` — since gastos counts them, so it reads the UF revaluation); the chart, the month/year table (desktop + mobile cards) and the per-account blocks (the property's row carries the carrying back too) all show them, Diario included (cash without checking has its own daily scope `flows.pl:cash_eqs`, the others share `pg:<slug>`). The flows overview's P/L is this builder's monthly / daily total. Before: cash_eqs whole, no real estate (last 12 months to 2026-09: 2x.xxx.xxx → 2x.xxx.xxx).
- **The flows overview is built on the server (2026-10-27; `flowsOverview.ts`, `GET /api/flows/overview?unit=clp|usd&installment_mode=split|total[&days=N]`).** Per calendar month (and day, for Diario): `income` (incl. the payroll deductions counted as gastos), `expenses` (gasto del mes of the mode; refunds negative), `savings` = income − expenses, `deposits` = the deposit events of every account NOT under Checking accounts (`accountIdsUnderCheckingAccounts`: corriente, vista, Santander USD, MACH — their own events are cash; money back into checking is negative; the linked mortgage deposit keeps its amortización), `deposits_pre_tax` (AFP/AFC cotizaciones), `card_spent` (the card lines' part of expenses, plus the amortización of a mortgage dividendo a card paid, on the depto payment day in both modes — a financing projection spreads only its gasto), `card_paid` (transfers checking → card master — `pago_tarjeta`, divisas at the pesos that left checking, the dollar account's at its dollars — less card → checking top-ups; card-to-card is not cash; `flowsOverviewCash.ts`), `cash_change` (the checking accounts' ledger change, Σ `accountMarkClpAtYmd` through the mark cache, today for the month in progress), `cash_fx` (2026-11-10: the fx result on what the checking accounts hold in another currency than the unit — Σ over the days of the holding at the end of the previous day × the day's change of its rate into the unit, the card's revaluation rule (`ccRevaluationCostByDate`) on the asset side, `checkingCashByDay` in `flowsOverviewCash.ts`: in pesos the dollar checking accounts at the sell rate, live today through `fxForLiveMtm`, in dollars the peso accounts at a peso's dollar value; one quantity and one rate per account and day serve both the value and the leg, so Δvalue = fx leg + the day's flows at the day's rate and the days of a month add up to the month; the checking accounts' own P/L, which `pl` leaves out by design), `cash_unexplained` = cash_change − (savings − deposits + card_spent − card_paid + cash_fx) — shown on /flows, never forced to zero (on the real DB 0 in 19 of the 24 months to 2026-09: what is left is a checking debit in no leg, e.g. a «deposits»-tagged debit whose deposit differs, an overdraft-line payment; a dollar flow's spread between the mid the legs count it at and the sell rate the dollars are held at; until 2026-11-10 the fx move on the dollars held in checking sat here too), `pl` (the /flows P/L page's own builder, `buildFlowsPlChartMonthly` / `buildFlowsPlDailyChartSeries` in `flowsPl.ts`, so the two read the same total every month — tested: brokerage, retirement, cash savings outside checking, real estate as market P/L — the checking accounts' own P/L is out, the property's mortgage carrying is added back), `investments_change` = deposits + deposits_pre_tax + pl (= Δ value of those accounts, checked to the peso) and `net_worth_change` = savings + deposits_pre_tax + pl + cash_fx. Cash leaves when a card is paid, not when it is used: the card line reads card_spent − card_paid. Full-history `totals`, `closed.{last_month,last_12_months}` — closed months only on the Chile clock: the month in progress reads as a deficit until its salary lands on the last business day — and `days` (Diario rows inside the `days` window) only when asked; totals and every rollup sum the base legs and re-derive the rest. The /flows overview (table + «Fx de caja» + «Sin explicar») and the home dashboard's flows card (`DashboardFlowsSummary`, surface `dashboard.flows`: Ingresos, Gastos | Ahorro, Depósitos, Tarjetas gastado − pagado, Variación de caja | P/L, Variación inversiones, Variación patrimonio — no «Fx de caja» line: its nine lines are pitched to the strip cards' divider on the big desktop, and the leg reaches the card through Variación patrimonio) only pick rows, clip them to their Rango and roll years up (`flowsOverviewRows.ts`). **The client holds no copy of the derived-field rule (2026-10-28):** each derived figure is linear in the legs, so a yearly row and the «en el rango» total are a field-by-field Σ of the server's rows; the income page reads its month rows, day points and total from `/api/income?unit=` `aggregates` (`buildFlowsIncomeAggregates`, the overview's own per-kind income buckets — the unread `monthly_totals` is gone), and the deposits page its day points (`chart_daily*`) and category subtotals from the server. The /flows/deposits page reads the same deposits: the checking accounts out and the mortgage carrying taken back out of the property's dividendo deposits as negative `mortgage_carrying` rows (`buildFlowsDepositsPayload({ excludeCheckingAccounts, withMortgageCarrying })`; the dashboard bundle's own call keeps every account and no carrying rows), so its total is the overview's deposits + deposits_pre_tax month by month (tested), shown split as post-tax / AFP+AFC (`post_tax_total_*`, `pre_tax_total_*`, `isPreTaxDepositRow`). The carrying is one list for the three readers (`flowsMortgageCarrying.ts`: from the line that is the payment, once per link — a financing projection is skipped — on the depto payment day in both installment modes). Cached as `flows.overview|<unit>|<mode>|<days>` over a shared `flows.overview.inputs|` (income, expense lines — `flows.overview.inputs|lines`, the carrying `…|carrying|<unit>` — deposits, card payments): a live-quote tick drops only the payloads, writes and historical market data drop both, and so does any API write once answered (category / income edits touch no other funnel; not the queue's and the run log's machine traffic — heartbeats, claims, run reports — which since 2026-11-07 drops nothing, `requestDropsResponseCaches` in `requestContext.ts`); the warmer builds the CLP and USD month payloads. **Income has a `rent` bucket (2026-11-10):** `buildFlowsIncomeAggregates` sums the income kind `rent` (a tracked place's rent) into its own bucket — `rent_clp` on the month rows, `rent` on the chart points — beside salary, severance, parent_gift and other; the income chart and month table draw it (`income.chart.rent`), the client only picks. Until then it was summed into «other».

## Loading states (2026-10-08; `components/ui/Loadable.tsx`, `components/charts/ChartEmptyState.tsx`)

- **No view is ever replaced by a loading screen.** Every page renders its real layout at once; while a query is pending each component renders its frame with the values it has — zero placeholders, held prior data, an empty table — and dims (`Loadable` / `loadableClass`: `.loadable.is-loading`, opacity .55, inert, `aria-busy`; nested markers never compound, and `.chart-panel-title-row` stays clickable inside a dimmed surface so a Período/Rango/view change is a new request, never blocked). The only loading text left is a button label while its mutation runs. **Eager pages, lazy charts, idle prefetch (2026-10-08; `App.tsx`, `lazyChunks.ts`, `components/charts/lazyCharts.tsx`):** the dashboard, group, Pasivos and account pages plus the flows and panel layouts ship in the entry chunk and render in the frame the app mounts, so a document load paints the page's own layout; the chart engine (recharts) is one lazy chunk reached only through `lazyCharts.tsx` (`chartsBundle.ts` re-exports the six chart components the eager pages use), whose Suspense fallback is the chart's own frame — title row with live controls plus the pending box — so a chart slot never shows text; the secondary pages stay lazy behind a `null` fallback (`v7_startTransition` keeps the previous page during an in-app navigation; the flows and panel layouts wrap their `Outlet` in their own `Suspense` so a direct load keeps the h1 and tabs), and `useIdleChunkPrefetch` imports every registered chunk — charts first — in an idle callback after the first paint, after which every navigation and chart is synchronous (`lazyComponent` renders a loaded module directly instead of suspending once). The entry chunk is ~825 KB raw (248 KB gzip) and carries no recharts (checked on the build). **A first-ever visit (no cached nav tree) is blank, not a message (2026-10-08):** the sidebar shows the Net worth link and the Panel footer; the dashboard and the group / Pasivos pages (`GroupInfoBase` `coldStripShape`) draw `PlaceholderCardsStrip` — nameless cards (blank title, value and metric rows at the real rows' height, same strip grid and card shells) in the shape `client/src/coldPageShape.ts` states (`DASHBOARD_COLD_STRIP` 2 summary + 4 bucket cards, `GROUP_COLD_STRIP` 1 summary card; a guess at what the tree will show, only the paint before it arrives, never routed through `requireNavCardMetrics`); a page title with no name yet (`PageTitleRow`) keeps a title's height, so the account placeholder is a blank title, not «Cuenta #id». Before this the flows, income, expenses, deposits, P/L, real-estate, rates, watchlist, projections, wealth, tax and panel pages returned a bare «Cargando…» paragraph until their payload arrived, the three card pages returned `null` without a cached shape, Flows P/L and Watchlist re-gated an already mounted page on a Diario or unit change, and the daily detalle tables and the CC historial replaced themselves with the text in day mode.
- **The home panels paint from the browser's last copy (2026-11-02; `queries/persistedPayloadCache.ts` since F8).** «Flujos» (its two closed periods, per unit and expense mode), «Próximos pagos» and «Últimos movimientos» (per unit) keep their last live payload in localStorage and show it, undimmed, until the server answers, like the strip's nav snapshot, the sidebar tree and a group page's shell. One key scheme for all of them, `nw:cache:<name>:v<version>[:<scope>…]`, the version being the payload's `schema_version` (`DTO_SCHEMA_VERSIONS` in contracts) or, for a shape the client builds itself, the client's own; older versions and the keys of the earlier conventions are swept at boot (`sweepPersistedPayloads`). A held payload of the other unit is never stored; an entry whose shape the client does not read (`isValid`) is ignored.
- **A background refetch never dims (2026-11-04; `queryShowsLoading` in `queries/pageShapeReady.ts`).** A query-backed view's `loading` is `isPending || isPlaceholderData` — no data yet (a disabled query included), or the previous page / filter held — never `isFetching`: the live-quotes stamp invalidates every query on each price move, and the flows tables (dashboard, group and account pages, the cartola month modal) and the dashboard's server-paginated detalle dimmed on every tick. `isLoading` is not the signal either: it is false while placeholder data is shown and for a disabled query. `npm run check:conventions` flags `isFetching` / `isRefetching` in `components/` and `pages/`. **A failed background refetch never hides data either (2026-11-04; `queryLoadError`, same file):** react-query keeps the last data and sets `error` when a refetch fails; a view shows its error instead of its content only when it has no data (`queryLoadError(q)` = `q.error` while `q.data` is undefined). A deploy restarting the server failed one refetch and replaced the home expenses panel with an error until the next one succeeded; the flows, expenses, deposits, rates, Pasivos, panel and tax pages, which swapped the whole page for the error, now keep their data too.
- **Every data component takes `loading?: boolean`** (charts, tables, sections, the CC surfaces, `PeriodReturnsWithBenchmark` with `data: … | null`) and dims its own root. A chart's empty branch is `ChartEmptyState`: while loading an empty `.chart-box--pending` at the chart's size (`boxStyle` for the fixed-height ones), otherwise the «no data» copy — loading and absence never share a message. Tables render their header with no rows while loading; the empty label is for a loaded, empty result.
- **A Diario chart never draws the monthly data (2026-11-04).** In day mode a chart shows its pending frame — the title row with its live controls over an empty box of the chart's size (`ChartEmptyState` with `loading`) — until its own daily payload for the current unit has arrived (query neither pending nor placeholder, `queryShowsLoading`); the page passes it no points (an empty block, `null`, `[]`), never `dailyBlock ?? monthlyBlock`. Monthly and yearly modes are unchanged. Applied to the dashboard overview, composition and Δ combo, the group and Pasivos valuation / composition / coverage / P/L charts, the Pasivos mortgage combos, the card historial and the account valuation and P/L combos. Until then each page drew the monthly block, dimmed, under a Diario control, so the chart read monthly figures as days while it waited. The home net-worth chart shows the same pending frame in Mensual and Anual until the real bundle is in (2026-11-12): the placeholder bundle's zero lines are not drawn there, since they only flashed before the lines' draw-in. A group's P/L combos stay mounted in day mode whatever their points say (`PortfolioGroupChartsSection`), so the controls can switch back.
- **Recharts is patched (2026-11-04; `patches/recharts+3.10.1.patch`, applied by the root `postinstall: patch-package`).** A Line's first draw-in never recorded that it reached the end: its final frame (elapsed 1) renders after `isAnimating` has turned false, so `useAnimatedLineLength` was never asked for it, and its 4-decimal rounding could leave even that frame short of `getTotalLength()`. The first data update then drew the last in-between length onward again — the right edge of every line redrawn on the first live tick.
