Sources: Yahoo's `events=split` on the live quote and the EOD fetches (`recordYahooSplits`), `POST /api/equity-splits` and `npm run equity:record-split -w nw-tracker-server -- --ticker= --ex-date= --ratio=3:1 [--apply]` (report-first). **Guard:** a stock price ×1.6 or ÷1.6 from its neighbouring stored close with no split recorded is refused — a live quote is not written (fetch error on the watchlist row, the readers keep the previous close), an EOD bar throws (`healIncomingEquityClose`, which also re-frames a close a source still sends as traded). Why: on 2026-11-05 Racional mailed the SOXX 3-for-1 at 02:35 Chile, Yahoo listed no split and kept its history unadjusted, and the first post-split price was valued against the old share count (−69%). Tax lots: a split multiplies every open lot's units, cost and purchase date unchanged; per-broker holdings scale each broker. **Racional (same date):** its split mail («SOXX tuvo una división de acciones», sent before the ex-date's open with the counts before and after) is a `broker.notifications` kind `stock_split` (`units`, `units_after`); the server (`brokerSplitNotifications.ts`) records the split on the mail's Chile day (source `broker`) when after ÷ before is a whole N:1 / 1:N and the ledger holds exactly the "before" count at Racional the day before — else the row is `conflict`, nothing is recorded and the import step exits 1. One split from several sources is one split: `recordEquitySplit` treats the ticker's split within 7 days as the same (it keeps its own ex-date; another ratio throws). The crawl's «Evento Corporativo» row (no instrument) is the holder's `stock_split` row within 3 days of its day when exactly one exists, else it blocks its read.

## Instrument registry and price providers (2026-11-10; migration 305, `marketSymbols.ts`, `priceProviders/`, plan `docs/demo-app-features-plan.md` §4)

- **An instrument's facts are registered, never read from its ticker.** `market_symbols` (the name kept; "the instrument registry" in the docs) holds per ticker `quote_currency` (any code of `currencies`, which the column references since migration 315 — `usd`, `clp`, `eur`, …; NULL for an index; `registerInstrument` refuses a code the table lacks), `market_kind` (`nyse` | `santiago` | `london` | `crypto24`: the calendar and sessions — NYSE, NYSE Arca, NYSE American, Cboe and Nasdaq are one market), `isin`, `display_symbol` (without a provider's exchange suffix: CSPX, CFIETFIPSA, SPY — the leaf name, the Racional match, the Bolsa nemo), `exchange` (the provider's own name, provenance), `name`, `instrument_type`, `source` (`yahoo` | `coingecko` | `bolsa_santiago` | `declared`) and `verified_at`. `requireMarketSymbol(ticker)` returns the row or throws («… is not registered in market_symbols; run `npm run register:market-symbols -w nw-tracker-server`», after one reload of the process cache, so a row another process wrote is found); `equityMarketKind` / `marketQuoteCurrency` are that row's fields. The ticker stays the app's instrument key, spelled as Yahoo's symbol in every table. Migration 305's hook filled `display_symbol` of the rows written before it through the Yahoo adapter's pure rule, and declared SPY (the seeded benchmark). **No exchange code (2026-11-11; migration 306 dropped `mic`):** nothing priced, scheduled or valued by it — `market_kind` is the fact — and holding the demo's declarations to Yahoo's label for the US venue failed the nightly demo-market publish (Yahoo says NYSE for SGOV, listed on Arca).
- **`registerInstrument(ticker, facts, source)`** writes or updates a row (stamped now) and refreshes the cache; it **throws when `equity_daily` holds bars for the ticker in another currency, or when a re-registration changes the market kind or currency of a ticker with stored bars** — a stored series is never reframed.
- **Price providers are adapters behind one interface** (`priceProviders/types.ts` `PriceProvider`: `describeSymbol`, optional `search`, `dailyCloses`, `liveQuote`). `yahoo.ts` is the only code that reads a Yahoo symbol: the `exchangeTimezoneName → market_kind` table, the USD / CLP rule with the pence refusal, `yahooDisplaySymbol` (strips `.SN`, `.L`, `.NYB` and the other known suffixes; keeps `BTC-USD`, `^GSPC`, `BRK.B` whole) and `checkYahooSeriesCurrency`. `coingecko.ts` and `bolsaSantiago.ts` declare the existing fetchers (`equityCoinGeckoEod.ts`, `equityBolsaSantiagoEod.ts`; daily closes only — CoinGecko describes the coins it knows, the Bolsa describes nothing). `priceProviders/index.ts`: `describeSymbol(symbol, provider = "yahoo")`, `priceProvider(id)`, `registerInstrumentFrom`, `ensureRegisteredInstrument(ticker, provider)` (the row, or ask and register).
- **The doors that register:** the panel's account create (`registerPanelAccountInstrument` before `createPanelAccount`: a stock from Yahoo, a coin from CoinGecko, an option contract's underlying from Yahoo; `createPanelAccount` itself refuses an unregistered ticker, so the broker-mail importers mark such a buy `requires_manual` naming the script), the watchlist add (`addManualWatchlistTicker(ticker, facts, source)`), the Risky Norris composition sync (each `price_ticker` before its history is fetched), migration 305 for the SPY benchmark, and the demo generator (`declareDemoInstruments`: `DEMO_TICKERS` carries each instrument's declared market, currency and display symbol, written `source: "declared"` before any account; the synthetic path never asks a provider; `loadDemoRealMarket` declares the same facts and holds each to Yahoo's `describeSymbol` — market, currency and display symbol — failing the load on a mismatch; `market_symbols` is a side-effect table of the market asset, re-declared by the generator rather than carried). The EOD and live syncs read their ticker lists from accounts, the watchlist, the benchmarks and the proxy basket and throw on an unregistered one — which the doors above make impossible for new tickers; older rows are the backfill script's (`register:market-symbols`). **`npm run register:market-symbols -w nw-tracker-server -- [--only=<tickers>] [--apply]`** (`scripts/register-market-symbols.ts`, plan in `instrumentRegistrationPlan.ts`) is report-first: it lists the tickers in use with no row and the provider-stated rows whose market, currency or display symbol changed (CoinGecko for the coins it knows, else Yahoo; `declared` rows are never re-verified), writes all or nothing through `registerInstrument`, and fails naming every ticker a provider cannot classify or `registerInstrument` refuses. **`scripts/server-deploy.sh` runs it with `--apply` after the client build and before the server restart** (a failure fails the deploy into its rollback). Warning: importing `db.ts` applies pending migrations to the database it opens, so the script migrates it (intended, it needs 305; rehearse on a copy through `NW_TRACKER_TEST_DB`). Vitest fixtures register theirs (`src/test/instrumentFixtures.ts`: `declareTestInstrument`, `VITEST_INSTRUMENTS` registered once per file by `vitest.setup.ts`).

## Options (contracts as accounts)

- **A listed option series is its own account, valued at intrinsic value (2026-11-10; migration 304, `optionContracts.ts`, `optionPosition.ts`, `optionFlowKinds.ts`, `optionAssignment.ts`; docs/demo-app-features-plan.md §3, wave O1).** `option_contracts` (account → underlying ticker, right C | P, strike, expiry, multiplier 100, OCC symbol) holds the terms the account is valued by; `accounts.equity_ticker` stays NULL on a contract (the EOD sync, the watchlist and `accountsWithEquityTicker` never see it), the profile decides `QuantitySource {kind: "option_contracts"}` from the row BEFORE the share-units branch, and the holding is `{kind: "option", occSymbol, underlying, right, strike, expiry, multiplier, quote: "usd"}` (a non-dollar underlying throws). Contracts are SIGNED on the ledger — a short call is −n — and never hidden: `unitsDeltaForAccountMovement` has a rule per kind (`option_sell_open` / `option_sell_close` debit −|n| on the contract from-leg, `option_buy_open` / `option_buy_close` credit +|n| on the to-leg, the single-leg `option_expire` / `option_assign` carry −(contracts held) as stored), `optionContractsThroughDate` sums them, and `qtyMarkNativeValue` multiplies by the multiplier, so a short position's value is NEGATIVE (a liability inside an asset-side account; `positionMarkFromMeta` takes it as the mark, `units_kind: "contracts"`). **The price is intrinsic value from the underlying's close** (`holdingPriceAt`'s option branch: the as-traded close of a past day, today's price today; `max(0, S − K)` for a call, `max(0, K − S)` for a put while unexpired, 0 on and after the expiry) — no history source exists for a contract, so time value is not priced (stated on the account page); a missing underlying close THROWS, and no ladder (`accountMarkClpAtYmd`, `accountTodayValue`, `accountValue`) ever falls back to a book mark. A closed, expired or assigned contract holds 0 and is chart-inactive like an emptied stock. `option_contracts` is a mark input (a change re-values the account's whole history, `OPTION_CONTRACTS_SPEC`); `option_assignments` links the two rows of an assignment.
- **Flow kinds** (`OPTION_UNITS_FLOW_KINDS`, in `BROKERAGE_FLOW_KINDS`, `BROKERAGE_NON_CASH_FLOW_KINDS`, the reconciliation's non-capital list, `intraDayFlowRank`, the client's `brokerageFlowKinds.ts`): `option_sell_open` (contract → USD cash, the premium received, −n), `option_buy_open` (USD cash → contract, the premium paid, +n), `option_buy_close` (closes a short), `option_sell_close` (closes a long), `option_expire` (single-leg on the contract, amount 0, units −held, like `stock_split`) and `option_assign` (single-leg, −held) + a `stock_sell` at the strike from the underlying's account (a `stock_buy` for an assigned put; an exercised long the other way round), linked in `option_assignments` and written together by `assignOptionContract` (`POST /api/accounts/:id/option-assignments`; a bare `option_assign` row is refused). `validateBrokerageTransferEndpoints` settles every premium in USD cash against a contract account, whole contracts, and a direction that fits the position held that day (an «open» never closes the other side, a «close» never past zero, nothing after the expiry); `option_expire` must take the position to exactly zero on or after the expiry. A stock account's form keeps `STOCK_ACCOUNT_FLOW_KINDS`; a contract's offers `OPTION_CONTRACT_FLOW_KINDS` (units required, `unit_label` «contratos»).
- **A premium is capital, signed like a dividend or a buy (`equityBrokerageCapitalFlows.ts`):** received = capital OUT of the contract (the `dividend_payout` mirror: − on the contract, + on cash), paid = capital INTO it (the `stock_buy` mirror), each at the day's USD reference rate, and the USD-cash loader mirrors the leg negated so the two cancel inside the bucket; an expiry or an assignment moves no money. The contract's value against its aportes is therefore the position's P/L — a sold call reads P/L = premium − intrinsic day by day, the premium becomes P/L at expiry with no further event, and an assignment's three legs reconcile (contracts 0, shares −100 n, cash +strike × 100 n; the bucket's P/L on that day is 0). Proven on a synthetic fixture (`optionContracts.test.ts`): 2 calls sold for US$600 → contract P/L 600 OTM, 0 with intrinsic 600, 600 at expiry; the assigned pair → contract +400, stock −600, cash 0, bucket +400, the bucket's flows the outside US$10.000 only. **The generic «any other dollar move is capital» branch of `loadForeignCashCapitalSortFlows` now THROWS on a kind it does not list** (`USD_CASH_GENERIC_CAPITAL_FLOW_KINDS`: a kind-less transfer, the cash deposits and withdrawals, the conversions, `other`, `pago_tarjeta`, `carga_tarjeta` — every kind the generated databases carry; a new instrument's leg must get its own rule, never read as a deposit).
- **Creating a contract:** `POST /api/accounts` with `account_type: "option"` and `option: {underlying, right, strike, expiry, multiplier?}` (`createPanelAccount`): the underlying must quote in USD, the OCC symbol is derived («SPY   250620C00600000», `occSymbol`), the name defaults to «SPY 2025-06-20 C 600», the leaf is `brokerage_opciones__<occ slug>` under the `brokerage_opciones` asset group (migration 304) and nav bucket (kind_slug `options`, `/inversiones/brokerage/opciones`, seeded and linked by `seedNavTree` like `brokerage_acciones`; empty, it is not in the sidebar), `import_key` `import:panel|option=<occ>|key=<slug>`; a contract account never moves between buckets. **Tax-lot events exist (2026-11-10, wave T0b; `taxLots.ts`, `optionTaxLotEvents.ts`):** the lot engine has short lots (`open_short` / `close_short`, their own pool, FIFO/LIFO like longs; `realizeTaxLots` also returns `shortCloses` and `openShortLots`; a `dispose` with no long lot still throws, a short is declared, never inferred; `taxLotTerm`: a short result is always short-term, §1234(b), a long one long-term after more than a year). `optionTaxLotEventsFromRows` / `loadOptionTaxLotEvents` map a contract's premiums (sell-open → open_short, buy-close → close_short, expiry → close_short / dispose at 0, buy-open → acquire, sell-close → dispose) and `optionLotResults` gives per-slice results with opened/closed dates and term. An assignment or exercise closes the contract with no gain of its own and its premium adjusts the linked share trade (Pub. 550 «Puts and Calls»: short call + premium to proceeds, short put − premium from cost, long call + premium to cost, long put − premium from proceeds): `optionAssignmentAdjustments(stockAccountId)` gives it by movement id and `equityTaxLotEventsFromRows` / `loadEquityTaxLotEvents` take it as an optional input (default none = unchanged). Still open: the Schedule D / F1040 wiring that reads these results and passes the adjustments (no existing report calls them yet), the demo's option events (wave O2), and a live chain price for today. **Their tax (2026-11-10):** the lots (`optionTaxLotEvents.ts`) reach the 1040's Form 8949 / Schedule D through `us1040/wired.ts` (an assignment's premium moves into the share trade's basis or proceeds, the 1040's equity walk only); on the F22 an option movement inside a Chilean-resident window throws (Ley 20.544 not modelled, `assertNoOptionsInResidentWindow` in `f22/blocks.ts`) and inside a non-resident one is an `excluded` item (`options`, reason `nonresident`: count and net premium, `taxFacts/options.ts`).

- **Demo: the options wheel (2026-11-10; `demoData/optionsWheel.ts`, `simulate.ts` `runWheel`, `generate.ts` `createOptionContractAccounts`, `invariants.ts` `checkOptionsWheel`; wave O2's story).** At IBKR, from 2025-01 every third month (the 15th or the next NYSE day), as a US resident: a cash-secured put ~5 % below the close, or a covered call ~5 % above while he holds 100 assigned shares at IBKR, on the monthly (third-Friday) expiry nearest 45 days out (the Thursday when that Friday is closed); premium = a fixed 0,04 % of the strike per day to expiry, whole contracts of 100 — story assumptions, no pricing model. The underlying is chosen on the first sale day that can be secured: the largest NYSE dollar ticker of the broker list (SGOV excluded) whose 100 shares cost no more than the collateral he actually has there (cash + the SGOV reserve); on the synthetic market that is OILK (the reserve was ~US$7.100 in 2025-01, NVDA/AMD/ZM did not fit), so the stated rule decides, never a hardcoded ticker. A put he cannot secure that quarter is not written. The outcome is the underlying's close on the expiry day (put assigned below the strike, call above, nothing at it) — never forced either way (the synthetic market: 7 expiries and one series open today, no assignment; with the strike at the money the same code assigns a put, then the covered call, which is how the pipeline was exercised). **Written through the real model:** the simulator emits the series (`Ledger.optionContracts`), `option_sell_open` transfers series → IBKR USD (units 1), `option_expire` single-leg rows, and a `DirectMovement` `option_assign` that `writeDirect` hands to `assignOptionContract` in date order (SGOV is sold first when the cash is short); the series accounts are created per pass by `createPanelAccount` after `simulate` (the pass's database reset drops them). Assigned shares are IBKR's holding of the ticker's ONE stock account (`heldUnits` is keyed `ticker|cash`), beside any Chilean-broker position. Invariants: 0 contracts from each expiry, −1 from the sale day, premiums and assignment legs equal the story's to the cent, one 100-share link per assignment, IBKR dollar cash never negative.

## Quote currency / Santiago (`.SN`) tickers

- **`equity_daily` stores `close` + `currency` ('usd' | 'clp')** (migration 152); `live_market_quotes` rows are `kind='equity'` + `currency`. Writers stamp `equityQuoteCurrency(ticker)` (`server/src/equityQuote.ts`: the instrument registry's `quote_currency`, see «Instrument registry and price providers»; an unregistered ticker throws); readers **throw on stored-currency mismatch** — fix rows, don't coerce.
- **A Bolsa de Santiago instrument is registered `market_kind = santiago`, `quote_currency = clp`** (Yahoo spells it `CFIETFIPSA.SN`; the spelling decides nothing since migration 305): session = Chile calendar day (live window ≈ 09:30–17:05 Chile on Chile business days — `isChileBusinessDay`, never weekday-only); EOD display = latest bar ≤ Chile today (on-or-before absorbs Chilean holidays).
- **MTM**: clp-quoted → `value_clp = units × close`, **no fx** (`computeEquityMtmClp`, `equityBrokeragePositionMeta`, `ccInvestmentProxy`, `marketSeries` derives `equity_usd = close / clp_per_usd`). USD composites throw if a clp ticker sneaks in (`watchlistComposite`).
- **EOD sync is its own source, `stocks_santiago`, on the Chile calendar (2026-09-07).** `.SN` used to piggyback `stocks_nyse`, whose due rule is the NYSE calendar: a Chilean holiday correctly never marked it stale, but the other direction was never handled — on a US holiday that is a Chilean business day (2026-09-07, Labor Day) no sync ran, the day's `.SN` close never landed, and after the 17:05 live window the resolver fell back to Friday's bar (IPSA read 1.383 as of 09-04 with a zero day change while the polls held 1.387,4 for the 09-07 session). Now `equityEodSantiagoSyncDue` (`equityEodSync.ts`) is due at **17:10 Chile on Chile business days** and **carries over** to the last closed Chile session until its bar is in DB (`isStocksSantiagoStale`, `equitySantiagoEodCaughtUp` over `listWatchlistSantiagoTickersForEodSync()`); the runner caps Yahoo's series at the due session so a forced run before the close never stores the in-progress bar; the scheduler's 3-minute poll applies only on the evening of the close (carry-over days keep 15 min, so an unlisted Chilean holiday can't hammer Yahoo). `stocks_nyse` syncs NYSE tickers only. `account_sync_sources` links `.SN` accounts to the new source (migration 179 rebuilt the CHECK-enumerated table). Keep `CHILE_CLOSED_YMD` current: a Bolsa closure missing from it leaves the source stale (visible in the sync panel) until the next real bar.
- **CLP-funded trades**: `stock_buy`/`stock_sell` on a clp-quoted stock transfer against **CLP cash** with a CLP-denominated amount (no USD leg; enforced in `validateBrokerageTransferEndpoints`). They count as `clp_wire` capital flows (`equityBrokerageCapitalFlows`) and the funding CLP-cash leg feeds the aportes line (`accountDeposits`). `dividend_payout` stays USD-only (throws for clp stocks until needed).
- Client: account summary / detail-bundle expose **`equity_quote_currency`**; the stock movement form shows Monto CLP + CLP-cash counterpart for clp-quoted tickers from that field alone (`buildBrokerageMovementPostBody(row, quote)`, `brokerageFlowKindNeeds{Clp,Usd}ForQuote`; the client's old `.SN` rule, `stockQuoteCurrencyForTicker`, is gone).
- Yahoo has **no EOD history for `CFIETFIPSA.SN`** before 2026-07-02 (bars accumulate from there onward); on-or-before quote lookups tolerate the gaps. Older history: **`npm run backfill:bolsa-santiago-eod -w nw-tracker-server`** pulls daily closes per `.SN` ticker from the Bolsa de Santiago point-history API — up to ~10 years, capped at the instrument's listing date (`server/src/equityBolsaSantiagoEod.ts`; run 2026-07-03, filled CFIETFIPSA.SN 2025-05-12 → 2026-07-02 = full history since its Mayo 2025 inception).

## London market (LSE) tickers

- **`.L` = London Stock Exchange = `EquityMarketKind "london"`, the fourth market kind (2026-11-10; `londonSession.ts`, migration 303, plan `docs/demo-app-features-plan.md` §2).** The market is the instrument registry's (since migration 305; see «Instrument registry and price providers»): `equityMarketKind` returns the registered `market_kind` and throws for an unregistered ticker, whatever its suffix — the Yahoo adapter maps `Europe/London` → london when it describes a symbol. Wave L's suffix table (`EXCHANGE_SUFFIX_MARKET`) and the bare-symbol NYSE default are gone; before wave L every unknown suffix fell back to NYSE, so `CSPX.L` would have been priced on New York's sessions and synced by `stocks_nyse` without a word. Every per-market reader is a `switch` over the kind that ends in `unknownEquityMarketKind` (never a `santiago ? … : nyse` default): the live gate (`shouldUseLiveEquityQuote`), the held print (`equitySessionClosedForDay` / `equityHeldSessionQuote`), the EOD resolver (`resolveLondonEodQuote`: the LSE display session's bar against the prior LSE session's, the NYSE shape), `equitySessionYmdForTicker` / `equityDisplaySessionYmd`, the day-change calendar (`tickerDayDisplay.ts` `"london"`: a hard 0 on a UK bank holiday), Yahoo bar labelling (`yahooBarYmdFromUnix`: Europe/London — Yahoo stamps an LSE daily bar at its 08:00 open; the weekend filter covers the weekday calendars, NYSE and London), the stream and poll session gates (`isEquityRegularSessionOpen(kind, now)`, one function for `streamTickInSession`, `subscribedMarketOpen` and `pricedSessionOpenPredicate`), the watchlist's 1W anchor (`londonSessionsBack(asOf, 5)`), the daily series' `market_day` (a day any priced market trades on: NYSE, Santiago or London) and the live prior close (`priorCloseForLiveSession` with `priorLondonSessionYmd`). The synthetic demo market skips non-LSE days the same way.
- **Clock and calendar:** `londonWallClock` (Europe/London, memoized per UTC minute like the NYSE one), regular session **08:00–16:30** on LSE trading days (`isLondonRegularSessionOpen`; `isAfterLondonRegularClose` from 16:31; `londonDisplaySessionYmd` = the prior session before the open, today from the open), `LSE_CLOSED_YMD` 2023–2027 in `marketHolidays.ts` = the England and Wales bank holidays as gov.uk lists them (substitute days included: 2023-01-02, 2026-12-28, 2027-12-27/28; the 2023-05-08 coronation), `isLondonTradingDay` / `priorLondonSessionYmd` / `londonSessionsBack`. **The 24 / 31 December half days (12:30 close) are modelled as full sessions:** Yahoo prints their bar at 08:00 like any other and the live window simply sees no prints after 12:30. Checked against Yahoo's whole CSPX.L history (2016 →): no bar on a weekend or a listed closure. In Chile's winter the session is 04:00–12:30 Chile, so a London line is live in the early morning, held until 16:40 London and on its bar after.
- **Quote currency: the registered row says USD, and every London write checks Yahoo's (`checkYahooSeriesCurrency`, `priceProviders/yahoo.ts`).** The LSE lists the same fund in pence (`GBp`, CSP1 / VWRL), dollars (CSPX / VWRA) and euros, so the `stocks_london` sync and the demo's real-market loader refuse a line whose Yahoo `meta.currency` is not the registered one before storing a bar — a pence line fails at the write, never sits valued as dollars; the Yahoo adapter's `classifyYahooSymbol` refuses it when it is described (pence by name; any currency `currencies` holds is accepted since migration 315, a code it lacks is refused naming it). A holding's price converts out of its quote currency through `rates.ts` (`holdingPriceAt` → `convertValue`), so a euro-quoted stock is valued; what still assumes pesos or dollars throws naming the currency: a stock trade's cash leg (`movementUnitsPolicy.ts`), a broker split's cash account, the proxy basket (USD only), art. 107 (pesos), option underlyings (USD), and `equity_dividends`' own CHECK (Phase B).
- **`stocks_london` sync source** (`syncSourceIds.ts`, the `SYNC_SOURCES` row in `globalSyncStale.ts`, i18n `importSync.sync.sources.stocks_london`): due from **16:40 London** on an LSE trading day (`LONDON_EOD_SYNC_AFTER_HOUR_LONDON` / `_MINUTE_LONDON`, `isLondonEodSyncWindow`, `equityEodLondonSyncDue`), **carrying over** to the last closed LSE session until its bar is in (`equityLondonEodCaughtUp` over `listWatchlistLondonTickersForEodSync`; `keepsForcedWhileStale`), day kind on London's own calendar (`londonDayKindAt`: the Boxing Day substitute is a holiday while Chile and New York trade), next sync `stocksLondonNextSync` (16:40 `Europe/London` — `SyncWallTime.timeZone` and the contracts DTO carry the zone, the client formats any IANA zone). `syncStocksLondonFromYahoo` is the Santiago shape (one chart request, series capped at the due session so a forced run before the close never stores the in-progress bar) plus the NYSE path's dividends and splits (`fetchYahooRecentDailyClosesWithDividends`; nothing for the accumulating UCITS lines, a distributing line later records `withholding 0, jurisdiction IE`). The scheduler polls fast only on the evening of the LSE close (`pollIntervalMsForStaleSources`); `inferSyncSourcesForAccount` links a `.L` account to it; `scheduleMeta` reads every source's own `dayKind` whether or not it is stale (the `stocks_nyse` special case is gone — the fx day reads open on a Chilean holiday too). Migration 303 rebuilt the `market_symbols` and `account_sync_sources` CHECKs (`london`, `stocks_london`); `syncRunLog.ts`, `globalSyncState.ts`'s legacy split and `/api/sync/force-stale`'s `equity_eod` alias list it. Tests: `londonMarket.test.ts` (the suffix fail-fast, the gates at fixed instants, the due rule and carry-over, the source row), fixture `test/londonTickerFixture.ts`.
- **Demo:** IBKR opens **2023-05** as a Chilean resident (`SecondBroker.from`, `ucits: { tickers: ["CSPX.L", "VWRA.L"], monthlyUsd: 1_000, sellAllOn: "2024-03-12" }`, `usFrom: "2024-05"`): a standing order of US$1.000 a month wired from the dollar checking account — outside the peso cushion's gates, which the 2024-03 pie reserve and the house hold close for most of 2023 — buys the two LSE lines in equal halves on the LSE trading day on or before the broker day (ten buys, 2023-05 → 2024-02, US$10.000), the sell-all on 2024-03-12 (an adviser's PFIC warning before the move; US$5.998,90 + 5.884,90 on the synthetic market, a gain of US$1.883,80 that `foreignShareGainsForYear(2024)` takes to AT2025's 1104 as a resident's foreign share gain — 921.113 + 818.978 CLP in the draft's `clp_ipc` mode), the proceeds idle in the sweep through the move, and from 2024-05 the SGOV reserve + SPY phase as before (the US$2k idle cushion is that phase's rule). `DEMO_TICKERS` carries both from 2023-05-01 (`EQUITY_ANCHORS` from Yahoo's closes, rounded; the dollar short-rate table starts in 2023 for the sweep interest), `generate.ts` names each leaf by its registered `display_symbol` (CSPX, CFIETFIPSA), and the real-market loader takes a USD `.L` ticker on the plain Yahoo path (history: CSPX.L since 2010-09, VWRA.L since 2019-07, both USD / Europe/London, checked 2026-11-10). **The market asset grew:** a Render build refuses an asset built for less than `demoMarketSpec()`, so the home server must publish the new asset before the next demo release (`docs/demo-market-publish-plan.md`).

## Deferred: value + currency refactor

**Goal (decided 2026-07):** reduce per-currency *code branching* with straightforward `amount` + `currency` splits where the new shape propagates through readers — not storage-pattern consistency for its own sake. A DTO/derived field named `value_clp` that is definitionally a CLP display value is **not** a violation (most of the ~360 `value_clp` string hits are this); the convention targets DB storage plus parallel `_clp`/`_usd` logic branches. Storage precedent: `equity_daily` / `live_market_quotes` (migration 152) — readers select `value` + `currency` and **throw on unexpected currency**, never coerce. Migrate opportunistically when touching an area — no big-bang renames.

Tranche status, by measured surface:

- **`valuations` — DONE (migration 154 split `value` + `currency`; migration 260 took any currency).** A row is a quantity of what the account holds in its `currency` (any lowercase code): generic readers convert it once on its own date at the account's side (`storedValuationInUnit`), kind-specific readers assert the currency their rows are stated in (`assertValuationCurrency(code, expected, ctx)` in `server/src/valuationValue.ts`); see «Stored valuations are in any currency» under the holdings section. The old CLP-only guard (`assertValuationCurrencyClp`) is gone. Display units are any core currency (`docs/any-currency-plan.md`); the remaining CLP/USD-fixed storage is listed in that plan's «Remaining work».
- **`movements` — DONE (migration 169, 2026-08-03, big-bang):** native `amount REAL NOT NULL` + `currency` ('clp'/'usd'/'eur'), with `counter_amount`/`counter_currency` carrying the **to-leg of cross-currency transfers** (fx_exchange: amount = CLP from-leg, counter = USD to-leg; a CHECK forbids the counter pair on single-leg rows and requires `counter_currency != currency`). `counter_units_delta` (migration 242, nullable) is the to-leg's units when a transfer moves units on both sides — an AFP fund switch: cuotas sold on the from-leg in `units_delta`, cuotas bought on the to-leg — read only through `transferToLegUnits` / `transferFromLegUnits` (`movementTransfer.ts`); every existing transfer has none, so a null counter means the to-leg takes `units_delta` as before. Sign conventions unchanged: single-leg CLP rows signed (deposit +, withdrawal −), transfers positive, single-leg USD rows positive with direction in `flow_kind`. Leg semantics live ONLY in **`movementAmounts.ts`** — `movementClpLegOrZero`/`movementUsdLeg` reproduce the legacy `amount_clp`/`amount_usd` exactly (0/null when absent) for aggregation over mixed sets; `requireMovementClp` **throws** on CLP-only surfaces (mirror pairs, cartola, depto); `MOVEMENT_CLP_LEG_SQL`/`MOVEMENT_USD_LEG_SQL` are the in-query CASE fragments (alias-qualified inline when the query aliases movements). The create API takes `amount`+`currency` (+counter pair; single-leg fx_exchange is rejected — conversions are always transfers); flows DTO rows carry the native fields (CLP rows = signed per-account delta, USD rows raw) and the FlowsTable renders one Monto column (fx rows as «$X → US$Y»); the XLSX Movimientos sheet keeps split monto_clp/monto_usd columns **derived** from the legs. Byte-parity vs pre-refactor main was verified on a real-DB snapshot (both dashboard bundles, flows P/L, deposits reconciliation, income, cc expenses, overview-daily, 4 group TS/perf payloads, every net_worth flows row) — only ephemeral synthetic gap-mirror ids differed. Two intentional exceptions keep the old names: `legacyNoteBackfills.ts` (migration-157/161 hook — runs BEFORE 169 on fresh DBs, must speak the old schema) and derived DTO/loader fields that are definitionally CLP (SQL-aliased `AS amount_clp`). Migration 169 runs with foreign keys OFF (`FOREIGN_KEYS_OFF_MIGRATIONS` in db.ts): with FKs on, `DROP TABLE movements` cascade-deletes every child-table row (depto_payments, movement_mirror_merges, …) — any future rebuild of an FK-referenced table must use the same mechanism. Since migration 314 every money table's currency column (movements and flows with their counter currency, the card, bank, payroll, dividend, wire, recurring-payment, loan-payment and fund-unit tables) is `REFERENCES currencies(code)` instead of a CHECK list, so a new currency is a `currencies` row (the market tables and the coins of `crypto_trade_details` keep their own lists).
- **Cash in any currency; flow kinds carry no currency (2026-11-12; migration 318, `cashCurrency.ts`, `foreignCashAccounts.ts`, docs/any-currency-plan.md C1).** A cash ledger account declares the currency it holds in `accounts.cash_currency` (references `currencies`, watched by the accounts mark trigger): the legacy `<key>__usd` / `<key>__clp` leaves name theirs (a declaration must agree; migration 318 filled it for every existing one), a `foreign_cash` leaf names none and must declare it (panel `account_type: "foreign_cash"` + `currency`). `accountCashCurrency` / `foreignCashCurrencyOf` / `isForeignCashAccount(In)` / `listForeignCashAccountIds(currency?)` are the one reading; the profile's quantity is `foreign_ledger {currency}` (was `usd_ledger`), its holding that currency, so `accountValueAt` converts it once in any display unit. The ledger is `signedNativeDeltaForAccountMovement(row, account, currency)` / `sumNativeThroughDate` / `foreignCashBalanceAt` (rounded to the currency's coin); a foreign cash event carries `amt_usd` for dollars and `amt_native` + `native_currency` otherwise (`nativeFlowLeg`, read by `flowEventInUnit` when the unit is that currency). `HOME_CURRENCY` / `isHomeCurrency` (displayUnits.ts) is pesos. Stored kinds renamed: `compra_usd_venta_clp` → `fx_exchange` (pesos → the foreign cash account's currency as counter leg), `compra_usd` → `fx_purchase`, `deposit_clp` / `deposit_usd` → `deposit`, `withdrawal_clp` / `withdrawal_usd` → `withdrawal`, `aporte_estatal_clp` → `state_bonus`, `traspaso_bonificacion_clp` → `state_bonus_transfer_in`; a single-leg foreign row's `deposit` / `withdrawal` move its own currency, the label names the movement's currencies. The flow-insert trigger was re-created with them (`reinstallTableTriggersInOrder`, `tableTriggerReinstall.ts`, order kept). Still dollar-only by nature: trades, dividends and option premiums (USD cash), the dollar checking listings, the lot walks and the card payment tracer (C2), the bid/ask table (`BID_ASK_FOREIGN_CURRENCY`); the client cash form shows CLP / USD fields only.
- **Card storage per currency (any-currency C3, 2026-11-12):** `cc_statement_lines` holds `amount` + `currency` + `cuota_amount` (319; the legacy `amount_clp` / `amount_usd` / `valor_cuota_mensual_*` are generated read-only columns), the billing-month snapshot is the long table `cc_billing_month_metrics(account_id, billing_month, as_of_date, as_of_kind, metric, currency, amount)` (320; `listCcBillingMonthBalances` pivots it, with `facturado_by_currency` / `saldo_total_by_currency`), the cupo is `credit_card_cupos(account_id, currency, amount)` and the feed's SALDO INICIAL `cc_feed_billing_close_amounts(close_id, currency, amount)` (321; the config DTO lists a cupo per currency the issuer bills in), and a traspaso link names its legs `from_*` / `to_*` with their currencies (322). What still reads pesos and dollars as two slots: the billing detail, the facturaciones and their dollar rates, the statement-frame split, the expense lines and the payment mirrors. The rest of this bullet is the original plan. Both are metric-pairs where CLP and USD coexist per row, so a split only reshapes storage while the loader pivots back to the wide DTO (no code propagation). The EUR project's critical path is elsewhere: `statementSlotsByBillingMonth`'s literal `{clp, usd}` slot model → per-currency map, and EUR conversion helpers over `eur_daily` (`clp_per_eur`, synced by `sbif_eur` — today a reference series read only by rates display). Long-table plan for the balances snapshot when that project lands: `cc_billing_month_metrics(account_id, billing_month, as_of_date, as_of_kind, metric, currency, amount, UNIQUE(…, metric, currency))`; single writer choke point `recomputeCcBillingMonthBalances` (full per-account DELETE+rebuild — the table is a derived cache), single reader `listCcBillingMonthBalances` pivoting back to `CcBillingMonthBalanceRow`; only other raw access is a column-agnostic DELETE in `ccConsolidatedCards.ts`.
- **`payroll_work_earnings` — líquido split done (migration 153):** `liquido` + `liquido_currency` ('usd' for Deel wires, 'clp' otherwise). The DTO keeps `liquido_clp`/`liquido_usd`; for usd rows the CLP equivalent derives at read as haberes − descuentos (the stored breakdown was converted at wire date, so the identity is exact). The remaining `_clp` columns are line-item metrics of a liquidación (units labels, not currency branching) and stay.

## Real-estate expenses (`/flows/expenses/real_estate`)

- **Model:** `expense_accounts` = tracked places, **fully data-driven** (no slug unions or hardcoded lists in code): occupancy in `active_from`/`active_to` (YYYY-MM), `property_account_id` FK → the net-worth property master (owned places), `comunidad_merchant_patterns` (comma-separated place-specific merchant patterns). Values are personal data, set in the live DB only. New rental = «Añadir lugar…» on the page (POST `…/places`); new property = same form with the property account picked — mortgage rows then attach automatically. Payload `places[]` drives sections, chart series (dynamic slug keys + indexed palette), and labels. Bills = `expense_entries` rows with `expense_account_id` set: kind in `category` (`RealEstateBillKind`, incl. `rent`/`water`), consumption in the **`kwh` / `m3` columns** (migration 161 promoted the legacy `kwh=`/`m3=` note tags; notes are human provenance). `real_estate_expense_links` ties a bill 1:1 to the CC/checking purchase that paid it (auto by merchant patterns + amount within bill+0/+2 months, or manual).
- **Purchase-first assign** (`POST /api/flows/expenses/real-estate/assign`, «Asignar compra…» per place): creates the bill FROM an unlinked purchase (amount/month from the purchase) plus the manual link in one tx — how uncovered history (arriendo a, rents) gets linked without inventing amounts. The assign modal pool (`GET …/unlinked-purchases`) defaults to category `bills` («Cuentas y servicios») scoped to the place's occupancy period (+2-month tail), kind-pattern matches float up, multi-select bulk-assigns runs of months. Consumption editable per row (`PATCH …/entries/:id/consumption`); billed month editable per row (`PATCH …/entries/:id/bill-month` — rewrites the month-end `spent_on`, rejects months that would put the linked purchase outside the +0..+2 window) and the assign modal has a «mes anterior» toggle (bill_month = purchase month − 1); `DELETE …/entries/:id` removes a bill outright (link/rejections cascade). Bulk history: `npx tsx scripts/suggest-real-estate-links.ts` (report-first, `--apply`; contribuciones + same-day/amount dups + odd-amount rents are review-only; never MUTUARIA/METLIFE/TOKU mortgage cash; cuota-split lines dedupe by purchase_key). Ran 2026-07-10 (~220 links: arriendo a 2019–2021 full incl. synthetic gap rows, arriendo_b, depto).
- **Mortgage rows are read-only ledger derivations** (depto): kind `mortgage`, `expense_entry_id: null`, amount = what the cuota paid, split in `mortgage_parts`: the scheduled amortización, the extra amortization above the minimum (`amortizacion_ext_clp`) and carrying = interés + seguros, from the `loan_payments` regular cuotas (pie/prepagos are capital events and stay out — the user's choice, 2026-10-28 —, and the movement's CLP `amount` is an equity mark, not the cash paid). Until 2026-10-28 the extra amortization was left out, so the page showed the minimum cuota (a 9xx.xxx cuota read 4xx.xxx). The chart draws the three parts as their own kinds, `mortgage_amortization`, `mortgage_extra_amortization` and `mortgage_carrying`. The extra amortization is drawn below zero (a negative chart value: saving into the property, not the month's cost; the scheduled amortization stays above with the cuota); a point's `total` stays what was paid, the sum of absolute values. Never stored as expense entries. `bill_month` = the cuota's **scheduled month**, derived from the cuota numbers (`mortgageCuotaBillMonths`: modal anchor of month(occurred_on) − cuotaN; throws on inconsistent numbering) — a skipped cuota paid the next month still lands in its own month; `spent_on` keeps the real payment date (shown as a «pagado» hint).
- **Billed-period framing lives here only:** the per-place view buckets by `bill_month` (what period is being billed); the general gastos view (`/flows/expenses`) shows real expense dates — the `cc_expense_gastos_period_month_overrides` smoothing table (two manual mortgage skip/double-payment rows) was dropped in migration 164.
- **No double counting:** the manual-gastos loader (`flowsManualExpenses.ts`) excludes place rows **structurally** (`expense_account_id IS NULL`) — a linked bill's money already flows through its CC/checking purchase line.
- **A bill exists only when a purchase backs it (2026-10-28).** Bills are created from a purchase (assign, or a bill mail paired with its charge) and always against whole purchases («Total» mode lines — never «Por cuota» pieces or financing-projection splits). The old spreadsheet import's expected rows were the only bills without one; the last five (three thirds of a financed ENEL charge, two contribuciones not paid as expected) were removed with `server/scripts/repair-real-estate-bills.ts --plan=<json> [--apply]` (report-first; delete / remonth / assign steps). Electricity bills sit one month before their card charge (bill = consumption month), and so do HOA payments (buildings issue each month's gastos comunes the following month; the receipt's month name is the issue month, not the month paid for). **A payment that covers several months is one bill with `months_covered` (2026-10-28; migration 245):** the months end at the bill month (`covers_from` on the slot), the chart divides the amount over them in whole pesos (`spreadOverCoveredMonths`, the last month takes the remainder) and the table shows «cubre …»; set from the bill-month editor (`PATCH …/entries/:id/months-covered`) or the repair script's `cover` step. Metrogas bills a two-month period (2 months each); HOA, internet and electricity were sometimes paid two or three months at once. Contribuciones stay in the month paid (user's choice). A payment the provider refunded is not a bill: a gas bill paid twice and refunded a month later into checking keeps one bill; the refund is marked a refund in «bills». **A link that no longer holds is reported, never deleted on read:** `findInvalidRealEstateExpenseLinks` marks the slot's `broken_link` (`missing_purchase` — the purchase key left the eligible lines; `outside_window` — its month left +0..+2) and the page shows it with Desvincular; only the explicit reconcile script deletes. Until then every page load deleted such links silently. A re-imported card line carries its bill link like its category (it hangs off the flow, so `flows/cardLineAdoption.ts` moves it with the rest; rejections too; a copy onto a sibling never takes it), and so does a daily checking row the cartola replaces (`adoptPartialIntoCartolaRow`, `docs/untangle-flow-identity.md`).
- **Chart by kind, per scope (2026-10-28; `chart_scopes` in `flowsRealEstateExpenses.ts`).** One block per scope — `all` and each place — with `series` (`<place>__<kind>`, stacked by place in date order then by `REAL_ESTATE_CHART_KINDS` — mortgage interest and insurance, amortization, rent, gastos comunes, electricity, water, internet, gas, contribuciones, and the extra amortization on top, the legend in the same order (the scope's `kinds`, so the client keeps no copy of it); a kind outside the list throws) and monthly `points` from the scope's first to its last bill. The client counts the Rango back from the scope's last point (a place that ended shows its own last years, not today's), colours each kind with one hue and alternates places between it and a slightly desaturated copy (`scheme`, by date order), so one place handing over to the next shows; a place page's total is that place's alone (it read every place before). Both this chart and the Expenses page's draw through one component, `StackedExpensesChart` (bar/line switch, optional year averages, a legend whose entries hide their series — several series per entry here, one kind across places; hiding changes the stack, never the Total).
- **A place may be DFL-2, and a checking credit may be its rent (2026-11-09; migration 300, `rentalPlaces300.ts`, `f22/rentals.ts`).** `expense_accounts.dfl2` with `dfl2_built_m2` (≤ 140) and `dfl2_reception_year` (set together, from «Añadir lugar…» or `PATCH …/places/:slug/dfl2`); the contribuciones halving window is the law's table in `legalParameters.ts` (`dfl2ContribucionesHalvingYears`: 20 years ≤ 70 m², 15 ≤ 100, 10 ≤ 140), served as `dfl2_contribuciones_halved_through`, never stored. The income kind `rent` (`CHECKING_INCOME_KINDS`, `FLOW_KINDS`) names its place: `upsertCheckingIncomeMovementOverride(id, {income_kind: "rent", rent_place_slug})` (`flow_income_overrides.rent_place_id`, required with `rent`, refused otherwise); `GET /api/income/rent-places`. In the F22 a non-DFL-2 place's rent is línea 5 a) code **1849** (summed into **955**, into 158) and its contribuciones of the year a línea 39 credit, code **1637**, capped at 1849 × round(157/170, 3) (`f22PropertyTaxCreditCap`; 304 = 157 − 136 − 1637 − 162 − 1018 − 610, `f22/identities.ts`); a DFL-2 place's rent is shown entering and declares nothing (Suplemento línea 9, 9.3 g); up to two DFL-2 places with rent in a year, a third throws. Rent and contribuciones reajustados to December with `ipcReajuste`. **Code 152 is never negative:** a net negative DJ 1890 interest (real interest under inflation) goes to 169 like any capital loss (`f22Code158` throws on a negative 152). The flows overview counts rent as income through its `other` bucket — a `rent` bucket for the income chart is still to add.
- **The agency's commission is a bill row of its place (2026-11-10; kind `management_fee`).** A rent forwarded through an agency that keeps a commission files that commission as an `expense_entries` row of the place, kind `management_fee` (`RealEstateBillKind`, `REAL_ESTATE_LINKABLE_KINDS`, `REAL_ESTATE_CHART_KINDS` after `contribuciones`, label `expenses.kinds.management_fee`, the assign modal and the chart's colour), created from the checking debit like any bill (`assignPurchasesToRealEstateExpenses`); `taxFacts/rentals.ts` then carries it in `bills`, which is where a tax return reads the cost of renting (Schedule E line 11). The demo generator writes one per forwarded rent (`simulate.ts` `placeBills`).

## Expense subcategories (2026-10-07; migration 224)

- A category may name a parent (`cc_expense_categories.parent_id`, one level deep — a reader throws on a deeper chain); today's categories are the parents. The category DTO carries `parent_slug`. Lines are always assigned a leaf; the expenses chart has a «Categorías / Subcategorías» switch (`useCcExpenseCategoryLevel`, per browser): at «Categorías» a subcategory's spend folds into its parent (`ccExpenseCategorySlugAtLevel`), at «Subcategorías» every category stacks on its own. Pickers list each subcategory right after its parent as «Parent › Child» (`ccExpenseCategoryPathLabel`). Seeded: «Impuestos» (`taxes`) and «Comisiones AFP» (`pension_fees`) under «Cuentas y servicios» (`bills`), for the payroll deductions (`docs/payroll-plan.md`). Slugs the code names (`bills`, `deposits`, `no_cuenta`, …) stay top-level. Gastos by month and category are built on the server (2026-10-08; `flowsExpensesGastos.ts`, `GET /api/flows/expenses/credit-card/gastos?unit=&exclude_big_groups=`): one view per installment mode × category level (`<split|total>|<category|subcategory>`), in the requested unit, with the excluded big groups left out of the chart only — headline totals, month and year table rows (trimmed to the mode's last real spend), monthly stacks (through Por cuota's last month in both modes), the unfiltered stacks for stack order, and full-history yearly averages. The Expenses page and the Flows overview only pick a view, then clip to their Rango. The lines payload no longer carries `by_month` / `chart_monthly*` / `total_*_clp`. Ported from the client aggregator it replaced (`ccExpenseGastosAggregate.ts`, deleted) and checked identical on the real DB for every unit × mode × level × exclusion set (384 comparisons). A category or big-group change shows in the chart once the gastos view refetches, not optimistically. **Big-group usage is the server's too (2026-10-28; `bigGroupUsageByMode`):** the gastos payload carries `big_group_usage` per installment mode (pesos, purchases, sorted by label) and the lines payload `active_big_group_slugs` (the default chart exclusion); the «Grandes gastos» section picks them (`ccExpenseBigGroupTotals.ts` deleted). The client keeps `countsTowardGastosMes` only to move a line between the month modal's sections as an edit applies optimistically, pinned to the server's by `server/src/test/gastosMesCountingCases.json`, which both suites assert.

## Manual expenses, line pieces and big groups (2026-10-10; migrations 235–236, `expenseLinePieces.ts`)

- **A big group belongs to the expense, not to the account that paid it:** `cc_expense_purchase_big_groups` is keyed by `purchase_key` alone (migration 235). Every purchase key already names one expense on its own (`line-pr:<parser row>`, `installment-h:<account>:…`, `checking-mv:<movement>`, `checking-cartola:<account>:…`, `manual:<id>`, a piece's `<line key>#split:<seq>`), so manual expenses take a big group like any line; payroll deductions still take none. Categories («Único») and notes stay keyed by (account, key).
- **Manual expenses** (`expense_entries` without a place) are created, edited and deleted from the Expenses page («Gasto manual»; «Editar» on a manual line in the month modal): `POST /api/flows/expenses/manual`, `PATCH`/`DELETE …/manual/:id`. They are for spending no bank or card line shows; the note keeps its `manual:` provenance prefix and the line shows what follows it. Deleting one deletes its big group. **A manual expense is in any core currency (2026-11-12; migration 316, `expenseAmountCurrency316.ts`):** `expense_entries` holds `amount` + `currency` (→ `currencies`), the API takes `amount` + `currency` (the amount to the currency's minor digits, `roundToCurrency`), and the dialog has a currency picker from `/api/currencies`. The line enters the pipeline with its own `amount` ({value, currency}); its pesos and dollars are the day's (`convertFlow`, rate source `day`; a peso expense keeps its buy-rate dollars). An edit restates its flow's and evidence's amount and currency. A place bill (`expense_account_id` set) stays in pesos by CHECK; every bill reader selects `placeBillPesosSql` and throws on any other currency (`placeBillAmount.ts`). The legacy `income_entries` got `amount` + `currency` too (no app writer; read as flows of their day), and the income lines no longer carry fixed `amount_usd` twins of their pesos: `amount_usd` is a credit's own dollars, null for a peso credit, whose dollars the unit conversion reads at the day's buy rate (`incomeLineUsdLeg`).
- **Line pieces** break a card or checking purchase line (a cash withdrawal, one charge that paid for several things) into pieces, each its own line with its own day (`cc_expense_line_splits.spent_on`, migration 236; null = the line's day), amount and category; its big group and note hang off the piece's key. Pieces need not cover the line: what they leave stays the line's own expense on its own day (`pieces_remainder`), and more than the line throws (`expandLineSplitsInDrafts`). Only a purchase line takes pieces, never a checking movement's deposit-paired portion, and no piece is dated before its line. `GET`/`PUT /api/flows/expenses/line-pieces` read and replace a line's pieces; a piece keeps its `seq` for life (its key), and removing one removes its big group and note. A withdrawal spent on one thing needs no pieces: its category and big group are set on the line itself. The older splits (excel-gap reconstruction, fully covering their line) read exactly as before. **A piece is held in its line's own currency (2026-11-12; migration 316):** `flow_parts.amount` is in the currency of the flow it is of (`flows.currency`), so a dollar card or checking line's pieces are dollars to the cent (their pesos by the piece's share of the line, the remainder by what is left); the line-pieces DTO carries `currency` and `line_amount`, and the dialog enters and shows amounts in it. A piece in another currency than its line throws; loan payment parts stay pesos (the property leg's flow).

## Home dashboard tables: wealth flows, latest expenses, Rentabilidad (2026-10-27)

- **«Flujos de patrimonio»** is the net_worth flows table without spending: `GET /api/groups/:slug/flows?exclude_spending=1` (`buildGroupFlows(…, { excludeSpending })`) drops every checking movement the Expenses page counts as spending — `checkingSpendingMovementKeys` (`checkingSpendingMovements.ts`): a movement whose every line in `loadFinalizedCheckingGastosLinesReadOnly` (debits, their pieces, refund credits) passes `countsTowardGastosMes`, the gastos totals' own rule. Deposits to investments, internal transfers, «no cuenta», a movement only partly spending, transfers between own accounts (never gastos lines), income, stock legs, mortgage and pension rows stay. Filter options describe the remaining rows. Other group pages do not pass the flag. Card spending was never in the flows (it lives in statement lines).
- **«Últimos movimientos»** (2026-11-11; was «Últimos gastos»; `GET /api/dashboard/latest-movements?limit=` default 15, max 100; `latestMovements.ts`, contract `LatestMovementsResponse` / `LatestMovementRow`): the newest movements, each on the day it happened (`on`), newest first. Expense rows: every `buildFlowsExpenseLines` line the Expenses page lists in «Total» mode, whether it counts toward gastos or not — internal transfers, «no cuenta», deposits, card payments (a card-to-card facturado payment shows both lines), credit notes and the purchases they annul (`listedAsLatestMovement`) — a purchase in cuotas once, as its plan's whole-price line (also a purchase that paid another card's facturado, though the financing projection excludes it from gastos), never its cuota lines or the «Por cuota» split lines; payroll deductions out. Card payment rows (`card_payment`, `latestCardPayments.ts`): every `pago_tarjeta` transfer into a card master, from a bank account or another card, once — «Pago Lider ·0101 · desde Santander ·0781» / «… · desde Cuenta corriente», the amount in the transfer's currency (a divisas purchase: the pesos that left, the dollars credited at `paid`; a dollar payment: the pesos its PAGO line shows, what those dollars cost) — with its evidence lines (the PAGO by payment identity, the paying charge or plan line) in its ⋯ panel and not listed on their own. Income rows: the income page's checking credits and manual entries (with their income kind), from the flows overview's cached income (`cachedFlowsIncome`). **A card credit is dated by when it arrived, never by its printed transaction date** (banks print a nota with the original purchase's day; `movementOn`): its flow's first-seen day (`flow_first_seen`, migration 310: the Chile day a card line's flow was first written, carried through re-imports and flow merges keeping the earlier day; recorded from 2026-11-11, no backfill), else the printed posting date when later than the transaction date, else the statement close (the transaction date while an open facturación's close is still ahead). Other lines keep their purchase day; nothing after Chile today. Expense rows go out whole with the categories and big groups, drawn with the Expenses page's own row (`CreditCardExpenseLinesTable`), dated by `on`; every amount in its own currency with the display unit's value beside it (`formatLineAmount`). A «Show more» button is drawn and does nothing yet.
- **Rentabilidad on home:** net worth has period returns (`groupHasPeriodReturns` in `portfolioGroupTree.ts` = net_worth or an investment group; it also gates the benchmark row and the daily warm, `rentabilidadDailyWarm.ts`). The home page, whose detalle table is server-paginated, reads them alone from `GET /api/groups/:slug/period-returns` (`getGroupPeriodReturns`: the consolidated monthly rows + the daily-chained windows + 1D/1W, without the consolidated-tables payload); `benchmarkComparisonForGroup` reads the same builder. GroupInfoBase props: `flowsTitleKey`, `flowsExcludeSpending`, `afterFlows`.

## Refunds inside gastos; additional-card reimbursements (2026-10-07; migration 217, `checkingExpenseRefunds.ts`, `additionalCardReimbursements.ts`)

- **A checking credit can be a refund of spending, not income** (`checking_expense_refunds`: movement → category). Someone paying back a shared expense, an additional cardholder paying back his charges. The income payload lists it apart (`refund_lines`, with its category). The expenses payload adds it as a negative line in its category (`checking_refund: true`, `checkingExpenseRefundLineDrafts`), so the category reads net of what came back. Its category lives on the refund: `assignFlowExpenseLineCategory` writes there for a refund line, and the expenses page edits it like any line's. The income page marks or unmarks one (`POST` / `DELETE /api/income/movements/:id/refund`, «Reembolso de gasto» in the type select; `markCheckingExpenseRefund` refuses anything but a single-leg credit on a checking-list account). Nothing marks a credit at runtime.
- **A card credit is spending coming back (2026-10-27; `markCardRefunds` in `flowsExpenses.ts`, `isGastosRefundLine`).** Every negative card line except a payment of the card (`isCcPaymentMerchant`), an ABONO DE DIVISAS, a traspaso de deuda and a nota de crédito paired with the purchase it annuls carries `card_refund` and counts negatively in its category, like a checking refund — a merchant refund («GLASS LIDER.CL (T)» −3x.xxx), a nota de crédito no purchase matches, the small unpaired notas (which counted in the gastos total with no category before and now stack in theirs). Its category still decides: one in no_cuenta or an internal transfer stays out of the totals. A paired nota and its purchase still net out on their own, so nothing counts twice. Card lines that move debt between the user's own cards are internal transfers (`checking_internal_transfer`), not spending: the peso leg of a «TRASPASO A DEUDA NACIONAL» and a «RECAUDACION …» that pays another card's facturado (six fixed 2026-10-27 with `server/scripts/archive/repair-flows-overview-cash-lines.ts`, report-first).
- **An additional cardholder's charges are category `additional_card`** («Tarjeta adicional», replacing `no_cuenta` for them; the auto-tag on origin in `additional_card_last4s` writes it). The Santander card feed says whose plastic made each settled row (`TipoBen` → the listing line's `holder`, absent on a pending authorization); an `additional` row is stored with the account's active additional card as its origin (`activeAdditionalCardLast4ForAccount`: the additional origin on the account's latest statement that printed one; none on record throws), so it is tagged the night it is listed, not when the statement arrives. His wires back are refunds in that category, so the category nets to what he still owes. Migration 217 moved the 535 auto-tagged lines over and turned the 89 `card_reimbursement` income overrides into refunds; that income kind is gone. The «Tarjetas adicionales» summary (`additional_cards` on the expenses payload: by month and year, running balance owed) is built from the lines alone: charges are `isAdditionalCardChargeLine` (source cc, additional origin, still `additional_card`; a line the user recategorized is his own spend), reimbursements `isAdditionalCardReimbursementLine` (checking refunds in that category). Classifying new wires stays a report-first tool: `npx tsx scripts/propose-card-reimbursements.ts --payer-note-token=<token> [--apply]` (from `server/`), which marks the matched credits as `additional_card` refunds.

## MACH account rebuilt from its mails (2026-10-07; `server/scripts/build-mach-account-from-mails.ts`)

- MACH (a BCI cuenta vista) never had statements; it is a `clp` account under Checking accounts (`listMovementBalanceCashAccountIds` includes `clp` leaves there, so its rows are gastos and income lines). Built once, report-first, from a plan file (`--plan=<json> [--apply]`, one IMMEDIATE transaction, ends at exactly 0 or throws):
  - card top-ups («MACH ONE CLICK» / «MACH WEBPAY ONECLICK» on ·0161) are transfers card → MACH, flow kind `carga_tarjeta` (a card leg emits no deposit event; `accountDeposits` skips every card leg); the card lines are set to internal transfer, line by line, since a line's own category beats the merchant rule;
  - withdrawals to and wires from the cuenta corriente become transfers through the mirror conversion;
  - «Le pagaste» mails are debits in the plan's category, «Te pagaron» mails refunds;
  - where the recorded events would take the balance below zero, a stand-in credit for exactly the shortfall (payments MACH did not mail), a refund in the plan's stand-in category;
  - a closing debit makes it end at 0.
- Notes are `import:mach-mail|…`, read like the 2019 `import:santander-mail|…` rebuild rows (`isMailRebuiltCheckingNote`, `listCheckingMovements`). **From July 2021 the app's own list replaces the guesses (2026-10-07; `server/scripts/apply-mach-app-history.ts`, rows transcribed from the app's «Movimientos» screen into `cfraser/mach-mail-archive/mach-app-movements.json`):** every top-up, withdrawal and wire it lists was already in the ledger; transfers with the corriente move to MACH's day with the corriente's kept as their bank posting day; the stand-ins and the closing after July 2021 were replaced by the app's 30 payments, purchases and «Pago recibido» rows, and the balance before its first row (5.700) is the one stand-in left there — the only start from which the app's rows end at 0 without going below it. On the real DB: 35 top-ups 3.xxx.xxx, 18 withdrawals −3.xxx.xxx, 2 wires in 40.000, 37 payments −9xx.xxx, 11 «Te pagaron» 1xx.xxx, 17 stand-ins 9xx.xxx (unclassified), closing −30.000.

## CMR Falabella card (2026-10-07; `server/scripts/import-cmr-statements.ts`, `ccIssuers.ts`)

- The CMR card (·0876, 2019-07 → 2021-06, retired) is a third issuer: `CC_ISSUERS` in `ccIssuers.ts` is the one issuer list (the rules a statement does not state, and the cards' order under Pasivos › tarjeta de crédito). Built once from its 19 mailed statements (decrypted, `pdftotext -layout` into `cfraser/cmr/text/`; each statement must reproduce its printed billed total from the previous one plus its lines). Lines as printed, except that payments are merchant «PAGO» and a «Devolucion Compras» refund «NOTA DE CREDITO» (the names every card's readers use); «Servicio Administracion» and «Impuesto ITE» are section-3 charges (`ccStatementLineRules.json`); the «Ajuste de sencillo» rounding pairs are internal transfers. The 36 «PAGO EN LINEA (PROM.) CMR FALABELLA» checking debits are `pago_tarjeta` transfers (`CC_PAYMENT_DESC_RE` knows the wording); the CMR → MACH top-up of 2020-09-02 is a `carga_tarjeta` transfer.
- **An issuer's statement currencies, cuota products and open-cycle listing are issuer data (2026-11-09; `ccIssuers.ts`, the demo's US chapter).** Every `CC_ISSUERS` row carries `statement_currencies: {primary, secondary[]}` — the currency every facturación prints (the Chilean three `clp`) and the ones a cycle prints only with activity (`["usd"]`) — plus `installments` (cuota products exist) and `unbilled_listing` (a feed or paste shows the open cycle). One close rule, `closedBillingMonthsFromStatements` (`cards/facturacion.ts`, pure): a month closes when the primary statement is in and each required secondary — `requiredStatementCurrenciesForAccount`, the primary ∪ the secondaries the imported stream carries — is in or proven not coming by the later-bank-close rule; the slot by month is a `Map<currency, statement>` and `hasEveryStatementTwinForBillingMonth` takes the required set. Month-end anchors are written for a card with plans or whose issuer has no cuota products (`hasBillingLedger`); `cardStoppedBilling` gives a card without an open-cycle listing one cycle of grace. The fourth issuer is **Lonestar**, a fictional US bank for the demo: `{primary: "usd", secondary: []}`, close + 25 days, the next cycle the day after the close, no cuotas, no listing; its payment lines («PAYMENT - THANK YOU», «AUTOPAY PAYMENT») and charges («INTEREST CHARGE», «LATE FEE», «MEMBERSHIP FEE», «FOREIGN TRANSACTION FEE») are in `ccStatementLineRules.json`. The import reconcile also accepts a facturado that bills interest and fees on top of the purchases. End to end on a synthetic fixture: `ccLonestarUsdCard.test.ts`.
- **CMR payments settle the same period's purchases**, which the billing detail does not model (it assumes a period's payments settle the previous facturación, and writes no anchors for a card without plans), so the card's month-end valuations are the running sum of its lines, written by the script and checked day by day against the owed walk (max 4xx.xxx, 0 at the last close). Nothing recomputes them: `upsertCreditCardValuationsFromLedger` writes nothing for a card without plans.

## Open-month CC bucket — truncated merchant duplicates

- The unbilled month is written by **two sources describing the same transaction differently**: the nightly scraper feed (`import:santander-movements` → `importCcWebPasteLines`, source `cc_santander_fetch`) carries the bank's full merchant string on settled rows, while a manual paste from Santander's web table — and, since 2026-08-28, the feed's own pending-authorization rows — carries it cut at **exactly 15 characters**. The one-shot dedupe key is `merchant | amount | date`, so the two never collide — and the bank usually restates the transaction a day later when it settles, so the dates differ too. Both rows survive and the open month over-reports.
- **`removeTruncatedMerchantDuplicateLines`** (`ccTruncatedMerchantDedupe.ts`) runs after every web-paste/feed write and drops the shorter re-listing once its fuller twin exists — two signatures, both scoped to `import:web-paste%` sources (a PDF statement is the bank's settled record and never carries a truncated or pending merchant), both requiring the same amount to the cent in the same currency, and **identical merchants never match** (two genuine charges of the same amount days apart are real spending; the one-shot key already handles true duplicates). It only fires when BOTH rows exist, so a paste — or a pending row — whose fuller twin has not arrived is untouched. (1) **Truncated** (`isTruncatedMerchantOf`): shorter merchant EXACTLY at the 15-char truncation width AND a strict prefix of the longer one, transaction dates within `RESTATEMENT_WINDOW_DAYS` (4 — a manual paste carries the authorization date, the settled feed row may carry the next day). (2) **Pending authorization → settled with terminal code** (`isPendingAuthorizationOf`, 2026-09-05): shorter merchant SHORTER than 15 chars (a longer pending name would have been cut, i.e. rule 1), the longer one ends in a spaced 4-digit acquirer terminal code (` NNNN`) whose core is the shorter name or the shorter name plus further clearing-name words (`BISONTE PALACE` → `BISONTE PALACE HOTEL 4995`), and the transaction date is the SAME day — the feed keeps the date when it settles (13 of 13 pairs observed), so a short name on another day is a different purchase. Only the spaced form is recognised: a glued code (`LA GUITARRITA CABALLI5080`, the bank's 25-char overflow) implies a name that was cut at 15 pending, which rule 1 already claims. The pass is idempotent (settled rows never match each other, so the hook re-running after every write finds nothing new); `planTruncatedMerchantDuplicateLines` is the read-only planner and `server/scripts/remove-cc-merchant-duplicate-lines.ts` runs it over every card (report-first, `--apply` = evidence-date read-before-delete + revaluation + billing-balance recompute).
- Found 2026-08-06: `ANTHROPIC* CLAU`/`ANTHROPIC* CLAUDE SUB` (US$xxx) and `MERPAGO*PLANOUT`/`MERPAGO*PLANOUTCANALDEVEN` (US$xxx,xx). Verified against the pre-fix snapshot: the rule removes exactly those 2 of 28 web-paste lines across all accounts and all history. **Not every same-amount pair is ours** — the `APPLE.COM BILL` / `APPLE.COM/BILL` 13,77 pair on 26–27/07 is in the bank's OWN feed twice (different `Ciudad` formatting), so it is bank data, would be re-inserted if deleted, and self-resolves at the facturación via `reconcileOpenWebPasteAfterPdfImports`.
- **Found 2026-09-05 — the feed lists pending authorizations (since the 2026-08-28 file).** `import:santander-movements` rows with `DescripcionRubro: null` / `TipoBen: null` are pending authorizations of INTERNATIONAL purchases: `Comercio` is the merchant name cut at the web table's 15 chars, and one or two days later the settled row restates it under the same `Fecha` and `Importe` with the full name plus the 4-digit terminal code, after which the pending row disappears from the feed. Rule 1 had been absorbing the ≥15-char names since 2026-08-29 (batch logs 204/209/211/216: `FARMACITY LA PL` → `FARMACITY LA PLATA 2 4584`, `CINEMARK CABALL`, `AV. LA PLATA 51`, `BILVANDAM SRL-C`, `LA GUITARRITA C`, …), so only the six SHORT names survived in the ·0901 open bucket — `SUPERCOOP F`/`SUPERCOOP F 5067` (US$xx,xx), `CHANA`/`CHANA 7142` (136,89), `TRGS`/`TRGS 4551` (16,64), `EMOVA SUBTE`/`EMOVA SUBTE 4042` (2,33 and 1,17), `BISONTE PALACE`/`BISONTE PALACE HOTEL 4995` (41,52) — US$xxx,xx over-reported. Removed with the script (snapshot `20260905-223920-before-cc-pending-authorization-dedupe`): 50 → 44 bucket lines, the 31/08 anchor 1x.xxx.xxx → 1x.xxx.xxx and today's stamp 1x.xxx.xxx → 1x.xxx.xxx (the removed dollars at the day's fx; the settled rows and every other line untouched). **Separate, deliberately unhandled pattern:** `APPLE.COM/BILL` pending rows restate with the IDENTICAL merchant and the date shifted +1 (30/8 → 31/8 US$xx,xx; 1/9 → 2/9 US$xx,xx) — same-merchant pairs are exactly what both rules refuse to touch, and like the July `APPLE.COM BILL` pair they self-resolve at the facturación via `reconcileOpenWebPasteAfterPdfImports`.
- **Cuota-billing reference rows never import (2026-08-26).** At a facturación close the feed (and the web table) re-lists each cuota billing that cycle as a row whose merchant is the bank's reference `CUOT: <cuota №>OPER: <plan №>`, valued at the monthly cuota. The installment schedule already bills those months (and billing moves debt between facturado/por-facturar — it never changes owed), so importing one double-counts the cuota; the overlap skip can't catch them because the reference merchant matches no plan and the row is dated at the cierre, not the purchase. `ccWebPasteToCsvRecords` routes any line matching `isCcCuotaBillingReferenceMerchant` (incl. the 15-char manual-paste truncation, which cuts the OPER half) to a reported `skipped_cuota_billing` bucket — batch log counter + import-panel skip reason, never a silent drop, and unconditional on the pattern (no real merchant is named like this; gating on a matching plan would let cuota-1 of a not-yet-converted plan through). One-time removal of pre-fix rows: `server/scripts/remove-cc-cuota-billing-lines.ts` (report-first, `--apply` deletes via evidence-date read-before-delete + revaluation).

## Lider boletas → grocery catalog (2026-08-08)

- **The Boleta Digital e-mail is the daily source for Lider purchases** (the user's own ~08:00 CSV scrape task was retired 2026-08-07; non-Lider charges on the card now arrive with the monthly statement PDF or by hand-paste — the web-paste box on the card page still works). `fetch:lider-boletas` (scraper, IMAP from `contacto@info.lider.cl`, subject «boleta») stages each mail's **`Boleta.pdf` attachment** + `meta.json` under `cfraser/lider-boletas/staged/<date>-<msgid>/` (document-ledger keyed by message id; `--all` for the full-history backfill). `parse-grocery-receipts.py` (parser v4, 2026-09 — the CLI walks BOTH staging roots and dispatches per chain: `grocery_receipt_parse.py` detects the chain from the issuer RUT printed in the header — `RUT: 76.134.946-5` on store boletas, the bare `76134941-4` line on hipermercado/delivery ones, Cencosud's `81.201.000-K` for Jumbo — or, on a cropped photo that lost the RUT, from the issuer's name opening a line («CENCOSUD RETAIL S.A.»; a RUT and a name naming different chains throw) — then runs the chain module, `grocery_receipt_lider.py` / `grocery_receipt_jumbo.py`; unknown RUT or neither throws, never a guess) parses items — barcode, description, qty (decimal; weighted `x 1.744 KG` continuation lines are kg items with derived per-kg price), unit price, and discounts in **two scopes that must not be conflated**: a discount printed directly under an item is product info and attaches to it; one separated by a blank line (Mi Club canje `RF CANJE PESOS MCL` — forced receipt-scope wherever it prints — whole-receipt coupons) is **receipt-level** (`grocery_receipts.receipt_discount_clp`) and never touches an item's effective price. The parse **fails unless Σ items − item discounts − receipt discounts == Σ payment legs** (TARJETA LIDER BCI / EFECTIVO net of VUELTO / PESOS MICLUB CANJEADOS / TBK) **and unless the printed «TOTAL NUMERO DE ARTIC VEND» equals the parsed article count** (unit items by qty, each weighed item once — verified on the whole corpus before it became a hard check). The movement date comes from the boleta's printed LOCAL datetime, never the mail's UTC date (an evening purchase crosses midnight UTC).
- **Photos of paper receipts go through OCR (2026-09-06):** a staged dir in the generic root whose `original_file` is an image (HEIC/JPEG/PNG) is OCR'd by **Apple Vision** through `grocery_receipt_ocr.swift` (compiled on demand by `grocery_receipt_text.py` into gitignored `ingest/python/.ocr_bin/`, keyed by the source's sha256 — no Python deps, reads HEIC, applies EXIF orientation; Tesseract is NOT used: only `eng` data, no HEIC). Vision returns text BLOCKS with geometry, so `assemble_lines` rebuilds rows: de-skew centres by the median text angle (a 2° phone tilt across a row exceeds a row's height), form rows from confident blocks only, attach low-confidence blocks to the nearest row (never let one bridge two — the misread `$` came back as one tall low-confidence block spanning two item lines), drop low-confidence digit-less junk from the text. Per dir: `ocr.json` (blocks, verbatim — cache keyed by engine version + image sha256), `ocr.txt` (what the parser sees), and **`ocr.corrected.txt` — the human repair path**: when present it is what gets parsed (`parsed.json.text_source` = `ocr_corrected`), the other two stay as evidence; a failed re-parse removes the stale `parsed.json` so nothing imports behind a failure. The Lider parser's OCR tolerances are each backed by the fail-fast checks: `$` optional before an amount, `,`/`.` both thousands separators, digit-less lines inside the item section skipped and reported (`ignored_lines` — pre-printed marketing text on the paper; the PDF corpus has none). Verified: a rendered boleta OCR'd end-to-end reproduces its PDF parse field-for-field, and the paper receipt's extra «COMPROBANTE VENTA LIDER BCI» block adds no payment leg.
- **Storage is store-agnostic with frozen provenance** (migration 172): `grocery_receipts` (chain/branch/city/purchased_at/payment legs) + `grocery_receipt_items`. **A receipt's identity is `receipt_key` = `<chain>|<receipt_number>|<YYYY-MM-DD>` (migration 178, NOT NULL UNIQUE; the formula lives in `groceryReceiptKey` and the 178 backfill hook — keep them in step); `source` / `source_key` (e-mail message id, photo sha256) are provenance of the document that OWNS the row.** The same boleta can arrive as the e-mail AND as a photo of the paper copy, so the import (`storeReceiptApply.ts` on the server, fed by ingest's `import:grocery-receipts`) collapses documents by rank: an `email` or `pdf` document outranks a `photo`; a higher-ranked document REPLACES the owner, a lower- or equal-ranked one arriving second is reported `skipped_duplicate` and never written (equal rank = first writer wins, so two photos of one receipt can't ping-pong the row between their source keys nightly), and a document whose identity now resolves to another document's row THROWS (a parser change moved an identity — surface it, don't reconcile by guess). It walks two staging roots (`defaultStagingRoots`): `cfraser/lider-boletas/staged/` (the e-mail fetcher's layout — source and chain fixed by construction, key = message id) and the generic `cfraser/grocery-receipts/staged/<date>-<source>-<key>/` (meta.json names `{source, source_key}`, parsed.json names `chain`; a missing or unknown value throws). **Drop zone: `cfraser/grocery-receipts/inbox/`** — `ingestGroceryReceiptInbox` (`ingest/src/grocery/inbox.ts`, first step of `import:grocery-receipts`) moves each photo (HEIC/JPEG/PNG…, source `photo`) or hand-saved PDF (source `pdf`, ranked with the e-mail) into `staged/<mtime-date>-<source>-<sha12>/` as `receipt.<ext>` + meta.json; the sha256 is the document identity, an exact duplicate of a staged document is parked in `grocery-receipts/duplicates/` and reported, and any other file type fails the whole batch before a single file moves. The canonical layer is `grocery_products` + `grocery_product_aliases` — alias identity = **(chain, barcode)** when the receipt prints one, else (chain, description); **items STAMP their `product_id`** (`product_source` 'alias' | 'manual') so re-pointing an alias later never rewrites history; alias assignment only ever fills `product_id IS NULL`; a re-import that CHANGES a printed description sends that item back to unclassified. Import (`import:grocery-receipts` = ingest → parse → import; run by `import:cfraser-inbox` when the inbox holds a file or `hasPendingGroceryReceipts` — a staged document with no parse, or a parse without a current import stamp) is idempotent — upsert by (receipt, position), product stamps preserved. **Import stamps (2026-09-06):** a successful import writes `imported.json` beside parsed.json (stamp version + parsed.json sha256 + the row it landed on + statuses); a dir whose stamp is current — and whose row still exists under that key, still owned by this document when the stamp says owner — reports `unchanged` and touches no table, so the nightly no longer re-upserts the whole corpus. A movement outcome that a later run could change is NOT stamped (`pending_branch`: the receipt waits for the bank's own line — see the branch-learning bullet below — and re-checks every run until it is paired or the branch is declared); `-- --full` ignores stamps, `-- --reparse` forces the parser, `IMPORT_STAMP_VERSION` forces everything once.
- **Card movements are a per-chain rule** (`CHAIN_CARD_RULES` in `storeReceiptApply.ts`; a chain without one is items-only, reported `chain_items_only`, unless it has a match rule — see «Jumbo receipts»). Lider: only when the boleta was PAID with the Lider/BCI card AND its date is inside the open facturación (`lastPdfBillingMonthForAccount` period end) — closed months are statement-covered, cash/TBK/other-card boletas store items only.
