The line goes through the web-paste path (`cc_lider_boleta` batch kind) on the registry-resolved master with the bank's exact merchant string from **`boleta_sucursal_merchants`** in `cfraser/cc-cards.json`, so dedupe collides head-on with the feed/statement rendering. **Each printed rendering of a branch is its own registry key**: the paper boleta prints the branch WITHOUT the e-mail's `, L.60` local suffix (`CALLE FICTICIA #123` vs `…#2249, L.60`), both map to the same merchant, and a branch neither the registry nor the learned map names is never guessed — see the next bullet.
- **Jumbo receipts (2026-09-30; `grocery_receipt_jumbo.py`, `CHAIN_MATCH_RULES`) are photos only and never write a card line** — Jumbo is paid on the Santander card, whose feed and statement already carry the purchase. The parser reads the store and comuna under the CENCOSUD line (the head office «KENNEDY 9001» is not the store), items with their code, the `N X $price` / `0,326 KG X $price` quantity line printed ABOVE the item (one the OCR merged into another row may wait one item; each item binds the first waiting line that fits, kg to ±1 peso) and the discount lines under it, and fails unless items = SUB TOTAL, discounts = DESCUENTOS, SUB TOTAL − DESCUENTOS = TOTAL and the payments net of VUELTO = TOTAL. Every 13-digit code must pass the EAN-13 check digit (an OCR misread fails the parse; `?` as the code in `ocr.corrected.txt` marks one nobody can read → barcode null). The import pairs the T. CREDITO pesos with an existing card line of the same pesos whose merchant says JUMBO or CENCOSUD (`findReceiptCardLine`), on the printed day: `matched`, `no_card_line` (older than 45 days — final; younger: `awaiting_card_line`, retried) or `ambiguous_card_line`. **Cropped photos** (`resolveReceiptFacts`): a receipt that lost its number is keyed on the photo (`photo-<sha12>`); one that lost its date takes its card line's date from the 7 days up to the photo's date — the photo date comes only from a file named `YYYY:MM:DD.<ext>` (`photoTakenOnFromName`, meta `photo_taken_on`) — else the photo's date itself; with neither, declare it in the correction file: `#! purchase_date: YYYY-MM-DD` (a directive line, never receipt text; it never overrides a printed date — disagreeing throws). The result carries `purchase_date_source` (printed / declared / card_line / photo).
- **New stores are learned from the card's own lines, never declared by hand first (2026-09-23; migration 184, `groceryBranchLearning.ts`).** The branch → merchant map has two sources — the registry seed (`boleta_sucursal_merchants`) and **`grocery_branch_merchants`** (`source` learned | manual; a declared entry that disagrees with a learned one throws at import). A card-paid, open-month receipt at a branch neither names is **flagged, not failed**: `grocery_receipts.card_line_status = 'pending_branch'` (+ `card_line_account_id`, the master it would have written to), no line written, the step exits 0, the receipts table badges it («Sucursal nueva · sin línea de tarjeta»). The bank's own line for the same day and the same pesos on that card IS the purchase, so `learnGroceryBranchesFromCardLines` runs inside the shared card-write funnel (`mergeCcAccountFromParsedRows` — the paste textarea, the nightly feed, the statement PDF/JSON all pass through it; result field `grocery_branch_learning`, also in the web-paste batch log) and again at each receipt import (a paste that beat the receipt pairs at once; a receipt still pending when its month closes gets one attempt against the statement's lines): exactly one candidate — one-shot, not a `<chain>-boleta|` line the importer wrote itself, not a line another receipt already learned from — stores (chain, branch) → the line's merchant with the statement's « (T)» stripped and marks the receipt `matched`; zero leaves it pending; two same-day-same-amount lines, or two pending receipts sharing a day and amount, are reported `ambiguous` and stay pending. Other statuses: `created` (the importer wrote the line) / `covered` (an existing line already carried it). First real case: the 2026-09-20 Hiper Lider Ficticio boleta (`CALLE FICTICIA #456`) paired with the hand-pasted `HIPER FICTICIO., SANTIAGO` line — under the registry-only rule it had sat as an unnoticed «UNKNOWN BRANCH» log line for three nights while the paste went in unpaired.
- **UI**: `/flows/expenses/groceries` (sidebar node seeded beside Inmuebles, gated on `grocery_receipts` rows) — cleaning workbench (unclassified (chain, barcode|description) groups ranked by occurrence, multi-select assign / create, similarity suggestions), per-product **price history** (chart + table, effective ↔ list ↔ per-base-unit price modes, branch/city/brand filters), receipts with item detail and a **source badge** per receipt (`GroceryReceiptRow.source` → `groceries.receipts.source.*`: E-mail / PDF / Foto — desktop column + mobile card row). Server computes everything (`groceryProducts.ts`, `/api/groceries/*`); the client picks.
- **Product config (migration 174)**: `grocery_products.base_unit` ('un' default | g/kg | ml/l | m) is both the comparable dimension and the display scale — **'un' is the COUNT dimension**, not a config-less default: a multipack alias («CRACKELET x3») carries content 3 with no physical unit, unconfigured = 1 (a product is one of itself), and per-unit price divides by the count; alias `content` is the package size stored in the CANONICAL small unit (g/ml/m) so kg↔g display changes never rewrite stored values (a cross-dimension base-unit change clears contents rather than reinterpreting them); normalized price = effective package price ÷ content, scaled — null when unconfigured, never guessed (kg-weighed rows convert directly on mass products). **Brands are a GLOBAL catalog** (`grocery_brands`, unique names — «Soprole» is one entity under leche and yogurt); aliases carry `brand_id`, the product↔brand relation is derived, delete only when unreferenced. **Merge** (`mergeGroceryProducts`) re-points aliases wholesale (global alias uniqueness makes source/target collisions impossible), restamps ALL items incl. manual stamps (a merge is an identity statement), deletes the source; in the UI the OPEN product is the survivor — the selected one is absorbed into it. **Mixed package sizes disable per-package price modes**: `groceryProductHistory` returns `heterogeneous_packages` (distinct alias contents, or any weighed row — every weighed purchase is its own package) and the client then offers only the per-base-unit mode (1 L at \$1.300 vs 200 mL at \$600 must never share a per-package chart). Config UI lives in the product detail: base-unit select, merge-into, per-alias brand (datalist pick-or-create) + content (value + dimension-restricted unit picker).

## Checking / cuenta vista ledger anchor

- A checking account's balance is **derived from movements**, not stored, so a synthetic **anchor** movement (`import:cartola|anchor|<period>|saldo final`, dated before the first real movement) carries the opening balance that makes the walk land on the latest cartola's `saldo_final_clp`. `ensureCheckingLedgerAnchor` re-derives it whenever an import advances the latest cartola period.
- **The derivation MUST use the same accounting as the balance reader** — `sumClpThroughDate` → `signedClpDeltaForAccountMovement` (matches `account_id OR from_account_id OR to_account_id`, signs each leg per account, and applies `flow_kind` semantics). Anything else silently produces an anchor that is internally consistent but disagrees with what the app displays.
- **Bank posting day (2026-09-30; migration 199, `movementBankPostings.ts`).** `occurred_on` is the best evidence of when money moved (a payment receipt, a broker mail, a card's credit date); `movement_bank_postings(movement_id, account_id, posted_on)` keeps the day a bank document filed it on one account when that differs — after Santander's 14:00 cutoff the next workday, sometimes next month. One row per (movement, account), because a corriente↔vista transfer has a posting on each side; sparse (no row = posted on `occurred_on`). **Every reader that reconciles against a bank document reads `COALESCE(posted_on, occurred_on)` through `BANK_POSTING_JOIN_SQL` / `BANK_POSTED_ON_SQL`**: the anchor derivation and the month table (`nonAnchorClpFlowTotals`), `checkingMovementBalanceAtMonthEnd` (`sumClpThroughBankPostedDate`), the timeline months, the xlsx/cartola dedupe (`partialMovementSupersededByCartola`, `findMatchingCartolaMovementNoteInDb`, the cartola import's count) and `findMatchingInternalTransferLegId` — a leg with a known posting matches only that day, one without matches the business-day window, across months too. Everything else reads `occurred_on`. **Writers** — whatever learns a bank date records it (`recordBankPosting`, which throws when the movement does not touch the account): a bank import deduped against a transfer leg (`claimTransferLegForBankRow`), a hand transfer that supersedes an imported bank row, a receipt re-date, a Fintual promotion, both mirror conversions (each deleted leg's date). This replaced the month-boundary rule that made every mail-sourced writer wait for the bank row (or keep the bank date) when the next business day fell in the next month — the receipt, Fintual retiro and Racional deposit deferrals, the mirror-pair checking-straddle block, the card-payment converter's same-month filter — because a transfer dated in month M whose bank row sat in M+1's cartola shifted the anchor by its amount. The migration backfilled 134 postings from `movement_mirror_merges`, the synthetic-transfer confirmations and re-dated rows; on the real DB that brought 13 checking months onto their printed saldo final and left the anchors and every display value unchanged. Regression: «bank posting day» in `checkingCartolaBalances.test.ts`.
- **Forward-posted rows count from today; history keeps bank dates (2026-09-06).** Santander dates an after-cutoff wire at the next workday and its feed lists the row early (a Sunday import carried a Monday-dated credit), so the ledger can hold movements dated after Chile today. One rule, `displayLedgerCutoffYmd` (`movementTransfer.ts`): a display read at a date before today is strict (`≤ date`), a read at today or later has NO upper bound — a movement we already know about counts now and keeps counting when its bank date arrives, so a value never steps twice. Applied at exactly two choke points: the ledger balance readers (`checkingMovementBalanceClpAt`, `clpCashBalanceClpAt`, `foreignCashBalanceAt` → every mark/live/consolidation caller inherits it) and the merged deposit-event timeline (`buildMergedDepositMap` re-dates such events to today and keeps the bank date as `posted_on`), so every flow-adjusted P/L — daily series, 1D/1W strip, dashboard day/month/year cells, the live month row, aportes lines — reads 0 for the row on every day it is shown; the old "skip events dated > today" caps (`flowsDeposits.ts`, `accountDeposits.ts`) were removed because with the balance counting the row they would have produced the phantom in the other direction. The day rollover is what moves the row: Sunday's live point carried it, Monday's rebuild of Sunday (strict, never cached while it was today) no longer does and Monday carries it — including across a month boundary (the old month's live «Mes» totals include it on the 31st; its closed row, built later, does not). **Reconciliation stays strict:** `nonAnchorClpFlowTotals` / the cartola anchor derivation and `checkingMovementBalanceAtMonthEnd` (cartola month table) compare against a bank document that is as-of its printed period end; the deposit-match candidate loaders in `checkingCartolaLoaders.ts` (expense↔deposit links, the deposits reconciliation, checking gastos/inflows pairing) read `loadMergedDepositInflowEventsBankDated` — pairing a fund deposit with the wire that funded it is a statement about real dates, and re-dating would have broken every far-future test fixture too; and the balance cache key carries the cutoff actually applied so a today-value is never served as that date's history. Listings keep the bank date: `AccountMovementApiRow.forward_posted` badges the row («ya en el saldo»). Equity unit walks are not covered (no feed produces forward-dated trades).
- **The bank's own charges are `cash_fee` (2026-10-05; `checkingBankCharges.ts`).** The plan's maintenance fee («COM.MANTENCION PLAN»), the línea de crédito interest and the overdraft tax are stored as `cash_fee` by both checking importers, decided from the bank's description at import: the deposit loaders skip the kind, so they are the account's P/L (a loss) instead of withdrawals, which is the only P/L a peso checking account has. Gastos still list them. The 55 rows imported before were tagged by `server/scripts/tag-checking-bank-charges.ts`.
- **Bug fixed 2026-08-06**: `sumNonAnchorMovementsClpAt` summed `MOVEMENT_CLP_LEG_SQL` over `WHERE account_id = ?`. A transfer row carries the account in `from_account_id`/`to_account_id` with **`account_id` NULL**, and `NULL = ?` never matches — so every transfer leg was invisible (and the CLP leg is unsigned, so an outbound transfer would have counted as an inflow anyway). On the real cuenta vista that hid 260 legs worth −1x.xxx.xxx: the anchor derived to −1x.xxx.xxx instead of +1.074 and the account read −1x.xxx.xxx instead of 0. It stayed latent for months because the anchor is only re-derived when a new cartola advances the period — an unrelated import is what detonated it. Regression test: «cartola-derived anchor with transfer legs» in `checkingCartolaBalances.test.ts`. **When touching any balance/anchor helper, check whether transfer rows (account_id NULL) are in scope.**

- **One source for a checking account's movements (2026-10-06; `listCheckingMovements` in `checkingCartolaLoaders.ts`).** Expenses, income, deposit matching, payslip pairing and income overrides ask for an account's inflows or outflows, never for a document: the function returns the cartola's rows plus the daily «últimos movimientos» rows its cartola has not replaced yet (one superseded but not pruned is left out, so nothing counts twice), and nothing else — not the anchor, not a hand-made adjustment like 12419. Before, income and the matcher's withdrawal pools read cartola rows only, so a month's credits (the salary landing on the 30th) were missing from income until its cartola arrived a week or more later, while gastos already read both. When the cartola supersedes a daily row, `prunePartialMovementsSupersededByCartola` moves what hangs off it by id — the payslip link and the income override — onto the official row (`carryMovementKeyedIncomeState`; both reference movements without a cascade, so a link left behind would fail the import). Which document a row came from is still read from its note in that one function and in the import-side key mechanics (stable purchase keys, the transfer-leg dedupe); a structured source column would retire that.
- **A foreign checking account's rows are gastos and income lines too (2026-11-09; any currency 2026-11-12; `listForeignCheckingAccountIds` / `listForeignCheckingMovements` in `checkingCartolaLoaders.ts`).** Any cash ledger held in a currency other than pesos (`foreignCashCurrencyOf`) under Checking accounts is one; its listed rows carry their own amount (`amount_native`, `currency`) and their pesos and dollars at the day's stored mid through `rates.ts` (`foreignCheckingAmountIn`). A dollar line keeps `amount_usd` native; a line in a third currency states its own `amount` (`foreignCheckingLineMoney`), an income credit its `native` leg (`incomeLineLegs` → `FlowLegs.native_amount`, so the payload in that currency reads it as is), a refund its `native`. Below, «dollar» reads «foreign» wherever these lists are concerned; the deposits reconciliation keeps reading dollar deposits against the dollar accounts' months only. A `usd`-kind account under Checking accounts (the demo's US bank account) has its bank-listed debits built as expense lines (`flowsCheckingGastos.ts`: category rules and «Único», no deposit matching, `amount_usd` native, pesos at the day's `fx_daily` rate — a row that moves no dollars or a day with no rate throws) and its credits as income lines (`flowsCheckingInflows.ts`), so a US pay stub pairs with its net credit. The expense-line funnel reads both lists (`loadCheckingGastosLinesForExpenses`). A single-leg dollar row with `units_delta` on an account that is not dollar cash — a payroll contribution buying 401(k) or HSA units — is a deposit event at the day's rate (`accountDeposits.ts`), pre-tax through `PAYROLL_FUNDED_KINDS` (= the cuota-ledger kinds `afp, afc, us_401k, hsa`); on the personal ledger no such row exists (checked on the 2026-11-07 snapshot). A listed single-leg `withdrawal` debit is a «used» dollar lot (`usdCashTaxLotEvents.ts`; realized under both oficios, deferred under `none`). **Its rows reach the notes, the refunds and the deposits reconciliation too (2026-11-10):** a note may sit on its gastos lines (`ccExpensePurchaseNotes.ts` allows the dollar checking accounts); a credit on it marked a refund (`checkingExpenseRefunds.ts`) is a refund of a dollar expense — its dollars (`amount_usd`), in pesos at the day's rate, listed in the income payload's `refund_lines` and a negative dollar line in its category; the deposits reconciliation (`flowsDepositsReconciliation.ts`) counts a dollar deposit of units (a single-leg dollar row with `units_delta` on a non-cash account, the deposit event) at its dollars priced as `accountDeposits.ts` prices it, reads its «checking present» from the dollar checking accounts' listed months, and treats those accounts as the funding source: out of the capital-return and deposit candidate pools (`loadNetWorthCapitalOutflowCandidates`, `loadDepositMatchCandidates`), as the peso checking is. The mirror pairs pair same-currency rows since 2026-11-12 (see «Historical mirror pairs»).

## Historical mirror pairs + movement search

- **Mirror pairs** (`/panel/mirror-pairs`): two pre-transfer-model single-leg rows (retiro + same-amount depósito on different accounts, inflow 0–5 days after outflow) are converted into one transfer row dated the **outflow day**; original legs (ids, dates, amounts, units, notes) live in the **`movement_mirror_merges`** table keyed by the transfer movement (ON DELETE CASCADE; the transfer note is a human summary) and conversion is undoable (`undoMirrorConversion` — a server-side function; its HTTP route was removed 2026-09-05, the UI never called it). **Any currency (2026-11-12, any-currency C4):** both legs in one currency, equal to its coin — pesos as before (no flow_kind, the sign is the direction), any other currency a `withdrawal` and a `deposit` single-leg row, no units, each on a cash ledger held in that currency (a foreign row states its amount positive and its direction in `flow_kind`); an `fx_purchase` credit is a conversion's arrival and never pairs, nor do two currencies. The conversion writes a transfer in that currency; the merge row's `out_amount_clp` / `in_amount_clp` then hold the legs signed in the transfer row's currency (the columns predate other currencies), and undo writes them back as `withdrawal` / `deposit`. The leg DTO carries `amount` (signed) + `currency` (was `amount_clp`); link-established pairs stay pesos (the deposit links are). Candidates: `listMirrorPairCandidates` (`server/src/movementMirrorPairs.ts`) — high confidence = unique both directions + within the cartola business-day window (`bankDateMatchesTransferDate`) + no month straddle; month-straddle pairs are ambiguous (converting moves one side's money into the other month on every display) but never blocked — each deleted leg's date stays as the transfer's posting day on its account (the old checking-inflow block went 2026-09-30, see «Bank posting day»). Excluded legs: flow_kind/USD legs, both-legs-cuotas, dap either side, afp/afc **inflows** (pre-tax payroll), deposits already in `expense_deposit_links`/`checking_gap_deposit_mirrors`/`payroll_work_earnings`, Buda/ahorro-split notes. Rejections persist in `movement_mirror_pair_rejections` (migration 155; ids cascade on full rebuilds). **Link-established pairs**: 1:1 `expense_deposit_links` rows (auto/manual, `checking-cartola:` keys resolved via `movementForCheckingPurchaseKey`, amounts equal) surface as `linked: true` high-confidence candidates — the link is the evidence, no window applies; the link row cascades away at conversion. Multi-allocation/partial links, synthetic links, and Buda-note deposits stay out. One transfer carries one `units_delta` (abs; cuota readers use `transferLegUnitsThroughDate`). **Month-precision legs** (cuenta_ahorro_vivienda — sheet records mm-yyyy only; movements dated conventional month-end): the pairing window is the whole month ± 7 days across the boundary (`monthPrecisionPairAllowed`) instead of the day-gap rule, the bank-window requirement is waived, and the converted transfer takes the **real-day (checking) leg's date** — so cartola re-import dedupe matches same-day and checking anchors are untouched (the checking-straddle hard-block is skipped when the out leg is month-precision). Conversion is balance- and aportes-neutral: the aportes/monthly-P&L reader (`loadTransferLegSignedFlowEvents` in `accountDeposits.ts`) buckets each side of a merged transfer on the day **that side's valuation evidence actually moves**, or flow and value are measured in different frames and the gap lands in P/L. **Month-precision legs keep their original `movement_mirror_merges` date** (their marks sit in the sheet month, not the transfer month) — that is what keeps a cross-month ahorro conversion from creating a −X/+X monthly-P&L couplet. **Every other leg uses the transfer's own date**, because balances and `transferLegUnitsThroughDate` both key off the movement: dating a real-day leg at its original settlement day instead produced a phantom ±X P/L couplet across the 1–3 day settlement gap (fixed 2026-07-24 — a checking → Fintual traspaso showed a phantom P/L couplet on 2026-04-21/23 that cancelled within the month but dominated the daily view; 101 legs across 137 gapped merges were affected). Month-precision kinds live in `monthPrecisionAccountKinds.ts` (`accountIsMonthPrecisionDated`), import-free so both `accountBucket.ts` and `flowsCheckingGastos.ts` can read them. Converted cartola credits leave the income pool (a correction) and mirror-merge transfers into checking self-resolve in the deposits reconciliation. **Amount and date cannot tell same-day siblings apart (2026-09-28):** the greedy pick between two same-amount debits of one day is only the lower id, and three historical conversions absorbed the wrong bank row that way (a wire to another bank or a PREVIRED payment instead of the traspaso beside it, one of them through an auto deposit link that had picked the earlier of two wires), while twelve more paired a debit with an unrelated same-amount credit (ATM withdrawals, wires to another bank, a third party's transfer). So every ambiguous candidate carries its competing legs (`out_alternatives` / `in_alternatives`), the panel shows them with their notes, and any of them converts in its place (`resolveMirrorPairRef`); an **auto** `expense_deposit_links` pair with a same-amount competitor in the window is ambiguous (a manual link stays high); a credit the user classified as income (`checking_income_movement_overrides` income kind / force-include) and a checking debit the user categorized as spending (a `cc_expense_unique_purchases` category outside unclassified / deposits / internal transfer / no_cuenta, or a `cc_expense_line_splits` split) are never legs. Repair: `server/scripts/repair-mirror-merge-legs.ts --plan=<json> [--apply]` (report-first, IMMEDIATE, idempotent; plan items `swap` a wrongly absorbed leg for its sibling, `undo` a non-transfer — both restore the original rows under their ORIGINAL ids so id-keyed splits and legacy `checking-mv:` keys resolve again, and an undo records the pairing as a rejection — or `reject` a never-converted pairing; month-end balances and checking anchors are verified unchanged inside the transaction).
- **Checking↔CC payment mirrors (2026-07-22):** a checking «Traspaso a T. Crédito»/PAGO TARJETA debit pairs with the card's payment evidence — a payment line (legacy formats; `isCcPaymentMerchant`, exactly PAGO / MONTO CANCELADO / ABONO — the one rule every payment reader shares since 2026-09-26, when this matcher and the cuota retirement dropped their own SQL `LIKE`) or the statement's `monto_pagado_anterior` header (current format; its date read through `requireHeaderPagoIso`, which throws unless ISO) — by exact amount within ±4 days — the debit may sit on any account under Checking accounts (`accountIdsUnderCheckingAccounts`, the asset leaves under the checking bucket, whatever their kind; until 2026-10-05 only the cuenta corriente, which left twelve 2019–2024 card payments from the cuenta vista unpaired) (`ccPaymentMirrors.ts`; candidates in the /panel/mirror-pairs «Pagos de tarjeta» section, POST `/api/movement-mirrors/convert-cc-payments`). Conversion writes one transfer (checking → CC master) dated the **card's credit date** (the immovable-evidence side — the inverse of the outflow-day rule, same spirit as the month-precision exception) with `flow_kind = 'pago_tarjeta'`; the merge row keeps the cartola leg (`out_*`) and references the evidence (`in_statement_line_id` / `in_statement_id` — migration 167 made `in_movement_id` nullable with a CHECK that exactly one in-ref is set). Statement evidence is never touched (the CC owed walk keeps reading it; the transfer's card leg is inert — CC valuations ignore movements), and `pago_tarjeta` transfers emit **no deposit/aportes events** (paying your own card is internal to the CC-netted cash bucket; `accountDeposits` skips the kind, so the daily/monthly P/L is zero by construction). Undo restores only the checking leg. All 63 date-skewed historical pairs (bank credits the card 1–4 days before the cartola debit — each a one-to-three-day cash bump in the daily view) were converted 2026-07-22. **Unblocked pairs auto-convert nightly (2026-08-07; month-straddling ones too since 2026-09-30):** `npm run convert:cc-payment-mirrors` (run by `import:cfraser-inbox` after the payment-receipt step, so a receipt-re-dated debit converts as a same-day pair the same night); the matcher is fail-closed, and the checking debit's date stays as the transfer's posting day on checking (see «Bank posting day»). **USD-debt payments convert too (2026-08-07):** a checking «Egreso por Compra de Divisas» debit pairs with the card's ABONO DE DIVISAS line — matched by the LINE's currency so the open web-paste bucket qualifies, cross-currency so the match is date window + one-to-one uniqueness + an implied-fx sanity band (300–2000 CLP/USD) instead of amount equality, traspaso-linked abonos excluded (debt reclassification, no cash) — and converts to the migration-169 cross-currency transfer shape: CLP from-leg = the exact pesos that left checking, USD counter leg = the card's abono, dated at the card's credit date. **Khipu transfers pair too (2026-10-27):** a checking debit «Transf a khipu …» (the payment gateway the BCI card was paid through) is a candidate like the payment wordings — it pairs only with CLP card evidence of the exact pesos inside the window, one-to-one; an unpaired one stays a plain debit (Khipu takes other payments). The two of 2025-08-29 / 2025-09-01 became transfers 13080 / 13081.
- **A closed month's month-end anchor adds what a later facturación bills (2026-10-06; `unbilledAtMonthEndAdjustmentsClp` in `ccBillingBalances.ts`).** The statement frame (facturado + cupo) covers what the statement billed; the month-end anchor adds every charge and payment dated on or before the calendar month-end that a LATER facturación bills, or none yet — chosen by billing (`facturacionMonthByStatementDate`, so a provisional month's bucket is billed by its month and a stale bucket by the open one), not by a date window starting the day after the close. The window missed two kinds: Santander purchases dated the close day (its next cycle starts ON the close day) and purchases posted a cycle late (·0161, 21–22/02/2023 billed in March), and the daily walk, which resets to each anchor, carried the shortfall to the next one. A line dated two cycles or more before the statement that bills it is a backdated posting (a nota de crédito carries the original purchase's date), not a late one, and stays out. The stream also carries the dated header-payment legs now (`cc_header_payment_legs`), which neither the walk nor the anchors subtracted before. The daily walk keeps its date window from its anchor (`postCloseLiveBalanceAdjustmentsClp`). Re-stamp: `scripts/restamp-cc-anchor-frame.ts`. Measured as the seam between each month-end anchor and the walk from the previous one: ·0101 0,19M → 0, ·0781 1,00M → 0,21M, ·0161 25,9M → 7,3M (5,0M with the parser fixes below).
- **A statement billed what it printed, credit included; a backdated line enters at its statement's close (2026-10-07; `statementHeaderFacturado`, `normalizedPostCloseLines`).** The import kept only a positive «Monto total facturado a pagar», so a period that ended in credit — ·0161's peso statement of 2018-06-25 (−2xx.xxx) and six dollar ones in credit (2017–2022) — read as «no header», fell back to its lines and left the credit out of the month-end anchor, while the walk counted it: the anchor and the walk from the previous one disagreed by the credit, one month up and the next down. The import, the statement JSON writer and every billing reader (`facturadoFromStatement`, the slot totals, `usdToClpAsDebt`) now take the printed figure whatever its sign; 0 is «nothing billed», only a missing header falls back to the lines (`scripts/backfill-cc-printed-facturado.ts` wrote the 13 stored rows). A line dated two cycles or more before the statement that bills it (a nota de crédito carrying its purchase's date: −4xx.xxx dated 22/02/2018 on the June statement, −3xx.xxx dated 18/06/2020 on August's) takes that statement's close as its date in the post-close stream, so the walk and the anchors count it in the same month (the anchors' own «backdated» exception went). On ·0161 the sum of |anchor − walk| over its 113 month-ends went 3,79 M → 0,83 M; what is left is a peso residue unrelated to credits (2019-07, 2021-11, 2020-02/03, 2018-11, 2024-01, each 50–113k).
- **Dollar header payments (migration 218).** A header leg has a currency and `amount`: old international statements print the period's abono only in the header («ABONO REALIZADO», no line: ·0161 2017-10-24 −249,86 and 2020-01-23 −78,40). `scripts/link-cc-usd-header-abono-legs.ts` (report-first) writes the gap between the header abono and the statement's negative lines (refunds count, traspasos do not) as a dollar leg, dated on the one checking «Compra de Divisas» debit of the period whose implied rate is plausible (which the converter then pairs), else on the previous pay-by. The walk values a dollar leg at its statement's debt fx; the cuota retirement reads peso legs only.
- **A dollar payment is valued at the debt fx of the facturación it paid (2026-10-07; `normalizedPostCloseLines`).** The month-end anchor values a facturación's dollars at its pay-by − 1; the payment that clears them (an ABONO DE DIVISAS / dollar PAGO line, a dollar header leg) is printed on the next statement, and valued at that statement's fx it read the month's fx move as owed: ·0161 2021-11, 3.xxx,xx USD at 839 against the debt at 803, −1xx.xxx between the anchor and the walk. The stream values it at the debt fx of the latest dollar statement that closed before it (the pairing `facturacionPaidOn` uses); a linked traspaso keeps its CLP twin's pesos. ·0781's anchor/walk disagreement 207k → 59k, ·0161 0,83 M → 0,51 M; what ·0161 still has above 50k is missing documents: the 2017-07/08 and 2018-10 dollar statements were never mailed, and the 2020-01..03 dollar statements have no text layer (their OCR'd headers do not add up). Those are estimated (`scripts/estimate-cc-missing-usd-statements.ts --plan=cfraser/cc-usd-estimate-plan-0161.json`): the three missing statements are written as `import:estimate|…` with one «COMPRAS INTERNACIONALES (ESTIMADO)» charge each (the gap between the printed balances either side) and their «ABONO DE DIVISAS» on the checking divisas debit that paid them (dollars = the debit's pesos at the day's dólar × 1,021, the median spread of 114 paired divisas payments); the three scans get their header abono from their own printed totals, an estimated charge for what the scan misses (6,09 / 2,29 USD) and dated dollar legs. ·0161 has no anchor/walk disagreement above 50k left (Σ 0,24 M).
- **Statements from outside the PDF corpus are named `import:<kind>|…`** (`isCcStatementPdfSource`): web pastes, the Santander statement JSON, the 2019 statements rebuilt from mails (`import:santander-mail|`), the CMR card's mailed PDFs (`import:cmr|`). The import-sync coverage requires an on-disk PDF only for the others.
- **Undated header payments get dated legs (2026-10-05; migration 213, `cc_header_payment_legs`).** The parser dates a statement's «monto pagado período anterior» only when exactly one body line carries it, so a period paid in several transfers, or by a debit the card printed no line for, left the header undated — invisible to the cuota retirement, which reads dated payments only: ·0161's facturaciones looked 5,9M unpaid by 2023 and its cuotas retired 3–7 months late, the «deuda en cuotas» line sitting above owed. A leg is one payment of such a header: card, statement close (not the statement row — re-imports replace rows), the day and pesos of the bank debit that paid it (`source = bank_debit`), or the previous facturación's pay-by when no bank record survives (`pay_by`: ·0161 2019-07, the lost corriente cartolas). The evidence list (`listCcPaymentEvidenceRows`, as header evidence of that close's statement) and the retirement (`listClpCcPaymentEventsForAccount`) read legs as dated payments; the pairing claims evidence by payment key, so a header's several legs pair separately. Written by `server/scripts/archive/link-cc-header-payment-legs.ts` (report-first, IMMEDIATE): per undated header, the dated payment lines in its period counted once (a statement's card groups repeat them), the remainder matched exactly by unlinked card-payment debits on the checking accounts (previous close, close + 4 days], a remainder a credit note explains skipped (BCI counts a nota in its «monto pagado»). Applied on the real DB: 43 legs (42 debits paired — 40 to ·0161, 2 to ·0781 — and the 2019-07 pay-by); the old-era «remainder ≠ debit» cases were the user paying the «disponible para pago», the overpayment carried as the next statement's negative saldo anterior. The pairing leaves «Traspaso … a Línea Crédito» out (overdraft-line payments; the shared `CC_PAYMENT_DESC_RE` keeps them for checking gastos, where they are internal).
- **A converted payment finds its card evidence by the payment, never by row id (2026-09-27; `ccPaymentMirrorEvidence.ts`).** Statement re-imports replace a statement's lines (new ids; `cc_statement_lines.id` has no AUTOINCREMENT, so a deleted top id can come back as another line), a PDF takes over a JSON close, and a pasted PAGO line settles into the statement's header payment — so the ids a conversion recorded went stale (most of them had, by 2026-09-27). Identity is card, currency, date and amount (pesos to the unit, dollars to the cent), the key the evidence list already folds statement versions and line + header on; the pairing side is its transfer's card leg (`to_account_id`, `occurred_on`, the USD counter leg of a divisas payment, else the pesos). `listCcPaymentMirrorCandidates` leaves converted payments out by that key (stale ids had let the nightly converter offer an already-converted payment again) and throws while one has no evidence; `usdPaymentLineClpResolver` reaches a line's divisas purchase through the evidence rows, so a traspaso-linked abono of the same day and dollars keeps its own pesos. `mergeCcAccountFromParsedRows`, the `--wipe` reload (one transaction now) and the web-paste line delete throw, naming the pairing, when a write removes a paired payment. `in_statement_line_id` / `in_statement_id` are conversion-time provenance only; `server/scripts/repair-cc-payment-mirror-evidence-refs.ts [--allow-kind-change] [--apply]` re-points stale ones (report-first) and checks that every pairing still has evidence.
- **Movement search lives in the flows tables** (the standalone `/search` page was removed; `/search` redirects home). `FlowsTable`'s filter bar carries the full set — year/type/account/bucket (`bucket` = the account's deepest portfolio-tree bucket; liability accounts resolve via kind to the `liabilities_*` nodes since they are not `portfolio_group_items`; options carry `label`/`label_i18n_key` and render via `resolveNavTreeLabel`, replacing the legacy asset_groups category filter 2026-08-03), `q` (note + account + counterpart + flow-type label), `date_from`/`date_to` (inclusive), `amount_exact` XOR `amount_min`/`amount_max` + `amount_currency` (CLP/USD selector, clp default: filters compare the rounded |leg| of that currency, and a row with no leg in it never matches; exact disables min/max) — on every consumer: the **net-worth dashboard is the master view** (`/api/groups/net_worth/flows`), plus group and account pages. Shared filter logic: `applyFlowFilters` + `parseExtraFlowsFilterParams` (`server/src/flowsApi.ts`), parsed by both flows routes in `routes/dashboard.ts`. Scope is **movements only** — CC purchase lines live in `cc_statement_lines` and are searched from the Expenses page.

## XLSX export (account / group pages)

- **Exportar** button (account detail toolbar + `GroupInfoBase` toolbar) → modal (range presets incl. per-year, section checkboxes, CLP/USD) → `GET /api/accounts/:id/export.xlsx` / `/api/groups/:slug/export.xlsx` (`from`/`to` = inclusive YYYY-MM, `sections=closings,aportes,pl,movements`, `unit=usd` optional). Builder: `server/src/exportWorkbook.ts` (SheetJS; one sheet per section, Spanish sheet titles, raw numeric cells). Sections reuse `monthlyPerfForAccountId` (Cierres + P&L; the dashboard card's series), `getMergedDisplayDepositInflowEventsForAccount` (Aportes — withdrawals are negative rows; `acumulado` runs over full history then range-filters), `listAccountMovementsForApi(Bulk)` (Movimientos). **Every figure of Cierres, P&L and Aportes is in the requested unit and its header names it (2026-11-12): `cierre_<unit>`, `pl_nominal_<unit>`, `monto_<unit>` / `acumulado_<unit>` (each aporte at its own day's rate, `flowEventInUnit`; the running total rounded to the unit's coin); Movimientos keeps each movement's legs as stored, `monto_clp` / `monto_usd` on every row and `monto_<code>` for any other leg currency, never converted.** Group export = per-member-account rows with a `cuenta` column (`listAccountsForGroupTab`, synthetic ids filtered). Client: `ExportToolbarButton` (`client/src/components/export/ExportModal.tsx`) + `downloadFile.ts` (blob download, filename from Content-Disposition).

## Wealth percentile (/wealth-percentile)

- **The distribution table is static data that must be refreshed by hand once or twice a year** (`server/src/wealthDistributions.ts`; procedure, source inventory and open items in **`docs/wealth-percentile-data-refresh.md`**). Anchors: UBS Global Wealth Databook 2023 (end-2016 → 2022, full country table) and UBS GWR 2026 (end-2025). The 2024 / 2025 / 2026 reports each state their figures are not comparable with the previous edition (US median 124,041 → 68,998), so medians are never mixed across editions: 2023/2024 are geometric interpolations (`†`), and a year with no printed median is calibrated on the Gini table + Millionaire Index (`‡`: CL 2025 median, CN 2025). The Spanish Latin-America edition of each GWR is the only place UBS prints per-adult levels for CL/MX/BR (2026-10-21: CL mean 66,400, MX 75,000 / 14,500, BR 27,000 / 5,090 — the last two replaced the reconstructed rows; Mexico's printed pair implies a fatter lognormal tail than its published Gini and millionaire count, kept as printed). `interpolatedRecords()` hardcodes the 2022→2025 pair — generalize it before the end-2026 row.

## Tax return (/tax-return, 2026-09-30)

- **A local Formulario 22 per año tributario** (`f22/draft.ts`, `f22/payload.ts`, `GET /api/tax-return?tax_year=`, `TaxReturnPage`): the return as filed, what third parties informed, and a draft that adds what the app knows. Inputs come from the SII's own documents, imported per year by `server/scripts/import-sii-tax-year.ts --year= --dir=cfraser/sii/AT<year> [--apply]` (migration 198): the «F22 Compacto» PDF → `sii_f22_filed` (`siiF22Compacto.ts` reads the form's fixed grid and checks its identities: 304 = 305, 170 = 158 − 750, 91 = 90 + 39) and each `DJ_<code>_<year>_<rut>.xlsx` summary → `sii_informed_dj` (`siiInformedDj.ts`; fields keyed by column letter + header path).
- **One set of tax-year facts, returns that map them (2026-11-10; `server/src/taxFacts/`, `server/src/f22/`; docs/demo-app-features-plan.md §5 items 13 and 15).** `taxFacts/` states what happened in a `DateWindow`, in the currency it happened in, with its source, and nothing a law decides: `dividendFacts` (net, the broker document's gross / withholding / jurisdiction, the receiving cash's payers, a US retirement account), `interestFacts` (every `savings_earnings` with its payers), `equityAccountDisposalFacts` / `cryptoDisposalFacts` / `fundDisposalFacts` (the lot walks' disposals with their slices, in the account's trading currency; the lot method is the caller's), `rentalFacts` (rent credits, every bill row, DFL-2, property, loan, first appraisal), `loanPaymentFacts`, `chileanPayslipFacts` / `usPayStubFacts` / `feeReceiptFacts`, `fundHoldingFacts` (cuotas and value at a window's open and close). Where an income comes from is `taxFacts/payers.ts` alone: a broker's country (`FOREIGN_BROKER_COUNTRY`, any other broker Chilean) and a bank's (`US_BANK_ISSUERS`), read from `broker_cash` roles and `bank_account_numbers`. No conversion, no reajuste, no code there. A return is a mapper: the F22's blocks under `f22/` (`salary`, `fees`, `rentals`, `art107`, `crypto`, `foreignShares`, `foreignIncome`, `dividends`, `usdFx` — the dollar lots stay F22-only, priced in pesos by the oficios —, `estimates`) take the facts and apply the Chilean rule (which enter, at which rate and reajuste, into which code); `blocks.ts` reads a window's blocks, `resident.ts` / `nonresident.ts` are the regimes, `chain.ts` the arithmetic, `sources.ts` the filed form and DJs, `offsets.ts` the pools, `draft.ts` composes. A new reader of a tax year's ledger data adds a fact, never a second SQL in a return. Parity check for a refactor here: `server/scripts/capture-tax-payloads.ts` (every F22 and 1040 payload of a database, sorted keys) before and after, byte-identical.
- **Fees from the boletas issued; the lender's interest through the cap (2026-11-09; migration 296 — numbered 269 on main until the untangle merge).** For a year with no filed form the draft builds the fees box from `sii_issued_fee_receipts` (the boletas de honorarios issued: gross, retention, who paid it): 547 gross, 494 presumed expenses (30 % of the gross, at most 15 UTA), 770 an APV régimen B deposit when no employer reported the year (DJ 1887 absent; 765 otherwise), 110 = 547 − 494 − 770, 198 the retention credit; a year with only boletas has base `fees`, and fees add to an informed or payroll base. The pension contributions the SII charges on fees (900) are not drafted (SIS, accident-insurance and coverage-ramp rates are not on file); a filed form carries them. The lender's DJ 1898 is the interest PAID (informed code 751); the deduction 750 is drafted from it by art. 55 bis (`mortgageInterestDeduction`: at most 8 UTA, cut between 90 and 150 UTA of gross income) in every base — it used to enter 750 in full, uncapped, and over a filed form it replaced the filed 750. The page labels any code `taxReturn.codes.<code>` names (`i18n.exists`; the hard-coded list that left 110 / 198 / 900 unlabelled is gone).
- **The chain must reproduce the filed return before it adds anything:** 158 = 1098 + 155 + 152 + 1032 + 1104 + 748 − 169, 170 = 158 − 750, 157 = IGC table (UTA of December; `IGC_TABLE_UTA`, AT2021+), 136 = 157 × 152 / 158, 304 = 157 − 136 − 162 − 1018; `buildF22Draft` throws when the filed codes do not give the filed 158/170/157/136/304 (AT2026 reproduces to the peso). Additions: crypto (`f22/crypto.ts`: code 1032, a net loss into 169 — average cost, commissions excluded, cost reajustado and the result reajustado to December with the SII's index-ratio percentages), foreign dividends (1104 net, 748 withheld, 1018 credit capped at 35%, at the 31-December observado; a dividend without its gross/withholding detail throws) and foreign share gains (`foreignShareGainsForYear`, default frame). The payment section (39, 90, 91, …) is shown as filed and left to the SII.
- **Every año tributario has a tab, form or not (2026-09-30):** from the one declaring the portfolio's first year through the current one (`availableF22TaxYears`: AT2017 → next April's), plus any older filed year. The draft's base is, in order: the filed form, the informed DJs, the imported liquidaciones (`f22/salary.ts`: 1098 / 161 / 162 rebuilt as DJ 1887 reports them — haberes less TOTAL NO IMPONIBLE and the worker's AFP, health up to 7% of the taxable base and cesantía, each month actualizado by the SII year-end factor; AT2023 and AT2026 to the peso), or nothing (only the app's codes, no tax). A month whose contributions or totals were not parsed is listed, never guessed (2018-10/12, 2019-01/05). An open income year is provisional: the latest UTA, dólar observado and IPC stand in for December's. The difference column needs both the filed and the draft value. A dividend without its gross / withholding detail is listed as pending and left out of 1104 / 748 / 1018. Codes the app states (1032, 1104, 748, 1018) replace the filed value. The chain reproduces every filed return AT2019–AT2026. The compact form's reader handles AT2019–AT2026 layouts (the Talana payroll parser also reads the TOTAL IMPONIBLE / NO IMPONIBLE header columns now).
- **Ledger estimates for what third parties report in March (2026-09-30; `f22/estimates.ts`):** only for a year whose base is the liquidaciones or nothing, the draft estimates fund redemptions (codes 155 / 1869, losses into 169: the non-APV Fintual goals, FIFO, lot cost IPC-reajustado, result actualizado — 2025 redemptions 74.050.000 equal Fintual's DJ 1894 redeemed + reinvested, gain within 0,9%) and mortgage interest (751 paid; 750 after art. 55 bis: min(interest, 8 UTA), proportional between 90 and 150 UTA — 2025 +1,9% vs the lender's DJ 1898). The rows are flagged `estimated` («≈» on the page). Open: the lender reported 1.327.923 of 2024 interest while the ledger has 3,38–3,54 M.
- **The IPC reajuste uses the printed index ratio inside a base** (`officialIpcVariationPctBetween`): January → November 2025 is 109,47 / 105,62 = 3,645%, the SII's 3,6% (chaining the one-decimal variations gives 3,7%); a base change (a printed ratio more than 1 point off the published variation) is bridged by that month's variation.
- **One legal source, one reajuste, one set of F22 identities (2026-11-07; `legalParameters.ts`, `siiOfficialIpc.ts`, `f22/identities.ts`).** `legalParameters.ts` is a leaf with the dated law: the IGC tables by año tributario (`igcTax`, `igcMarginalRate`; the monthly impuesto único is `iuscTax`, the same factors in UTM), the pension cap by year (the unemployment cap derived at 1,5×), the mandatory 10 %, health 7 %, AFC and AFP rates, the minimum wage and the gratification cap, the boleta retention schedule, the independent worker's contribution and presumed-expense rates, and the SII's one-decimal reajuste rounding; a year a dated table does not list throws (refresh it each January — the demo needs next year's caps and minimum wage by then). The F22 draft, the payslip checks and the demo read it. Every IPC reajuste goes through `ipcReajuste({round, floorAtZero})` (`pct`, `yearEndPct`, `december` → `decemberReajuste(incomeYear, latestMonth)`), with `monthBeforeYmd` beside it. The F22 identities (170, 305, 91) live in `f22/identities.ts`: the compact-form reader checks with them and the draft computes with them. Taxable pay, the legal health contribution and the impuesto único base are decided once in `payslipLineKinds.ts` (`payslipHaberIsTaxable`, `legalHealthClp`, `iuscTaxableClp`) for the checks, the payroll tax year and the gross-vs-net breakdown.
- **Art. 107 LIR: fund units and shares sold on the exchange (2026-10-09; `art107Instruments.ts`, `f22/art107.ts`, migration 230 `art107_instruments`).** `art107_instruments` (ticker → `fund` | `share`, seeded with `CFIETFIPSA.SN` as a fund) marks the instruments: the `.SN` suffix never decides it, since a Chilean share is art. 107 N°1 (code 1809) and a fund N°2 (1813). `f22/art107.ts` takes the lots through `taxLots.ts` (FIFO default) and prices each disposal under both resident cost options: `cost_paid` (the pesos paid, reajustados by IPC from the month before the purchase) and `close_dec31` (the 31-December close of the purchase year from `equity_daily`, a stand-in for the CMF's precio de cierre oficial, reajustado from November; null when no close is stored or the lot was bought in the open year). The draft takes the lower result (`default_option`) and reports both, since the choice is irrevocable once filed. The regime is by sale date (`art107RegimeForSale`): `inr` before 2022-09-02, `tax_10pct` from 2022-09-02 through 2026-12-31 (flat 10% único, outside the IGC base), `inr` from `ART107_INR_FROM` = 2027-01-01 (ingreso no renta: listed with its result, no code, no carry). Codes: 1813 / 1809 = the year's `tax_10pct` results under the default option, 1814 = their sum, 1815 = the previous AT's negative 1816 reajustado (the filed form's when filed, else the app's own: `carried_loss_source`), 1816 = 1814 + 1815, line 66 1829 = max(0, 1816) and 1830 = 10% of 1829; `305 = 304 + 1830 − 198 + 900` in the draft and in `assertF22Identities`, and 304 / 158 / 170 / 169 never see these codes. A loss is pooled only with other art. 107 gains, never with 169. Fund distributions are dividends afectos al IGC in línea 2: code 105 is estimated from the CLP `dividend_payout` out of the fund account (`art107DistributionsForYear`), replaced by DJ 1922 B3 when imported, and 610 is the DJ 1922 B4 credit; `computeF22Tax` adds 105 to 158 and subtracts 610 in 304, and 105 joins `CAPITAL_LOSS_POOL_CODES`. `f22/foreignShares` skips art. 107 accounts explicitly.
- **Foreign currency is tax lots with one walk under the card rate and the tax (2026-10-09; any foreign currency since 2026-11-12, docs/any-currency-plan.md C2; `foreignCashLotWalk.ts`, `foreignCashTaxLotEvents.ts`, `f22/foreignFx.ts`, `bcentralObservado.ts`; plan `docs/tax-usd-lots-plan.md`).** `foreignCashLotWalk.ts` keeps one FIFO queue of slices per foreign cash account, in the account's currency (the scope maps each account to its `cash_currency`; a slice counts integer minor units, `currencyMinorDigits`), with the original purchase date and cost (Oficio 233/2018: FIFO or LIFO, never average), the request-time reservation of a broker withdrawal, own transfers carrying slices intact (only between two accounts of the SAME currency), and a caller-priced inflow. **A conversion between two foreign currencies** (a transfer between two foreign cash accounts of different currencies: from-leg in one, counter leg in the other) is a disposal of the first (`convertedTo` on the outflow, nothing carried) and an acquisition of the second (source `conversion`, priced by the caller with the slices the row consumed): the cost tracer carries those slices' pesos over, the tax module prices the new lot at its own observado and realizes the old one under 2573 and 2390 (deferred under `none`). The cost tracer (`foreignCashCostLots.ts`, `feeCostToNet: true` — the delivered money bears the fee's pesos, one `own_transfer` lot per carried slice) and the tax module (`feeCostToNet: false`) both run on it. `foreignCashTaxDisposals({posture, purchaseCost})` lists every disposal over every foreign cash account, each tagged with its currency and priced in pesos at that currency's Banco Central observado (`observadoClpOnOrBefore`: the dollar's `fx_daily_bcentral`, the euro's `eur_daily` — Oficio 2573/2022 states its rule for «moneda extranjera» at «el tipo de cambio observado de la moneda que se trate, publicado por el Banco Central»; any other currency throws, naming it; at most 7 days carried): a `stock_buy` (realized under `oficio_2573`, deferred under `oficio_2390` / `none`), a `pago_tarjeta` or any outflow to an account outside the scope (Oficio 2573's «utilizarlos»: realized under 2573 AND 2390, deferred under `none`), a reconversion to pesos (realized unless `none`), a `cash_fee` at zero proceeds tagged `fee` (a lost cost, never deductible — the SII's crypto FAQ citing Oficio 1474/2020, Oficio 2208/2022); anything else throws. `foreignFxTaxGainsForYear` reajusts the RESULT to December (the cost is not reajustado, Oficio 2390), sums the currencies into one December result (`byCurrency` states each one's share) and gives the F22 codes by route: `idpc_1901` (default; AT2026 instructions line 58 d): first-category income, code 1901 summed into 158, the 25 % IDPC reported beside and credited back in line 5, never in the 169 pool, a loss lost) or `igc_1032` (adds to 1032, a loss into 169 like crypto). `buildF22Draft(taxYear, today, {foreignFx: {route, posture, purchaseCost, load}})`; the payload's `foreign_fx` rows carry `currency` + `amount` and `by_currency` (pesos), and the page's «Diferencias de cambio» section, report-only, lists the amount in its currency and one line per currency when there are several. The dollar-only readers keep `dollarObservadoOnOrBefore` (foreign shares, the year-end rate). Renamed from the `usd*` modules with every CLP / USD payload byte-identical on a copy of the 2026-11-12 database (all 11 F22 years and the 1040-less capture, the disposals under all six posture × cost options, the tracer, the cards' paid rates, dollar payments and expense lines). On the real ledger (2026-10-09): 26 disposals, none open; under 2573 the 2026 ones are realized, under 2390 only the 10-09 card payment.
 DJ 1891 «Monto Total Ventas» and DJ 1922 B1 («…actualizada») are the informed cross-checks in the payload's `art107` block (`informed_sales_clp`, `informed_result_clp`), with the sales and distributions behind them; the caja's `cash_fee` stays out of the return.
- **A declared tax residency decides the F22's regime (2026-11-10; migration 302 `tax_residency_periods`, `taxResidency.ts`, `dateWindow.ts`, `GET|POST /api/tax-residency`, `DELETE …/:id`, the Settings panel's «Tax residency»; docs/demo-app-features-plan.md §1, wave R1).** Where the person is a tax resident is DECLARED from a day (`cl` | `us`; one open period, a new one closes the previous the day before; only the latest can be deleted; the demo generator declares `person.taxResidency` before the April loop) — never inferred from stubs, days abroad or accounts, and every return built on a declaration says so (`residency.assumption`, i18n `taxReturn.residency.assumption`: «Declared, not inferred. Chile may hold that domicile continued while the main seat of business stayed in Chile (LIR art. 4)…»; the SII's Oficio 2306/2016 reads the asiento principal as where most income is earned and the main interests sit). With nothing declared a year is Chile-resident, as always. `residencyForIncomeYear` gives `resident`, `nonresident` or `split` (the departure year; a return to Chile or two changes in a year throws). **A non-resident year** declares Chilean-source income only (LIR art. 3) under the **Impuesto Adicional of art. 60 inc. 1, 35 %, F22 línea 72**: the taxable rent still runs 1849 → 955 → 158, then 32 = max(0, 158) (Instrucciones 72.2 a): the SUB TOTAL, so the rebajas 750 / 765 never reach it), 34 = max(0, round(35 % × 32) − 76), 304 = 0, 305 = 304 + 34 + 952 + 1830 − 198 − 833 + 900 (`f22/identities.ts`, held by `assertF22Identities`; `computeF22Tax(…, "nonresident")`); a DFL-2 rent is exempt for the non-resident too (DFL N° 2 art. 15: «no se considerarán para los efectos del Impuesto Global Complementario ni Adicional»); art. 107 sales keep línea 66's 10 % único at the general-rule cost (N°7; the Dec-31 close is the residents' option) with the broker's withholding (N°6) — the plan's «INR for a non-resident» reading was wrong. **What a non-resident window does not declare stays in the payload as `excluded` items with a reason**, never silently gone: the US salary, foreign dividends / interest / share gains and the dollar lots (`nonresident`, art. 3); crypto (`treaty_art13_8`: the Chile–US convention leaves gains on «any other property» to the residence State — the SII's own position on a non-resident's digital assets was not found, UNVERIFIED); a Chilean fund's redemptions and distributions (`fund_unico_withheld`: Ley 20.712 art. 82 B's 10 % único withheld by the administradora; línea 69's 951 / 952 only when the gain could not be determined; whether convention art. 13(8) reaches fund cuotas is UNVERIFIED); Chilean bank interest (`withheld_at_source`, arts. 59 N°1 / 74 N°4); 750 / 751, 765 and 1637 (`no_igc`). A Chilean salary or boleta inside a non-resident window throws (art. 60 inc. 2 not modelled). **The departure year is split:** every year loader takes a `DateWindow` (`payrollTaxYear`, `rentalsForYear`, `cryptoTaxGainsForYear`, `foreignShareGainsForYear`, `usdFxTaxGainsForYear`, `art107ForTaxYear` (+ `costChoice`, `carry`), `loadF22Dividends`, `foreignSalaryForYear`, `foreignInterestForYear`, `fundRedemptionGainsForYear`, `mortgageInterestForYear`); the resident part is the usual computation through the declared day, the non-resident part the Adicional on the Chilean-source items after it; one form cannot carry two bases, so the combined codes are the resident part's plus 32 / 34 (the part's own 1849 / 955 / 158 stay in `nonresident.codes`), art. 107 summed with one carry, 305 over both; a yearly DJ figure (1887, 1890, 1894, 1899) stays whole in the resident part, the lender's DJ 1898 is replaced by the ledger's interest through the day. LIR art. 103's departure return (prorated brackets and credits) is noted (`art103_departure_return_built: false`), not built. The filed-form check reproduces each regime's own chain. `tax_filed` / `tax_draft` on the payload are 304 + 34. The demo: AT2025 split (Chilean salary Jan–Apr under the IGC, Vitacura's rent after 2024-04-15 at 35 %), AT2026 non-resident (the rent alone: 32 = 13.136.220, 34 = 4.597.677); the 1040 side is untouched (wave R2 reads the Adicional).
- **A Form 1040 per US tax year (2026-11-09; the worldwide return 2026-11-10; `server/src/us1040/`, migration 301; the law read from primary sources in `docs/us-1040-readings.md`, which is the spec).** One pure module per form, each `(facts, law) → lines` with its own test (`forms.test.ts`): `scheduleB.ts`, `form8949.ts`, `scheduleD.ts` (the US$3.000 loss limit and the Capital Loss Carryover Worksheet), `qualifiedDividendsWorksheet.ts` (line 16 at 0 / 15 / 20 %), `form8960.ts` (NIIT), `schedule1.ts`, `schedule2.ts`, `dualStatus.ts`; `identities.ts` states how the lines add up (roles in the 2024 numbering; the 2025 form prints 7a, 11a / 11b, 12e — box 12c the dual-status alien —, 13a and adds 13b: `f1040PrintedLine`, `f1040RoleOfPrintedLine`, `f1040YearHasLine`; a 2024 return carrying 13b throws), `facts.ts` reads the jurisdiction-neutral `taxFacts/` into dollar items, `fx.ts` is the one currency rule (`fx_daily` mid of each item's own day, at most 7 days back — basis at purchase, proceeds at sale, income at receipt; the observado stays the F22's), `pfic.ts` the one PFIC predicate (a Santiago fund per `art107_instruments`, a London ETF, a fund account outside a treaty pension; the APV ASSUMED an Art. 18(4) pension, `APV_TREATED_AS_TREATY_PENSION`), `draft.ts` composes and `payload.ts` serves `GET /api/tax-return/us` (`UsTaxReturnResponse`: the rows, `schedules`, `schedule_b`, `form_8949`, `capital_loss_carryover`, `dual_status_statement`, `foreign_accounts`, the W-2s, FICA, the settlement). **Residency decides the return** (`usResidencyOfYear(residencyForIncomeYear(year))`): full → the ordinary return on worldwide income; dual-status (resident from a day) → the 1040 for the resident window (every fact dated from the day), no standard deduction, plus the nonresident statement's US-source dividends taxed at the rate in force on each pay day (`usDividendWithholdingRateForChileanResident`: 30 % before 2024-02-01, the convention's 15 % from then), its tax added to line 16 (`DUAL_STATUS_STATEMENT_TAX_LINE`, a reading the sources do not quote); nonresident with US stubs → not drafted (`usReturnStatusForYear`: a Form 1040-NR filer; the payload carries `drafted: false` and the W-2s; `buildF1040Draft` throws); nothing declared → the old v1 return (W-2s + US bank interest, full-year resident) with the note `residency_undeclared`. **What enters:** interest and dividends of every payer outside US retirement accounts and treaty pensions (Schedule B; qualified = a US-listed non-PFIC instrument, minus `INTEREST_DERIVED_FUND_TICKERS` such as SGOV; a dollar dividend without its `movement_dividend_details` throws), US tax a broker abroad withheld is a Form 1042-S payment on line 25c, never a foreign tax; disposals FIFO at every broker (box A/D through a US broker, C/F without a 1099-B, I/L for a coin from 2025), crypto with `feePolicy: "included"`, historic cost (no step-up on residency), PFICs left to Form 8621; the carryover in is the prior year's draft's. **The schedules are composed inside the draft (2026-11-10; `us1040/wired.ts`), every reading a named constant in its own module with its alternative:** Schedule E per rented place (`scheduleE.ts`: rents, the agency's commission, the loan's interest and fire insurance — the desgravamen left out —, contribuciones, gastos comunes, ADS 30 years with the land an assumed `LAND_SHARE` 20 %; Centro, a rental since 2020, on `PRE_RESIDENCY_DEPRECIATION = "from_placed_in_service"`, the alternative `fresh_on_arrival`; Vitacura at the lesser of cost and value on its conversion), UF amounts through `fx.ts`'s `clpPerUfOn`; Form 8582 (`form8582.ts`) at the modified AGI without the passive loss, the suspended loss carried from the prior draft → Schedule 1 line 5 and Form 8960 4a; Form 8621 Part IV for every `isPficInstrument` holding (`form8621.ts`), the first US year from `PFIC_ARRIVAL_BASIS_DAY` (the greater of value and cost on January 1 for units owned that day, units bought later at cost), later years from the prior draft's state → line 8z and Form 8960 5a, a sale's capital part a Form 8949 row read long-term (`PFIC_CAPITAL_LOSS_TERM`); the option lots (`optionLotResults`: written always short-term, bought by dates, an assigned or exercised lot no row — its premium enters the share walk, `equityAccountDisposalFacts(..., { optionAdjustments: true })`, the 1040 only); Form 1116 passive (`form1116.ts`, `classifyChileanTaxes`): Chile's taxes of the same income year read from the F22's non-resident part (`f22TaxOnUsWindow` in `f22/year.ts`: code 34, the filed form's when stored, and the art. 107 1830, which is not creditable) only when Chile taxed the window (a rent, an art. 107 sale), translated at the year's average `fx_daily` (`FTC_ACCRUED_TAX_TRANSLATION`, `usdClpYearlyAverage`: Pub. 514, §986(a)(1); the alternative the payment-day spot), the limit on the rents as Form 8582 passes them plus foreign dividends and interest, ten-year carryforward (no carryback) → Schedule 3 line 1 → line 20; dividend tax another country withheld is shown, not credited. An open year reads every schedule through today. **Carryovers** (capital loss, suspended passive loss, FTC, the 8621 state) come from the prior year's draft, fully composed: `buildF1040Draft` memoizes each year once per call and stops before the first drafted declared year. **The two returns read each other only through readers that assert `taxReturnOrder`** (`assertReadsEarlierReturn`): the F22's `f1040TaxForF22` (`f22/draft.ts`; a nonresident alien's year reads `nonresident_alien_not_drafted`, no credit until the filed 1040 is stored) and the 1040's `chileanTaxForF1040`; `f22/foreignIncome.ts` and `f22/year.ts` import no 1040 module, so there is no import cycle. **Stated as not modelled** (payload notes): the qualified-dividend holding period, wash sales on the stocks and ETFs, §988 (the UF loans' repayments included), Form 2210, Form 8621 Part V (flagged `pfic_part_v_possible`), the FTC carryback, the covered-call straddle rules, the Form 8833 positions (flagged), the state return (`no_state_return`); FBAR / Form 8938 are flags computed at month ends (coins at an exchange left out, a payerless broker cash ledger read as Chilean), never filed. **Order:** `taxReturnOrder(incomeYear)` (`server/src/taxReturnOrder.ts`): a Chilean-resident year drafts the 1040 first (the F22's foreign-salary credit reads its line 24), a split or non-resident year the F22 first (the 1040's Form 1116 will read Chile's tax); the demo's April loop (`aprilSettlementsFromTaxModules`) and `writeDemoFiledReturns` follow it per year. Filed storage: `us_f1040_returns` / `us_f1040_filed_lines` / `us_f1040_settlements`, `recordFiledF1040` (write-once, checked by `assertF1040Identities`) and `linkF1040Settlement`. The W-2s come from the stored stubs (`usW2.ts`); the law from `usLegalParameters.ts` (a closed year it does not list throws; the open year takes the latest listed year with `law_provisional`). On the demo (synthetic market): 2024 dual-status (from 2024-04-15) owes US$763 (the rental loss US$350 allowed), 2025 owes US$777 (8z US$1.555 from the two PFICs, US$124 of expired puts, the rental loss US$2.251 suspended), Form 1116 credits nothing in either (the rental nets to a loss, so the limit is 0) and carries US$3.064 + 4.770 forward.
- **The F22 across the border (2026-11-09; readings re-read against the sources 2026-11-10; `f22/foreignIncome.ts`; the demo's US chapter).** Sources: the AT2026 form (`f22_at2026.pdf`) and the SII's per-line instructions (`sii.cl/servicios_online/renta/2026/archivos/l<N>_instruccion.pdf`), Circular 31/2021 (art. 41 A), observation G08, the Chile–US convention — each quoted in the module header. A Chilean resident's US W-2 salary (the `jurisdiction = 'us'` payslips through `usW2.ts`, never `payrollTaxYear`) is **code 1030, línea 13** («Sueldos y otras rentas similares de fuente extranjera», declared «hayan soportado o no impuestos en el extranjero»; 161 = 1098 + 1030, 158 includes 1030): **the gross pay less the US income tax, nothing else** — Circular 31 §1.2.2.2.2 lets an art. 42 N°1 income deduct only the compulsory pension and health contributions «establecidas en la legislación chilena», so Social Security and Medicare stay in (until 2026-11-10 they were taken off; the 401(k), HSA and premiums were always in) — **converted at the dólar observado of 31 December, no IPC reajuste** (art. 41 A N°7 letra a) as Circular 31 §1.3.2.2 reads it for a person without books; its annex 3 converts a June salary at the year end; before, each stub at its pay day and reajustado). The **credit** is the 1040's line 24 (the filed return's when stored, else the draft's; FICA is no creditable tax — the convention's art. 2 excludes social security taxes) at the same year-end rate, the Crédito Total Disponible being the tope individual only (min of the tax and 35 % of the gross, `legalParameters.ts`), entered as the incremento in 748 (línea 14) and the credit in **1018 (línea 45**, «arts. 41 A N°4 letra B) o N° 5»), then capped at the IGC on that income by Circular 31's one-decimal proportion (`f22ForeignSalaryCreditCap`: 157 × round₁(100 × (1030 + its 748) / 158) %); no refund of the excess. **746 is línea 49, a Chilean company's pass-through credit on its dividends (Certificados 43 / 44 / 70 / 71), not a natural person's own foreign dividends — those stay in 1104 / 748 / 1018 as the instructions for líneas 12, 14 and 45 say; G08 names both codes because it covers both.** The chain reads a filed 746 as a credit in 304 (`f22Code304`: 157 − 136 − 1637 − 162 − 1018 − 610 − 746) and never drafts it. A US salary before `CHILE_US_TREATY_FIRST_INCOME_YEAR` (2024) throws. **IBKR's sweep / SGOV interest** (`savings_earnings` on the dollar cash of a `FOREIGN_BROKERS` role holder) and a US bank account's interest (`usBankAccounts.ts`, since 2026-11-09) are 1104 at the 31-December observado with no credit; its dividends were already 1104 / 748 / 1018 through `movement_dividend_details` (0 % withheld adds 0). The payload carries `foreign_income` (`TaxReturnForeignIncome`) and the F22 tab a «Foreign income» section. **DJ 1898 is one column per lender** (`informedDjSum` adds them; the art. 55 bis cap applies across both), and `mortgageInterestForYear(year, loanAccountId?)` reads one loan. **The demo's April loop files each year's returns in `taxReturnOrder`'s order (see the 1040 bullet):** each pass computes the 1040 settlements (`aprilSettlementsFromTaxModules`: a refund «IRS TREAS 310 TAX REF» 21 days after filing on about the 8th, or a payment «IRS USATAXPYMT» on the 15th, on US checking, written with `recordFiledF1040` + `linkF1040Settlement`), and the F22's (a Chilean-resident year's F22 reads the filed 1040's tax); the simulator's own first 1040 estimate is W-2 + interest only (`f1040LinesFromW2s`), so the 1040 needs a second pass once dividends enter it (its savings interest moves with the wires the F22 settlements size, one more pass) and the F22 needs a second pass for the salary (3 passes, ~1,5 min on the demo). Known: a Chilean resident's dollar spending abroad stays a dollar-lot disposal under the módulo's postures (AT2025: 286 disposals on the demo) — the user's decision, not the plan's. **A DJ 1898 column is headed by the lender (2026-11-10; `lenderNames.ts`):** `lenderDisplayName(loanAccountId)` names a loan's lender from its `lender_pattern` role — the vendor catalog's display name for the first pattern it knows, else the pattern spelled as a name; a loan without the role throws — and the demo's `taxDocuments.ts` heads each lender's column with it («Hipotecaria Pacifico», «MetLife Hipotecario»), where it printed the app's loan account name. The draft reads the column by its interest header, so nothing it computes moves.

## Projections (/projections)

- `GET /api/projections` (`server/src/projections.ts`, pure engine `runProjectionEngine`): one **real** (today's-money) trajectory in the display unit — accumulation to age 65 (born 1992-01 → retiro 2057-01; invested compounds monthly + aporte, RE/cash hold real value flat), then three drawdown strategies over the **drawdown pot** = invested + `liquidate_other_pct`% of the non-invested remainder (default 0 — FIRE framing; 100 ≙ total NW, anything between = partial RE sale at 65) to `end_age`. `monthly_rent` (today's money) adds passive income to every strategy; the fixed-income strategy targets TOTAL income so its pot only funds `target − rent` (SWR fixed-real %, % of current balance, fixed real monthly income; depletion ages in `summary`). The invested trajectory is emitted as its own projected line (`proj_invested`). Nominal line derives from the real path with the unit's own inflation (`inflation_pct[unit]`; one input per core currency, query key `inflation_<code>_pct`, defaults in `PROJECTION_INFLATION_DEFAULT_PCT` — CLP 3,5 %, USD 2,5 %, EUR 2,0 %, assumptions, served as `inflation_default_pct` and stated under the form; a core currency without a default throws) — every currency view shares the trajectory (independent nominal paths would drift). Money inputs (`monthly_aporte`, `monthly_income`, `monthly_rent`, 2026-11-12) are amounts in the currency the request names (`<key>_currency`, default the unit), converted into the unit at today's stored rate (`projectionParamsFromQuery`), echoed in the unit. Defaults: aporte = trailing-24m personal deposits into retirement+brokerage (pesos, converted); base = last `getDashboardOverviewBlock` point; params bounded (`ProjectionParamError` → 400). USD milestones (250k–2M) as constant reference columns, converted at today's stored rate.
- Client `ProjectionsPage` (`/projections`, sidebar link `projections` seeded by `seedNavTree`): number inputs display server-resolved params, overrides persist in localStorage `nw-tracker.projections.overrides.v2` as the query parameters the user changed (a money input with the currency it was typed in, so it survives a unit switch; the old peso-keyed `nw-tracker.projections.overrides` is migrated once, `projectionsOverrides.ts`; empty = server default; Restablecer clears); unit follows the global display unit; chart = AppLineChart with dashed reference lines.

## Net-worth portfolio tree (`portfolio_groups`)

- **Single nav tree** from `net_worth`: sidebar and group pages use `portfolio_group_items` (`accountIdsInPortfolioGroup` in `server/src/portfolioGroupTree.ts`).
- **`group_kind`**: `bucket` (routable leaf/hub with accounts), `nav_bucket` (sidebar only — home cards use children with `dashboard_bucket_slug`), `liability_group`, `reference`.
- **Pasivos › tarjeta de crédito holds its cards directly (2026-11-04; migration 262).** The issuer groups (`credit_card_groups` / `credit_card_group_items`) and their pages (`/liabilities/credit-card/<issuer>`, which now 404) are gone: `seedLiabilitiesTree` links every operational card master (superseded ones left out) to `liabilities_credit_card` as a `liability_group_items` account item, in issuer order (`listCreditCardMasterAccountIdsInNavOrder`), the way `liabilities_mortgage` holds its account. A card's issuer is `credit_card_account_config.issuer` (backfilled from the import key, which stays the card's identity only); `listCreditCardMasterAccountIdsForIssuer` serves `import:cc-parsed --santander`. A nav-retired card leaves the nav tree but stays in the group's `member_account_ids`, its tab rows and its charts. A Pasivos group takes a colour like a portfolio group (migration 263): `liability_groups.color_rgb`, set from the panel's groups table (`PATCH /api/liability-groups/:slug/color`), else its largest member's (`colorRgbOfLargestBalanceAccount`); its nav node carries `liability_group_id` as the colour target. **A page's accounts tree places what the nav leaves out by membership (same date):** «Cuentas en esta vista» (`enrichNavTreeWithAllAccounts`) adds each account of the page's list that the nav tree omits (emptied accounts, retired cards) under the deepest group whose `member_account_ids` names it, and throws for one no group names; it used to match bucket slugs, and the last card group visited won every tie (·0161 and CMR listed under BCI). The Pasivos root's members are its liability children's (`withLiabilitiesChildren` in `navTree.ts`; it reported none).
- **`kind_slug`**: behavior id (`afp`, `cuenta_corriente`, …) on leaf buckets — prefer `kindSlugForAccount` / `accountKindSlugForAccountId` over parsing `__` from slugs.
- **`accounts.primary_portfolio_group_id`**: deepest portfolio link (migration `098_…`); set by `seedNavTree`.
- **API**: prefer `?portfolio_group=retirement_afp_afc` on `/api/accounts`, valuation TS, consolidated tables. Legacy `?group=&subgroup=` resolves via `resolvePortfolioGroupSlugForLegacyTab`.
- **Home bucket cards**: `getDashboardLayoutCards()` unwraps `nav_bucket` under `net_worth` (e.g. `inversiones` → brokerage + retirement; `cash_eqs` hub → `cash_savings` card only).
- **Bucket totals**: sum accounts via `accountIdsInPortfolioGroupForTotals(portfolio_group_slug)` — not per-account `dashboard_bucket_slug` tags. `portfolio_groups.slug` / `kind_slug` are stable node ids and behavior keys, not parsed `__` heuristics.
- **A managed portfolio is one bucket, filed under Acciones (2026-09-28; migrations 190 + 192–194, `linkBucketSubtree` in `seedNavTree.ts`).** A broker-managed portfolio (the Racional «Portafolio IPSA»: its ETF holding + the caja the 1%/yr fee is charged from as `cash_fee`) is a brokerage bucket backed by its OWN asset group, with the members' leaf asset groups **reparented** under it — leaf slugs, and so each account's behavior kind (`accountBucketKindSlug`: ticker, `clp`), stay as they were, and `portfolioCajaClpAccountId`'s `brokerage_cash__caja_*` glob still finds the caja. Filed apart, the fee read as −5 %/month on a tiny cash account; together the bucket's % is (holding P/L − fee) ÷ the whole portfolio, and brokerage / inversiones / net worth are unchanged (same member set, verified to the peso). Since migration 192 the unit's asset group AND portfolio group sit under `brokerage_acciones`, labelled «IPSA» (193) at route `/inversiones/brokerage/acciones/ipsa` with members «CFIETFIPSA» and «Caja IPSA» (194; old URLs 404, slug `brokerage_portafolio_ipsa` unchanged), so Acciones holds its stocks and the unit side by side (the caja's pesos and the fee count in Acciones). **A portfolio group may hold accounts beside sub-buckets** (the old «sub-groups XOR accounts» rule is gone for the portfolio tree; the credit-card and liability trees keep it): `assertNavGroupItemsShape` instead requires each account in at most one group, and linked groups / expense places never mixed with anything — it runs inside the seed's transaction, so a violation rolls the seed back. `linkBucketSubtree` links a bucket's own leaf accounts plus, as child groups, the buckets filed under it (`portfolio_groups.parent_id`) whose asset group is nested in its own (their accounts only there), all by name — the sidebar's order. A sub-bucket beside accounts reads as a leaf of that page: one line per first-level child in Agrupado, by balance (`mixedChartChildren`; Sin agrupar opens it into its accounts), one row of the accounts table with the server's card metrics and `card_metrics_by_slug[slug].row_pct` (nav-snapshot cache v9), one card-breakdown line, and a plain sidebar entry that does not expand. The Panel offers a bucket as an account home when it has no sub-buckets or already holds accounts (`resolveBucketParentAssetSlug`, `listLeafPortfolioGroupBuckets`). Another portfolio is the same three rows (asset group, leaf reparent, `portfolio_groups` row with route/api/kind), no code. The nav label is the data label. Caveat: the Racional fee import requires exactly one `brokerage_cash__caja_*` account, so a second Racional portfolio needs that lookup keyed on the bucket instead.
- **A broker's cash balances are cash, not investments (2026-10-21; migration 241).** Racional CLP (`import:panel|kind=clp|key=clp`) and Racional USD (`…|kind=usd|key=usd`) sit under Efectivo › Cash savings on their own leaves (`cash_eqs__cash_savings__racional_clp__clp` / `…_racional_usd__usd`), like the Fintual balance accounts since 2026-10-05; the Brokerage › Cash bucket (`brokerage_cash` asset groups + portfolio group) is gone from the seeds and the demo (`cash_eqs__cash_savings__usd`). A portfolio caja keeps its `brokerage_cash__caja_*` leaf under the unit's own asset group (`portfolioCajaClpAccountId` matches the slug, not the parent). Consequence: a `stock_buy` funded from Racional CLP is an aporte into Brokerage and a sale's proceeds a withdrawal, as with Fintual.
