The first `.SN` sale (the IPSA liquidation of 2026-10-20, booked by hand as 13038/13039) also showed that `accountUsesEquityMtm` must exempt CLP cash as it does USD cash: the proceeds leg of a `stock_sell` is the wallet, never the equity side.
- **Panel / nav / dashboard** all use the same tree: `GET /api/meta/sidebar-nav` → `net_worth` (`portfolio_groups` + `portfolio_group_items`). `/api/meta/asset-tree` is legacy `asset_groups` (deprecated; do not use in new UI).

## Group page charts — server-side "Agrupado" bucketing

**All grouped chart series come from the server; the client never re-aggregates.** On group pages the "Agrupado" toggle (and the Pasivos grouped charts) collapse per-account lines into per-child-bucket lines. This is computed **server-side** in `server/src/groupChartBuckets.ts` (bucket-node selection ported from the sidebar `NavTreeNodeDto` walk) and emitted as sibling fields on the existing responses — no extra HTTP round-trips, toggle stays instant:

- **Valuation** (`getGroupValuationTimeseries(..., { groupedBlocks: true })`, `valuationTimeseries.ts`): `nav_grouped_blocks: { grouped, ungrouped }` + `nav_grouped_proportional`, or `liab_grouped_block` + `liab_grouped_proportional` (Pasivos, single mode — no toggle). (The `*_pie` blocks were replaced by the proportional share blocks 2026-07-26.) Built from the **pre-clip consolidated** block with `__group_val_total` / `__group_dep_total` (and the Total line) copied **verbatim**, then each grouped block is display-clipped independently.
- **Monthly P/L bars** (`getGroupMonthlyPlSeries(..., { groupedBlocks: true })`, `groupMonthlyPlSeries.ts`): `nav_grouped_bars` / `liab_grouped_bars` with `pl_nav_<slug>` / `pl_liab_<slug>` keys = per-row sums of member `pl_<id>`; an account in no chart bucket keeps its own bar, so grouped bars add up to `delta_total`.
- Only the group HTTP routes pass `groupedBlocks: true`; internal callers (dashboard bundle, totals) skip the extra work. Client `groupPageChartViews.ts` only **picks** the precomputed block by toggle state (`resolveGroupPageChartContext` reads toggle visibility from payload presence). There is no client bucket aggregation.
- **Pasivos buckets** read each liability group node's `member_account_ids` (which keep the retired cards the nav tree omits); a card bucket is the card itself — never account-name heuristics.
- Bucket line/bar/pie labels carry `name_i18n_key` (client resolves at render — translate, don't cache `t()`; deposit-line label keys `charts.groupAccumulatedDeposits` / `charts.accumulatedDeposits`).

**Tail clip is display-only** (`timeseriesTailClip.ts`, the final payload step): it nulls sold-out trailing series and bundles each line's aportes with its value (they clip together) — but it must **never** rewrite an aggregate. The group Total line (`dataKey === "__group_val_total"`) is the sole line excluded from deposit bundling, and `__group_val_total` / `__group_dep_total` are never recomputed post-clip. A client Σ over already-clipped series is the bug this replaced (brokerage "Total aportes acum." disagreed with the summary card).

## An account's value: what it holds, converted once (2026-11-02; `docs/holdings-currency-plan.md`, `accountValue.ts`, `holdingPrices.ts`, `valueConvert.ts`, `rates.ts`)

- **An account's value at a date is a quantity of what it holds, converted ONCE into the display unit.** `accountHolding(id)` (`holdings.ts`) says what that is: a currency (pesos, dollars; the property and the mortgage UF), a stock (in its quote currency), a coin (its price series in dollars), a fund's cuotas (priced in pesos) or a card. `accountHoldingQtyAt(id, ymd)` gives the quantity, `holdingPriceAt(instrument, ymd)` one unit's price in its quote currency (a past day the stored observation on or before it, kept in the history tier; today the choice today's valuation always made — the live quote in session, the held print after the close, the display session's bar, the newest bar; a fund's live / per-date Risky Norris proxy), and `accountValueAt(id, ymd, unit)` = quantity × price → `convertValue` (`valueConvert.ts` over `rates.ts`). No figure is built in pesos and converted back: a dollar stock in USD is shares × its dollar price, dollar cash in USD its ledger dollars, a peso fund in USD cuotas × peso price at the day's rate. The historical stack is `accountHistoricalQtyMark` (`accountMarkClpAtYmd.ts`: the old branch order, each branch now returning a quantity; an instrument is the asset only on a day its price is on file, else the stack falls through as before); `accountMarkClpAtYmd` is that in pesos.
- **Spread side: what closes the position.** An asset converts at what selling it would get — foreign → CLP at the sell (bid) rate, CLP → foreign at the buy (ask) rate; a liability (mortgage, card) flips it (`holdingIsDebt`). UF and EUR have no spread. A stored day reads `fx_daily` with the bid/ask observed that day or later, else the mid ∓ half of `FX_BID_ASK_SPREAD_CLP`. Flows keep their per-event rule at the mid (`flowEventInUnit`, `convertTs`, `clpToUnitAtMid`), as do benchmark levels.
- **Today reads the live rate** (USD/CLP live while the fx day is open, `fxForLiveMtm` inside `rates.ts`), so dollar cash in pesos and a peso fund in dollars move with the peso during the day. `accountTodayValue(id, slug, opts, now) → {value_clp, native_value, native_currency, side, as_of_date, priced_on}` is the one today value (dashboard row, account header, today's mark, the perf live row, every chart's live point); a reader that wants another unit converts the NATIVE value with `todayValueInUnit(id, v, unit)`. `priced_on` is information only: no conversion reads it.
- **An account's pesos handed in: `accountValueClpToUnitAt(id, clp, ymd, unit)`** converts a peso-native value at the account's side and re-reads a value native in another currency (`accountValueCurrency`) from what it holds. `clpToUnitAt(clp, ymd, unit, {side})` is for an amount that is not one account's value (the linked cards netted from cash and the liability totals at `side: "debt"`). Bucket and net-worth sums in a unit are Σ of each account's value in that unit on every day (`buildDashboardBucketDailySeriesInUnit`, the daily series, the overview, the all-time high) — never Σ pesos ÷ one rate.
- **A card's value in a unit goes through one seam, `cardValueInUnit.ts`** (today: its owed walk in pesos at the debt side); the card phase replaces its body with the per-currency sub-balances. The property and the mortgage are still the depto ledger's pesos (peso quantities) until the loan phase.
- **Stored valuations are in any currency (2026-11-02; migration 260, `valuationValue.ts`).** A `valuations` row is a quantity of what the account holds, in `currency` (any lowercase code; the CHECK no longer names clp / usd). Generic readers convert a row once, on its own date, at the account's side (`storedValuationInUnit`: `valuationLatest.ts`, `bookValuations.ts`, the chart's raw in `valuationTimeseries.ts`; the historical stack returns it as a quantity of its currency, `accountMarkClpAtYmd.ts`, whose book carry is pesos, so a non-peso book mark with a carry window throws); kind-specific readers assert the currency their rows are stated in (`assertValuationCurrency`: a card's CLP anchor values, an AFP's or a Fintual fund's peso marks); a property's rows are its UF appraisals and every mark reader leaves them out (`valuationsAreAppraisals`); the mortgage has none (migration 261 deleted its peso month-end rows: the depto ledger values it on every date, and the property's and the mortgage's chart month-ends come from the ledger, `augmentChartDatesForDeptoAccounts`). `GET /api/accounts/:id/valuations` returns `value` + `currency`; `POST` writes pesos and refuses a property.
- **The daily cache stores quantities** (`markSeriesStore.ts` cells `{qty, asset}`), values in a unit are built at read from the memoized day price and rate (`accountValueSeriesOnGrid`), so one series serves every unit; a rate row (`fx_daily`, `fx_daily_bid_ask`, `uf_daily`) trims only the accounts whose quantity is a peso figure built with rates (cards — from the row's own day: a card's value on a day reads that day's rate only —, the depto ledger, book marks), never shares, coins, cuotas or a dollar ledger (`setRateIndependentAccountsResolver`). Price rows (`equity_daily`, `fund_unit_daily`) still trim their holders: which asset a day holds depends on whether its price is on file.

## Nav card metrics — server-side (2026-07-15; cards period-independent since 2026-07-26)

**Card figures (balance, deposits, cumulative P/L, period Δ, %) come from the server; the client never re-sums account rows for a nav node.** Since 2026-07-26 every card renders ALL THREE period slices at once (Día/Mes/Año rows) — no card reads a period preference, `title_delta` was dropped from the payload (with its totals input and prior-close helpers), and the nav-snapshot cache is v8. `server/src/dashboardNavCardMetrics.ts` walks the net_worth nav tree and emits `card_metrics_by_slug: Record<portfolio_group_slug, { child, parent }>` on the page-bundle dash, nav-context, and nav-snapshot payloads (each variant = `{ day, month, year }`; `child` = the node as a strip detail card, `parent` = the node as its own page's compact card — the two compositions differ, e.g. net_worth parent = Σ strip-children child-metrics; the inversiones hub parent bakes in the served `inversiones_period_metrics` slice). The port was verified against the old client Σ with a parity script on the real DB (2,584 comparisons, zero mismatches) before the client switched; scope rules (bucket membership, cash_eqs savings-only period P/L, chart-inactive nav coverage) live only in this module now — unit-tested in `dashboardNavCardMetrics.test.ts`.

- **One series behind the card and the table (2026-10-27; `accountPeriodMetrics.ts`).** A dashboard row's month / year / lifetime deposits and P/L are `accountPeriodMetricsFromMonthlyRows` over `monthlyPerfRowsForAccount(id, slug, unit)` — the same per-account rows the group consolidation sums (`groupMonthlyPerfConsolidation.ts`: the valuation-series performance for invested accounts, the ledger walks for checking / USD / CLP cash / cuenta de ahorro) through the same picker (`pickMonthlyPerfRowsByMonth`, one representative row per month with the month's flows summed). A node's card is Σ of its rows and its «Detalle por mes / año» table Σ of the same rows per month, so the two agree to the cent on every asset group node in both units (`dashboardCardMetricsVsConsolidated.test.ts`; lifetime P/L = the table's «P/L acum.», never `current − deposits` — where that identity breaks the series has a hole to fix). Before: the card summed its own `accountCardPerformanceMetricsFromPerf` (a different picker), replaced the property's USD year P/L with the CLP figure at today's rate (`dashboardPropertyDeltaUsd`: +1.xxx against the table's −1x.xxx — the USD frame keeps the peso's move inside P/L on every row, the property included) and ran the cash bucket through a balance-change reconcile whose USD prior close was missing for checking accounts, so their USD P/L dropped out as null: the home card read 2026 at US$1x.xxx against the table's US$1x. The lifetime figures disagreed by 7x USD for two more reasons, both fixed: (1) **a sold-out line keeps the zero it reached** (`trailingZeroRunClipStartIndex`): the monthly grid is densified to month-ends with nulls, and the clip counted them as the zero run, so it kept a null gap month and dropped the liquidation zero after it — account 105 (the pre-Fintual Principal) had no row for 2018-07 and its −2x.xxx CLP realized loss was never in the tables; (2) **the live current-month row's USD frame is the mark date's fx** (`usdFromClpAtLiveFrame`, the dashboard row's own rule): it read the stored row on or before today, 0,xx USD off the AFC card (its mark is dated the previous business day), and the crypto position is now dated on the day its pesos were framed on (`accountPosition.ts`: the UTC session with a live quote, else the mark date), not the bar's `trade_date`, so a USD reader divides by the fx that multiplied and gets units × price back. The reconcile module (`dashboardCardMetricsReconcile.ts`) and `dashboardAccountCardMetrics.ts` are gone. There is one value of an account today, `accountTodayValue`, and one conversion of it, `accountValueClpToUnitAt` (see «An account's value: what it holds, converted once» below): the card, the header, the perf live row, today's mark and every chart live point read them.
- **One month series per account, every reader (2026-10-28).** Every surface that shows an account's or a group's month P/L reads `monthlyPerfRowsForAccount` (`groupMonthlyPerfConsolidation.ts`) through `pickMonthlyPerfRowsByMonth`; the valuation-series builder it wraps for invested accounts is `valuationSeriesMonthlyPerformance` (`accountPerformance.ts`, renamed from `getAccountMonthlyPerformance`), and `monthlyPerfRowsForAccount` is its only production caller. (1) **Group P/L charts** (`groupMonthlyPlSeries.ts`, `GET /api/groups/:slug/performance-monthly`): per-account bars are the picked rows, `delta_total` / `ytd_group` / `accumulated_earnings` the consolidated row's `nominal_pl` / `ytd_nominal_pl` / `cumulative_nominal_pl`, one point per consolidated month (current month dated today); checking accounts are bars now and excluded accounts are not, so Σ bars = `delta_total` = the «Detalle por mes» row (`groupMonthlyPlSeriesVsConsolidated.test.ts`). The old builder (`getGroupMonthlyPerformanceSeries` + its own picker) read the valuation series alone, dropped checking and counted excluded accounts; `getStocksLifetimeEarningsSeries` and its unused route went too. The home «Δ mensual y depósitos por clase» combo is served whole (`inversionesPerfSeries.ts`): the page bundle's `inversionesPerf` (Σ retirement + brokerage = `buildInversionesConsolidatedMonthly`, deposits joined) and `GET /api/dashboard/inversiones-perf-daily`, whose YTD / all-time levels are anchored on the monthly consolidation so the day view equals the monthly chart at the window's first month-end; the client only clips to the Rango and rolls years up. **Every day-mode P/L combo is served (2026-10-28; `dailyPerfLevels.ts`):** `GET /api/daily-series?…&perf_levels=1` adds `perf_levels` {`delta_total`, `ytd`, `accumulated`} index-aligned with the points — the scope's day `pl` with its levels anchored on the scope's own monthly rows (a group page on `getGroupConsolidatedMonthlyRows`, an account page and the mortgage section on `monthlyPerfRowsForAccount`; `anchorDailyLevels`: the window's first month-end reads the monthly row exactly), the anchor the home combo uses too. The client only maps each line's `pl` onto its bar key (`servedDailyPerfComboPoints`); the client back-solving (`dailyPerfCombo.ts`) is gone, and the `perf` variant is its own query entity so neither payload is held as the other's placeholder (`dailyPerfLevels.test.ts`). On the real DB every group and account met its monthly row at the first month-end in both units; net worth's last day sits ~11k CLP off its monthly line because its day P/L over a month does not add up to the monthly P/L, as before. (2) **Account readers** (account page «Detalle por mes» and Rentabilidad, `/api/accounts/:id/performance-monthly`, the XLSX export, the benchmark row, tail-inactive detection) read `monthlyPerfForAccountId(id, unit)` = `{account_id, bucket_slug, monthly, yearly}`, so a page equals its card (`accountDetailMonthlyVsCard.test.ts`); ledger cash used to show the valuation series (USD / CLP cash) or nothing (checking — the page hides that section for checking and the cuenta de ahorro). Ledger-cash rows now carry `pct_month` (`flowAdjustedPct`). (3) **Cuenta de ahorro** closed months take their deposit events per `(prev month-end, month-end]` at each event's own rate (`ledgerCashNetFlowBetween`); `balanceOnlyMonthlyRowsAsc` requires `netFlowBetween` — the balance − interest default moved the USD fx revaluation into the flow — and the account is dispatched by kind (its leaf is `cash_eqs__cuenta_ahorro_vivienda`; the bare-slug test had never matched, so the book path never ran on real data). (4) **A credit card's row** reads `accountPeriodMetricsFromMonthlyRows(monthlyPerfRowsForAccount(card))` (`creditCardMonthPerf`: −financing per CALENDAR month, as its «Detalle por mes»), its day P/L converts to dollars with `convertTs` at today like the rows, and the Pasivos / credit-card group cards follow (`dashboardCcCardVsDetalle.test.ts`); the billing-month summary (`creditCardFinancingPlSummaryForDashboard`, `buildCreditCardFinancingPlByBillingMonth`: «this month» could be a past facturación, dollars at today's rate) is gone. A card with no performance rows reads «—», not 0. (5) **Yearly rows and running totals are built once** (`monthlyPerfRollup.ts`): `rollupMonthlyPerfYearly` serves every «Detalle por año» (`consolidated_yearly` on consolidated-tables, `yearly` on the account payload, the server-paginated `period=year` page; the client rollup in `MonthlyPerfDetailTable` is gone and the table throws without `yearlyRows`), and a year whose months all lack a P/L or a % reads null, never 0; `withRunningPlTotals` replaced the six YTD / cumulative loops, and `monthEndCloseForAccountInUnit` (exported from accountPerformance.ts) the consolidation's twin wrapper.
- Client `portfolioNavDashboardCards.ts` only **picks**: `requireNavCardMetrics(dash, node)` (fail-fast on a missing slug — no re-summing fallback), consumed by `mainValueForNavChild` / `navParentMainValue` / `navChildCardHasActivity`, the accounts table (`PortfolioNavAccountsSummaryTable`) and the card breakdown (`navCardBreakdown.ts`). **The balance is the server's too (2026-10-28):** each entry carries `value` — `child` (the detail card and its parent's table row: the dashboard bucket total for a bucket's own node and every cash child, CC-netted, else Σ of the counted member rows), `parent` (the page's summary card and its table «Total» row: one bucket total, Σ of the bucket totals for net worth and a hub, else Σ of every subtree row), `members` (Σ of the counted member rows, never netted: a breakdown group line, so the cash hub's savings line stays raw beside the pinned card line), `fx_missing` / `sync_stale` — and `liquidated.value` the «Liquidadas» row's. The input takes the payload's own `buildDashboardNwBucketTotals`. Until then the card, the table row and the breakdown summed rows with three different client formulas (the table's group rows counted accounts excluded from group totals and never netted cash); `usesFullDashboardBucketTotals`, `isCashSavingsNavNode`, `navMetricsAccountIdSet`, `portfolioNavParentTitleModeForNavNode`, `stripMetricsRowsForNavChild`, `cardGroupMetricsFromAccounts`, `accountBelongsToDashboardBucket` and `inactiveAccountNavLeavesWithActivity` were deleted from the client. Single-account rows (table account rows, account detail) are 1-row projections (`cardMetricsByPeriodForAccountRow`). The client's one remaining copy, the counts-toward-totals visibility filter (`accountGroupTotals.ts`), is pinned to the server rule by `server/src/test/accountCountsTowardGroupTotalsCases.json`, asserted by both suites.
- The localStorage nav-snapshot cache (`dashboardNavSnapshotCache.ts`) is one of the persisted payloads (`queries/persistedPayloadCache.ts`: key `nw:cache:<name>:v<version>`, the version the payload's `schema_version` in `DTO_SCHEMA_VERSIONS`, so a changed shape drops the stored copy); a cached snapshot is shown with the numbers it carries and spins to the live ones when the bundle lands (2026-10-26: the random 0,85–0,95 scaling of every cached amount, `perturbCachedAmount.ts`, and `AnimatedNumberFlow`'s random same-digit-count mount seed are gone — together they painted three different figures on a reload; the `nw:numberFlowMount:*` keys are swept at boot), and a unit is served only by its own cache (2026-10-27: the CLP→USD synthesis of missing usd fields at one fx rate, `synthesizeMissingUsd.ts`, and the `nw:fx-latest-v1` cache feeding it are gone — a first USD visit from a loaded CLP page keeps the CLP cards, dimmed, until the USD payload lands — blank only when neither unit has anything; 2026-09-27: the client's own bucket-total re-summing, `portfolioGroupTotals.ts`, is gone). **A cached snapshot must cover the current nav tree (2026-09-28; `navSnapshotCoversNavTree`):** every group node of the net_worth tree and the Pasivos root needs a `card_metrics_by_slug` entry, or `readDashboardNavSnapshotCache` drops the stored snapshot (checked against the sidebar-nav cache, which a fresh tree reaches first) and `useDashboardNavSnapshot` hides and resets one already in the query cache, so the live snapshot loads — a snapshot saved before migration 190 added a brokerage node crashed every card page through `requireNavCardMetrics` and survived reloads. Only localStorage placeholders are checked; a live payload missing a slug still throws.
- **Membership comes from the tree, never from slug prefixes (2026-10-21).** Every group node carries `member_account_ids` — the account ids under it as `portfolio_group_items` (and the liability / credit-card group items) link them, the chart-inactive leaves the sidebar tree omits included — and `navMetricsAccountIdSet` (`dashboardNavCardMetrics.ts`; the client's port went 2026-10-28) reads nav leaves ∪ that list; the old fallback matched an inactive row's asset-group slug against the node's slug prefixes, which the reparented leaves of a managed portfolio defeat: the day the IPSA unit was liquidated (2026-10-20) both its accounts went chart-inactive, its node lost its leaves, its row read «—», CFIETFIPSA (slug `brokerage_acciones__…`) leaked into Acciones' own rows, and the caja (slug `brokerage_cash__caja_…`) fell out of the Acciones, Brokerage and Inversiones cards altogether. A chart-inactive sub-bucket listed as a row of its parent's table follows the sold-out account's rule (`navChildCardHasActivity`: shown while any period has activity, then gone); sidebar-nav cache v6, nav-snapshot cache v11.
- **A card line's label is the account's name (2026-11-09).** The account rows carry no `nav_label` (nav-snapshot cache v19): the strip breakdowns and the accounts table read `name`, the same words as the account's page and the sidebar; the hardcoded lowercase short labels by kind slug («spy», «risky norris», «apv-a-fintual», `accountNavLabel` on both sides) are gone. The real-estate card lists several properties at the first level, each with its valor and hipoteca under it (`buildRealEstateCardBreakdown`); one property keeps the head line = the property.
- **A hub the strip spreads into its children's cards gets its own summary card in row 1 (2026-09-26).** Home shows Inversiones as Retiro + Brokerage cards, so no row-2 card carried its total; `portfolioStripSummaryHubs` (`portfolioNavFromApi.ts`, the same predicate that spreads the hub) puts a card for each such hub beside the page's own, read from the nav tree, never a slug. It is drawn by the one `NavSummaryCard` that draws the hub page's hero (`parent` metrics variant + `value.parent` through `navParentMainValue`), so home Inversiones ≡ `/inversiones` by construction. With several summary cards in row 1 each is titled, the page's own too (repeating the page title), so their rows line up. Row 1 holds only summary cards: on desktop the first card after them starts a new row (`cards.css`; this replaced the hidden `.row-spacer`, which pinned row 1 to a single card).

## Data provenance (`DataOrigin`)

API payloads must **not** use top-level `source: "db"` / `"csv"` — the client always reads SQLite. Provenance describes how data entered the system:

| Layer | Field | Examples |
|-------|--------|----------|
| Account | `accounts.import_key` (identity; `notes` = same string kept as human provenance) | `import:excel|key=mortgage`, `credit_card_master|santander|<last4>`, `import:panel|ticker=QQQ|key=…` |
| Row | DTO `origin` | `import_document`, `manual` (from `cc_installment_purchases.source`: `pdf` → `import_document`) |
| Market data | (future) | `api_sync` / `api_yahoo` for `equity_daily` |

Shared type: `server/src/dataOrigin.ts` (`import_document` \| `manual` \| `api_sync`).

## Import sources vs runtime

**Rule:** files under `cfraser/` are **import inputs only**. HTTP, dashboard, valuation, and sync read **SQLite**; missing data → error or “run import”, not a silent file fallback.

- **Depto:** runtime reads the **movement ledger** — one ledger per property since 2026-11-09 (`loadDeptoLedgerForProperty(id)`, cached `depto.ledger|rows|<property id>`; `deptoLedgerForAccount` / `deptoLedgerForLoan` / `loadAllDeptoLedgers`; the pairs are `propertyLoanPairs()` in `accountRoles.ts` — a `property` role holder and its `loan_of` account; a second property's identity is `propertyAccountImportKey(key)` / `loanAccountImportKey(key)` → `import:property|key=<k>` / `import:loan|property=<k>`, migration 299's triggers seed the roles; every reader — «Próximos pagos», the valor/hipoteca overlays, the mortgage sections, the card pairing, the real-estate mortgage rows — loops over the pairs, and the lender's portal reading names its loan by its `lender_pattern`) joins property-account movements with **`loan_payments`** (migration 258, replaced `depto_payments`; docs/holdings-currency-plan.md phase 4): one row per payment movement, `leg` `property` | `loan` (the account the movement sits on), `loan_account_id`, cuota, origin, and what the bank states in the currency it states it — `balance_after`, `min_payment`, `max_payment` in `loan_currency` (UF), `principal`, `extra_principal`, `interest`, `insurance_fire`, `insurance_life` in `payment_currency` (pesos); readers throw on any other pair. Nothing derived is stored: the parts' UF (pesos ÷ UF día, 4 decimals; the pago to 5), net equity (appraisal − balance), `pagado_neto_uf` (balance after the pie − balance), `pago_acumulado_clp` (Σ pesos in ledger order), UF-day/deltas/acumulados/analytics are computed when read from `uf_daily`. The property's appraisal is a **`valuations` row of the property account in UF** (one per payment day; migration 260 moved them out of the stopgap `property_appraisals` of 258 and deleted the property's old peso month-end rows, which nothing read as its value; the ledger forward-fills the latest one on or before each row; a new payment writes none): a property's valuations are its appraisals (`valuationsAreAppraisals` in `valuationValue.ts`), so the generic mark readers leave them out and its chart samples every month-end from its first ledger row (`augmentChartDatesForPropertyAccounts`). The property's value stays net equity (`deptoNetEquityUfBySnapshotDates`). Rows are **sequential daily balances** (2026-07-21): ledger order = `deptoLedgerChronoCompare` (date, then decreasing crédito restante for same-day prepago+cuota pairs), the loader fail-fasts via `assertDeptoPrepagoSequencing` (a prepago must not raise the balance — regular cuotas may, grace-period capitalization is real), and the snapshot fills use exact-date cutoffs with prepago rows included (the first-of-month → month-end cutoff expansion and the prepago-skip are gone; they created one-day equity spikes on every 1st and ±19.7M P/L couplets around prepagos in the daily view). Nov-2024 cuota 9 ↔ prepago 1 balances were repaired in the live DB on 2026-07-21 (a one-off script, deleted with migration 258). `mortgagePaymentCreate` writes the movements (human notes, mortgage `flow_kind` in the column) plus both `loan_payments` rows; manual-payment recompute updates the table in place (the old `depto_dividendos_sheet_rows` staging was dropped in migration 158). No `depto_snapshot`: the dashboard RE card synthesizes valor/hipoteca from the property + mortgage account rows (same path as the demo).
- **CC installments:** `import:cc-parsed` / PDF → `cc_installment_*` / `cc_statements`; no runtime CSV.
- **CC parse is incremental too (2026-08-10).** The per-PDF parse cache's identity is **(PDF bytes sha256, parser version) only** — the old `mtime_ns`/size veto produced permanent false misses after ns-level timestamp drift (backup/copy round-trips), re-parsing 4 statements nightly. On a cache hit the PDF is **never read**: the readability peek (full OCR for image-only scans) and the one-shot qpdf repair attempt run on cache-miss files only, and the hit path validates the cached meta (`require_statement_meta`) instead of re-deriving it via `pdftotext -layout`/OCR — cached meta was finalized by `parse_one_pdf` under the same version hash. Nightly `parse:cc-pdfs` went **~11 min → ~1s** (301 hits). Corollary: two different downloads of the SAME facturación filed under different filenames make the incremental import ping-pong that statement's row between the two source names on every run — exact-duplicate downloads are quarantined under a `duplicates/` folder inside the card slot (excluded via `is_excluded_cc_pdf_path`, same mechanism as `pending-review/`). A statement re-downloaded after its close arrives named by its pay-by date; the parse reads the real close and skips it as an already-parsed copy, and it belongs in the slot's `duplicates/`. An old parse stored such copies as their own closes; `server/scripts/remove-cc-duplicate-download-statements.ts` (report-first) finds and removes any left.
- **Scanned documents are read with one OCR model (2026-11-10; `ingest/python/ocr_tessdata.py`).** PyMuPDF's OCR reads the `eng.traineddata` under `TESSDATA_PREFIX`, and another build of the model reads the same scan differently: on the home server MacPorts' `tesseract-eng` dropped 48 lines from the 15 scanned ·0161 statements after a parser-version bump re-parsed them, and the statement import failed its reconcile two nights running. `use_expected_tessdata()` (the card, payroll and checking-cartola OCR call it) takes `TESSDATA_PREFIX` when set, else the first standard folder (Homebrew, MacPorts, `/usr/local`, `/usr`) whose model has the expected sha256, and throws naming what each folder holds. The home server keeps a copy of the laptop's model and sets `TESSDATA_PREFIX` in its installed ingest plist. Changing the model means re-parsing every scan with it and then updating the hash.
- **CC statement import is incremental (2026-08-05; fingerprint redefined by F13, migrations 171 and 292).** The import (`card.parsed_statements`; `import:cc-parsed` by hand) skips any statement whose content is what was imported last time — no re-import, no re-reconcile. Each statement carries an `import_fingerprint`: a hash of the statement's stored columns and of each line's stored columns (`cards/statementStorage.ts`, the one definition; `ccStatementFingerprint.ts` is the leaf that filters unchanged statements), written by `importCcStatementsMerge` itself, because only that loop knows which row the statement resolved to (the close-date fallback can match a row filed under a different `source_pdf` when a statement is re-downloaded). It hashes what the LEDGER keeps of the document, not the bytes a source printed, so two renderings of one statement hash alike; the identity columns, the derived ones (kind, origin-currency label, dedupe keys, ids) and the lines the import drops as repeats stay out. Trade-off: a parser change that touches only fields the ledger does not store (plan principal columns, parser layout, canonical row id) needs `--full`. A statement whose import skipped a line as a duplicate of a line on ANOTHER statement hashes differently from its stored lines, so migration 292 re-derived every stored fingerprint and such a statement re-imports once. Effect on the nightly run: **9,9s → 0,55s**, and one unresolvable legacy statement no longer aborts the whole import. The gate is only ever a *skip*: a statement that does import still gets the full reconcile. **`--full` forces the from-scratch pass** (`import:cfraser-inbox -- --full` passes it through).
- **CC statement source ownership (2026-08-05, «JSON leads, PDF import guarded»):** `cc_statements` is UNIQUE on (account_id, card_group, source_pdf, statement_date), so one facturación arriving from two sources would duplicate the ledger. Ownership is period-level over (statement close, currency), and **the PDF outranks the JSON** (2026-08-06; `ccStatementJsonSource.ts`). The Santander statement-JSON importer (`npm run import:santander-statements [-- --apply]`, ingest: decoded and sent as `card.statement`; server: `cardStatementApply.ts` over `santanderStatementImport.ts`) skips PDF-owned closes (report-only cross-check — the diff that has already caught real PDF data loss) and writes unowned ones through `mergeCcAccountFromParsedRows` with a stable `import:santander-json|<currency>|<close>` source id; and an incoming PDF for a JSON-owned close **supersedes** it inside `mergeCcAccountFromParsedRows` — the JSON statement rows are deleted (lines cascade) and the PDF is written in their place, with those closes reported as `json_closes_superseded_by_pdf`. The superseded statements' keys are added to `replaceStatementKeys` so the fail-fast reconcile does not count their lines against the incoming PDF. **Why not first-writer-wins:** the JSON is the fast writer (available the day a facturación closes) but not the authoritative document — the international endpoint is dateless with all-NULL headers, so a JSON USD statement carries lines only. Since the real PDF now arrives by e-mail a day or two later, first-writer-wins would have dropped it forever and pinned the ledger to the thinner source. The nightly cross-check pairs JSON rows with ledger lines by normalized merchant + absolute amount and then, for leftovers, by amount + merchant prefix (`pairStatementLines`, reported apart as «matched by prefix»): the PDF layout can glue the charge-type column onto the merchant — «SEG AUTO SANTANDER COMPRAS P.A.T.» for the JSON's «SEG AUTO SANTANDER» (19 stored lines since 2023 carry that padded column; the parser was left alone because a version bump re-parses the corpus). JSON statements are real statements everywhere else: they count toward facturación close and open-web-paste supersede via `isPdfStatementSource` (= not web-paste), participate in traspaso links, and their line dedupe keys live in a `json|`-prefixed key space (stable across re-imports so replacement and category restore work). Import refuses (fail-fast) when the bank's Cuenta→master mapping disagrees with the card registry's plastic routing (`assertNoCardRoutingConflict`) and skips a dateless international response that merely re-serves an older cycle's rows (`usdStatementIsStaleEcho` — the intl RESPUESTA is all nulls, so USD statements carry line data only and headers stay NULL). **A facturación's two currencies pair on (account, NumExtracto) (2026-09-27; `assembleSantanderStatementBatch`, `ingest/src/santander/statementJson.ts`):** the international file takes its close, pay-by, next close and titular card only from the national of the SAME extracto (from the staged name `<Cuenta>-extracto-<NNN>-…` or a capture's `requestBody.INPUT.NumExtracto`; neither, or `extracto-0`, throws); one without a twin is reported «unpaired», never dated from another close; the batch throws only when ambiguous (one extracto with two closes or two differing copies, two extractos claiming one close). Writes go per (account, close). `--apply` moves a superseded facturación (not its account's newest extracto) whose every statement verified into `santander-statement-json/archive/`; dirty groups stay («KEPT»). The pairing's third pass matches a merchant differing only in rendering (`matched_by_rendering`: a trailing 4-digit terminal code only the JSON prints, «KIOSCO 1234» / «KIOSCO»; punctuation, «ABC] 12» / «ABC! 12»). Before this the grow-only dir's first facturación transition (extracto 022 → 023) made the 2026-09-26 nightly throw «Two national closes for account … in one batch».
- **Account identity:** `accounts.import_key` (unique, nullable; migration 159) — the find-or-create dedupe key and well-known-account lookup (`import:excel|key=mortgage`, `import:fintual|cert|key=…`, `credit_card_master|<issuer>|<last4>`, `import:panel|…`). Every account creator writes it; `notes` keeps the same string as human provenance but has zero machine readers. CC card→account resolution uses `credit_card_account_config.card_last4` (every master has a config row). (This reverses the pre-2026-07 “keep identity in notes” convention.)
- **Per-card CC behavior is config, not code:** consolidation redirects, superseded masters, and statement-classification tokens load from gitignored `cfraser/cc-cards.json` via `server/src/ccCardRegistry.ts` / `ingest/python/cc_cards.py` (missing file = empty registry — demo/CI). Tests always use the committed synthetic `server/src/test/ccCardsFixture.json` (`NW_TRACKER_CC_CARDS`, set in `vitest.config.ts`); never hardcode real card last4s in code or tests. Real values + shape: PARSERS.md.
- **Fintual:** the certificado importer is standalone — **`npm run import:fintual-cert`** (ingest reads the CSV and sends `fund_account.transactions`; the server's `fintualCertImport.ts` reconciles), run by `import:cfraser-inbox` (report only) when a certificado lands. It is a **non-destructive reconcile**: report by default, `--apply` only ADDS certificado rows no existing flow covers (matching includes mirror-merge/manual transfer legs, exact amount within a ±5-day settlement window); it never deletes or overwrites curated rows, and preserves externally-set flow kinds (the APV-A state bonus is indistinguishable from a personal deposit in the certificate). Deposit classification is written to `movements.flow_kind` at import (state bonus / traspaso; plain personal deposits stay NULL). `fund_series_key` is set by the account ensure.
- **Fintual retiros are synthesized from the mail (2026-08-19).** A «Pagamos tu retiro» e-mail names the exact amount, payment date, goal and cuota count, so `import:fintual-emails` writes the goal→checking transfer immediately even when the checking credit is not imported yet — a morning retiro otherwise strands its NAV drop in the day's P/L until the 22:00 xlsx import (the hourly e-mail poll can see the mail but can never fetch the bank leg). One row only, whichever side lands first: an already-imported credit is still **promoted in place** (`fintualWithdrawalPairing.ts`), and a synthesized transfer relies on the checking importers' existing `superseded_by_transfer` skip (`findMatchingInternalTransferLegId` — signed amount + posting-window match) to absorb the bank's later listing, in both the daily xlsx and the monthly cartola. Each synthesis is recorded in **`fintual_synthetic_retiro_transfers`** (migration 177, message-id-keyed — UNIQUE, so a duplicate-guard miss cannot write one mail twice; `fintualSyntheticRetiros.ts` is a leaf module so the checking importers can stamp it without a cycle), and the skip stamps `confirmed_on` — the only trace the bank ever listed the money, since the skip inserts nothing. `import:fintual-emails` exits non-zero (→ failed step → notification) when a synthesis is unconfirmed past paid_on + 2 Chile business days: the promised wire never appeared in any bank feed, and a phantom credit would otherwise be silently absorbed by the next checking-anchor re-derivation. A retiro paid on the month's last business day is synthesized too (the month-boundary deferral went 2026-09-30): the credit that lands next month brings its posting day, see «Bank posting day»; a promoted credit keeps its bank date the same way. **A retiro to the Fintual balance (2026-09-29):** the mail then reads «tus $X pesos chilenos quedaron disponibles para invertir en Fintual … equivalente a N cuotas» (no bank, no parenthesised count); the classifier sets `paid_to: "fintual"` and the import writes goal → «Fintual CLP» (`import:panel|kind=clp|key=fintual_clp`, Efectivo) with the cuotas — no synthesis record, no checking pairing. Fintual wires an uninvested balance back after 7 days («Devolvimos tu saldo»), which is still manual. The first one (100.000, 2026-09-29) had sat as `requires_manual` all day, the Reserva NAV drop reading as −100k Efectivo P/L.
  - **Gmail's IMAP `SINCE` is blind on a DST-transition day (2026-09-08).** `UID SEARCH … SINCE 06-Sep-2026` returned NOTHING while `05-Sep` and `07-Sep` returned the mails — 2026-09-06 is Chile's spring-forward day and Gmail evaluates the date in the account's zone. The watermark had landed on that day (the «Pediste retirar» request mail, 02:12), so every poll for two days searched `SINCE 06-Sep` and missed the «Pagamos tu retiro» that paid 5.xxx.xxx (the Reserva NAV drop stranded in P/L all day). `fetchBrokerEmails` now searches from the watermark day minus one and, when that returns nothing, once more from minus two (`searchSinceCandidates`) — two consecutive days cannot both be transition days, and the exact-timestamp filter already drops everything already seen.
  - **Two rules hardened 2026-09-05 after a four-day silent outage.** (1) **Scan files overlap, so mails collapse by Message-ID** (`collapseBrokerEmailEventsByMessageId` in `ingest/src/brokers/brokerEmail.ts` since 2026-09-30, before anything is sent; the `broker.notifications` contract refuses a payload that repeats a message id): the fetcher's IMAP `SINCE` is day-granular, so every poll re-fetched the watermark message's whole day and the newest mail of each scan landed in the next scan file too; the Fintual e-mail import (`ingest/src/brokers/stagedScans.ts`) concatenates every `scan-*.json` on disk and only deduped against the ledger, never within the batch. A duplicated «Pagamos tu retiro» mail therefore planned `promote <credit>` twice, the second `promoteCheckingCreditToTransfer` threw (the row was already a transfer) inside the batch `db.transaction`, and the rollback took the first write with it — 39 consecutive failed hourly runs from 2026-09-01 22:30, nothing written, while the Reserva NAV read the retiro as −100k P/L. The fetcher now also drops messages `<= last_seen_at` post-search (`fetch.ts`), and `refusePromoteCollisions` sends a second in-batch claim on the same credit to `requires_manual` instead of the throw. (2) **The pairing window is one-sided: `[paid_on, paid_on + 5]`** (`findUnpairedCheckingCredit`). A wire can post the payment day or later, never earlier, and the old ±5 let the 2026-09-01 $5x.xxx retiro — whose real bank leg the 22:00 xlsx had not delivered yet — claim an unrelated $5x.xxx credit dated 2026-08-28 and rewrite it in place (identity note overwritten, goal cuotas sold three days early; the real credit then sat unpaired). Repair path for that class: `server/scripts/unpromote-checking-credit.ts --movement-id=NN --note='<original partialMovementNote key>'` (report-first, `--apply`; the note is the xlsx dedupe identity, so it must be byte-exact), then let the importer re-pair the mail against the right credit.
- **AFP UNO:** the cuota ledger is certificate-backed — rebuilt 2026-10-06 from the full-history Certificado de Movimientos and the Superintendencia's official valor cuota (one account per fund manager since 2026-10-22, `server/scripts/split-afp-ledger-into-fund-accounts.ts`, see «The AFP account's ledger and price»). Cuotas = Σ `movements.units_delta` + transfer legs, full stop — no website-target snapping or statement-drift substitution. New rows come from the nightly certificate read (`pension_account.certificates`); the price from the `afp_uno` sync.
- **Statement line sections are one rule set (2026-09-27; `server/contracts/data/ccStatementLineRules.json` since 2026-10-02).** The payment literals, ABONO DE DIVISAS, traspaso tokens, CLP/USD section-3 patterns, the garbled-row pattern and the layout lists are data read by both `ccStatementLineRules.ts` and `ingest/python/cc_statement_line_rules.py`; `classifyCcStatementLine` / `classify_statement_line` place every non-installment line for the import gate and the parse-time check, and both suites assert `server/contracts/data/ccStatementLineSectionCases.json`. A USD payment line is never section 3 (the legacy USD section-3 total leaves MONTO CANCELADO out; three ·0161 statements would fail otherwise); BCI section-3 rows net by layout. The rules file is part of the parse-cache version, so editing it re-parses the corpus once. Before, Python matched payments by prefix and had no BCI branch.
- **A charge named «PAGO …» is a charge (2026-10-06; `_is_printed_payment` in `parse-cc-statement-pdfs.py`).** The parser read any line opening with PAGO / MONTO CANCELADO / ABONO as a payment to the card and dropped it (one path even flipped its sign), but «PAGO FACIL», «PAGOS.FLOW.CL (WEB)», «PAGOS.F*COMUNIDAD …» and «PAGOS MASIVOS …» are bills paid WITH the card and print positive: 13 ·0161 charges (1.xxx.xxx, 2020-12 → 2023-09, gastos comunes among them) were missing from the ledger and from Gastos. A line is a payment when its name is exactly a payment name or it opens like one and prints negative — every real payment in the corpus prints negative. The parse reconcile never noticed: its operaciones check is skipped whenever the period had a payment (nearly always) and allows 3%, and the billed check accepts the printed total as a candidate. **`operaciones_shortfall`** (`cc_statement_reconcile.py`) now fails a Santander peso statement read from its text whose parsed operaciones fall short of the printed «TOTAL OPERACIONES» by more than a peso; it would have flagged exactly the 12 statements, each short by its missing charge. A parse that counts MORE is the line classifier setting a refund aside, which the printed total nets; an OCR'd scan (flat text) is exempt.
- **Two lines one statement prints are two charges (2026-10-06; `oneShotLineFuzzyMatchExists`).** The import's fuzzy duplicate check (same day, same pesos, a merchant one truncation away) scanned the whole account, lines just written from the same statement included, so «CASTANO 890» and «CASTANO LAS CONDES 890» of 08/09/2017 imported as one. It now ignores the lines this import wrote into the same statement (`excludeLineIds`); a line already on file still counts, so a re-import of a statement stays deduplicated.
- **Stale statement copies from an older card-group assignment (2026-10-06; `scripts/replace-stale-cc-statement-copies.ts`).** The parser's card group comes from the file name; an earlier parse filed the ·0113 / ·0274 statements of the ·0161 master under group B, today they parse as A, and the incremental import (keyed on group, file, close) added the A copy beside the old B one while its dedupe skipped every line B already had — 22 statements (2017–2020) with the full set on a stale, fingerprint-less B row and 0–5 leftover lines on the A row, which the walk and the anchors counted twice. The script (report-first, IMMEDIATE) deletes such stale rows and re-imports their twins in full from the parse, carrying expense assignments to the new lines and failing when a paired payment loses its evidence. Applied: 138 categories, 3 notes and 6 splits carried, none unpaired. Statements the current parse cannot read (zero rows) keep their old rows: they are the only record of those lines.
- **International rows come from whole printed lines (2026-09-27).** A raw-text (vertical) chunk becomes a line only when it holds exactly one printed line's two amounts and no «N DE M» page counter; any other chunk is marked `VERTICAL_MULTI_LINE`, keeps its slot's place in the merge (row ids and fingerprints stay stable) and is dropped unless a whole row replaces it. Such chunks had produced a page number as a US$ amount, another line's US$, or a payment's unsigned origin amount as a positive US$ (which also counted as a bank charge in the financing-cost P/L). In the layout reader, PAÍS is the two-letter cell right before the two amounts. Stored ones: `server/scripts/remove-cc-intl-extraction-artifacts.ts` (report-first, `--apply`). The three USD statements that had sat in `pending-review/` for these artifacts now parse to their printed totals and are back in the corpus. PAÍS is the merchant's country, not the currency of the charge — see «A card line's original currency is decided by value».
- **Payroll labels read their own line (2026-09-27).** `amount_after_label` never crosses a line break and skips a rate printed before the amount («Isapre 7%»); Talana's header columns go through `amount_in_header_column` (0 when blank). «DESCUENTO APV» had read the days worked below it, and the Nuevo Chile health discount read the 7 of «7%». Review `import:payroll-liquidaciones -- --dry-run` (it prints a per-field change report) after a parser change.
- **A card line's original currency is decided by value, on import (2026-09-27; `ccOriginCurrency.ts`).** International statements print MONTO MONEDA ORIGEN beside each US$ but never its currency, and PAÍS is the merchant's country (a US merchant billing from Chile charges pesos). The parser writes the origin exactly as printed and leaves `orig_currency` empty (the column stays, so peso statements' fingerprints don't move); `importCcStatementsMerge` labels every line with `ccLineOriginCurrency`: `usd` when origin = US$ to the cent, `clp` when origin ÷ US$ is within 5% of the `fx_daily` rate on or before the line's date (+ half a cent ÷ |US$|), else null — never a country guess — and refuses records that carry a label. The statement JSON writes its origin as the same Chilean cell (`ccOriginAmountCsvCell`); web paste has no origin. The old country table also rounded dollar origins to whole units and read a middle dot as a decimal (grouped amounts stored 1000× too small). Nothing computes with either field. Stored lines: `scripts/relabel-cc-line-origin-currency.ts [--origins-csv=…] [--apply]`, run after the parse + import that follow a parser change (it reaches web paste, JSON and statements outside the parsed corpus).
- **One parser per format (2026-09-27).** Dates: dd/mm/yyyy → ISO is `parseDdMmYyToIso` (`server/contracts/src/formats/dates.ts`; card code reads it through `normalizeTransactionDateIso`, and a statement line's date is `statementLineDateIso`, transaction else posting, both in `ccInstallmentPayBy.ts`), mirrored in Python by `parse_dd_mm_yy_to_iso` (`ingest/python/statement_values.py`), both asserting `server/contracts/data/ddMmYyToIsoCases.json` (the case files of F7 live in `server/contracts/data/`, one per format); ISO → dd/mm/yyyy is `ddMmYyyyFromIso` (`formats/dates.ts`, throws on anything but YYYY-MM-DD). Far-future test fixtures use ISO dates: a dd/mm year past 2038 reads as a jammed parser token. Numbers come in two styles and each source declares which it reads — never guess from the separators («1.234» is 1234 Chilean, 1.234 US): `parseChileanNumber` / `parseUsNumber` (`formats/numbers.ts`, both throw; `isChileanNumber` for cells that may hold text, `parseOptionalChileanInteger` for the parsed-statement CSV's integer cells) and Python `parse_clp_amount` (a decimal part rounds ties-to-even: real documents only print ,00). Still on their own copies: BCentral and Quetalmi (raw format unconfirmed), `ingest/src/brokers/brokerEmail.ts`, the Racional crawl (`ingest/src/racional/steps.ts`, `racionalMovements.ts`).
- **Cartola rows printed without a year (2026-09-27; `ingest/src/formats/cartolaMovementDate.ts`, `cartola_row_date` in `ingest/python/cartola_layout.py`).** The candidates are the years from DESDE's to HASTA's where dd/mm is a real date inside the period; exactly one is the date; none, several, or a missing bound is an error — both languages assert `server/contracts/data/cartolaMovementYearCases.json`. PDF cartolas list rows oldest first, so the PDF parsers add a tie-break (`PdfCartolaRowDates`): an ambiguous row — only an annual cartola prints one — takes the earliest candidate on or after the previous row's date, else that cartola's parse fails with the file and row. The daily XLSX never spans a year, so the TypeScript side has the base rule only. Before, the XLSX took HASTA's year (a «31/12» row of a 31/12→31/01 cartola became next December) and the two PDF parsers disagreed (first vs last candidate).

Runtime code must not use `fs.readFile*` / `readSemicolonCsv` on `cfraser/` except in `server/scripts/` and import helpers. Import-sync document coverage (`GET /api/import-sync/…`) may list files for admin tooling only.

## Server runtime modes (local vs hosted demo)

One binary, env-driven (`server/src/httpSecurity.ts`, root `.env` loaded at boot):

- **The primary instance runs as a LaunchAgent (2026-09-30): `com.user.nw-tracker-server`** (`server/com.user.nw-tracker-server.plist`, installed by `server/scripts/install-server-agent.sh`, which refuses while the port is taken). `tsx src/index.ts` without watch, KeepAlive, log `cfraser/server.log`. Restart after a code change with `launchctl kickstart -k gui/$(id -u)/com.user.nw-tracker-server` (the user's shell alias `nw-server-restart` bootstraps it when not loaded). Its scheduler will own the nightly ingest runs (see `docs/ingest-split-plan.md`), so it must not depend on an open terminal.
- **The primary runs on a home server and deploys from GitHub `main` (2026-10-30; `scripts/server-deploy.sh`, agent `com.user.nw-tracker-deploy` from `scripts/install-deploy-agent.sh`).** An always-on 2019 Intel MacBook Pro (macOS Sequoia, lid closed, `pmset disablesleep`) runs the server and the ingest service; the development laptop runs no agents and has no live database (dev servers there use the test or demo data, or a snapshot copy with `BACKGROUND_JOBS_ENABLED=0`). Pushing to `main` is the deploy: every 3 minutes the server fetches `origin/main` and, when it moved, enqueues a `deploy` job in the server's job queue (see «The job queue»; one queued deploy, moved to the newest commit) and claims it, polling the claim for up to ~150 s and otherwise leaving it queued for the next run (a deploy the agent stops re-enqueuing goes stale after 15 min, so a dead agent cannot keep the queue drained). The claim is granted only when nothing else runs (`server-restart`): an ingest run in flight finishes first, nothing new starts once the deploy is due, and the executor that boots after the restart keeps every job waiting until the agent completes the job (or its 20-minute lease runs out — then it is lost and badged). Once granted it snapshots the database (`before-deploy-<sha>`), fast-forwards, runs `npm ci` only when a package file changed, rebuilds the client into `client/dist.next` and swaps it in with two renames (the previous one kept as `dist.prev`; until 2026-10-31 the build emptied `client/dist` in place and the running server answered 404 for the whole build), restarts the server (and the ingest service when `ingest/`, `server/contracts/` or a package file changed) and waits for `/api/health` to report the new commit, then completes the job; otherwise it rolls back to the previous commit, completes the job as failed, and that commit is not retried (the next push is). With the server not listening it deploys without the queue. Only the newest commit is deployed; a checkout with commits not on `origin/main`, or modified tracked files, refuses. Outcomes in `cfraser/deploy.log`, the job, and the app's messages (a failure as a notification); `scripts/server-deploy.sh --check` says what it would enqueue. The server's GitHub access is a read-only deploy key. That machine differs from the laptop in ways a deploy must not undo: Homebrew no longer installs on Intel, so poppler/qpdf come from MacPorts (`/opt/local/bin`, on the installed plists' PATH, which carry its home folder — the tracked plists still carry the laptop's paths and are adapted at install), the vendored Python deps (`ingest/python/.pdf_deps`, `.numbers_deps`) are Intel builds installed there with `pip install --target`, and npm 11 skips the install scripts it has not been allowed (the deploy checks that `better-sqlite3` loads).

- **An open tab reloads onto a deployed client (2026-11-04; `server/src/clientBuild.ts`, `client/src/clientBuild.ts`).** The live-quotes stamp poll (every 5 s while visible) carries `client_build`: the content-hashed `/assets/` script and stylesheet URLs the served `client/dist/index.html` loads, sorted and space-joined (read from disk, cached on its mtime and size; null when the server serves no client — the Vite dev server). The tab's own id is the same URLs in the document it was loaded from, read once at boot (both sides assert `server/src/test/clientBuildIdCases.json`). When they differ, the tab reloads at its next in-app navigation (landing on the new route) or once it is hidden, never under a form being filled; a deploy that changes no client file keeps the id and reloads nothing. One reload per served build (sessionStorage `nw-tracker.clientBuildReloadedFor`): a tab that still differs after reloading warns in the console instead of looping. Production builds only.
- **`HOST`** — bind host; default `127.0.0.1` (keeps the unauthenticated local API off the LAN). Hosted demo (Render) sets `HOST=0.0.0.0`.
- **`CORS_ALLOWED_ORIGINS`** — comma-separated allowlist; defaults to Vite dev/preview origins (`localhost:5173`/`4173`). Never use a reflect-any-origin CORS config — any website could read the API.
- **`DEMO_MODE` is the only access-control switch — there is no login.** The hosted demo used to sit behind a shared-password `/login` page (any syntactically valid email + one public password, logged to `demo_auth_logins`); recruiters reached the gate and left without trying it, so the whole mechanism was removed 2026-07-24 (`AUTH_PASSWORD`, `SESSION_SECRET`, `nw_session`, `/api/auth/*`, the client `AuthContext`/`LoginPage`). Every deployment is now unauthenticated: local mode is bound to `127.0.0.1`, and the public demo is protected by being **read-only** instead — `demoReadOnlyMiddleware` (`server/src/httpSecurity.ts`, mounted only when `DEMO_MODE=1`) answers `403 { error: "demo_read_only" }` to any `/api` request that is not GET/HEAD/OPTIONS, and the client maps that sentinel to the localized `demo.readOnly` message (`client/src/api.ts`). The one exempt mutation is the analytics beacon (`DEMO_PAGEVIEW_PATH`); since 2026-11-07 a short allowlist of edits is open too (see «The demo is writable, a little» under Hosted demo). **Do not put real data behind `DEMO_MODE`** — it is an open door with a read-only latch, not authentication.
- **`DB_BACKUP_ENABLED` / `DB_BACKUP_KEEP` / `DB_BACKUP_DIR` / `DB_BACKUP_DIR_KEEP`** — daily auto-snapshot of the DB while the server runs (`dbBackupScheduler.ts`; default on). **Retention is one snapshot per day for the last `DB_BACKUP_KEEP` days (default 30) and one per month before that, whatever the label (2026-11-09; `dbSnapshotRetention.ts`, pure):** the `auto-daily` ones, the deploy agent's `before-deploy-<sha>` ones and hand-made `db:snapshot` files alike — until then only `auto-daily` was pruned (to 14) and 171 deploy snapshots had piled up in ten days, 4,2 GB. The optional second copy in `DB_BACKUP_DIR` (`~/Documents/backups/nw-tracker`, set in root `.env`) keeps `DB_BACKUP_DIR_KEEP` daily days (default 60). The scheduler prunes after each daily snapshot; `npm run db:prune-snapshots -w nw-tracker-server [-- --dir=<folder> --apply]` is the same rule by hand, report-first.
- **`CACHE_WARM_ENABLED`** — proactive dashboard cache warmer (`dashboardCacheWarmer.ts`; default on, `0` disables). Rebuilds the page-bundle caches in the background at boot, at 00:05 Chile (day-rollover clear), when `data_version` shows an external write, and debounced (30s) after in-process invalidations — so no interactive request pays a cold aggregation build. Not on a live price change (2026-11-04): those responses are rebuilt by the request that needs them (see «Polls follow the market clock»). The warmed object itself is the served response (`dashboard.page_bundle|<unit>` in the aggregation cache), not just its inner aggregations. CC billing detail lives in the aggregation cache (`cc.billing_detail|<accountId>`; day/data_version fresh + invalidated by CC write hooks), not rebuilt per request. **Requests go first (2026-10-31; `apiRequestsInFlight.ts`):** a middleware counts the `/api` requests in flight (not the health check, the page-view beacon or the machine traffic under `/api/ingest` and `/api/jobs`; the live-quotes stamp poll counts, its answer triggers the client's refetch burst) and every warm step starts only once none has been in flight for 250 ms (a request older than 20 s stops counting, one wait lasts at most 60 s); a page opened meanwhile builds its own payloads and the step then hits the cache. Only the gaps between steps wait, never a step's inside: a request may be awaiting the promise the step builds.
- **Every flag is parsed once, by `server/src/config.ts` (2026-11-07).** One truthiness rule (`1/true/yes` on, `0/false/no` off, unset or empty the default, anything else refuses to boot); one default order: explicit env, then the demo's defaults, then the background-jobs defaults, then the built-in one; `initServerConfig()` at boot after `loadRootDotenv()`, so a flag change needs a restart (credentials — `BCENTRAL_*`, `COINGECKO_API_KEY`, `INGEST_TOKEN` — are still read where used, so a key added to `.env` is picked up live). The **deployment profile** (`deploymentProfile()`: demo, read-only API, the sync sources it never runs, the AFP source, the market awake gate, the usage log's file) is what leaf modules read instead of checking `DEMO_MODE`.
- **`BACKGROUND_JOBS_ENABLED`** — umbrella for the in-process schedulers (`config.ts`): `0` defaults `GLOBAL_SYNC_ENABLED` / `LIVE_QUOTES_SYNC_ENABLED` / `DB_BACKUP_ENABLED` / `CACHE_WARM_ENABLED` / `USAGE_LOG_ENABLED` (the usage log, see «Usage and performance log») to `0`, and turns off the ingest scheduler and the job queue executor whatever their flags say; an explicitly set per-job flag wins for the others. Set on every `.claude/launch.json` server config so agent-spawned dev servers never run syncs/backups against the shared DB — only the user's primary app instance does (see Global sync scheduler).
- Express async route handlers must be wrapped in `asyncHandler` — see GUIDELINES.md «Async route handlers must be wrapped».

## Hosted demo (Render)

Single Render web service (blueprint: **`render.yaml`** at the repo root) serving synthetic data, open to anyone and read-only (see above). Local mode is untouched — everything is env-gated.

- **Release branch `demo` (2026-11-02; `scripts/release-demo.sh`, `npm run release:demo [-- <sha> | --yes | --rollback <sha>]`).** A push to `main` deploys the home server only (`scripts/server-deploy.sh`); Render builds the `demo` branch (`render.yaml` `branch: demo`, and the Blueprint is linked to `demo`, so a `render.yaml` change also waits for its release). The script is the only way onto `demo`: it fast-forwards it to a commit already on `origin/main` (its tip by default), lists what goes out and asks; it refuses a commit not on `main`, a non-fast-forward (`--rollback` moves `demo` back, with a lease) and a dirty tree. Never push to `demo` by hand or unasked — releasing is the user's call. The repo is public: `demo` only ever carries commits `main` already published.
- **`DEMO_MODE=1`** (`server/src/demoMode.ts`, runs at boot after `loadRootDotenv()`): requires `NW_TRACKER_TEST_DB` to point at a dedicated demo file (throws otherwise — demo mode can never open the real `nw-tracker.db`); when that DB has no accounts, boot fills its market tables from the real sources and then generates the `demo` preset on them (`loadDemoRealMarket(demoMarketSpec())` + `generateDemoDb({ person: DEMO_PERSON, market: "real" })`, same as `server/scripts/generate-demo-data.ts --real-market`). **On Render the build generates it (2026-10-31; `npm run demo:generate -w nw-tracker-server` at the end of `buildCommand`)** and the boot finds it populated: with the persistent disk Render stops the old instance before the new one boots, so a boot that fetched and generated cost minutes of downtime, while a build runs with the old version still serving (a source that stays down fails the build, never the live demo). The boot path stays for a local rehearsal on an empty DB. Every deploy still builds the demo from current source — it always matches the deployed code. Demo mode defaults `DB_BACKUP_ENABLED` to `0` and `GLOBAL_SYNC_ENABLED` / `LIVE_QUOTES_SYNC_ENABLED` / `USAGE_LOG_ENABLED` to `1` (explicit env wins; the deployment profile in `config.ts`); the cache warmer stays default-on so first paint is warm.
- **The demo runs on real market data (2026-10-31; `demoData/demoRealMarket.ts`, `demoMarketActivity.ts`).** At boot, before generating: Yahoo daily closes of every ticker the person holds (`DEMO_TICKERS`, `equity_daily`, BTC-USD included) — a `.SN` ticker's from the committed Bolsa de Santiago snapshot, `server/data/demo/santiago-closes.csv`, plus Yahoo's bars dated after it (2026-11-09: Yahoo keeps no past bars for the thin Santiago tickers — one bar, today's, for CFIETFIPSA.SN — and Render's servers get an HTML page from the Bolsa's API where the laptop gets JSON, so the build never calls the exchange; `npm run demo:refresh-santiago-snapshot -w nw-tracker-server` rewrites the file through yesterday from a machine that reaches it, the build refuses a snapshot ending more than `DEMO_SANTIAGO_SNAPSHOT_MAX_AGE_DAYS` = 7 days before its yesterday, naming the command, and `release:demo` refuses first; `equityBolsaSantiagoEod.ts` lives in `src` for the refresh) — plus every `equity_with_dividends` benchmark ticker, with that ticker's dividends (`equity_dividends` — without them the Rentabilidad benchmark row read «—» on every cell, 2026-10-31), and CLP=X (`fx_daily`); Banco Central's observed dollar, euro, UF, UTM and IPC (`BCENTRAL_EMAIL` / `BCENTRAL_PASSWORD`, a Render secret entered by hand — `sync: false` in `render.yaml`; the boot fails without them); AFP UNO's valor cuota (below). History stops at yesterday (Chile; the last completed UTC day for crypto). Each source retries with backoff for ~10 minutes (`DEMO_MARKET_RETRY_DELAYS_MS`), then the build fails (the boot, on an empty DB) — Render keeps the previous version live. The generator's unit math reads those stored closes (`dbMarketReader`), so holdings are valued at real prices while the story (salary, spending, buy dates) stays the person's. **The AFP is a cuota ledger on sources Render can reach (2026-10-31; `afpUnoApi.ts`, `loadDemoPensionMarket`):** «Modelo-A» (`afp_modelo_cuota_a`) from 2018-03, switched into «UNO-A» (`afp_uno_cuota_a`) in 2020-02 (`DEMO_AFP_SWITCH_MONTH`, an `afp_fund_switch` transfer with both cuota counts). The Superintendencia's site refuses connections from Render's servers (the first deploy's boot retried until the demo went down), so: Modelo's official fund A values come from a committed snapshot of the SP's (`server/data/demo/afp-modelo-fund-a-2018-2020.csv`, 2018-01 → 2020-03, `parseDemoAfpModeloCsv`; a closed fund never changes), and UNO's from AFP UNO's own API (`POST https://www.uno.cl/api/valor-cuota-buscar/historico`, `{fechaInicio, fechaFin}` ISO; newest first, decimal comma, one row per calendar day, whole history since 2019-10-01 in one request; a `codigo` other than "0" throws — `parseUnoValorCuotaHistorico`), both stored in `pension_fund_unit_official` like the SP's, the UNO display series derived as on the personal instance. UNO's API matches the SP's values to the cent but for 15 days of March 2020 (≤ 0,27 %) and publishes one business day after it. **The live `afp_uno` source reads it on the demo only:** `afpUnoOfficialSource()` (`afpUnoOfficialSync.ts`) is `uno_api` under `DEMO_MODE`, `sp` otherwise — the one place the choice is made; it picks the runner (`afpUnoOfficialSyncRunner`: `syncAfpUnoFromUnoApi` re-reads from 60 days before the latest stored day) and adds the API's business day to the expected official day (`AFP_UNO_SOURCE_EXTRA_LAG_BUSINESS_DAYS`), so the demo is not stale every evening. **The AFC is a synthetic book account** (no reachable source of the cesantía fund's values; `afc_cic` reads «Not configured»): marked on every Chile business day at about 3,5 %/yr from its contributions (`writeAfc` in `demoData/generate.ts`), in both people. **The demo's Render service has a persistent disk, so a deploy stops the old instance before the new one boots — no zero-downtime: a boot that fails or hangs takes the demo down, and «Restart service» starts the latest build, not the previous one.** That is why the build, not the boot, fetches the market and generates the database: the downtime left is the restart and the cache warm. **Syncs run only while someone browses:** every request of a real visitor (the visit log's countable requests — the open tab's background polling included, bots never) notes activity; the global sync and the live quotes skip their ticks while the demo is asleep and run at once when it wakes (`onDemoMarketWake`), and it sleeps 30 minutes after the last request (`DEMO_MARKET_IDLE_MS`; the client polls only while its tab is visible). On the demo the author-only sources (`DEMO_SYNC_DISABLED_SOURCES`: Fintual goals, the official-serie check, the Risky Norris composition and the CMF filing) read «Not configured», are never polled and never dim anything; the demo holds no Fintual fund. `demoModeEnabled` lives in the leaf `demoModeFlag.ts` so low-level modules can ask without importing the bootstrap. Rehearsal: `server-demo` / `client-demo` with the two Banco Central values in the server's environment.
- **The home server fetches the demo's market; Render downloads it (2026-11-09; `demoData/demoMarketArtifact.ts`, `demoMarketRelease.ts`, `demoMarketPublishJob.ts`, `scripts/publish-demo-market.ts`, `docs/demo-market-publish-plan.md`).** Two releases failed on 2026-11-09 at the build's fetch step. Now the primary runs the loader above every night at 01:00 Chile (and at boot when the last success is over 20 h old) in a child process against a scratch DB (`server/data/demo-market-publish.db`; never the live one — the loader writes through the process's one `db`), with the Santiago closes from the Bolsa itself (`loadDemoRealMarket(…, {santiago: "bolsa"})`), exports the market tables (`DEMO_MARKET_TABLES`; the script fails when the loader moved any other table, `assertOnlyMarketTablesChanged`) as gzipped JSON (~0,3 MB: `schema_version`, `generated_at`, `complete_through`, the spec, columns and rows per table) and uploads it to the fixed GitHub release `demo-market` (`demo-market.json.gz` + `demo-market.meta.json`; upload under a `.next` name, delete, rename; never the repo's latest release). On with a `DEMO_MARKET_PUBLISH_TOKEN` (fine-grained, Contents: read and write) at boot and `DEMO_MARKET_PUBLISH_ENABLED` (default on, off with `BACKGROUND_JOBS_ENABLED=0`, never on the demo); without the token «not configured». A failure is a notification, retried after 15 and 45 min. Render's build (`npm run demo:generate` = `generate-demo-data.ts --market-asset`) downloads it from `github.com/<repo>/releases/download/…` (no token) and refuses another version, one complete through more than `DEMO_MARKET_ASSET_MAX_AGE_DAYS` = 3 days before its yesterday, one built for less than its `demoMarketSpec()` or with other columns — naming the job; `release:demo` checks the header first (it replaced the Santiago snapshot check). `--market-asset-file=<path>` rehearses from a local file; `--real-market [--santiago=bolsa]` (`demo:generate:fetch`) is the rollback. Rehearsed: generating from the artifact and fetching directly gave identical market tables and valuations (US$427.157). `BCENTRAL_*` stay on Render for the wake-on-visit syncs. Optional, off: `NW_DEPLOY_RELEASE_DEMO=1` + `RELEASE_DEMO_PUSH_REMOTE` (a write-capable remote) in the deploy agent's plist make `server-deploy.sh` release each healthy deploy (`release_demo_after_deploy`).
- **The synthetic databases are a person told through documents (2026-10-31; `server/src/demoData/`, plan in `docs/demo-generator-plan.md`).** One engine, two people (`person.ts`): `DEMO_PERSON`, the hosted demo, and `testPerson(today)`, the Vitest database (about twenty months ending today, one contrato job then a contractor job, a rented apartment, a Santander and a BCI card with cuotas, SPY plus one play, a Fintual-style mutual fund, AFP, AFC, APV — short so the suite stays fast). `DEMO_PERSON` is the hand-tuned story (born 1995, dummy university and two boleta internships, an allowance from dad, two contrato jobs with liquidaciones, two foreign contractor jobs paid in USD through a Deel-style platform, three homes and the purchase of his own apartment, two cars, trips and tech in cuotas, an SPY anchor with a few plays, BTC, APV); no personal data. `simulate.ts` is the life ledger: day-dated events per month from seeded per-month streams (`rng.ts`: adding a month never moves a past one), balances kept in calendar order and never negative, two cash bands (2026-11-09; `CHECKING_BAND` / `SAVINGS_BAND` in `person.ts`): checking keeps 1–2 months of trailing expenses (below its low topped up to 1,5 from savings at payday, above its high trimmed to it), the cushion — 3–6 months plus the known needs of the next six months (a car paid in cash, the December APV, a pie, a move-in, a month between jobs) — sits in the savings vehicles (`Person.savings`: a money-market fondo mutuo «Fondo Mutuo Money Market» from the second contrato job, cuotas on the series `demo_fm_money_market`, which accrues the story's term-deposit curve less a 0,3 % fee; the DAP's principal is drawn from it while it runs), a need moves savings → checking at the payday of the month before it is paid, the cushion above the savings band goes to the broker; a person with no savings vehicle (the Vitest person) keeps the savings band on checking itself, as before; a lifestyle that ratchets slowly toward a concave function of smoothed income. `render.ts` turns it into the documents and `generate.ts` imports them through the real import functions: cartolas (`applyBankAccountStatements`; a transfer between own accounts is written first, so its cartola row is kept as the transfer), this month's checking listing (`applyBankAccountMovements`), every card facturación in pesos and dollars as statement-parser rows (`applyParsedCcStatements`) plus the open cycle (`importCcWebPasteLines`), the card-payment mirrors (`convertCcPaymentMirrors`), Previred parameters and liquidaciones (`applyPayrollParameters`, `applyEmploymentPayslips`). Written directly, as no document carries them: the dollar checking account (no statement import exists), the broker and exchange ledgers, the AFP cuota ledger (Modelo → UNO), the AFC, APV and money-market book marks (the fund's cuotas × its series; the cartola prints its «TRANSF A AGF ANDES FM MONEY MARKET» / «RESCATE …» rows, which the import keeps as the transfers), contractor payslips (USD, `origin = 'rebuilt'`, no deposit link: their wires land in the dollar account), the mortgage ledger, and the official IPC derived from the UF. Contractor years: boleta «retención por el contribuyente» paid by F29 every month, the April F22 settles IGC against it net of the independent contributions (credited to the AFP in July); APV goes to régimen B when the year's marginal rate passes 15 %. **Every April is the tax module's (2026-11-09; `taxDocuments.ts`):** the generator writes what a year's documents state — the boletas issued (`sii_issued_fee_receipts`), each dividend's gross and 15 % US withholding (`movement_dividend_details`), and in March the employer's DJ 1887 (from the imported liquidaciones through `payrollTaxYear`), the AFP's DJ 1899 (APV régimen B, UF) and the lender's DJ 1898 (`mortgageInterestForYear`) — then asks `buildF22Draft` for each income year: its 305 plus the contributions on fees the simulator charged (code 900, which the module does not draft) is the settlement. The simulation runs again from a checkpoint taken just before it (`saveDemoPristineCopy` / `resetDemoDatabaseToPristine` on `<db>.pretax`), the settlements fed back through `simulate(…, { aprilSettlements })`, until every booked settlement equals the module's (`MAX_TAX_PASSES` 6; the demo person settles in 4, ~1 min on synthetic market); then each past year's return is stored as filed (`sii_f22_filed`: the draft, 900 and the resulting 305) and linked to the movement that paid or refunded it (`f22_settlements`). The simulator's own tax estimate only seeds the first pass and chooses the APV regime. The tax page is shown on the demo (`/api/meta/sidebar-nav` no longer hides it). `invariants.ts` fails the build when the app's balances differ from the story's, a cartola account dips below zero, a payslip or a card payment is left unpaired, or net worth lands more than 25 % from US$400k.
- **The demo's front door (2026-11-07; plan `docs/demo-about-plan.md`).** In the demo build (`VITE_DEMO_MODE`) `/` is the About page and the dashboard lives at `/dashboard` (`client/src/dashboardPath.ts`: `DASHBOARD_PATH`, `isDashboardPathname`; a personal instance serves the dashboard at both `/` and `/dashboard`). Both demo routes render **one** `DemoHomePage` (`client/src/pages/DemoHomePage.tsx`) that keeps the dashboard mounted: on About it is drawn smaller and tilted with a CSS transform at the content's full width (the grid follows the viewport, so it must never be laid out narrower), and «Open the dashboard» removes the transform with a CSS transition on the same instance, so nothing reloads. While on About and during the move in, the tiles below the dashboard's first screen (strip, main charts, rail) are not shown (`DashboardFoldContext`, `dashboardFold.ts` → `GroupInfoBase` `fold`): `cut` (not rendered) until the browser is idle on About, then `hidden` — rendered and laid out but invisible and inert (`BelowFoldHiddenContext` → `PageGridTile` `.page-tile--fold-hidden`, no wrapper element, so grid placement and element identity survive), with the tiles' own queries off (the returns, the detalle and flows tables, the Diario combos) — and `full` on the frame's `transitionend` (fallback 1,2 s): the move ends with a style switch, no rendering and no layout change. While loading, the tables keep their loaded height: one page of placeholder rows (the detalle's placeholder months; `FlowRows` `placeholderRows`), the flows filter bar with every select (disabled), the pager drawn disabled while the first page loads (`PaginatedTable`), and the returns table's cells with blank lines in the loaded lines' styles until the returns and the benchmark comparison are in (checked: every tile the same height hidden, revealed and loaded). Line charts (`ValuationLineCharts`) size from their layout box (`AppLineChart` → `useElementSize`) and draw their legend as HTML under the plot (`.chart-box--legend`): Recharts measures a chart's size and its legend's with the on-screen box, which the tilt shrinks, and kept those sizes after the move until something re-measured them (a narrower chart, then a taller plot that shrank on the first re-render after the first move). A wrapper that carries a page is `.page-root` in `page-grid.css`.
