About holds the description, the «How it works» diagram (Sources → Ingest service → Server → Client) with links into the app, GitHub / privacy / contact links; the sidebar shows «About» above «Dashboard» and «Demo tour» · «Privacy» in its footer. **The tour** (`DemoTour.tsx`) opens on the dashboard only: for 24 h from the first visit until dismissed or finished (reaching the last step counts), steps outline their targets above the ticker (`.demo-tour-target`; `fit: "content"` targets get a ring around their text and icons). `/privacy` (demo only) states what the demo records. **Scroll position** is remembered per URL for the page load (`useScrollMemory.ts`, the document scrolls). The link-preview image `client/public/og-image.png` is rendered by `node scripts/render-og-image.mjs --url=<demo>` from `/dashboard` (sidebar hidden, text on a translucent panel overlapping the tilted screenshot); `release:demo` notes when client code changed after it. **About layout (2026-11-09):** on desktop the text column takes 45 % of the content width on one translucent panel of the page colour, and the tilted dashboard is scaled to cover the last ~60 %, behind it; the preview is cut right below the net-worth chart's legend (`usePreviewHeight`: the tile's untransformed layout offset, observed as it loads). Returning to About hides the below-the-fold tiles again (they stay rendered); both ways animate (0,7 s; the transitions sit on each destination state: into the dashboard the preview's radius and shadow drop at once, back to About they fade in with the tilt and the text fades in); the frame pivots on its top-right corner in both states, since a pivot change is not animated. During the move the frame takes its natural height. **The preview is pinned while About scrolls (2026-11-09):** on desktop a zero-height sticky wrapper (`.pin`, a `page-grid`) holds it at its own resting offset (`--about-frame-top`, measured), and while About shows, `.layout-main` only clips sideways (`overflow-x: clip`) — its `overflow: auto` makes it the sticky scroll container, and it never scrolls. From a scrolled About the page keeps its About state (`leaving`, decided while rendering the route change) while the text fades out (0,2 s) and the page returns to the top behind the pinned preview; only then is the pin released and the move starts (unscrolled, it starts at once); the dashboard always opens at the top from About (`skipNextScrollRestore` in `useScrollMemory.ts` keeps the memory from restoring an earlier `/dashboard` position mid-move). The market ticker and the currency selector (`AppDisplayPreferencesBar`) are not shown on `/` and mount once the dashboard has moved in (`demoTickerGate.ts`; the ticker's strip stays reserved; About keeps the ticker's query warm). On the About preview only, the net-worth lines are wiped in left to right over 3 s the first time (`DashboardAboutPreviewContext` → `drawInMs` on the line panel, a CSS clip on `.recharts-line`, so the hand-drawn pens animate too); a direct `/dashboard` load draws at the usual speed. The About tab title is the bare app name. **First paint (2026-11-09):** the preview (and, on the demo, `/dashboard`'s content) stays invisible until the dashboard's cards carry real numbers — the bundle, a held bundle or a stored snapshot, never the zero placeholder (`DashboardStripReadyContext`) — and its height has been measured at that size (the strip is observed too: its real lines push the chart down without resizing it); then it fades in, nothing inside it ever dims, and the slow draw-in waits for it (`DashboardPreviewShownContext` → `drawInHold`). `index.html` preloads the first calls on `/` and `/dashboard` (`<link rel=preload as=fetch>`: the sidebar tree, the page bundle in the stored unit and, with no chart preference stored, the net-worth chart's default daily series, `overview-daily?days=1096`), so they run while the JavaScript downloads; the cache warmer builds that series in both units right after the bundles. The preview fades in rising 1,25rem (`translate`, 1,2 s, beside the tilt's `transform`). **A line chart's loading frame (same date):** `ChartEmptyState` with a `legend` (ValuationLineCharts, and the lazy `LineChartPanel` frame with `ChartLegendPlaceholder`, one legend row built like a real item) keeps the loaded chart's plot + legend layout, so the plot never shrinks when the data lands; its plot draws no stand-in grid (it could not match the chart's ticks), and the chart's own grid and axes fade in (`.chart-box--legend`). The ATH marker waits for the lines' first draw (80 % of the slow wipe, else the 300 ms line animation) and fades in.
- **The demo person's US chapter (2026-11-09; `docs/demo-us-chapter-plan.md`, `demoData/usPayroll.ts`).** Contractor at Northwind Labs 2022-10 → 2024-03, then its US W-2 employee in Austin from 2024-04 (`UsW2Job`: US$150k, a 4 % raise every April — the 2025 review adds a US$10k market adjustment on top, `raiseExtraUsd`, which later raises compound on — a March bonus of 8 % (12 % in 2026, `bonus.fractionByYear`; both 2026-11-10, so IBKR receives more than the car, the Roth, the Chilean April F22 and the wires left it), biweekly stubs through `applyEmploymentPayslips` with `jurisdiction: "us"` — FICA, percentage-method withholding, 401(k) with the employer match as a `memo` line, medical, dental, HSA; every stub pairs with its net credit on «Checking (US)», a `usd` account under Checking accounts with a `bank_account_numbers` row). The dollar lifestyle is debit from that account (until the US card lands); the US checking keeps the checking band (1–2 months of Austin spending), the cushion and the US reserves (the car, the Roth contribution, the December flight, April's 1040) sit in «DEMO BANK TEXAS SAVINGS» (2026-11-09; `Person.savings.usd`, a `usd` ledger under Cash savings declared in `bank_account_numbers` under the same issuer), moved to checking the month before they are paid; the savings above the savings band go to IBKR by ACH; the US statement prints the moves as «ONLINE TRANSFER TO / FROM SAVINGS» rows, which the generator keeps as the transfer legs (never gastos lines); the account pays `savings_earnings` on the last day of each month at `usdYieldPct(year) − 0,3`, the 1040's line 2b and the F22's 1104. The monthly wire to Chile covers the checking band's target × `chileFixedCosts(month)` (the cuotas as the card charges them, gastos comunes, contribuciones, the Chilean subscriptions, less the rents net of the agency's commission) plus `chileUpcomingNeeds`; the Chilean fund keeps the savings band's low × those costs from the move (sold down to it in the move month, the rest to the broker) and the Chilean bands read them abroad. Retirement splits Chile / US (`ensureRetirementCountrySplit`): the 401(k) a cuota ledger on the USD series `demo_us_target_2060` (90 % SPY, 10 % at 3,5 %/yr, from the stored SPY closes), the HSA likewise, the Roth IRA SPY shares bought every October from 2025 through the IRA's cash sweep («Roth IRA cash», a `usd` leaf under the Roth bucket, `rothCash`: the contribution lands there from US checking and the buy is a `stock_buy` from it, so the Roth's shares have one cash leg). **The Roth's SPY earns its dividends (2026-11-10):** paid whole into the sweep — a US person, no withholding — and reinvested the same day as a `stock_buy`, the pair every reader knows (tax lots, deposits, broker holdings); the sweep ends every day at zero, so it is chart-inactive and out of the nav. Growth inside a Roth is income to neither return: `usRetirementAccountIds("roth_ira")` (`usRetirementAccounts.ts`, the accounts whose import key starts with `usRetirementAccountImportKeyPrefix`) is the one rule, read by the F22's `loadF22Dividends` (a Roth from-leg stays out of 1104 / 748 / 1018) and by the generator's dividend details (no `movement_dividend_details` row: the custodian issues no 1099-DIV), never a name (`usRetirementAccounts.test.ts`). The 1040 draft reads no dividends. W-2s are derived from the stubs and checked, not stored. `testPerson` has no US chapter. **Two rentals and the US card (same date, wave 3):** `person.ts` has `houses: House[]` — Centro (1.800 UF bought 2020-03, 360 UF pie, lender «HIPOTECARIA PACIFICO», DFL-2 55 m² reception 2016, rented from 2020-04 at 380k) and Vitacura (rented from 2024-06 at 1,15 M) — each with its own `propertyAccountImportKey(key)` / `loanAccountImportKey(key)`, `lender_pattern` role, pie, cuotas (a Chilean card line under the lender's merchant) and prepagos; the agency forwards the gross rent and debits its 7 % the same day; every rent credit carries a `rent` override, gastos comunes and contribuciones are bill rows of the place (Centro's contribuciones halved while `dfl2_contribuciones_halved_through` lasts, re-indexed by the UF at each lease anniversary, one vacancy month a year). **Every regular cuota's card line gets its `funded_deposit` link:** `generate.ts` calls `syncMortgageExpenseDepositLinksFromSheet` directly after the card statements and the mortgage payments — without it the whole cuota counted as a gasto beside the dividendo deposit and the flows overview's «sin explicar» read one cuota a month from 2024-04 (the pre-plan demo had it) — and `invariants.ts` asserts `cash_unexplained` within 1.000 pesos every month of the story's own conversion costs (2026-11-10; the overview's `cash_fx` leg carries the fx move on the dollars held in checking since): the bank's spread on each conversion between two checking accounts (a sale at `fx × 0,992`, a purchase at `÷ fx × 0,995`), dollars at the sell rate the app holds them at, and the half spread on the dollar flows the legs count at the mid (the US salary and the other single-leg rows of a dollar checking account, its card autopays) — worst month 3 pesos (it read 150k of tolerance and 37.040 after an fx estimate of its own before). The US card «Lonestar Rewards» (opened 2024-07, closes on the 15th, US$95 a year) carries the US variable spending and is paid by autopay from US checking (a `pago_tarjeta` in dollars, 28 of 28 paired); rent, utilities and the wire stay debits. `assignPurchasesToRealEstateExpenses(items)` (`realEstateExpenseMatching.ts`) writes a batch of bills in one transaction reading the expense lines once (the single assign calls it; ~150 bills × 0,8 s before). Known: `syncExpenseLinksNow()` inside a transaction takes ~46 s on the demo (`dbStateMemo` is bypassed there, so `listRealEstatePropertyAccountIds` rebuilds the nav model per call) — the generator calls the mortgage sync outside one; every category write in the app runs that sync. The demo person's target is US$460k (`netWorthTargetUsd`, restated 2026-11-10 from 445k with the salary tuning, and on 2026-11-09 from 400k once the F22 taxed the US salary: the US savings rate and the two rentals' equity make it the honest figure; the synthetic market reads US$461k, ±25 % for the real-market plays). The About page tells the US chapter (`demo.about.story` / `storyTax`) and the tour has a «Retirement, Chile and US» step on the Retiro page and a «Two returns each April» step on the tax page's tabs.
- **The lifestyle is one multiplier over a per-city cost-of-living table (2026-11-09; `demoData/costOfLiving.ts`).** `CityCostOfLiving` states, per city (`santiago`, `austin`), the monthly baseline of a single mid-high-income person per category in the city's currency at a reference month — Numbeo-style, hand-tuned, never scraped (the story's assumptions, so stated in the file) — and how each row is paid: `drawn` (merchant draws from the city's pool), `bills` (the household bills by share), `list` (subscriptions and the gym at list prices; the multiplier does not apply) or `housing` (the lease / owner models pay it). The person's lifestyle is a dimensionless multiplier m (the ratchet, concave in smoothed income; `lifestyleReal` is kept as the level in Santiago basket units, m = level ÷ 525k) applied to the city he lives in that month: budget = m × baseline × season × the city's price index (Santiago the real IPC through the UF as before, Austin a yearly 3 % CPI-ish rate); the hardcoded category weight maps are gone and Santiago's baselines are the old shares × 525k bit for bit, so the Chilean history is byte-identical. Abroad the ratchet reads last month's US net pay at Austin purchasing power (`santiagoBasketPerUnit`, ≈ 401 basket-pesos per dollar), so m rises with the US pay (1,04 → 1,24 by 2026-11; Austin's baselines were tuned so the US total stayed within 10 %). **Austin's debit era and card era differ:** groceries, rent, utilities, the wire and the arrival one-offs (IKEA, Wayfair, Home Depot) are debit; once the Lonestar card opens the drawn rows go on the card — restaurants, delivery, transport (rideshare and CapMetro until the car), fun, clothing, personal care, healthcare, others — with Austin seasons (December gifts, Thanksgiving groceries, SXSW in March with a wristband, ACL Festival in October) and a May long weekend in Mexico City whose six charges abroad each carry a «FOREIGN TRANSACTION FEE» at 3 % (a section-3 line, `fee: true`, billed like the annual fee); no interest, he pays in full. Subscriptions: the US side on the card (Spotify, iCloud, ChatGPT, GitHub, Notion from the move; Netflix, NYTimes, Hulu with the card), the Chilean side keeps Entel and Disney+ and adds El Mercurio and Estadio TNT from the move (`onlyAbroad`), no service on both sides, each Chilean card still billing monthly. Category rules are global per vendor, so a new merchant must not share a vendor with one in another category (Prime → Hulu; the arrival's Target → Wayfair).
- **Generated ingest history (2026-11-07; `server/src/demoData/ingestHistory.ts`).** The generator's last step writes what the scheduler would have recorded over `person.ingestHistoryDays` days (45 for the demo, 3 for the Vitest person) ending the night before `today`: an `ingest.nightly` job + `ingest_runs` row at 22:00 Chile and an hourly poll at :30 (with the Santander balance poll), through the real writers (`enqueueJob` / `claimJob` / `completeJob`, `markIngestRunRequested`, `recordDailyRun` / `recordHourlyEmailRun`, `recordBankCupoCapture`, `applyBankAccountBalances`, the `cc_bank_cupo_check` task), re-dated to each run's instant. Statement and cartola mails land in the hour polls import them; a bank login fails about once a month and a mail step about once in 240 polls, each recovering on the next run. Per-day seeded streams. On the demo the import-sync panels hide «Run now», «Retry», «Cancel» and «Next», and say the history is generated; Notifications does not mark messages read.
- **The demo is writable, a little (2026-11-07; `server/src/demoWritePolicy.ts`, `demoWritable.ts`).** One shared database. Open: an expense line's category («Único»), assigning an existing big group, marking a credit a refund, a purchase's cuotas count, line pieces, and manual expenses whose description is one of a fixed list (free text would show to every visitor). Everything else keeps `403 demo_read_only`, and the client hides those controls on the demo (`client/src/demoEdits.ts`). 100 writes per daily visitor id (`429 demo_write_limit`; no visitor id = bot = 403). The database returns to `<demo db>.pristine` (a `VACUUM INTO` copy saved by the generator, or at boot when missing) once something was written and either the demo has been idle 30 minutes or it is the first check after 04:00 Chile: every table emptied and refilled from the attached copy in one transaction with foreign keys off, `mark_input_changes` cleared, `clearAggregationCache()`.
- **`SERVE_CLIENT_DIST=1`** (`server/src/staticClientDist.ts`): the API server serves the built client (`client/dist`) with an SPA fallback for non-`/api` GETs — one service, same-origin, no `CORS_ALLOWED_ORIGINS` needed. Hashed `/assets` are cached immutable; missing assets 404 (never the SPA fallback); `index.html` is `no-cache`. Fails fast at boot if `client/dist/index.html` is missing (`npm run build -w nw-tracker-client`).
- **`render.yaml`**: build = `npm install --include=dev` (Render sets `NODE_ENV=production`, which would skip devDeps) + client build; start = `npm run serve -w nw-tracker-server` (**tsx from source** — the compiled `dist/` would not ship `server/migrations/*.sql`); health check `/api/health` (exempt from auth); `PORT` injected by Render.
- **Deploy:** Render dashboard → New → **Blueprint** → connect this repo (picks up `render.yaml`). No secrets to set — the demo has no login. ✅ **The repo is safe to make public** — tree and full git history are free of personal data: balances/amounts purged (migrations squashed 2026-07-07), account/contract numbers redacted, cfraser data purged, and card last4s scrubbed from all history 2026-07-08 (per-card config lives in gitignored `cfraser/*.json`; tests use synthetic cards). The only personal data is the author's name/email as git attribution (plus the extracted landing page's contact link in git history), which is intended for a portfolio repo. Full pre-scrub history is bundled in `~/Documents/backups/nw-tracker/`.
- **Visit analytics (replaced the login log):** `server/src/demoAnalytics.ts` records one `demo_visits` row per visitor per day, with **no login, no cookie and no stored IP** — the visitor id is `hmac(daily key, ip|user-agent)` where the daily key mixes in the calendar day, so ids cannot be correlated across days; referrers are truncated to their origin and bot/uptime user agents are skipped. Page views and endpoint timings are the shared usage log's (see «Usage and performance log»): the demo writes it like the personal instance, each page view tagged with the visitor id (a bot's is not recorded), and `?src=` on the landing URL rides the page-view beacon onto the visit, so publish tagged links (`?src=linkedin` / `cv` / `homepage`). Storage is a **separate SQLite file** (`DEMO_ANALYTICS_DB` → `/var/data/…` on the persistent disk) since the demo DB is regenerated every deploy: the visit table plus the usage tables (`usageDb.ts` applies migration 247 there at first use and, once, copied the old `demo_pageviews` per-day counts in as `untimed` views, 2026-10-30); salt in `DEMO_ANALYTICS_SALT`. The demo refuses `/api/usage/*` reports (it is public; the client build hides the panel tab via `VITE_DEMO_MODE`, derived from `DEMO_MODE` in `vite.config.ts`). **The author's own browsers are counted apart (2026-10-31):** opening the demo once with `?owner=<DEMO_OWNER_CODE>` (Render-generated; read it from the service's Environment tab) sets an HttpOnly `nw_demo_owner` cookie for 5 years (`demoOwnerCookieMiddleware`), and every visit carrying it is flagged `demo_visits.owner` (sticky within the day); the report leaves owner visits and their page views out of every table and lists them last. Per browser and device; no code configured = off. The visitor id hashes Cloudflare's `CF-Connecting-IP`, not `req.ip`: behind Render's Cloudflare edge `req.ip` changed request by request, splitting one person into a dozen ids (history before 2026-10-31 has that artifact). The README's demo link is tagged `?src=github`. Report: **`npm run demo:visits`** (visitors/day, src, referrers, top pages with load times, browse depth, slowest endpoints, owner visits) — run it from the Render shell. `DEMO_MODE` gates the visit middleware, so local mode records no visits.
- **Domains (Namecheap `crfrsr.io`):** blueprint requests `nw-tracker.crfrsr.io` on the web service (paid `starter` plan — no cold-start spin-down). The landing page (personal projects + work history) lives in its own repo — [crfrsr-site](https://github.com/cristianfraser/crfrsr-site) (`~/Projects/crfrsr-site`; extracted from `homepage/` 2026-07) — on **Cloudflare Pages**, with `crfrsr.io` + `www.crfrsr.io` pointing at it. Namecheap Advanced DNS: `@`/`www` → the Cloudflare Pages host, `CNAME nw-tracker` → the nw-tracker-demo `.onrender.com` host; the existing `poketeam` CNAME stays. Render auto-issues TLS once DNS verifies.
- **Local rehearsal:** `.claude/launch.json` `server-demo`/`client-demo` (ports 3210/5210; preset `DEMO_MODE=1` / `VITE_DEMO_MODE=1` so the read-only guard, the visit analytics and the usage log are exercised, writing to `server/data/demo-analytics.db`), or boot the exact prod shape: `PORT=… DEMO_MODE=1 SERVE_CLIENT_DIST=1 NW_TRACKER_TEST_DB=/tmp/demo.db npm run serve -w nw-tracker-server` after a client build.

## Schema baseline (fresh DBs)

`initSchema()` executes `server/src/schemaBaseline.ts` — the full schema dumped from the live DB (as of migration 155), all `IF NOT EXISTS`. On a brand-new DB it marks migrations ≤ 155 as pre-applied. **Migrations ≤ 155 were squashed into the baseline 2026-07-07 and their files deleted** (personal-data ones also purged from git history; copies of everything live outside the repo in `~/Documents/backups/nw-tracker/{removed,squashed}-migrations/`). The only pre-baseline files kept are the 8 reference-row seeds in `BASELINE_REFERENCE_DATA_MIGRATIONS` (db.ts) — idempotent category/uniqueness seeds the schema-only baseline skips, which still run on fresh DBs. A recorded-but-missing file is harmless to the runner (the live DB keeps all 155 ids in `schema_migrations`), so numbering gaps are expected; post-baseline migrations (156+: fintual flow_kind, depto_payments + movement_mirror_merges + staging drop, accounts.import_key) apply normally everywhere. A migration may pair with a `POST_MIGRATION_HOOKS` entry in `db.ts` (pure TS module, runs inside the migration transaction — see `legacyNoteBackfills.ts` for 157). Tables dropped by a migration must ALSO be removed from the baseline, or every boot resurrects an empty shell via `IF NOT EXISTS`. When squashing again: `npx tsx scripts/regenerate-schema-baseline.ts --last <newest-migration>` (from `server/`), then delete the newly-covered files (keep the reference seeds). Migration SQL is naively split on `;` (no string-literal lexing) — no triggers or `;`/`--` inside literals; use a post-migration hook in `db.ts` for those.

## Server verbose logging

- **`DEBUG_VERBOSE=1`** — enables all channels below (stderr).
- **`DEBUG_HTTP=1`** — `[api] -->` / `[api] <--` for every incoming Express request (method, path, status, ms).
- **`DEBUG_HTTP_OUT=1`** — `[http-out]` for outbound `fetch` (BCentral, Yahoo, the Superintendencia de Pensiones, Fintual sync).
- **`DEBUG_DB=1`** — `[db]` for SQLite `prepare`/`exec` slower than **`DEBUG_DB_SLOW_MS`** (default 20). **`DEBUG_DB_ALL=1`** logs every statement.
- **`DEBUG_PERF=1`** — `[heavy] …ms` for labeled expensive work (`server/src/heavyWork.ts`).

## Usage and performance log (2026-10-30; migration 247, `usageLog.ts`, `usageReport.ts`, `usageDb.ts`, `/panel/usage`)

- **What is recorded, so optimization follows real use** (one log for the personal primary and the hosted demo). Every `/api` request (`usageLogMiddleware`, mounted first): duration from arrival to the last byte, status, the payload size before compression (`res.locals.usageBodyBytes` from `jsonResponse.ts`, else `Content-Length`; none for a 304) and the aggregation-cache outcome — `requestCacheStats.ts` opens an `AsyncLocalStorage` store per request and `getAggregationCached` reports each lookup into it: warm = every lookup hit, cold = at least one miss, neither = none made. Aggregated per UTC hour × method × route PATTERN (`req.baseUrl + req.route.path`, `(unmatched)` for a 404) × the query parameters that change the work (`USAGE_KEY_QUERY_PARAMS`, an explicit list: `unit`, `days`, `period`, `portfolio_group`, … — never `account_id` or free-text filters) into `usage_endpoint_hourly`: count, total / max ms, 4xx / 5xx, bytes, cache warm / cold and a 15-bucket histogram (`USAGE_HISTOGRAM_BOUNDS_MS`, columns `h00..h14`) the report estimates p50 / p95 from. Every in-app route change (`useUsagePageTiming`, `client/src/usagePageTiming.ts`, beacon `POST /api/usage/page-view`, not itself timed) into `usage_page_views`: path, route (numeric segments as `:id`), whether it was the load's first page, and its settle time — from the route change (page start for the first page) to the end of the last query fetch, once nothing has fetched for 400 ms; a view left, hidden or still fetching after 60 s has no time (`outcome`). Nothing but the path is stored (plus, on the demo, the daily visitor hash, `visitor`).
- **Requests only touch memory:** the buffer is written once a minute in one transaction (`flushUsageLog`, upsert adding onto the hour's row), so a restart loses at most that minute; endpoint rows older than 90 days are deleted once a day; page views are kept.
- **Where it is written:** `usageDb()` — the main DB on the personal instance, the persistent `DEMO_ANALYTICS_DB` file on the demo. `USAGE_LOG_ENABLED` (default on) is defaulted to 0 by `BACKGROUND_JOBS_ENABLED=0` (every `.claude/launch.json` server), so secondary dev servers never write the primary's DB; demo mode defaults it to 1 first (it writes its own file). While it is off the beacon answers 204 and records nothing.
- **`/panel/usage`** (`GET /api/usage/report?days=1|7|30|90`, `GET /api/usage/endpoint-trend?method=&route=&params=&days=`): most visited pages (views, settle p50 / p95 / max — exact over the stored rows — views left before settling, mean queries fetched), endpoints by p95 (count, p50 / p95 / max, total server time, warm-cache share, errors, mean size) and, for the endpoint picked, its daily trend over every Chile day of the window. The server computes every figure; the page renders rows. Refused on the demo (404 `usage_report_not_public`), whose figures `npm run demo:visits` prints.

## Dashboard performance profiling

- **`npm run profile:dashboard`** (from `server/`): sequential timings for dashboard builders on the test/dev DB (`NW_TRACKER_TEST_DB` or default `server/data/nw-tracker.test.db`).
- **`DEBUG_PERF=1`** (or **`DEBUG_VERBOSE=1`**) on the server logs `[heavy] …ms` spans for labeled work (`server/src/heavyWork.ts`) during HTTP requests.
- Home page: **`GET /api/dashboard/page-bundle`** — one client call replaces the old 5-way fan-out. The whole bundle response is served from the aggregation cache (`dashboard.page_bundle|<unit>`, built by the warmer; cached value is the in-flight promise so concurrent cold requests share one build). Every explicit invalidation drops it — `invalidateCcBillingDetail` and `invalidateMarketDataAggregations` are the two funnel points — so a warm request is ~5ms; only requests landing in an invalidation→rewarm gap pay the full build.
- Nav strip (sidebar / group pages): **`GET /api/dashboard/nav-context`** — account rows, card metrics and the value map, no net-worth series (2026-11-02: it carried one nothing read, as the account bundle carried every retirement account and the home bundle the deposits by category — all three dropped) (client fetches only on group/liabilities routes and account detail when the nav node has routable children; home uses `page-bundle` only). **Scoped to the page (2026-11-02; `?root=<nav node slug>`, required; `navContextScope.ts`):** the card metrics of the node's subtree and of the groups any node in it hosts (`linked_card_slugs`: Efectivo's credit-card cards), the account rows those nodes hold plus every liability account (the property card names the mortgage), and the value map under the node; the bucket totals, layout and liabilities breakdown stay whole. The Pasivos tree is searched first (net worth carries a childless `liabilities` node). The client keys the query on the root and merges the payload into the stored nav snapshot (`mergeNavContextIntoSnapshotCache`; nothing is written without one, since a page's slice does not cover the tree); the warmer builds each visited group page's own. Demo sizes: 95 KB whole → 4–28 KB per page. **The strip waits for nothing else on the page (2026-11-02):** nav-context is requested beside the page's own requests (it used to wait for the bundle, a leftover of the perturbed-snapshot design), the strip's rows come from it as soon as it lands, and the strip is dimmed until then rather than until the page has loaded (on a cold demo server: Acciones' strip live at 1,7 s, the page at 4,5 s).
- Dominant cost is usually the portfolio-group totals aggregation inside **`getDashboardValuationTimeseries`** / **`getGroupValuationTimeseries`**; `@heavy` marks those entry points in source.
- **JSON responses are serialized once and compressed (2026-10-31; `jsonResponse.ts`).** `jsonResponseMiddleware` replaces `res.json` on `/api` (routes keep calling it): the body, `Content-Type` and weak ETag are what Express's own `res.json` sends (the ETag over the uncompressed bytes, so a 304 works across encodings); a body of 1 KB or more carries `Vary: Accept-Encoding` and goes out brotli (preferred on a q tie — browsers offer it on HTTPS only) or gzip when accepted, never over an existing `Content-Encoding` or `no-transform`. A payload a response cache marks with `markJsonResponseCacheable` keeps its bytes, ETag and compressed forms in a WeakMap keyed by the payload object, so they live and die with the cache entry (no key of their own to invalidate). Marked: the group daily-series and account daily-series responses (`groupDailySeriesResponse.ts`; the account one is cached since this change, `daily.series|response|account|`, dropped by API writes like the group one), `cachedGroupResponse`, the page bundle, nav snapshot / context, the account detail bundle and both card ledgers. **Only an immutable cached object may be marked** — an object a route mutates between requests would be served stale bytes; everything else is stringified per response and compressed at gzip 1 / brotli 1 (cached ones at gzip 6 / brotli 5, once per build). Synthetic demo DB: Pasivos daily full history 982 KB → 74 KB gzip / 31 KB brotli, a warm hit 6,8 → 1,5 ms identity, 0,5 ms gzip.

## Server Vitest (SQLite)

- Vitest uses **`server/data/nw-tracker.test.db`**, never `nw-tracker.db`. Both are gitignored (`server/data/*.db`).
- **`NW_TRACKER_TEST_DB`** is set in `server/vitest.config.ts` (`test.env`) and in the server **`npm run test`** script so it is present before `db.ts` loads.
- **`server/src/vitest.globalSetup.ts`**: when `nw-tracker.test.db` is missing, generates the **Vitest person** (`generate-demo-data.ts --test`, `testPerson` in `src/demoData/person.ts`) — tests must not depend on personal data. Delete `nw-tracker.test.db` to rebuild; the file is never refreshed automatically, so a stale pre-synthetic dev-DB copy on disk silently keeps being used (symptom: suites failing on missing `demo:*` fixture accounts or real-data invariants — delete the test DB). Escape hatch: `NW_TRACKER_TEST_FROM_DEV=1` copies the local `nw-tracker.db` instead (reproducing an issue against real data).
- **`server/src/vitest.setup.ts`** → **`server/src/test/vitestDbSeed.ts`**: ensures an isolated Santander CC master `credit_card_master|santander|vitest-fixture` (never a real card) for merge/import tests.
- Override the test file with `NW_TRACKER_TEST_DB` (absolute path, basename under `server/data/`, or `:memory:`). Vitest uses `pool: "forks"` with one sequential fork (`fileParallelism: false`) — better-sqlite3 segfaults nondeterministically in worker threads at teardown, killing the run after a few files; parallel forks would race migrations on the single SQLite file.
- To remove Vitest fixture rows from **`nw-tracker.db`** (e.g. after running tests against the wrong DB): `sqlite3 server/data/nw-tracker.db < server/scripts/cleanup-cc-vitest-pollution.sql`.

## UI headings (2026-10-26)

Section titles follow the base heading rules in `client/src/styles/base.css`: `h2` 1.1rem, `h3` 1rem, `h4` 0.95rem, all weight 600 in `var(--muted)`. The levels are the document outline (a page's section is an `h2`, its surfaces `h3`); a component never sets a heading's size, weight or colour inline — the Pasivos page's «Historial» / «Detalle por mes» (`h3`) rendered in the browser default (body-white, bold) beside muted `h2`s because no `h3` rule existed, and ~35 headings carried an inline `fontSize`, six of them the `h2`'s. The two deliberate exceptions are explicit: `.chart-panel-title` keeps every chart title at the `h2` look whatever its level, and the checking ledger-anchor form's heading matches its form text (`--font-size-ui`).

## UI tables — desktop/mobile sync

Parallel-renderings rule: GUIDELINES.md. In this app: desktop columns are `<td className="desktop-only">`; the mobile rendering is `<TableMobileCard>` / `<TableMobileCardRow>` inside a `<td className="mobile-only">`. Both variants are usually in the same component or a companion file (e.g. `CreditCardSections.tsx` + `CreditCardPurchaseMobileCard.tsx`).

**A table shows its cards whenever its desktop rendering does not fit (2026-11-01; `useTableFit.ts`, `Table` `fitToWidth`).** The cards show below the 880 viewport line and, at any width, when the table's desktop columns are wider than its own `.table-wrap`: `Table` measures the desktop rendering after every render and on every width change of the wrap (class removed, measured, set again — synchronous, before paint, no React state) and sets `.table-wrap--cards`, which tables.css styles exactly like the narrow viewport (the two rule sets sit together). On by default for every `table--parallel-mobile` table, so both renderings always switch together. A fixed breakpoint cannot do this: a table is as wide as its data (peso balances in the hundreds of millions, «$X → US$Y» conversions), and between 880 and ~1100 the content column (694 px at 880) was narrower than the Rentabilidad, «Detalle por mes» and «Flujos de patrimonio» tables, which scrolled the main column sideways on the dashboard and the group pages. The Rentabilidad table (`PeriodReturnsTable`, one markup flipped by CSS, fixed layout with a 34rem / 42rem-with-benchmark floor) flips by a container query on its wrap (`period-returns`) at its floor instead of by the viewport, so it stays wide at 600–879 when it fits. Never solve an overflow with a scroll box (see `.table-wrap`). Unrelated but found the same day: a right-edge card's number-flow mask spills ~6 px past the content (cards have no padding), so a card block at the content edge clips it (`overflow-x: clip`: `.cards-strip`, the home «Flujos» period cards). Check: every page at 375 / 599 / 600 / 879 / 880 / 1024 / 1279 / 1280 / 1439 / 1440 / 1600 / 1920 with `.layout-main` scrollWidth = clientWidth.

**Expense line lists (2026-10-31; `CreditCardExpenseLinesTable.tsx`).** The month, facturación, «sin categoría», big-group and deposit-matched expense lists are one responsive list, not a table: no horizontal scroll, the same rendering at every width (a container query moves the pills under the merchant in narrow lists). A row is the origin icon (`ExpenseOriginIcon`: card / bank / manual / payslip from the line's structured `origin`, the account in a hover-or-tap card), the date, the big-group pill once set, then the category pill (`PillPicker`: a popover of option pills, × on hover to clear, «Único» inside the category popover; an unset big group is picked from the extras), the merchant with its note and an ⓘ card (counterparty, receipt, deposit link), the amount with the installment detail in superscript, and a ⋯ button that opens the row's extras (big group while unset, note editor, pieces, cuotas, delete), with a red corner badge when the line waits on the user. The header is three sort buttons (date, category, amount). A popover anchored inside a `Modal` renders into its `<dialog>` (`AnchoredPopover`), since `showModal()` puts the dialog in the top layer.

**The flows list (2026-11-02; `FlowRows.tsx`, under `FlowsTable`'s filters).** «Flujos de patrimonio» and every account's flows are a row list like the detalle rows, not a table: while every row fits on one line the rows are subgrids of the list under a header line — date, type (a small pill), accounts, amount (units as a superscript), note (the one flexible column, cut with an ellipsis) — and below the list's breakpoint (a container query on its frame, `flow-rows-wide` 58rem for a list that names both accounts, `flow-rows-narrow` 44rem on an account page; see the detalle rows) a row is three lines: date and type with the amount on the right, the accounts, the note. **A transfer between two accounts of a group shows once (`assembleFlowRows`, `flowsApi.ts`):** the origin's row, `internal`, its amount unsigned and a type that names no side (`internalTransferFlowType`: a kind-less transfer is «Traspaso <CODE>» in its own currency, or «Cambio de divisas» when it converts between any two, a share purchase or card payment keeps its type); the account and bucket filters match either side (`counterpart_bucket_slug`). A transfer with one side outside the group keeps that leg, signed. Every movement row carries `from_account_*` / `to_account_*` and `money_direction` (`moneyDirectionForAccount`: a transfer by its leg, anything else by its signed amount in its own currency, then its units; null only on a movement of nothing — a zero opening-balance anchor; any other undecidable row throws). A label that names a currency takes it from the movement (`movementFlowTypeLabel(type, {currency, counter_currency})`): «Depósito CLP», «Retiro USD», «Compra USD / Venta CLP» are the row's codes, never the kind's name. The accounts cell (`flowAccountsCell`): on a group or the dashboard always origin → target (money from outside «→ account», money leaving «account →»); on an account's page the page's account is not named, the arrow says whether money left (→) or arrived (←), then the other account.

**Names an expense row shows (2026-10-31; `expenseDisplayNames.ts`, `vendorCatalog.ts`, `expenseNameText.ts`, `chilePlaces.ts`, migrations 248–250).** Display only: a line's `merchant` stays the bank's text (merchant rules, the receipt matcher and the real-estate matcher read it). The server adds `display_merchant` (the bank's clutter dropped — « (T)», «COMPRAS P.A.T.», «, SANTIAGO», a leading account number, «Agustinas » or an old listing's «COMPRA» / «Compra Nacional» —, the processor prefix taken off into `merchant_processor` — MERPAGO\*, PAYU \*, HAULMER\*, DL \*, DP \*, PAYPAL \*, SQ \*… —, then the vendor catalog; when the catalog does not know the bank's name and a shop's or processor's own receipt is linked (Shopify, Flow, Pago Fácil, the shops' order mails — never a platform selling for others: Uber Eats, MercadoLibre, the App Store, stays), the receipt's payee is the merchant («COMERCIALLASI» → «Corrales del Sur») and the label, now repeating it, is hidden; a payslip line reads its employer without the legal suffix and its words spelled («Impuesto Unico (LQ) · EMPRESA CHILE SPA» → «Impuesto Único · Empresa Chile»); a card merchant the listing cut — «PAYU \*UBER EA»: the web table and the feed's pending rows cut at 15 characters, and the bank collapses spaces before sending it, so the stored length proves nothing — reads as the longer merchant it starts on the same account's lines, only when every such merchant reads the same through the catalog; a checking transfer with a paired mail reads «Transf a <label>») and `link_label` (`arrow`, `text`, `nights`, `inline`): a receipt's payee, never the processor, « - X» and a known brand + branch as «Brand (X)», MercadoLibre sellers title-cased with «+N», a URL its host, an e-mail or placeholder nothing, a stay its city + nights; an App Store app before its tagline; a transfer by RUT through the catalog, else the name the RUT is known by (a contact the user named over the bank's capitals, then the most frequent), a company without its legal suffix, a person as first name + first surname. `inline` is false when the shown merchant already contains the label. The row shows the label after the note, the row's extras repeat it, the ⓘ card carries the full detail plus the bank's own text. **The vendor catalog** (`vendor_catalog`: `exact` / `prefix` / `branch` «Brand (Branch)» / `rut`, folded patterns; a branch the name already sets apart, «(Isidora)» or after « - », is unwrapped, not wrapped again; a branch that is only a legal form is dropped; a branch's places are spelled as written — «PENALOLEN» → «Peñalolén» — and a name the statement may have cut (its 22-character column) completes a branch ending mid-word to the one place it starts — «VINA DEL MA» → «Viña del Mar», store numbers kept —; when it could be several places the row carries `branch_choices`, its ⋯ shows the red badge and «¿Qué sucursal?», and the pick is stored as a `choice` row (`POST /api/flows/expenses/vendor-branch-choice`, matched after user rows and before seed rows, never touched by the loader)) holds generic brands as migration seed rows; the user's own entries (people, RUTs, places) are personal data, loaded on the live DB from untracked `cfraser/vendor-catalog.json` by `server/scripts/load-vendor-catalog.ts [--apply]` (report-first, replaces every user row) and matched before seed rows. `server/scripts/report-expense-link-labels.ts` lists the labels, counterparties by RUT and merchants as printed, to grow the catalog. Mail-written transfer notes no longer carry the payee's bank («Transf a <payee>»), and transfer notices are repaired of UTF-8 read as Latin-1 on import (`textRepair.ts`); `server/scripts/archive/sanitize-transfer-mail-notes.ts [--apply]` rewrote the stored ones (merchant rules copied to the new key).

**Display units are currencies (2026-11-12; migration 312, `displayUnits.ts`, `rates.ts`, `fxPairsSync.ts`, `docs/any-currency-plan.md`).** The global toggle offers every **core** currency of `currencies` (CLP, USD, EUR; `GET /api/currencies` gives code, symbol, minor digits, tier — the client's picker and formatters read it). A request names its unit with `?unit=<code>` (`requestDisplayUnit`; the old `include_usd` / `include_uf` flags are retired and ignored) and every payload is built in that one unit — no `*_usd` twins, no fixed-unit `Record`s, no `unit === "usd"` branches outside `rates.ts` / `valueConvert.ts`: the dashboard rows, nav cards, value map and bundles (`nav_strip` 20, `group_shell` 3), valuation and daily series, performance and Rentabilidad, benchmarks, export, flows (overview, gastos, deposits, P/L, reconciliation, income, payroll), latest movements, cards and liabilities, market ticker, watchlist, market series, projections, wealth percentile. **Rates:** `rates.ts` walks a direct pair first, else through USD, else through CLP (UF and Banco Central's EUR/CLP are peso pairs). EUR/USD lives in `fx_rates` (Yahoo `EURUSD=X` since the portfolio start, write-once, bars dated on the London day and due after London midnight; global-sync source `fx_pairs`, `scripts/sync-fx-pairs.ts`); EUR/CLP reads `eur_daily` directly. Every core pair has a live rate (see «Every core pair has a live rate»): EUR/USD reads its live quote today while the fx day is open, and today's EUR/CLP crosses the two. A flow converts at its day's rate (`flowEventInUnit` / `convertFlow`: its own dollar leg in USD, the buy rate for pesos → dollars, the day's mid for any other pair). **Native currencies are not display units:** a card's dollar sub-balance, a statement's `facturado_usd`, the card billing ledger and facturaciones (pesos), the tax return and 1040, groceries and the fx bid/ask gaps keep their own. `ZERO_CLOSE_EPS` (`perfKernel.ts`) needs each currency's smallest coin. Projections in EUR use the USD inflation default (2,5 %). **Fx coverage** (`fxCoverage.ts`, the page bundle's and market series' `fx_coverage`, `FxCoverageBanner`) checks, per pair, every pair a conversion into the requested unit walks from each held currency (movements' both legs and valuations, plus pesos and dollars; the walk is `conversionPairs` in `rates.ts`, the rows `pairStoredDates` / `pairHasStoredQuote`): `{unit, pairs: [{base, quote, complete, first_missing_date, …}], …}`; the copy names the pair and the unit. The warmers build every core currency (`warmedDisplayUnits`). The synthetic market (test and demo databases) writes EUR/USD ~1,10 and EUR/CLP = USD/CLP × EUR/USD; the demo's real market loads EUR/USD and its asset carries `fx_rates` (asset schema 2 — the home server must publish a new asset before the next demo release). Checked on a copy of the 2026-11-12 database: every CLP and USD payload equal to the previous code's (renames aside), EUR lines and rows equal the pesos at the day's EUR/CLP.

**An expense line's money is its own currency (2026-11-11/12; `lineMoney.ts`, `wireExpenseLine.ts`, contract `Money` / `LineConverted`).** On the wire a line carries `amount: {value, currency}` (the currency it happened in), `converted: {value, currency, rate_source} | null` in the REQUESTED unit (every lines endpoint takes `?unit=`; null when the line is already in it) and `value_clp` (its pesos, for the peso documents: the facturación and month modal totals, financing candidates). `rate_source`: `native`, `paid` / `live` / `pay_by` (a card's dollars in pesos at the rate its facturación was paid, today's while unpaid, the pay-by − 1 rate when none is on file: `facturacionUsdRateWithSourceResolver`; a dollar payment line at the pesos paid for it, `usdPaymentLineClpResolver`), `day` (the day's rate). Into a third currency (EUR) a line goes through its pesos at that rate and keeps the pesos' source. The builders' `amount_clp` / `amount_usd` / `amount_usd_at_expense` are construction data inside the server (the in-pipeline matching steps read them); finished-line readers ask `lineValueIn(l, unit)` / `lineAmount(l)` / `linePesos(l)`. The client never converts: `formatLineAmount` / `MoneyAmount` show the native amount and the converted one beside it («US$13,30 (≈ $13.083)»; «≈» = `live`), and below 450 px of list width (a container query) only the converted value. A peso document (a utility bill, demo place bills) matches only a peso line; real-estate bills and declared recurring payments still read a dollar line's pesos. Manual expenses are in any core currency since migration 316 (the flow triggers read the row's amount and currency).

**A card-to-card facturado payment is one transfer (2026-11-11; migration 311 `card_to_card_payments`, `cardToCardPayments.ts`, `scripts/sync-card-to-card-payments.ts`).** Each part of a facturado payment record — a one-shot charge (its line's pesos and day) or a `finances_facturado` plan (its principal on its purchase day) — is one `pago_tarjeta` transfer from the paying card master to the paid card master, recorded in `card_to_card_payments` (movement, financing link, part kind + ref; deleting the row deletes its movement). Derived state: `syncCardToCardPaymentTransfers` creates, replaces and deletes them after every card write (after the facturado matcher) and after a hand-made financing link changes; a dollar part throws (`usdDebtPaymentsForAccount` would). Both card lines stay statement evidence and every reader of money skips the transfer (audited: deposits, cash, card owed and P/L unchanged; checked identical on the 2026-11-11 database, 15 transfers 2025-09 → 2026-11). Guards: the nightly payment-mirror converter never offers a paid card's PAGO a card-to-card transfer explains (`cardToCardPaymentEvidenceClaim`: a part, a day's parts or the whole record, inside `MATCH_WINDOWS.financing_payment`), and the manual card-payment task refuses a card as the source.

**A card payment is named by what it paid (2026-11-11; `expensePaymentNames.ts`, `payment_name` on the expense lines, `display_brand` on `CC_ISSUERS`).** Display only, from the payment records, never the bank's text: a one-shot charge recorded as paying another card's facturado (`cc_facturado_payment_charges`) and every line of a `finances_facturado` plan (its «Total» line and its cuotas) read «Pago Lider ·0101» (`pays_card`, the paid card); the payment line on the paid card reads «Pago con tarjeta Santander ·0781» (`paid_by_card`) when it is the whole facturado, or — a facturado paid in parts — one payment per charge or plan adding up to it, dated inside `MATCH_WINDOWS.financing_payment` of the latest paying day and matched by payment identity (`ccPaymentEvidenceKey`), so the statement PAGO that replaces a planted one keeps the name; a card payment line that is the card leg of a `pago_tarjeta` transfer from a bank account reads «Pago desde <account>» (`paid_from_account`; a divisas abono by its dollars). Any ambiguity names nothing. The client words it through i18n (`expenses.creditCard.paymentName.*`, `expensePaymentName`) in place of the merchant; the ⓘ card keeps the store's name and the bank's text. On the 2026-11-11 database: 27 `pays_card`, 15 `paid_by_card`, 227 `paid_from_account`.

**Expense category rules are global, keyed on the vendor (2026-10-31; migration 252, `expenseCategoryRules.ts`, `bankMerchantName.ts`).** A rule no longer belongs to a card or bank account: `expense_category_rules` holds a general rule per vendor and specific rules per vendor + link. The vendor (`vendorRuleKey`) is the vendor catalog's brand for the bank's text — a `branch` entry's brand without its branch, so every Jumbo branch is one «JUMBO», and a processor prefix is off, so «PAYU \*UBER EATS» and «UBER EATS» are one vendor — else the cleaned name; a rule learned on a name the web listing cut still reaches the full name (`vendorRuleKeyCandidates`). The link is a receipt's store (by brand), an App Store app, or a transfer's recipient (by RUT, else by name); a recipient rule ignores the bank's wording of the transfer (vendor `TRANSFERENCIA`). Precedence on a line: its own category («Único», a per-line override) > a specific rule > the general rule > «sin categoría»; the general rule decides where categories always did (`resolveCcExpenseCategory`), the specific one in a late step once the link is known (`withSpecificCategoryRules`, after the subscription step, for card and checking lines whose category only the bank's name decided, an «Único» row with no category included). Each line carries `category_link` (kind, key, label, `specific_default`) and `category_specific`; the category picker's «Específico» checkbox decides which rule a change writes, on by default except for food delivery (Uber Eats, Rappi), and the route takes it as `rule_link`. Migration 252's hook merged the 1.222 per-account rules into 431 vendor rules (a vendor whose old rules disagreed took the category most rules carried; `server/scripts/report-expense-category-rule-migration.ts --db=<file>` lists the merge and the conflicts, opening the file read-only) and dropped `cc_expense_merchant_categories`; the rule-copying between cards went with it.
